# Data consistency and pipeline review

Reviewed 2026-09-03 at HEAD `23d08ea`, including the uncommitted split-basis work of the same day, against the live database.

## Method

Twenty-two independent reviewers worked blind to one another. Fourteen covered the planned scope: six audited the live database read-only, eight reviewed pipeline code. A completeness critic then named the areas nobody had claimed, and eight further reviewers audited those.

Every finding had to survive an adversarial verifier that reproduced the evidence, looked for documented intent or an existing guard, and judged real impact, defaulting to refuted when unsure. Findings rated high or critical went to a second verifier that only tried to reproduce them, and the lower of the two severities was kept. A final pass merged findings that different reviewers had filed as the same defect and re-rated them.

55 findings were refuted and dropped. 160 survived, consolidating into 122 distinct defects.

## Assessment

Coverage was genuinely deep on the hot path — prices, splits, indicators, intraday and the MCP read layer were each audited to the level of independent recomputation, and the negative results are as informative as the findings: OHLC ordering, PK/FK integrity, holiday and weekend calendars, intraday bar alignment and write-path fidelity, dividend identity, GICS resolution and schema-vs-live column diffs all came back clean. The confirmed findings cluster into four believable themes: (1) frozen bases — anything storing a price- or share-denominated value that is not stock_prices or technical_indicators silently drifts as splits are re-applied; (2) unscheduled manual jobs — earnings, and (uncaught by any dimension) `sawa quarterly`, both months stale with doctor windows too loose to fire; (3) a dead universe — companies.active is never set false, no new listing has been added since 2026-05-25, and index membership is 110 days old; (4) an intraday session that is SIGKILLed daily and truncated by 20 minutes. The audit's blind spots are consistent: reviewers audited what a tool reads and skipped what nothing obviously reads. That left the entire fundamentals stack (146 MB, 408k rows) unowned, the stock_character subsystem (81 MB written nightly, zero consumers) unvalidated, the repositories layer that every Polygon-calling job depends on unread, and — the most consequential omission — operational substrate that no dimension claimed: the weekly Postgres backup has failed 14 consecutive times since 2026-05-31 because it still targets the docker socket after the podman migration, leaving the newest recoverable snapshot 102 days old and predating every migration and split repair in this audit. Two spot checks confirm the fundamentals gap is not theoretical (financial_ratios prices are frozen 10x high for KLAC and 2-5x for seven other tickers) and that the volume-collapse defect is understated at medium (70% of WHLR's bars carry volume=0 on days the stock demonstrably traded). On findings quality: the substance held up everywhere I probed, but roughly a dozen items are the same defect filed by two or three dimensions with divergent counts and severities, and the APH matview finding is a transient that self-heals at tonight's close. Deduplicate before sizing the fix queue. The working tree is green — 1,080 tests pass in 18 seconds, which every dimension but two declined to verify.

## Consolidation notes

160 findings consolidated into 122 groups; 29 groups contain duplicates (23 pairs, 4 triples, 1 quad, 1 quintuple), so 38 filings were duplicates of another reviewer's work. Every id is used exactly once.

Severity distribution: 1 critical, 27 high, 48 medium, 46 low.

Re-ratings I made against the reviewers' labels, with reasoning:
- UP. Volume collapse (3/144/146/150) to high: reviewers split medium/high, but the volume column is destroyed in place for 81 tickers, not merely rescaled, and the zeros are already baked into technical_indicators. "Loses data" is the rubric's high bar.
- UP. financial_ratios stale basis (98/107) to high from two independent mediums: get_financial_ratios returns `price` as a plain dated field (verified at mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:180-193, no basis label), so it contradicts get_stock_prices for the same ticker and date by up to ~800x.
- UP. Dividend-record abort (12/45) to medium from two lows: one bad provider row makes the entire corporate-actions step a no-op — no dividends AND no splits AND no heal — for up to 365 days.
- UP. raw_split_jump_present (44) to medium from low: its failure mode is committing a split multiplication over a correctly adjusted tail with no post-check.
- UP. drop_all_tables victims (91/152) to medium: one documented command destroys the only copy of the 379 quarantined rows and another application's production data.
- UP. Ticker-reuse splicing (0/46) to high: reviewer 46 rated its DFNS instance low, but the general defect corrupts every row-window value for ≥71 tickers by unbounded factors.
- UP. Coverage-check defect (117/156) to medium: these are the FAIL-severity checks meant to trigger a retry, and they cannot detect a partial run.
- DOWN. YTD base date (71) from high to medium: this is a convention choice worth roughly one session's move, exactly the case the prompt says is not high — though it is systematic and undocumented.
- DOWN. Index-constituent staleness (23) from high to medium: coverage drift, not wrong values.
- DOWN. Delisted-not-demoted (2/22) from high to medium: ~2.5% of the universe, affects breadth counts, serves no wrong prices.
- DOWN. Intraday SIGKILL (32/61) from critical to high: real daily unrecoverable loss, but bounded to the intraday tail that EOD rows later supersede. The backup failure is the only genuine critical — it is the one defect that leaves the system unrecoverable.
- DOWN. Split-heal churn (37/43/79) from high to medium: the written values are correct; the cost is runtime, provider load, bloat and an all-or-nothing failure surface.

Grouping calls worth flagging to the parent:
- The two intraday data-loss findings are deliberately NOT merged: the SIGKILL discards bars that were received, the early stop means bars are never emitted. Round 2's own reviewer noted they are independent, and they need different fixes.
- The five doctor/monitoring-coverage findings (35, 82, 142, 145, 158) are merged into one group. Their individual checks are separate code, but they are one decision and one owner, and keeping them apart would have produced five near-identical rows. The data defects they would have caught are ranked separately.
- 106 (eps_growth_yoy artifacts) is folded into the income_statements EPS-basis group because restating the stored series eliminates it entirely — it is not an independent fix.
- 24 and 25 are merged after checking sectors.py: both tools default to taxonomy='gics' and route through get_gics_sector there, but use bare c.sic_description on the sic branch. One change — send every sector-labelled field through the mapper — fixes the silent 55% drop, the NULL sector name and the cross-tool semantic split.
- 66 and 68 are merged: two tools, one uncapped-truncation wrapper (mcp_server/database.py:55 MAX_ROWS=1000, confirmed no env override).
- Kept separate despite shared themes: 42 (small-ratio splits never applied) vs 51 (fractional splits skipped in coldstart) — different code paths; 48 (rounded ratios) vs 14 (1:1 no-ops) — same rounding origin but different fixes; 18 vs 56 vs 57 — three distinct EMA failure modes; 113 vs 119 — the same miscalibration pattern on two different statistics; 127 vs 132 — two different sparkline defects.
- Cross-cutting pattern the parent may want to note in the master list: five separate groups (15/53, 54, 96, 5, 97/105/106) are all "the code was fixed, the stored data never was". Each needs its own backfill, and none of them has a doctor check.

## Findings

### Critical (1)

#### 1. The weekly PostgreSQL backup has failed silently on all 14 runs since 2026-05-31 and WAL archiving is off, so the newest recoverable snapshot is 102 days old. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** backup-recovery
- **Reviewers:** db-health-concurrency, scheduling-and-doctor-gaps
- **Location:** `/home/seed/scripts/backup_postgres.sh:12 (cron: 0 1 * * 0)`
- **Location:** `/home/seed/scripts/backup_postgres.sh:12 (crontab: 0 1 * * 0)`
- **Evidence:** cat /data/db-backups/backup.log shows a clean weekly run through 'Sun May 24 01:00:49 UTC 2026: Backup successful', then 14 consecutive failures: 'Sun May 31 01:00:51 UTC 2026: Backup FAILED' followed by 'Sun Jun 7 ... FAILED / permission denied while trying to connect to the docker API at unix:///var/run/docker.sock' repeated for Jun 14, Jun 21, Jun 28, Jul 5, Jul 12, Jul 19, Jul 26, Aug 2, Aug 9, Aug 16, Aug 23 and Aug 30. ls -la /data/db-backups shows the newest tar is postgres_backup_20260524_010001.tar (3,851,303,936 bytes, May 24). Cause: the script runs `docker exec "$CONTAINER" pg_dump -U postgres -F t postgres` (line 12) but Postgres was moved to rootless podman on 2026-06-04 (`systemctl --user status stocksdb` -> 'Stocks Postgres (rootless podman)', active since 2026-08-30, and `podman volume inspect stocksdb-data` CreatedAt 2026-06-04); as user seed `docker ps -a` returns the same permission-denied. There is no fallback: `grep -rln 'pg_dump|pg_basebackup' /home/seed/scripts /home/seed/code/sawa/scripts` returns only this one file, `systemctl --user list-units` shows only stocksdb.service, and there is no WAL archiving (archive_mode = off, archive_command = (disabled), archive_library = '', wal_keep_size = 0, and pg_replication_slots is empty). Meanwhile pg_database_size('postgres') is now 7234 MB against the 3.6 GB last dump.
- **Impact:** A volume or filesystem failure loses every write since 2026-05-24: migrations 42 through 48, today's split-basis repair (2,171 stock_splits rows, 6,874 restored archive rows, 12 re-fetched tickers), three months of daily prices and the full technical_indicators recompute. Neither logical dump nor PITR can recover any of it. The failure is invisible: the script's exit 1 goes only to backup.log, cron mails nothing on this host, no ntfy notification is sent (unlike every other pipeline step), and `sawa doctor` has no backup-age check.
- **Recommendation:** Change line 12 to `podman exec "$CONTAINER" pg_dump ...` (or drop the container indirection entirely and run `pg_dump "$DATABASE_URL" -F t` from the host), take a manual dump immediately, and route the FAILED branch through the same ntfy notifier the daily/weekly jobs use. Add a doctor check that fails when the newest file in /data/db-backups is older than ~8 days. Consider enabling archive_mode with a local archive_command so the RPO is minutes rather than a week.
- **Evidence:** crontab -l: `0 1 * * 0 /home/seed/scripts/backup_postgres.sh >> /data/db-backups/backup.log 2>&1`. The script runs `docker exec "$CONTAINER" pg_dump -U postgres -F t postgres` with CONTAINER=stocksdb, but the database was moved to rootless podman. tail /data/db-backups/backup.log shows 14 consecutive failures: 'Sun May 31 ... Backup FAILED', then Jun 7/14/21/28, Jul 5/12/19/26, Aug 2/9/16/23/30, each followed by 'permission denied while trying to connect to the docker API at unix:///var/run/docker.sock'. The last success line is 'Sun May 24 01:00:49 UTC 2026: Backup successful'. `ls -la /data/db-backups/` confirms the newest tar is postgres_backup_20260524_010001.tar (2026-05-24, 102 days before today 2026-09-03). Failure output goes only to backup.log — the script sends no notification, and `grep -c backup sawa/doctor.py` = 0, so no doctor check looks at backup age or success.
- **Impact:** The production database (10.0M stock_prices rows, 10.0M technical_indicators rows, plus all fundamentals, splits, dividends and news) has had no recoverable backup for 14 weeks. Every schema migration and every destructive repair since 2026-05-24 — migrations 44-48, the 2026-09-01 outage repairs, and today's split-basis rewrite (6,874 archived rows restored, 131 tickers re-adjusted, TA recomputed) — is unrecoverable. Because nothing alerts and nothing checks, this can only be discovered by a human reading a log file that no process reads.
- **Recommendation:** Point the script at the podman container (`podman exec stocksdb pg_dump ...`) or run pg_dump over TCP with the DATABASE_URL credentials; make the cron entry alert on failure (pipe through `sawa notify --level error`, or add a heartbeat ping); and add a doctor check that FAILs when the newest file in /data/db-backups is older than ~10 days. Verify one existing tarball restores before trusting the retention policy, which only prunes on a successful run.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Two reviewers filed the identical cron failure (/home/seed/scripts/backup_postgres.sh, 0 1 * * 0); one rated critical, one high. Critical is correct under the stated rubric: it leaves the system unrecoverable — a volume failure loses migrations 42-48, today's split-basis repair and three months of daily loads, and every destructive operation in this audit (coldstart --drop-existing, schema replay) runs without a net.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections, one of which makes the finding worse. (1) Cause is only partially right: the FIRST failure, Sun May 31, predates the podman migration (podman volume stocksdb-data CreatedAt 2026-06-04) and logged no stderr at all -- the docker.sock explanation covers only the 13 failures from Jun 7 onward, so May 31 failed for a separate, unlogged reason and the script's `cat /tmp/pg_backup_err.log` captured nothing. (2) AGGRAVATING: the surviving dumps are on the same filesystem as the live database. `podman volume inspect stocksdb-data` gives Mountpoint /data/podman/seed/storage/volumes/stocksdb-data/_data and the backups sit in /data/db-backups -- both on /data. So the 102-day-old dump does not survive the single failure mode (loss of /data) it exists to protect against, and there is no offsite or second-filesystem copy anywhere. Effective RPO against a /data failure is not 102 days but total loss.
- **Verifier correction:** One material correction, plus one confirmation of a round-1 point. (1) CORRECTION — "the newest recoverable snapshot is 102 days old" is wrong; it is 91 days old. The finding (and round 1) read only the `postgres_backup_*.tar` glob and missed the sibling directory `/data/db-backups/migration-2026-06-04/`, which holds `dumpall-final.sql` at 3,938,644,575 bytes dated 2026-06-04 22:28 — a complete cluster dump, not a truncated one: it opens with "-- PostgreSQL database cluster dump", closes with "-- PostgreSQL database cluster dump complete", its `dumpall-final.err` is 0 bytes, and `restore-final.out`/`restore-final.err` show it was actually replayed into the podman instance successfully (the only stderr is the benign `role "postgres" already exists` / `current user cannot be dropped`). So the true recovery point is 2026-06-04, i.e. 91 days, and the recoverable dataset is 11 days newer than the finding claims. Note this file was left behind by the one-off migration, not produced by any scheduled job — the retention line in backup_postgres.sh only globs `postgres_backup_*.tar`, so it is unmanaged and would not be replaced or rotated by any process. Everything the impact paragraph names still postdates it: migrations 44-48, today's split-basis repair, and June-through-September prices/indicators are in neither snapshot. (2) CONFIRMED, and it makes the corrected 91 days largely academic: the June 4 dumpall sits in /data/db-backups on /dev/sdb1, the same filesystem as the live volume at /data/podman/seed/storage/volumes/stocksdb-data/_data (both `df` to /dev/sdb1, 137G, 33% used). Against loss of /data — the dominant failure mode a dump defends against — every surviving snapshot dies with the database, and no offsite or second-filesystem copy exists anywhere on the host. (3) Minor: `/tmp/pg_backup_err.log` no longer exists, so the unlogged May 31 failure that round 1 flagged cannot be re-diagnosed; the log's bare "Backup FAILED" with no following stderr line for May 31 (versus a docker.sock line for all 13 later runs) is all the evidence that survives, and it does support round 1's reading that May 31 failed for a different, pre-podman reason.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections. (1) The newest usable dump is NOT the 2026-05-24 tar. /data/db-backups/migration-2026-06-04/dumpall-final.sql is a 3.9 GB full pg_dumpall dated 2026-06-04 22:28 — a one-off taken during the docker-to-podman migration, so the real recovery point is 91 days old, not 102. It is not part of any rotation and will never be refreshed. (2) 'unrecoverable' overstates part of the loss: stock_prices and technical_indicators are re-derivable from Polygon's rolling ~5-year history via `sawa coldstart`, so the ~10M+10M rows are expensive but reproducible. What is genuinely unrecoverable is the non-reproducible state: news_articles (~42k rows, Polygon news is not a full backfill), intraday bars, stock_character_classification history (weekly snapshots back to at least 2026-06), earnings history, and stock_prices_unadjustable_archive including today's split-basis repair. Severity stays high on that basis.
- **Verifier correction:** Three refinements, none of which weaken the finding. (1) I independently confirm round 1's correction about the recovery point: /data/db-backups/migration-2026-06-04/dumpall-final.sql is a 3,938,644,575-byte pg_dumpall dated 2026-06-04 22:28 whose tail reads "-- PostgreSQL database cluster dump complete", i.e. structurally complete. It is a one-off from the docker-to-podman migration, outside any rotation, so the true newest recovery point is 91 days old rather than 102 — the 102-day figure is right only for the rotating series. Both artifacts pass a cheap structural check (the May-24 tar lists toc.dat plus per-table .dat members). (2) 14 "Backup FAILED" lines, but only 13 carry the docker-socket error text; the first failure (Sun May 31) logged no stderr detail, because /tmp/pg_backup_err.log was empty or cleared. Immaterial to the conclusion, but the evidence string implies all 14 had the message. (3) New root-cause detail beyond "socket group membership is gone": seed is in no docker group whatsoever (groups=seed,sudo,users,xrdp-users), so this is not a revocable group-membership blip — and since seed IS in sudo, the historical successes must have run through a docker setup that no longer exists on this host. The fix must target podman (`podman exec stocksdb pg_dump ...`) or TCP pg_dump with DATABASE_URL; re-adding a docker group would not help, as the container runtime holding the data is podman. Also worth noting for the recommendation: the live database is 7234 MB against a 3.6 G tar, so retention sizing should be re-checked when the job is repaired.

### High (27)

#### 2. Split adjustment rounds volume to a bigint with no zero-collapse guard, destroying the volume column for deep reverse-split tickers — 16,248 bars at 0 and 22,781 at 1-9 shares across 81 tickers — and the zeros are baked into technical_indicators (14,428 volume_sma_20 = 0, flat OBV). *(filed independently by 4 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** volume-integrity
- **Reviewers:** prices, volume-and-archive
- **Location:** `sawa/database/load.py:633 and sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:447-455`
- **Location:** `sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:447-455 (SplitAdjuster.adjust_row); same pattern in sawa/split_adjust.py rebase_rows_before_date (uncommitted)`
- **Location:** `stock_prices.volume (81 tickers, 2021-02-18..2026-04-17)`
- **Location:** `technical_indicators.volume_sma_20 / dollar_volume_sma_20 / volume_ratio / obv`
- **Evidence:** The price path has an explicit zero guard, the volume path does not:
    if scaled <= 0 or scaled >= MAX_STORABLE_PRICE:
        raise ValueError(f"split-adjusted {field} ... outside the storable price range")
    ...
    volume = (raw_volume * factor.numerator / factor.denominator).to_integral_value(rounding=ROUND_HALF_UP)
    if volume < 0 or volume > MAX_BIGINT:
        raise ValueError(...)
A nonzero raw volume that rounds to 0 passes both checks silently. For a reverse split the factor is 1/N, so volume is divided by the cumulative ratio. The uncommitted sawa/split_adjust.py repeats it in SQL: `volume = round(volume * %(num)s / %(den)s)::bigint`, again unguarded.

Measured effect — rows with a real intraday range (so trading certainly occurred) but volume = 0:
  SELECT count(*) FROM stock_prices WHERE volume=0 AND NOT (open=high AND high=low AND low=close) AND date>='2024-09-03';
   -> 1121 rows across 30 tickers
All 30 sit on tickers with large cumulative reverse ratios (100% correlation):
   WOK  240 rows, cumulative 1,000,000x      PAVS 232 rows, 2,400,000x
   SMX  164 rows, 813,120,000x               WHLR  85 rows, 7,257,600,000x
   ADTX  63 rows, 488,160,000,000x
Example row: PAVS 2025-11-10 open 67200.00 high 71520.12 low 67200.00 close 70320.00 volume 0.

Precision loss extends well beyond outright zero. Over 10 affected tickers since 2024-09-03:
   volume = 0      :  980 rows
   volume 1-10     : 1093 rows
   volume 11-100   :  614 rows
   volume > 100    : 2228 rows
2,687 of 4,915 rows (55%) are degraded to <=100 shares.
- **Impact:** Volume is irreversibly destroyed, not merely rescaled — a 0 cannot be recovered from the stored row. Every volume-derived value is wrong for these tickers: OBV, volume ratio / relative volume, VWAP, dollar-volume liquidity filters, and volume-anomaly detection. Dollar-volume screens silently rank these tickers as untradeable, and a zero-volume bar carrying a genuine 6% intraday range is internally contradictory. The CHECK constraint permits volume >= 0, so nothing catches it.
- **Recommendation:** Mirror the price guard on the volume path: when raw_volume > 0 and the adjusted value rounds to 0, either clamp to 1 or raise, rather than storing 0. Better, stop destroying the raw figure — keep as-traded volume in its own column and derive the adjusted value on read, since volume (unlike price) has no continuity requirement across a split. Apply the same fix to rebase_rows_before_date before that working-tree change is committed. Add a doctor check for rows where volume = 0 while high > low.
- **Evidence:** WITH f AS (SELECT ticker, exp(sum(ln(split_from::numeric/split_to::numeric))) AS cf FROM stock_splits GROUP BY ticker), deep AS (SELECT ticker FROM f WHERE cf>10) SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE p.volume=0 AND p.high>p.low), count(DISTINCT p.ticker) FILTER (...), min/max(p.date) FILTER (...) FROM stock_prices p JOIN deep USING (ticker) -> 16248 bars | 81 tickers | 2021-02-18 | 2026-04-17. Same join banded by volume: 16,248 at 0 shares, 22,781 at 1-9, 40,588 at 10-99 (79,617 bars <100 shares across 390 tickers). Per ticker: WHLR 961/1385 bars destroyed (69.4%), VMAR 893/1393 (64.1%), XXII 873/1393 (62.7%), ADTX 822/1262 (65.1%), CETX 767 (55.1%), APVO 646, SXTC 636, GNLN 595, ZCMD 579, PAVS 548/859 (63.8%), SMX 538/877 (61.3%). Quantization proof (adjacent days flip): SELECT date, close, volume FROM stock_prices WHERE ticker='XXII' AND date BETWEEN '2024-09-03' AND '2024-09-10' -> 2024-09-03 c=303389.55 v=1; 09-04 c=265291.20 v=0; 09-05 v=1; 09-06 v=0; 09-09 v=1. Polygon adjusted=false for 2024-09-03..06 returns v=492764/338562/485253/449243.
- **Impact:** Every volume-derived answer for these 81 tickers before their most recent split is wrong: share volume, dollar volume, OBV, VWAP, liquidity, and any chart. Rounding is symmetric, so bars also read 1.9x too HIGH where the true value was 0.55 (XXII 2024-09-03: stored dollar volume 275,631 vs a true ~145,809). 390 tickers sit under 100 shares/bar where a half-share rounding error is >0.5%.
- **Recommendation:** Stop deriving the stored volume from the adjusted basis. Either store as-traded volume alongside the adjusted price (dollar volume is then exact and split-invariant), or widen volume to NUMERIC so the ratio survives. Until then, backfill the 81 tickers by re-fetching with adjusted=false and scaling in the read path, and refuse to write a bar whose volume rounds to 0 while high>low.
- **Evidence:** WITH deep AS (cumulative reverse factor >10) SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE volume_sma_20=0), count(DISTINCT ticker) FILTER (...), count(*) FILTER (WHERE dollar_volume_sma_20=0), count(DISTINCT ticker) FILTER (...), count(*) FILTER (WHERE volume_ratio IS NULL AND volume_sma_20 IS NOT NULL), count(*) FILTER (WHERE volume_sma_20 < 100) FROM technical_indicators JOIN deep USING (ticker) -> 14428 rows / 66 tickers at volume_sma_20=0; 8744 rows / 44 tickers at dollar_volume_sma_20=0; 8744 NULL volume_ratio; 67,091 rows with volume_sma_20<100. Frozen OBV sample: SELECT t.ticker,t.date,p.volume,t.volume_sma_20,t.dollar_volume_sma_20,t.obv ... WHERE volume_sma_20=0 -> ACON 2022-10-10..2022-10-20, obv identical at 166 for every one of 8 consecutive sessions, dollar_volume_sma_20 decaying 84920 -> 37471 and then flat. XXII 2024-09-04: bar dollar volume 0.00 against a true ~96,000 (338,562 shares x $0.2848 raw).
- **Impact:** get_technical_indicators, get_latest_technical_indicators, screen_technical_indicators and every historical chart serve a 20-day average volume of zero and a dollar-volume series that decays to zero for stocks that were trading normally. OBV, being cumulative, propagates the loss forward past the collapsed span rather than self-healing.
- **Recommendation:** Recompute technical_indicators for the 81 affected tickers after the volume column is repaired; until then have the TA writer refuse to store volume_sma_20=0 / dollar_volume_sma_20=0 for a ticker whose bars show high>low, and surface it as a data-quality warning instead.
- **Evidence:** Provider side (one verification GET): GET /v2/aggs/ticker/XXII/range/1/day/2024-09-03/2024-09-06?adjusted=false -> {"adjusted":false, results v=492764, 338562, 485253, 449243} against stored volumes 1, 0, 1, 0. Code side: every price fetch hardcodes adjusted=true — sawa/api/client.py:482 `params={"adjusted": "true", ...}`, sawa/daily.py:206 same, sawa/repositories/polygon_prices.py:181 same; grep for `adjusted=False` / `"adjusted": "false"` across the tree returns zero hits, and sawa/api/async_client.py:120's `adjusted: bool = True` parameter has no caller that overrides it. Local side: the coldstart flat files carry as-traded volume (documented at sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:353-357) and sawa/database/load.py:633 passes `split_adjuster.adjust_row` as the row_transform, so sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:447-455 `volume = (raw_volume * factor.numerator / factor.denominator).to_integral_value(ROUND_HALF_UP)` collapses it before the row is ever written. The range check on line 451 tests `volume < 0 or volume > MAX_BIGINT` — zero passes.
- **Impact:** The loss is entirely self-inflicted and reversible: the exact share counts exist both upstream (within Polygon's ~5-year window, which still covers 2021-09 onward) and in the flat-file artifacts, and are thrown away by our own write path. It also means any repair must be done before a ticker's history ages out of the provider window.
- **Recommendation:** Fetch and persist as-traded volume (adjusted=false, or the untransformed flat-file value) and apply the split factor in the read path where it can stay fractional; at minimum, backfill volume for the 81 damaged tickers from adjusted=false aggregates before their pre-2021-09 history leaves the provider window.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** One root cause at sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:447-455 (and the same pattern in the uncommitted split_adjust.rebase_rows_before_date) with one remediation pass: stop rounding, restore volume, recompute volume-derived TA. Members are the mechanism (3), the price-table damage (144), the indicator-table damage (146) and the recoverability analysis (150) — the last confirms the data still exists upstream and in the flat-file artifacts, so this is self-inflicted and reversible. Severity high (reviewers split medium/high): the data is irreversibly destroyed in place, which the rubric puts above medium.
- **Verifier correction:** The bucket census is close but not exact; over the ten tickers I sampled (WOK, PAVS, SMX, WHLR, ADTX, LBGJ, NIVF, VCIG, GCTK, NXTT) since 2024-09-03 I get 1,009 rows at 0, 925 at 1-10, 581 at 11-100 and 2,461 above 100 - i.e. 2,515 of 4,976 (51%) degraded to <=100 shares, versus the reported 980/1093/614/2228 and 55%. Same magnitude, different sample. Also worth stating: 81 tickers are affected across the full history, not the 30 visible in the 2024-09-03 window.
- **Verifier correction:** Headline numbers are exact (16,248 bars / 81 tickers / 2021-02-18..2026-04-17). Two band counts are stale: at current DB state the deep-ticker bands are 24,968 bars at 1-9 shares (claimed 22,781) and 42,114 at 10-99 (claimed 40,588). Table-wide, 243,667 rows across 2,916 tickers sit at 1-99 shares, though not all of those are adjustment artefacts (genuinely illiquid OTC names qualify). Add: because sawa/split_adjust.py:279 rebases in place, a collapsed bar can never be recovered by a further adjustment — only a re-fetch with adjusted=false can restore it.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections to the record, both in the first verifier's favor being wrong rather than the finding's. (a) The first verifier's corrected_details is itself mistaken: it reports the 1-9 band as 24,968 and the 10-99 band as 42,114 and calls the finding "stale". Those larger numbers come from dropping the `high > low` predicate. With the finding's own predicate applied consistently, the bands are exactly 22,781 and 40,588 as originally written, and they sum with the 16,248 zero bars to exactly the claimed 79,617 across 390 tickers. The original evidence needs no correction. (b) New, and it strengthens the finding: this corruption is exclusive to the adjustment path, with no false positives. Table-wide there are 79,909 rows with volume=0, but 63,661 of them have high=low (genuine no-trade days on illiquid names). Every one of the remaining 16,248 rows where high>low belongs to the deep-reverse-split set — the count table-wide and the count inside the deep join are both 16,248 across the same 81 tickers. So "volume=0 while the bar has a price range" is a perfect, zero-noise signature of the rounding bug, which makes it directly usable as a repair selector and as the write-time refusal predicate the recommendation asks for. Also confirmed: the loss is unrecoverable from stored data. sawa/split_adjust.py:279 rebases in place, and 0 * num/den = 0, so a bar already collapsed stays 0 through every subsequent reverse split; only a re-fetch with adjusted=false can restore it. XXII has had four further splits since 2024-09, so 873 of its 1,393 bars are now permanently zero.

The dollar_volume_sma_20=0 rows are the unambiguous corruption — dollar volume is split-invariant, so 0 is provably wrong against a true ~96k-146k/day.
- **Verifier correction:** Three overstatements, none touching the core defect. (1) The 67,091 rows at volume_sma_20<100 is NOT blast radius and should be dropped from the evidence: at each ticker's latest row, 103 tickers sit under 100 and none sit at exactly 0, and the smallest are genuinely illiquid defined-outcome ETFs (MFEB, RCLY, PMMR, MNVR all at volume_sma_20=6 against a same-day raw volume of 6 shares). That band conflates real illiquidity with the reverse-split collapse. Only the =0 rows are demonstrable corruption. (2) The impact sentence overstates live exposure: no ticker's latest TA row carries volume_sma_20=0 (ACON's current value is 71,403, XXII's 45,164), so get_latest_technical_indicators and a default-date screen_technical_indicators do NOT currently serve an exact zero. The corruption is served by get_technical_indicators over a historical window, and by screen_technical_indicators only with an explicit historical target_date — 15,846 rows across 154 active tickers through 2026-06-29. (3) OBV propagation is real but noise-level at the current date: ACON's present OBV is 49,134,373 against a ~166-unit corrupted base, so the inherited error never washes out but is not materially visible today. Severity stays high on the strength of the split-invariant dollar_volume_sma_20=0 rows (8,992, provably wrong against a true $96k-$146k/day) and the fact that these rows need their own deliberate ta_backfill.recompute_ta_for_tickers pass — repairing stock_prices.volume will not heal them.

The actionable core stands: no call site ever requests adjusted=false, 16,248 bars over 81 tickers now read volume=0 despite high>low, and only 14,722 of them are still inside Polygon's rolling window (1,526 predate ~2021-09-07 and are already unrecoverable from the provider).

#### 3. mv_52week_extremes is refreshed only when the daily run sees max(date) advance, so any historical price rewrite leaves it permanently stale — APH is currently served a 52-week high of 178.52 (2.2x the true 89.26) and reads as both -54% off its high and below its low. *(filed independently by 3 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** derived-views
- **Reviewers:** economy-derived, mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/screener.py:846 and :68-73 (join mv_52week_extremes e ON e.date = latest_52w); refresh exists only in sawa/daily.py:411; scripts/restore_quarantined_prices.py and sawa/split_adjust.py never REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW`
- **Location:** `mv_52week_extremes (ticker='APH'); refresh gate at sawa/daily.py:403`
- **Location:** `sawa/daily.py:403`
- **Evidence:** Today's split backfill inserted APH 1:2 execution_date 2026-09-03 (stock_splits.created_at 2026-09-03 05:04:52) and stock_prices/technical_indicators were re-based; mv_52week_extremes was last refreshed 2026-09-02 ~21:51 (pg_stat last_autoanalyze) and was not.

SELECT sp.ticker, sp.date, sp.high, sp.low, mv.high_52w, mv.low_52w FROM stock_prices sp JOIN mv_52week_extremes mv USING (ticker,date) WHERE sp.date='2026-09-02' AND sp.low < mv.low_52w;
 ticker |    date    |  high  |  low   |  high_52w  |  low_52w
 APH    | 2026-09-02 | 81.159 | 77.705 | 178.520000 | 105.450000

Recompute of the matview's own definition over current stock_prices gives high_52w=89.26, low_52w=54.34 (exactly the stale 178.52/2). stock_prices is the correct basis: APH close 2026-09-02 = 80.04 and technical_indicators.sma_20 = 81.2685 on the same date. The invariant 'high <= high_52w and low >= low_52w' is violated on 23 rows since 2026-08-01, all APH — no other ticker is affected.
- **Impact:** mcp_server/tools/screener.py:421/776/846 (get_52week_extremes, screen_stocks, the near-high/near-low screens) join mv_52week_extremes and currently report APH as -54% off its 52-week high and below its 52-week low simultaneously. Any 'near 52-week low' or '% off high' screen ranks APH on doubled levels.
- **Recommendation:** REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY mv_52week_extremes now. Then make every path that rewrites price history (sawa/split_adjust.py, sawa/corporate_actions.py, scripts/restore_quarantined_prices.py) refresh it, the way they already recompute technical_indicators.
- **Evidence:** refresh_52week_extremes_if_needed returns False when `latest_extremes_date >= latest_price_date` (sawa/daily.py:403-405). Today's split repair rewrote APH's whole history and restored 5,202 archived 2021 rows for 84 tickers without changing max(date): stock_prices max date = 2026-09-02 and mv_52week_extremes max date = 2026-09-02, so the gate reports 'up to date'. Row counts prove the divergence: SELECT count(*) FROM stock_prices = 10,032,550 vs SELECT count(*) FROM mv_52week_extremes = 10,027,348 (5,202 rows short; e.g. LGVN 134 rows 2021-02-18..2021-08-27, CRWD 98, KLAC 83 all present in stock_prices and absent from the matview). doctor.py:584-608 checks the same max(date) equality and therefore reports the matview healthy.
- **Impact:** A rebase or restore run any time except immediately before a new trading date lands (e.g. Friday evening, a weekend, or a market holiday) leaves wrong 52-week extremes served for days with no alert — exactly the window in which maintenance scripts are normally run.
- **Recommendation:** Trigger the refresh on content, not on max(date): compare stock_prices row count / a checksum against the matview, or have the rewrite paths set an explicit dirty flag that daily honours. Add the same content check to doctor's mv_52week_extremes.freshness.
- **Evidence:** SQL: stock_splits APH execution_date 2026-09-03 split 1->2. For APH at 2026-09-02: close 80.04, mv high_52w 178.52 / low_52w 105.45, tool high_52w_pct = ROUND((80.04-178.52)/178.52*100,2) = -55.16; recomputed over the last 252 stock_prices rows: high 89.26 / low 54.34, true pct -10.33. Whole-universe check of MV vs recomputed 252-row window at 2026-09-02: 10000 rows, 1 mismatched (APH). MV also lacks the 2026-06-09 row that the re-fetch added.
- **Impact:** get_52week_extremes and screen_stocks high_52w_pct/low_52w_pct are wrong for APH right now (and for any ticker re-based by the new repair path until the next nightly daily.py refresh); a 'near 52w high' screen will miss it and a 'lows' screen may include it.
- **Recommendation:** Have scripts/restore_quarantined_prices.py and the split-adjust path issue REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY mv_52week_extremes after re-basing prices (same as TA recompute); run the refresh now.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Two reviewers filed the live APH symptom (28 high, 67 medium) and one filed the gate that causes it (29). Same fix: refresh on content change, and have the repair scripts (split_adjust.py, restore_quarantined_prices.py) refresh the matview. High because it is serving a value off by a split factor right now, through get_52week_extremes and the screen_stocks near-high/near-low filters.
- **Verifier correction:** Only the high is exactly 2x the true value (178.52 vs 89.26). The stale low 105.45 is NOT 2x the recomputed 54.34 (ratio 1.94) — the restored 2021 rows shifted the 251-row window as well, so the low is wrong for two compounding reasons. APH is the ONLY ticker on 2026-09-02 whose mv values disagree with a recompute (checked against all 12 re-fetched tickers and all 84 restore beneficiaries), not merely the only one violating the high/low invariant. The staleness is self-healing at the next daily run (new date 2026-09-03 trips the gate), so the correct framing is 'wrong for one trading session', not 'wrong until someone notices'.
- **Verifier correction:** Three corrections/additions to the finding and to the first verifier's notes:

(a) The low's 1.94 ratio has a concrete, verifiable cause that neither report pinned down. stock_prices holds 1,395 APH rows; mv_52week_extremes holds 1,394. The row present in prices but ABSENT from the matview is 2026-06-09 (high 77.46, low 72.61, close 77.035) — a restored intra-history row. Because the definition windows by ROWS BETWEEN 251 PRECEDING (not by date), that one extra row shifts the window by one trading day. Consequently the low ratio is exactly 2.0000 on 2026-08-25 through 2026-09-01 (mv 105.45 vs recompute 52.725) and only becomes 1.9406 on 2026-09-02, where the recompute's window has rolled past the 52.725 low to 54.34. So the first verifier's "restored 2021 rows shifted the window" is right in effect but wrong in specifics: it is a single 2026-06-09 row, and the low is a clean 2x on every date except the last.

(b) The blast radius is bounded more strongly than "the 12 re-fetched plus 84 restore beneficiaries". I recomputed the matview's definition at 2026-09-02 for all 8,695 tickers that have a full 252-row window and diffed against the matview: exactly one mismatch, APH. That is a universe-wide bound, not a subset check.

(c) The finding's headline "APH trades at 80" is the 2026-09-02 close (80.04); the live price served alongside the stale extremes today is 81.87. Both are far below the served low_52w of 105.45, so the direction and magnitude of the claim hold.

Also worth recording for the fix: sawa/doctor.py:584-610 would NOT have caught this — it compares only MAX(date) of the matview against MAX(date) of stock_prices, and both are 2026-09-02, so the mv_52week_extremes.freshness check reports OK while the values are wrong. Any fix should add a value-level guard (e.g. assert no row violates high <= high_52w AND low >= low_52w over a recent window), not just a date comparison.
- **Verifier correction:** The 5,202 missing rows span 84 tickers (not the '5,202 rows for 84 tickers' framing implying they matter downstream) and are exclusively 2021 dates. Because both screen_stocks and get_52week_extremes bind to `(SELECT MAX(date) FROM mv_52week_extremes)`, those absent 2021 rows are never read by any MCP tool; the only user-visible consequence of the gate is the stale APH row. The `since_date` path at screener.py:776 is the one place historical mv rows are read, and it would only under-report new extremes for pre-2022 since_date values.
- **Verifier correction:** Real, but narrower and shorter-lived than 'high' implies, so severity lowered to medium. (a) Blast radius verified: comparing the MV at 2026-09-02 against a recomputed 252-row window for all 134 tickers whose historical stock_prices rows were rewritten today, APH is the only mismatch — the other 133 restored/re-fetched tickers agree. (b) It self-heals: sawa/daily.py:398-411 refreshes whenever MAX(stock_prices.date) exceeds MAX(mv_52week_extremes.date), and tonight's daily run adds 2026-09-03 rows, so the window of wrong data is bounded by the next daily load (up to ~3 days if a repair lands after a Friday load). The systemic gap — repair paths recompute TA but not the MV — is genuine and will recur on every future split repair.

#### 4. Schema replay re-runs the superseded widening migrations 24, 42 and 31, which now NARROW stock_prices (24,8 -> 20,8) and technical_indicators (20,4 -> 16,4) below the live data, so `sawa coldstart --no-drop` and `python -m sawa.database.schema` abort with numeric field overflow and no future migration can be applied through the documented path. *(filed independently by 3 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** schema-drift
- **Reviewers:** coldstart-loaders, schema-drift
- **Location:** `sqlschema/24_widen_price_precision.sql:12-16, sqlschema/42_widen_price_for_reverse_splits.sql:27-31, sqlschema/31_widen_indicator_precision.sql:15-35 (replayed by sawa/database/schema.py:217-249 via sawa/coldstart.py:1139-1145)`
- **Location:** `sqlschema/24_widen_price_precision.sql:13-18 and sqlschema/42_widen_price_for_reverse_splits.sql:31-36 (vs sqlschema/48_widen_price_for_compounded_reverse_splits.sql:82-86)`
- **Location:** `sqlschema/31_widen_indicator_precision.sql:17-36 (vs sqlschema/47_widen_indicator_overflow_headroom.sql:26-47)`
- **Evidence:** Live types: stock_prices open/high/low/close NUMERIC(24,8) (migration 48), technical_indicators sma_*/ema_*/bb_*/atr_14/vwap/macd_* NUMERIC(20,4) (migration 47). Replayed 24 and 42 execute `ALTER TABLE stock_prices ALTER COLUMN open TYPE NUMERIC(20, 8), ...` and 31 executes `ALTER COLUMN sma_5 TYPE NUMERIC(16, 4), ...`; these are narrowing casts, not no-ops. Read-only proof: `SELECT max(high)::numeric(20,8) FROM stock_prices WHERE high >= 1e12;` -> `ERROR: numeric field overflow DETAIL: A field with precision 20, scale 8 must round to an absolute value less than 10^12.` (7 rows have high >= 1e12, max 1,928,232,000,000). `SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE bb_upper >= 1e12 OR sma_5 >= 1e12 OR ...) FROM technical_indicators WHERE ticker IN (SELECT ticker FROM stock_splits WHERE split_from >= 10)` -> 23 rows, max bb_upper 1193075633339.4624 (> NUMERIC(16,4) cap 10^12). execute_sql_files_atomically runs every NN_*.sql in one transaction and rolls back on the first psycopg.Error, so the whole no-drop upgrade fails. Comments in 42 ('Idempotent: ALTER COLUMN TYPE is a no-op if the column is already the target type') and 24 ('NUMERIC(20,8) is the final monotonic target ... never narrows') were true before 47/48 and are now wrong; sqlschema/README.md advertises `sawa coldstart --no-drop` as 'Preserves data; applies all SQL atomically'.
- **Impact:** The only documented non-destructive schema path (and `python -m sawa.database.schema` without --drop) is unusable against the current database; the atomic runner protects the data, but no future migration can be applied through it. Even on a database without >=1e12 values the replay would silently rewrite the 10M-row stock_prices and technical_indicators tables (narrow then widen) on every no-drop run.
- **Recommendation:** Make 24/31/42 widen-only: wrap each ALTER TYPE in a DO block that reads atttypmod and skips when the current precision is already >= the target (48 shows the pattern), or drop the ALTER TYPE from 24/31/42 and leave 47/48 as the authoritative types. Add a test that replays REQUIRED_SCHEMA_FILENAMES against a fixture already at 48's types and asserts no column is narrowed. Correct the idempotency comments and the README claim.
- **Evidence:** Live: information_schema.columns -> stock_prices.open/high/low/close = numeric(24,8) (migration 48, applied). Files 24 and 42 both contain unconditional `ALTER TABLE stock_prices ALTER COLUMN open TYPE NUMERIC(20, 8), ... close TYPE NUMERIC(20, 8)`; 24's header even states "NUMERIC(20,8) is the final monotonic target ... never narrows a schema already upgraded by 42" (written 2026-08-30, commit 40d1156) — 48 (2026-09-02, commit 0781f4b) broke that invariant. Live data exceeds the narrower type: `select max(high), max(open), max(low), max(close) from stock_prices` -> 1928232000000.00000000 | 1357084800000.00000000 | 1283860800000.00000000 | 1484006400000.00000000. Direct test of the coercion Postgres applies when narrowing: `select 1928232000000.00000000::numeric(20,8)` -> ERROR: numeric field overflow, DETAIL: A field with precision 20, scale 8 must round to an absolute value less than 10^12. Runner: sawa/database/schema.py execute_sql_files_atomically() re-executes every numbered file (README.md 'The runner re-executes every file on --no-drop upgrades'; docs/MAINTENANCE.md:76-78 says this is the default coldstart behaviour; :354 'sawa coldstart --no-drop # re-applies all SQL idempotently'). tests/test_schema_sql.py only guards DELETE/DROP TABLE/CASCADE, not ALTER TYPE narrowing (18 passed).
- **Impact:** Running the documented upgrade path (plain `sawa coldstart` or `--no-drop`) against the production database will abort at file 24 and roll back the whole schema transaction; no schema change (e.g. a future migration 49) can be applied through the supported tooling until 24/31/42 are fixed. If the offending rows were ever absent, the replay would instead silently rewrite the 1.7 GB table twice under ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock. Not data loss (atomic rollback), but the upgrade mechanism is broken today.
- **Recommendation:** Make every widening migration monotonic: wrap the ALTER in a DO block that reads the current typmod (atttypmod from pg_attribute) and only widens when the live precision is narrower than the target, or retarget 24 and 42 to the current final width NUMERIC(24,8) (they are replayed anyway, so the final state is defined by the latest file). Add a static test that, for each (table,column), the NUMERIC precision declared by any later migration is >= every earlier declaration. Consider a runner-level guard that refuses to narrow a NUMERIC column.
- **Evidence:** Live: technical_indicators.sma_5..atr_14 = numeric(20,4), bb_width_pct/volume_ratio = numeric(16,6) (migration 47 applied). File 31 unconditionally runs `ALTER TABLE technical_indicators ALTER COLUMN sma_5 TYPE NUMERIC(16, 4), ... atr_14 TYPE NUMERIC(16, 4)` — a narrowing against the live schema. Live values that do not fit NUMERIC(16,4) (max abs < 10^12): `select max(bb_upper), max(sma_5) from technical_indicators` -> 1193075633339.4624 | 1064188800000.0000. Same runner semantics as the previous finding (every file replayed; single transaction).
- **Impact:** Independently of the stock_prices issue, a schema replay would fail (numeric field overflow) at file 31 or, absent such rows, force a full rewrite of the 3.6 GB technical_indicators table under ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock before 47 widens it again.
- **Recommendation:** Same fix as for 24/42: conditional widening (only when current precision < target) or retarget 31 to NUMERIC(20,4)/leave 47 as the only ALTER; cover with the same monotonicity test.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** One reviewer filed the whole replay failure (47); another split it per table (89 stock_prices, 90 technical_indicators) — same root cause (non-idempotent ALTER TYPE narrowing in superseded migrations) and one fix (make those files no-ops or clamp to GREATEST of current precision). High: it makes the only documented non-destructive upgrade path unusable.
- **Verifier correction:** Accurate as written. One clarification worth recording: migration 24 is the first of the three to execute and would abort the transaction there, so 31 and 42 are never reached — but 31 is independently broken (23 ADTX technical_indicators rows exceed NUMERIC(16,4)), so fixing 24/42 alone would not unblock the path.
- **Verifier correction:** Finding is accurate as written. Two refinements worth recording. (1) Ordering: migration 24 is the first of the three to execute and aborts the transaction there, so 31, 42, 47 and 48 are never reached in a real run — but 31 is independently broken (23 ADTX technical_indicators rows exceed NUMERIC(16,4)), so fixing 24 and 42 alone would not unblock the path. (2) The "silently rewrite the 10M-row tables" impact claim is now empirically confirmed rather than asserted: on PostgreSQL 18.4 a narrowing numeric typmod change (24,8)->(20,8) changes relfilenode (full table rewrite) while the widening (20,8)->(24,8) does not, so migrations 31 and 47 (and 24/42 then 48) form a narrow-then-widen pair in which only the narrowing half rewrites. This also makes the "Postgres widens NUMERIC in place: metadata-only, no table rewrite" comments in 31 and 47 correct for widening but silently inapplicable to the narrowing they now perform.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections that make the finding stronger, not weaker. (1) The existing static test does not merely fail to catch the narrowing -- it actively enforces it: tests/test_schema_sql.py::test_every_price_precision_migration_is_monotonic_at_final_scale asserts `sql.count("TYPE NUMERIC(20, 8)") == 8` for both 24_widen_price_precision.sql and 42_widen_price_for_reverse_splits.sql, so retargeting either file to NUMERIC(24,8) breaks the suite; the test itself must be updated as part of the fix. (2) The test file has 13 tests, not 18 (`pytest tests/test_schema_sql.py -q` -> 13 passed). Precise blast radius: exactly 7 ADTX rows in stock_prices exceed 10^12, and they are enough to abort the entire replay at file 24 and roll back all 49 files.
- **Verifier correction:** Three refinements, all strengthening the finding. (1) A SECOND, independent narrowing exists in the same replay: sqlschema/31_widen_indicator_precision.sql:16-27+ targets `TYPE NUMERIC(16, 4)` for the technical_indicators columns, while live technical_indicators carries 20 numeric(20,4) columns (migration 47) and `select max(greatest(sma_200, ema_200, bb_upper, atr_14)) from technical_indicators` -> 1193075633339.4624, which also exceeds the 10^12 limit of NUMERIC(16,4). File 24 aborts first, so 31 is currently masked; fixing 24 and 42 alone would just move the abort to 31. The finding names 31 only in passing in its impact text — it belongs in the location list. (2) The existing static test does not merely miss the narrowing, it pins it: tests/test_schema_sql.py:89-93 test_every_price_precision_migration_is_monotonic_at_final_scale asserts `sql.count("TYPE NUMERIC(20, 8)") == 8` for both 24_widen_price_precision.sql and 42_widen_price_for_reverse_splits.sql, so retargeting either file to NUMERIC(24,8) breaks the suite and the test must change with the fix. That file has 13 tests, not 18 (`uv run --no-sync python -m pytest tests/test_schema_sql.py -q` -> 13 passed in 0.38s). (3) The fresh-install path is unaffected: sqlschema/02_market_data.sql creates the columns at NUMERIC(20,8) and 48 widens them at the end, so `--drop-existing` coldstart on an empty database applied all 47 files cleanly in my container test — the breakage is specific to replaying against a database that already holds the >=1e12 rows, i.e. production today. The confirmed blast radius on live is 7 ADTX rows in stock_prices, which is enough to roll back all 47 files; on that empty-DB pass the replay also silently rewrote stock_prices twice under ACCESS EXCLUSIVE (24 and 42 narrow, 48 widens again), matching the finding's secondary impact claim.
- **Verifier correction:** The impact sentence is mis-ordered. A replay would abort at file 24 (stock_prices, finding 0) first, not at file 31 -- 31 never executes today. 31 is the second failure in the same transaction, surfacing only once 24/42 are fixed. Also the finding's 'or, absent such rows, force a full rewrite ... before 47 widens it again' is right about the rewrite but the rewrite happens in either case: narrowing numeric always rewrites (numeric_support relabels only for widening), and 47's later widening back to (20,4) is then metadata-only, so the cost is one full 3.6 GB rewrite, not two.
- **Verifier correction:** Impact ordering (confirming the first verifier's correction, with fresh numbers): a replay aborts at file 24, not 31. Live stock_prices.open/high/low/close are numeric(24,8) (migration 48 applied), sqlschema/24_widen_price_precision.sql:13-23 narrows them to NUMERIC(20,8), and `select max(greatest(open,high,low,close)), count(*) filter (where greatest(open,high,low,close) >= 1e12) from stock_prices` returns 1928232000000.00000000 | 7 — so 24 overflows first and rolls back the whole transaction. File 31 therefore never executes today; it is the next failure in line, surfacing only once 24/42 are fixed, which is precisely why it needs its own fix. Also, the finding's "or, absent such rows, force a full rewrite ... before 47 widens it again" understates nothing but mis-frames the cost: narrowing numeric always requires a rewrite (the no-rewrite fast path applies only to widening at equal scale), so the cost is one full 3.6 GB ACCESS EXCLUSIVE rewrite at 31 and a metadata-only relabel back to (20,4) at 47 — not two rewrites. In practice the rewrite at 31 aborts partway with numeric field overflow on the 23 ADTX rows.

#### 5. income_statements stores EPS and share counts on the as-filed split basis, so a single ticker's quarterly series jumps bases with no corporate action (KLAC: 0.91, 0.87, 8.47, 9.05) and get_fundamentals reports pure-artifact eps_growth_yoy values such as -9635% and +330%. *(filed independently by 3 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** split-basis
- **Reviewers:** fundamentals-tables, split-basis-inventory
- **Location:** `income_statements.basic_earnings_per_share / diluted_earnings_per_share / basic_shares_outstanding / diluted_shares_outstanding`
- **Location:** `income_statements.diluted_earnings_per_share / basic_earnings_per_share / diluted_shares_outstanding; served by mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py:96-97,118-127`
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py:121-124`
- **Evidence:** Polygon re-emits a period whenever a later filing reports it as a comparative, so a period refreshed after a split lands on the new basis while its neighbours keep the old one. Nothing re-bases stored EPS. Running the EXACT eps_growth_yoy expression from mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py:118-127 for BKNG (1:25 split 2026-04-06):
  period_end   diluted_eps   shares         prior_pe     prior_eps   eps_growth_yoy
  2026-03-31   1.3600        794,000,000    2025-03-31   0.4000      240.00
  2025-12-31   1.7700        815,975,000    2024-12-31   1.2900       37.21
  2025-09-30   84.4100        32,558,000    2024-09-30   74.3400      13.55
  2025-06-30   27.4300        32,619,000    2024-06-30   44.3800     -38.19
  2025-03-31   0.4000       827,000,000     2024-03-31   22.3700     -98.21
  2024-12-31   1.2900       851,600,000     2023-12-31   0.3000      330.00
Adjacent quarters flip between 32.5M and 815M shares. KLAC (1:10) shows the same: 134,858,000 shares at 2024-09-30 vs 1,339,260,000 at 2024-12-31 vs 133,750,000 at 2025-06-30.
Scope: 2,243 adjacent-quarter share-count breaks (ratio >1.7x or <0.6x) across 930 tickers in income_statements quarterly.
- **Impact:** Every EPS chart, EPS series and eps_growth_yoy value served by get_fundamentals for 930 tickers is wrong — the -98.21% and +330% above are pure basis artifacts, not earnings changes. Distinct from the confirmed round-1 `earnings.eps_actual` finding: that table is frozen on ONE basis; here a single series carries TWO bases simultaneously.
- **Recommendation:** Store the as-filed values but add a split-adjustment factor at read time: multiply stored shares (and divide stored EPS) by the cumulative split ratio for splits with execution_date > filing_date. Alternatively re-base all rows to the current basis whenever the split heal runs, the same way stock_prices is re-adjusted.
- **Evidence:** KLAC has exactly one split on record: `SELECT * FROM stock_splits WHERE ticker='KLAC'` -> 2026-06-12, 1:10. Its stored quarterly series:
  period_end   basic_eps  diluted_eps  diluted_shares_outstanding
  2026-03-31     0.9200     0.9100     1317500000
  2025-12-31     0.8700     0.8700     1320090000
  2025-09-30     8.5100     8.4700      132381000
  2025-06-30     9.0900     9.0500      133750000
  2025-03-31     0.8200     0.8200     1333030000
  2024-12-31     0.6200     0.6200     1339260000
The basis alternates quarter by quarter -- not one boundary at the split date. Same pattern in BKNG (25:1 on 2026-04-06): 2025-09-30 shows 84.86 EPS on 32.4M shares while 2025-03-31 shows 0.41 on 821M shares, both rows created 2026-02-12, before the split.
Root cause visible in filing_date: the restated prior-year comparative period from a later 10-Q is written over the original row under PK (ticker, period_end, timeframe) -- KLAC's 2025-03-31 row carries filing_date 2026-04-30 and its 2024-12-31 row carries filing_date 2026-01-30, one year off.
Scale: adjacent-quarter diluted_shares_outstanding jumps >3x number 1,203 across 527 tickers; 138 of them across 92 tickers have a jump ratio that matches a recorded split ratio for that ticker to within 3% while no split executed between the two period_ends. Separately, 3,364 rows across 165 tickers were loaded before a split that has since executed and were never re-based.
- **Impact:** get_fundamentals (mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py:96-97) returns this series verbatim as basic_eps/diluted_eps, so a quarterly EPS trend for any split ticker shows a 10x or 25x step that no corporate action explains; v_latest_fundamentals.basic_eps inherits it; any P/E computed from these EPS values against the adjusted price is off by the split ratio.
- **Recommendation:** Either key income_statements on the filing that produced the row (add filing_date or an accession id to the PK) so a comparative period cannot overwrite an original, or normalise every row to the current basis on write via SplitAdjuster.factor(ticker, period_end) and re-base the whole table in the split heal. Add an integrity check that flags any adjacent-quarter share-count ratio outside, say, 0.5-2.0.
- **Evidence:** The tool pairs each quarter with the row 11-13 months back and reports ((b.diluted_eps - prior.diluted_eps) / ABS(prior.diluted_eps) * 100) with no basis normalisation. Reproducing that exact pairing against income_statements and flagging pairs whose diluted_shares_outstanding differ by >3x:
  ticker  period_end  eps_now   shares_now  prior_pe    eps_prior  shares_prior  share_ratio  served_growth
  ADTX    2026-03-31  -790.4900     20174   2025-03-31    -8.1200     703666      0.029x     -9635.1%
  APVO    2026-03-31    -6.4100   1060678   2025-03-31 -1581.8100       4051    261.8x        +99.6%
  ASST    2026-03-31    -4.5300  61630003   2025-03-31    -1.6500    2275940     27.1x       -174.5%
  BCTX    2026-04-30    -0.9900   7250487   2025-04-30   -16.4200     373067     19.4x        +94.0%
Scale, restricted to period_end >= 2024-06-01: 565 of 13,449 YoY pairs (4.2%) across 242 tickers straddle a >3x share-basis break.
- **Impact:** Every eps_growth_yoy value for a ticker that split in the trailing year is a split ratio dressed up as fundamental growth, in the same sign and magnitude range a reader would treat as a real result. Reverse-split microcaps, which dominate the affected set, are exactly where a +94% or -9635% EPS swing would be acted on.
- **Recommendation:** Normalise both sides of the comparison before dividing -- multiply the prior period's EPS by prior.diluted_shares_outstanding / b.diluted_shares_outstanding, or apply SplitAdjuster.factor between the two period_ends -- and return NULL for eps_growth_yoy when the share bases differ by more than a tolerance rather than emitting a number.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** 97 and 105 are the same unrestated-EPS defect described from two angles (930 tickers affected; KLAC is the worked example). 106 is the derived growth figure computed across that boundary — folded in because restating the stored series eliminates it entirely, so it is not an independent fix. Two reviewers said high, one medium for the derived value; high is right, the served numbers are split ratios presented as earnings growth.
- **Verifier correction:** The data corruption is confirmed exactly as claimed; the causal mechanism is stated with more confidence than the evidence supports. All the KLAC rows in question share created_at 2026-02-12 (a single download), and KLAC rows carrying the post-split basis have provider filing_dates of 2026-01-30 / 2026-04-30 that precede the 2026-06-12 split — so 'a period refreshed after a split lands on the new basis' is not fully established. Polygon's filing_date on these rows is itself unreliable (the live COO GET shows period_end 2010-10-31 stamped filing_date 2012-12-20, and rows exist with filing_date later than created_at), so the exact re-emission rule is unverified. What is verified: a single ticker's stored quarterly series carries two share/EPS bases simultaneously, and get_fundamentals computes eps_growth_yoy across them.
- **Verifier correction:** Two numeric overstatements in the write-up, and one under-stated dimension:

1. Of the six BKNG figures quoted, only -98.21% is a cross-basis artifact. +330.00 (2024-12-31 vs 2023-12-31) compares 1.29 @ 851,600,000 shares against 0.30 @ 913,250,000 — both already on the 25x basis — and net income moved 222M -> 1068M (+381%), so that value is roughly real. Likewise +240.00 (2026-03-31 vs 2025-03-31) is same-basis (794M vs 827M shares; NI 333M -> 1083M = +225%), and 13.55 / -38.19 are both pre-split-basis pairs. The quoted table demonstrates the mixed-basis series correctly, but only one of its growth numbers is a pure artifact.

2. "Every EPS chart, EPS series and eps_growth_yoy value ... for 930 tickers is wrong" is broader than what the data supports. Measured: 3,279 of ~51,986 YoY quarter pairs (LAG-4 approximation) straddle a >1.7x/<0.6x share-basis change, across 817 tickers — those growth values are artifacts. Separately, 1,335 tickers have a >1.7x max/min share spread inside their 20 most recent quarters, i.e. the EPS *series* the tool can serve is internally incoherent for those, which is the larger and more certain harm. Also note not every one of the 930 break tickers has a split: only 514 of them appear in stock_splits at all, so a share of the 2,243 breaks are genuine dilution/reverse-merger events rather than basis flips.

3. The corruption is not confined to timeframe='quarterly'. trailing_twelve_months rows flip too (KLAC 2024-09-30 TTM 135,788,750 shares / 21.89 EPS vs 2024-12-31 TTM 1,352,067,500 / 2.38), so any TTM-EPS consumer inherits it.

4. Mechanism: round 1's doubt is partly resolvable. created_at is written only on first insert (the loader's ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE column list is built from the CSV columns and does not include created_at), so KLAC's post-split-basis rows carrying created_at 2026-02-12 are consistent with a later refresh after the 2026-06-12 split. The provider filing_date remains unreliable (BKNG's 2025-03-31 row carries filing_date 2026-04-28 with created_at 2026-02-12, i.e. a filing_date later than the row's creation), so the exact re-emission rule is still unverified — but the flip factor equaling each ticker's own split ratio makes split basis the mechanism, not tagging noise.
- **Verifier correction:** Two scale figures do not reproduce. (a) The split-ratio-matched subset: with the finding's own stated test (jump ratio within 3% of a recorded split ratio, no split executed between the two period_ends) I get 90 jumps across 70 tickers, not 138 across 92. (b) The '3,364 rows across 165 tickers loaded before a split that has since executed' figure reproduces under neither natural definition -- 17,892 rows / 863 tickers by period_end preceding a split, or 5,695 rows / 270 tickers by created_at preceding a split. The stated root cause is also only half right: KLAC's 2025-12-31 row carries filing_date 2026-01-30 with a post-split (1.32B) share count, i.e. a filing five months BEFORE the 2026-06-12 split already holds post-split numbers, so provider-side restatement on refetch is at least as likely a mechanism as comparative-period overwrite (created_at is not refreshed on conflict, so it cannot date the content). Both mechanisms land in the same place and neither changes the served defect. Minor: the finding says v_latest_fundamentals.basic_eps inherits the problem -- true (sqlschema/06_views.sql:33), but that view has no consumer anywhere outside sawa/database/schema.py's expected-object list, so it adds no blast radius.
- **Verifier correction:** Two sub-figures do not reproduce, and one impact claim is understated.

(a) "138 jumps across 92 tickers" matching a recorded split ratio: applying the finding's own stated test (jump ratio within 3% of a recorded split ratio for that ticker, no split executed between the two period_ends) I get 163 jumps across 100 tickers. Verifier 1 got 90/70. Three formulations, three answers — the exact number is not reproducible, though the order of magnitude (roughly 100-160 jumps, 70-100 tickers) holds.

(b) "3,364 rows across 165 tickers loaded before a split that has since executed" reproduces under neither reading: 8,073 rows / 863 tickers for quarterly rows whose period_end precedes a split, or 2,587 rows / 270 tickers for quarterly rows whose created_at precedes a split. Verifier 1's larger numbers (17,892 and 5,695) come from including all timeframes. No formulation lands near 3,364/165.

NEW, and it makes the impact worse than the finding states. The finding claims impact only on the raw EPS series, but get_fundamentals also derives `eps_growth_yoy` from the same mixed-basis column (mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py:122-125, pairing each row with the row 11-13 months back). 70 such year-over-year pairs across 41 tickers have their comparative on a different split basis, with a ratio matching a recorded split and no split executed in the intervening 12 months. KLAC is one of them: for period_end 2025-03-31 the tool computes (0.82 - 4.43)/4.43 = -81.5%, presenting an 82% earnings collapse, when the true same-basis comparison is 0.82 vs 0.443 = +85% growth. The sign flips. Other served values: HBIO 2025-03-31 at -10,281.8%, BGLC 2025-03-31 at -3,400%, NDLS 2025-04-01 at -1,028.6%. Contrary to my initial reading, KLAC's YoY is not incidentally basis-aligned — the 2025-03-31 row was restated to post-split while 2024-03-31 (filing_date 2025-05-01) was not.

Note also that a separate, unrelated unit-scale defect contaminates this table (e.g. FWONK 2025-06-30 stores diluted_shares_outstanding = 1,000 with EPS 204,000; EVEX 2025-03-31 stores 1,000 shares with EPS -48,784). Those are not split-basis cases and are excluded from every count above, but they inflate any naive share-count-jump screen and are worth a separate finding.

On the disputed root cause: I agree with verifier 1 that comparative-period overwrite is not the whole story. KLAC's 2025-12-31 row carries filing_date 2026-01-30 with a post-split 1.32B share count, and that filing predates the 2026-06-12 split by five months, so provider-side restatement on refetch is also in play. Both mechanisms terminate at the same PK-level overwrite and neither changes the served defect.
- **Verifier correction:** The counts and the causal attribution both need correcting. Reproducing the tool's pairing over period_end >= 2024-06-01 with the tool's own CASE guards (prior eps NOT NULL and <> 0) yields 26,175 served eps_growth_yoy values, of which 1,060 across 348 tickers straddle a >3x share-basis break -- not '565 of 13,449 across 242'. The percentage the finding quotes (4.2%) does match mine (4.05%), so only the absolute counts are off. More materially: only 527 of those 1,060 pairs (169 tickers) actually have a split executing between the two period_ends. The other 533 have no split at all and are genuine dilution in nanocaps, where a 20x share increase and a correspondingly huge EPS swing is a real result, not an artefact. So the population that is 'a split ratio dressed up as fundamental growth' is ~527 pairs across 169 tickers, about 2.0% of served values, not 4.2%. The recommendation to return NULL above a share-basis tolerance would also suppress those 533 legitimate dilution cases, so it should key on a recorded split between the period_ends rather than on the share ratio alone.

#### 6. The earnings table has not been written since 2026-02-24 because populate_earnings.py is manual and unscheduled, so the forward calendar is empty and ~2,000-2,900 already-reported Q1/Q2-2026 results are still served as 'upcoming' with NULL eps_actual. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** earnings-data
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions, ops-scripts-monitoring
- **Location:** `earnings (all 55,848 rows created_at = 2026-02-24), scripts/populate_earnings.py (manual, never scheduled)`
- **Location:** `scripts/populate_earnings.py (manual only); sawa/doctor.py (no earnings check); scripts/market_scheduler.sh (no slot)`
- **Evidence:** min/max created_at = 2026-02-24 03:38:09 / 12:00:34 for all 55,848 rows. Per-quarter actual completeness: 2025Q4 2417 rows/2375 actuals; 2026Q1 2358/1127; 2026Q2 1211/0; 2026Q3 5/0. Rows with report_date between 2026-02-25 and today = 2341, of which 0 have eps_actual. Replaying get_earnings_calendar('2026-03-01','2026-09-02', upcoming_only=True) returns 1,993 rows — every one a report that already happened. Replaying it forward, get_earnings_calendar('2026-09-03','2026-10-31', upcoming_only=True) returns 1 row; SELECT count(*) FROM earnings WHERE report_date >= CURRENT_DATE = 1.
- **Impact:** Two failure modes at once: the forward earnings calendar is empty (1 row for the next two months instead of ~2,300 per quarter), and the `upcoming_only` filter mislabels 1,993 completed Q1/Q2-2026 reports as upcoming because it infers pendingness from a NULL actual rather than from the date. Any 'who reports next week' or 'did they beat' question is answered wrongly.
- **Recommendation:** Schedule populate_earnings.py (weekly, market-cap ordered) or move earnings onto the automated pipeline. Independently, harden the tool: `upcoming_only` should require `e.report_date >= CURRENT_DATE` in addition to (or instead of) `eps_actual IS NULL`, and get_data_status should surface max(earnings.updated_at) so staleness is visible.
- **Evidence:** SELECT max(updated_at) FROM earnings → 2026-02-24; SELECT count(*), count(DISTINCT ticker) FROM earnings WHERE eps_actual IS NULL AND report_date < CURRENT_DATE → 2947 rows / 2524 tickers (max report_date 2026-08-06); 20 NULL-actual rows sit within 7 days of an actual for the same ticker (the phantom pattern prune_phantom_estimates targets, only cleared on a re-run); docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:198-210 says to run the script 'periodically (e.g. once a quarter)'.
- **Impact:** MCP earnings tools serve two missed reporting seasons as estimates with no actuals/surprise, plus stale phantom duplicates; no alert ever fires.
- **Recommendation:** Add a scheduler slot (e.g. first closed evening of each month after weekly, `--auto-resume`) or a documented cron; add a doctor WARN on earnings max(updated_at) age > 45 days and on past-dated NULL-actual rows.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same root cause (no scheduled run) and same fix; the two reviewers counted the mislabelled rows differently (1,993 vs 2,947) depending on whether they filtered to reported-and-still-NULL or all past-dated rows. Both symptoms — empty forward calendar and past reports labelled upcoming — follow from the single missing refresh.
- **Verifier correction:** The 'served by get_earnings_calendar(upcoming_only=True)' half is not reachable through the MCP server: upcoming_only is absent from the tool's input_schema (mcp_server/server.py:1545-1574) and the dispatcher at :2505-2514 never passes it, so it is always False for MCP callers (unlike get_dividends, where it IS exposed at :1464/:2478). The 1,993 mislabeled-as-upcoming rows are a latent defect in the Python function for direct callers, not something an MCP user can trigger today. The user-visible impact is the near-empty forward calendar plus 2,341 reported-but-NULL actuals.
- **Verifier correction:** Four corrections plus one new defect found while tracing.

(a) Minor evidence slip: min(created_at) is 2026-02-24 03:22:01.368545, not 03:38:09 — 03:38:09 is min(updated_at). Same day either way; conclusion unaffected.

(b) The "per-quarter" table in the evidence is bucketed by calendar quarter of report_date, NOT by the earnings.fiscal_year/fiscal_quarter columns. Grouping by the actual fiscal columns returns zero rows, because they are NULL on all 55,848 rows (count(fiscal_year) = count(fiscal_quarter) = 0). The report_date-bucketed numbers match the finding exactly.

(c) NEW, separate defect: scripts/populate_earnings.py DOES derive and write those columns — `derive_fiscal_period(report_date)` at :152/:180-186, bound into the INSERT at :247-264 — yet 100% of live rows are NULL for both. The version that last ran on 2026-02-24 predates that code (or it returned NULL). Consequence: get_earnings_calendar (corporate_actions.py:337-338) and get_earnings_history (:379-380) both SELECT e.fiscal_quarter and e.fiscal_year, and both always return NULL for every row, while the get_earnings_calendar docstring advertises "fiscal period (calendar quarter of the report)".

(d) I confirm the first verifier: upcoming_only is absent from get_earnings_calendar's MCP input_schema (mcp_server/server.py:1545-1574 — the properties are start_date, end_date, index, timing, limit) and the dispatcher at :2505-2513 never passes it, so MCP callers always get the default False. get_dividends does expose it (:1464 schema, :2478 dispatch), so the omission is inconsistent rather than deliberate.

(e) Sharpening the impact, which changes what should be fixed first: in the 2026-03-01..2026-09-02 window, upcoming_only=True and upcoming_only=False return the SAME 1,993 rows, because every row in that window already has NULL eps_actual. The flag is a no-op there. So the mislabeling is not what corrupts the answer — the missing actuals are, and they are served identically to MCP and direct callers by both get_earnings_calendar and get_earnings_history. The undated `eps_actual IS NULL` predicate is still a real latent bug worth hardening (it will mislabel again the moment the table is refreshed and genuinely-pending rows coexist with reported ones), but it is second in priority behind refreshing the table. Also note the tool caps results at limit=200, max 500, so a caller sees at most 500 of the 1,993 rows regardless.

One favourable note for the fix: the ON CONFLICT clause at populate_earnings.py:262-269 uses COALESCE(EXCLUDED.eps_actual, earnings.eps_actual), so a plain rerun fills the NULL actuals from yfinance without needing a delete, and would repopulate fiscal_year/fiscal_quarter at the same time.

#### 7. Stored obv and vwap are window-relative garbage for ~420k rows across ~9,000 tickers between 2026-05-15 and 2026-08-14 (pre-seed incremental code) and were never repaired after the seeding fix landed. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** indicators, ta-engine
- **Location:** `technical_indicators.obv / technical_indicators.vwap (sawa/database/ta_load.py:236 get_cumulative_indicator_seed; sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:252)`
- **Location:** `technical_indicators.obv / technical_indicators.vwap (written by sawa/daily.py:1060-1118 via _new_ta_rows; seed fix in sawa/database/ta_load.py:236-286 only affects rows written after 2026-08-30)`
- **Evidence:** Exact SQL recompute of both cumulative series over the whole table (SUM(...) OVER (PARTITION BY ticker ORDER BY date) on current stock_prices):
  obv:  429,395 rows wrong across 8,995 tickers, min date 2021-02-18, max date 2026-08-13
  vwap: 416,712 rows wrong across 8,498 tickers, same range (tolerance = 0.05% of value)
By year, obv bad rows / tickers: 2021 1,242/7 | 2022 1,776/8 | 2023 2,003/9 | 2024 2,223/11 | 2025 2,908/15 | 2026 419,243/8,995. Zero bad rows on or after 2026-08-14.
Concrete AAPL rows (stored vs exact):
  2026-05-14 vwap 179.0940 vs 179.0940 (diff 0); obv 2,443,442,410 vs 2,443,442,410 (diff 0)
  2026-05-15 vwap 255.5536 vs 179.1686 (diff +76.39); obv 958,901,254 vs 2,498,305,613 (diff -1,539,404,359)
  2026-06-15 vwap 206.7290 vs 180.5455 (diff +26.18)
  2026-08-13 vwap 216.3238 vs 183.8779 (diff +32.45); obv max abs error over the run 1,726,600,484 (~59% of AAPL's true OBV level)
  2026-08-14 vwap 183.9152 vs 183.9152 (diff 0)
MSFT shows the same step (2026-05-15 stored vwap 453.2908 vs exact 346.7241). Cause traced in code: cumulative_seed / get_cumulative_indicator_seed was added on 2026-08-30 in commit dec5e9f (`git log -S 'cumulative_seed' -- sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py`); before that the bounded incremental window restarted OBV at the window's first bar and VWAP at the window's first bar, so every daily append after 2026-05-15 wrote window-relative values. The docstring at sawa/database/ta_load.py:243 promises only that "future incremental rows self-heal" — nothing ever rewrote the ~419k already-stored rows.
- **Impact:** get_technical_indicators, get_advanced_volume_indicators, detect_volume_anomalies and any historical OBV/VWAP chart return values that are wrong by up to 59% of level (OBV) and 43% of level (VWAP) for a 3-month window covering nearly every ticker in the universe, including AAPL and MSFT. OBV is used as an accumulation/distribution signal, so a 1.7e9 offset inverts the visible trend; the fake 2026-05-15 jump and 2026-08-14 snap-back look like real accumulation events.
- **Recommendation:** Run a full-history TA recompute (`sawa ta-backfill` with replace_existing, or recompute_ta_for_tickers over all tickers) so obv/vwap are rebuilt from the cumulative seed for every stored date. Then add a cheap invariant check to doctor: obv_t - obv_{t-1} must equal +volume/-volume/0 against the current stock_prices row, which detects this class in one indexed pass.
- **Evidence:** OBV recurrence check (obv[d]-obv[d-1] must equal +vol/-vol/0 by close direction, joined to stock_prices) by date: 8,761 broken of 10,289 steps on 2026-05-15; 8,027-8,124/day through 2026-06-15; 5,829-6,041/day 2026-06-16..2026-08-14; then 22 (08-17), 2, 4, 1, 1, 0 thereafter; 6-24/day before 05-15.
