Full-history recompute vs stored (ta_compare.py): AAPL 2026-06-10 obv stored 714,944,881 vs full 2,441,545,365; AAPL vwap 2026-06-12 stored 265.3279 vs full 180.4877; SCHL obv 18,068,902 vs 31,815,638; SYNA obv 12,373,925 vs -19,826,436 (sign flipped); FCFS vwap 180.38 vs 113.13. 15 of 16 randomly sampled tickers show 20-62 wrong obv/vwap rows, all in 2026-05-15..2026-08-13. Only 148 of 10,404 tickers have rows since 05-15 created today (full recompute); the rest keep the bad rows. Consumers: sawa/repositories/database.py:1043-1137 and sawa/ta_query.py:109,122 serve obv/vwap from this table. sawa/doctor.py:395-530 has no check that would detect it.
- **Impact:** get_technical_indicators / screeners return OBV values that are off by up to 70% (or wrong sign) and VWAP values off by up to 47% for ~62 trading days across ~5,800-8,700 tickers; any OBV-divergence or VWAP-based analysis over May-Aug 2026 is invalid. The daily path never rewrites rows older than the 14-day overlap, so this persists indefinitely.
- **Recommendation:** Run the full-universe `sawa ta-backfill --workers N` that ta_engine.py:342-345 and docs/OPERATIONS.md:65 already prescribe after code changes (the 2026-06-13 and 2026-08-30 fixes shipped without it). Add a doctor check that samples tickers and asserts the stored OBV recurrence (and VWAP monotone cumulative identity) holds over the last N days so future incremental regressions are caught.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Identical defect and date range; counts differ only in sampling (419k/8,995 vs 424k, errors up to 59%/70% OBV and 43%/47% VWAP). Needs a backfill, not just the already-landed code fix.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed but two impact claims are wrong: get_advanced_volume_indicators does NOT read stored obv/vwap - it recomputes both in Python anchored at the requested window start (volume_analysis.py:390-450), and detect_volume_anomalies references neither column. The genuinely affected consumers are get_technical_indicators (stored vwap/obv columns) and screen_technical_indicators (obv filter). Severity high rather than critical: the corruption is a closed historical window (2026-05-15..2026-08-13) in 2 of 30 indicator columns; every value from 2026-08-14 onward, including all latest-date tools, is exact. Also note the fix was deployed with commit dec5e9f on 2026-08-30 and the 14-day repair overlap of that run is what set the 2026-08-14 boundary - a full ta-backfill is still needed for the ~3 months before it.
- **Verifier correction:** Two impact claims in the finding are wrong (confirming the first verifier's correction): get_advanced_volume_indicators does NOT read stored obv/vwap — it recomputes both in Python from raw prices at mcp_server/tools/volume_analysis.py:401-448 — and detect_volume_anomalies references neither column (it selects only date/high/low/close/volume at volume_analysis.py:55,222). Two affected consumers the finding missed: sawa/ta_query.py:109 and :122 (CLI display of vwap/obv) and mcp_server/tools/screener.py:152 (`"obv": FilterSpec("ti.obv", "obv")` — screen_technical_indicators filters on the stored column). The confirmed affected set is therefore get_technical_indicators (market_data.py:417,424), screen_technical_indicators, and the ta_query CLI. The VWAP error magnitude is understated: max absolute error in the 40-ticker random sample reaches 102.45% of the true value, not 43%. The claimed pre-2026 bad rows (1,242 in 2021 across 7 tickers, etc.) did not appear in the 40-ticker sample — consistent with them being a handful of tickers, but not independently confirmed here. One detail to note for the repair: a small residue of invariant violations persists after the boundary (22 rows on 2026-08-17, then 2, 4, 1, 1 through 2026-08-21), separate from the main window and plausibly from today's split-basis TA recompute.
- **Verifier correction:** Bad range is 2026-05-15..2026-08-13, not ..08-14: AAPL's stored obv equals the full-history value exactly from 2026-08-14 onward (diff 0 on 08-14, 08-17, 08-18, ...) while 08-13 is off by +321,085,178. Scope: 394,555 TA rows across 6,458 tickers in 05-15..08-13 have a first price bar older than the 1280-day fetch window (so a truncated VWAP/OBV basis), and the 05-15..06-12 sub-window used an even shorter lookback, affecting ~8,100 tickers/day - so ~400-460k wrong rows, consistent with the claimed ~424k. The parenthetical about a '14-day overlap' is wrong: the ordinary daily path is strictly incremental - sawa/daily.py:55-70 (_new_ta_rows / _ta_rows_to_persist) keeps only rows with date > last_ta - so it never rewrites any older row at all, which makes the persistence argument stronger, not weaker. Also, get_advanced_volume_indicators is NOT a consumer of the bad columns (it recomputes them); the real consumers are get_technical_indicators / get_latest_technical_indicators and the screener obv filter.
- **Verifier correction:** Corrections and additions to the finding as written:

1. BAD RANGE is 2026-05-15..2026-08-13 inclusive (62 trading dates), not "..08-14". 2026-08-14 rows already match the full-history recompute exactly for every ticker sampled (AAPL, MSFT, XOM, KO, SYNA, SCHL, FCFS all show 0.0% error on 08-14). The 6,041 broken steps dated 08-14 are the *exit* step from the last bad row (08-13) into the first good row (08-14). The first verifier's correction on this point is right and I confirm it independently.

2. SCOPE: I measured ~409,000 wrong rows universe-wide (400-ticker random sample: 15,704 wrong of 374,441 rows, 344/399 tickers = 86% affected, extrapolated over 10,404 tickers), slightly below the claimed ~424k but the same order. 630,818 TA rows exist in the window in total. Broken OBV steps: 406,552 across 8,993 tickers.

3. The impact paragraph's "The daily path never rewrites rows older than the 14-day overlap" is imprecise. The 14-day figure is PRICE_REPAIR_OVERLAP_DAYS (sawa/daily.py:52), which governs price re-fetch, not TA. The TA path is strictly incremental with no overlap at all: sawa/daily.py:55-70 (_new_ta_rows keeps only date > last_ta; _ta_rows_to_persist widens this only when a price repair supplied recompute_from). This strengthens the persistence claim.

4. created_at IS NOT VALID EVIDENCE of "never rewritten" and should be dropped from the argument. sawa/database/ta_load.py:61-67 builds ON CONFLICT (ticker, date) DO UPDATE SET over TechnicalIndicators.column_names() only, which excludes created_at (column default CURRENT_TIMESTAMP), so an upsert leaves created_at at first-insert time. Rows for 08-14..08-29 are provably correct yet would still carry their original created_at. The direct stored-vs-recomputed value comparison is what proves the defect; the lineage argument in the first vote is unsound even though its conclusion is right.

5. CONSUMERS: mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:417,424 and 460,467 select vwap and obv straight from technical_indicators (verified live - the MCP tool returned AAPL 2026-06-10 obv 714944881 / vwap 264.7016), and mcp_server/tools/screener.py:152 maps an "obv" filter to ti.obv. get_advanced_volume_indicators is NOT a consumer: mcp_server/tools/volume_analysis.py:400-448 recomputes OBV and VWAP from prices in Python.

6. The title's causal claim ("for every ticker with history longer than the lookback window") does not hold exactly. REAX has 1,885 calendar days of history (well beyond the 1280-day window) yet has zero wrong rows, and KW (1,914 days) is wrong only in 05-15..06-12. So per-ticker full recomputes (split repairs, price-repair-triggered recompute_from) also cleared some tickers. This changes the wording, not the conclusion or the measured scope.

7. Doctor gap confirmed: grep for obv|vwap across sawa/doctor.py returns no match at all, so no existing check could detect this. The recommended remedy (`sawa ta-backfill --workers N`, prescribed at sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:337-345 and docs/OPERATIONS.md:65, docs/MAINTENANCE.md:363) is the correct one.

#### 8. `sawa intraday` is launched as a background job from a non-interactive bash script so it inherits SIGINT=SIG_IGN, ignores the scheduler's graceful stop, and is SIGKILLed 60s later, discarding the entire unflushed bar aggregator every session. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** intraday-stream
- **Reviewers:** intraday-live, intraday-live-code
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:431`
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:431,476,490-493; sawa/api/websocket_client.py:875-957 (run() relies on asyncio cancellation, no add_signal_handler); sawa/intraday.py:156-163`
- **Evidence:** The live process: `cat ~/.sawa/scheduler/intraday.pid` -> 144278; `grep -E '^Sig(Ign|Cgt):' /proc/144278/status` -> SigIgn: 0000000001001006 (bits for signal 2=SIGINT and 3=SIGQUIT set), SigCgt: 0000000100000000 (SIGINT bit NOT set). The process cannot receive SIGINT at all. Cause: market_scheduler.sh:431 starts it as `sawa intraday --log-dir "$PROJECT_DIR/logs" >/dev/null 2>&1 9>&- &` — an asynchronous command in a non-interactive, job-control-free shell (cron), which POSIX requires to inherit SIG_IGN for SIGINT/SIGQUIT; CPython installs its own SIGINT handler only when the inherited disposition is SIG_DFL. Consequence in ~/.sawa/scheduler/scheduler.log: `grep -c 'did not exit gracefully, sending SIGKILL'` -> 13, i.e. 13 of 13 stops, e.g. lines 9424/9430 '[2026-09-02 16:00:02 ET] Stopping intraday (PID 116429)...' then '[2026-09-02 16:01:02 ET] WARN: Intraday did not exit gracefully, sending SIGKILL'. Corroborated from the other side: `grep -c 'Shutting down\|Shutdown complete\|Interrupted by user'` over logs/intraday_2026090{1,2,3}_133002.log -> 0 in every file; the last line of each is a routine '💾 Saved N bars'. So sawa/api/websocket_client.py:959 shutdown() -> :970 _flush_completed_bars(flush_all=True) -> final _batch_write_to_db(), and sawa/intraday.py record_stream_outcome(), have never executed in production.
- **Impact:** Every session, the in-memory bar_aggregator (one partially-filled 5-minute window per ticker for ~9,300 tickers, plus any window whose per-ticker watermark had not yet advanced) and up to batch_timeout=30s of buffered-but-unwritten bars are destroyed without a trace. Because record_stream_outcome() never runs, none of the loss telemetry the client carefully maintains (dropped_buffered_bars, minute_events_accepted, invalid_minute_events, provider_status_errors, stream_recovery_pending) is ever emitted, and stdout/stderr go to /dev/null. The operator receives an ordinary info-level 'Sawa Intraday Stopped' ntfy notification that is indistinguishable from a clean shutdown. A session that streamed nothing at all, or that dropped 50,000 bars at the retry-buffer cap, would look identical.
- **Recommendation:** Two independent fixes, both cheap: (1) in sawa/cli.py's intraday entry point, explicitly re-arm the handler at startup — `if signal.getsignal(signal.SIGINT) is signal.SIG_IGN: signal.signal(signal.SIGINT, signal.default_int_handler)` — so an inherited SIG_IGN cannot silence the shutdown path; (2) in market_scheduler.sh, prefer SIGTERM over SIGINT for stop_intraday (line 476, and the cleanup kill at line 445) and install a SIGTERM handler in the client that sets self.running=False and awaits shutdown(). Additionally, have stop_intraday send an ERROR-level notification (not info) whenever it has to escalate to SIGKILL, and log the intraday process's exit status instead of discarding it.
- **Evidence:** ~/.sawa/scheduler/scheduler.log: every one of the 13 stops since 2026-08-20 reads 'Stopping intraday (PID …)' at 16:00:01/02 ET then 'WARN: Intraday did not exit gracefully, sending SIGKILL' at 16:01 (grep -c 'did not exit gracefully' = 13 = grep -c 'Stopping intraday'). logs/intraday_20260902_133002.log ends at 2026-09-02 20:00:08 UTC with a normal '💾 Saved 208 bars … 19:10 - 19:35' written 6 s AFTER the SIGINT; grep -c 'Shutting down|Shutdown complete|Interrupted' = 0 in both the 09-01 and 09-02 logs, i.e. shutdown() (websocket_client.py:959-995, first line logs 'Shutting down...') never ran. Reproduction in scratchpad/sigtest.sh using the scheduler's exact launch shape (set -euo pipefail; `python3 … >out 2>&1 9>&- &`): prints 'child SIGINT disposition: 1' (SIG_IGN), 'asyncio would install handler: False', and after `kill -INT $pid` the child prints 'survived SIGINT, still running'. bash manual: when job control is off, asynchronous commands ignore SIGINT/SIGQUIT; CPython keeps an inherited SIG_IGN, and asyncio.run() installs its SIGINT handler only when the current handler is default_int_handler.
- **Impact:** Daily data loss and wrong data served: everything still in the in-memory aggregator/buffer at 16:01 is discarded, so the persisted session ends at the 15:35 window (last save range 19:10-19:35 UTC on 09-01 and 09-02). From 16:00 until `sawa daily` lands EOD (daily_done flags at 22:03-22:07 UTC = ~18:05 ET), stock_prices_live / get_latest_price(use_live) / get_stock_prices(use_live) / get_intraday_bars(aggregate=true) serve a 'today' candle whose close is the ~15:39 price and whose volume lacks ~25 min including the closing auction (AAPL 2026-09-02 via Polygon 1-min data: through-15:39 C 325.11 / V 24.95M vs official 324.96 / 33.59M). record_stream_outcome() and monitored_run never execute, so dropped_buffered_bars / invalid / late telemetry and the intraday FAILED notification are never emitted (the 2026-08-31 whole-session loss was invisible to alerting). sawa doctor has zero intraday checks, so nothing detects the truncation.
- **Recommendation:** In PolygonWebSocketClient.run()/run_intraday, register explicit handlers with loop.add_signal_handler(SIGINT/SIGTERM, …) that set running=False and cancel the main task (signal.signal overrides an inherited SIG_IGN); alternatively `set -m`/`trap - INT` before the `&` in start_intraday, and prefer SIGTERM. Because load_intraday_bars issues one INSERT per bar (~2k rows/s observed: 4,176 bars in ~2 s), switch the final flush to executemany/COPY and raise INTRADAY_STOP_TIMEOUT well above 60 s so a ~40k-bar final flush cannot be SIGKILLed mid-transaction. Add a doctor check that today's MAX(timestamp) in stock_prices_intraday reaches the 15:55 window after the close, and add a test that a SIGINT/SIGTERM delivered to a running client produces the shutdown flush.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same defect (scripts/market_scheduler.sh:431 plus the absence of add_signal_handler in websocket_client.run()); one reviewer rated critical, one high. High is the defensible rating: it destroys data daily and unrecoverably (no REST backfill exists), but the loss is confined to the tail of the intraday table, which EOD rows later supersede. Distinct fix from the stop-timing defect below, as the round-2 reviewer explicitly noted.
- **Verifier correction:** All factual claims verified. Two calibrations: (a) the per-session data actually destroyed at kill time is smaller than 'one partially-filled window per ticker for ~9,300 tickers' -- the periodic flush fires within 10s of the stop (logs show `Saved 199-208 bars ... 19:10 - 19:35` at 20:00:0x UTC each day), so what is lost is only the still-partial newest window(s), and those windows are structurally unfillable anyway because of finding 1; (b) the dominant impact is not the bar loss but the permanent disabling of ALL intraday failure telemetry and notification. Severity high rather than critical: the authoritative EOD path (stock_prices) is unaffected and does alert independently (Daily FAILED notifications did fire on 2026-08-31), so the operator is blinded to the intraday subsystem only, not to the pipeline as a whole.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections, both to details rather than to the finding itself. (a) Stop counts: the finding says 13/13 and the first verifier says 14/14. ~/.sawa/scheduler/scheduler.log is a single unrotated file starting 2026-08-25 00:45 ET and I count 8 `Stopping intraday (PID` and 8 `did not exit gracefully, sending SIGKILL` — 8 of 8. The 100% ratio, which is the load-bearing part, holds; the absolute counts in both prior reports do not match the current log. (b) I disagree with the first verifier's downward calibration of the bar loss. They wrote that what is destroyed is "only the still-partial newest window(s)" which are "structurally unfillable anyway." The database contradicts this: stock_prices_intraday on 2026-09-03 has complete 5-minute coverage every window up to 15:35:00 ET (5,419 tickers) and then nothing at all until the 16:00 close — the 15:40/15:45/15:50/15:55 windows are entirely absent. On the 15-minute delayed feed, roughly the 15:40 and 15:45 windows (about 11,000 bars across ~5,500 tickers) had already arrived and were sitting in bar_aggregator when SIGKILL landed; the per-ticker watermark can never mark them complete because no later bars arrive after the close, which is exactly the case flush_all=True was written for (see the docstring at sawa/api/websocket_client.py:657). So a material, recurring, quantified loss of the last ~25 minutes of every trading session — including the closing move — is directly attributable to this finding, and it degrades the MCP get_intraday_bars tool the server instructions designate as the preferred tool for "today's price action." I agree with the first verifier that the dominant harm is the permanent disabling of all intraday failure telemetry and notification, and I agree with high over critical, since stock_prices remains authoritative and independently alerted.
- **Verifier correction:** The mechanism, the SIGKILL-every-day evidence, and the silent-alerting impact are all confirmed. One quantitative correction: the marginal per-day data loss attributable to THIS finding is smaller than stated. Measured write latency today is a uniform 16.1 min after each window's end (window 09:30 first written 13:51:05 UTC; 62 windows, all 16.0-16.2), so at the 16:01 SIGKILL the newest event received is ~15:45 and only the 15:40 window (plus in-flight correction rewrites of the 15:20-15:35 windows) is lost to the kill. The fact that persisted data ends at the 15:35 window is mostly finding 1's stop time, not this one. The dominant, correctly-identified impact is the permanently blind failure path (2026-08-31).
- **Verifier correction:** Two factual corrections, neither affecting the verdict. (1) Count: ~/.sawa/scheduler/scheduler.log currently holds 8 "Stopping intraday" and 8 "did not exit gracefully" lines, not 13 — the earlier entries have aged out of the retained log; the ratio is still 100% (every stop ends in SIGKILL, never once gracefully). (2) Attribution of the data loss: measured write latency today is ~16 min after each window's end (19:30 UTC window first written 19:51:08; 19:35 UTC first written 19:56:05; 19:00 -> 19:21:06; 18:30 -> 18:51:06), so at the 16:01 ET SIGKILL the only data still in memory is the partial 15:40 ET window plus in-flight correction rewrites. The observed truncation of persisted data at the 15:35 window, and the missing closing auction, are dominated by the 15-min delayed feed combined with the 16:00 ET stop time — a separate defect, not this one. The fully attributable and dominant impact is the permanently blind failure path (record_stream_outcome + monitored_run never run), demonstrated by 2026-08-31. Additionally, the recommendation's "prefer SIGTERM" is insufficient on its own: my reproduction shows an uncaught SIGTERM terminates the process immediately, still skipping shutdown()/the final flush, so explicit loop.add_signal_handler (or signal.signal, which does override an inherited SIG_IGN) is required for either signal.

#### 9. The scheduler stops the stream at the first 'closed' tick although the Polygon feed is 15 minutes delayed, so the last four 5-minute windows of every session (15:40-16:00 ET) are structurally never captured and today's live candle is served with a pre-close price, truncated high/low and short volume. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** intraday-stream
- **Reviewers:** intraday-live, intraday-live-code
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:687-693 (stop_intraday on status != open) and :10 (*/15 cron); sawa/api/websocket_client.py:22,39-42 (DELAYED_WEBSOCKET_URL, 15-min delay acknowledged)`
- **Location:** `stock_prices_intraday / stock_prices_live (intraday UNION ALL arm, sqlschema/45)`
- **Evidence:** Maximum bar window ever persisted, per session, from the intraday logs: 2026-09-01 -> 19:35 UTC, 2026-09-02 -> 19:35 UTC (= 15:35:00 ET under EDT). A full regular session has 78 five-minute windows (09:30..15:55 ET); only 74 are ever written. Per-ticker census parsed from the complete 2026-09-02 logs (9,445 tickers): max windows = 74, with 2,769 tickers at exactly 74 and zero tickers above it. Cause: scheduler.log line 9420-9424 shows the market-status check flips to 'closed' at 16:00:02 ET and stop_intraday fires immediately, while the feed is the 15-minute-delayed endpoint (DELAYED_WEBSOCKET_URL, websocket_client.py:21) — the 15:40-15:59 ET minutes are still ~15 minutes from arrival. Finding #1 then removes the flush_all drain that would at least have saved partial windows. Measured error, comparing each ticker's last captured close against its official stock_prices close for 2026-09-02, restricted to the 2,769 tickers with the full 74 windows: mean absolute error 0.4182%, median 0.209%, p90 0.875%, p99 2.316%; 645 tickers (23%) off by >0.5%, 39 by >2%. Summed intraday volume averages 68.3% of the official daily volume (2,566 of 2,769 tickers below 90%). Spot check AAPL 2026-09-02: intraday-derived close 325.11 vs. official 324.96; intraday volume 24,946,797 vs. official 33,592,505.
- **Impact:** The stock_prices_live intraday arm builds today's candle as open=first bar / high=MAX / low=MIN / close=last bar / volume=SUM over exactly these truncated bars. From 16:00 ET until the daily job lands EOD rows and cleanup_today_intraday_data wipes them (~17:15 ET on a good day), every consumer of the view — get_stock_prices(use_live=true), get_latest_price, get_top_movers, screen_stocks, get_sector_performance, get_market_breadth, get_52week_extremes, get_daily_range_leaders — serves a 'today' OHLCV row whose close is the ~15:40 ET price rather than the official close, whose high/low omit the last 20 minutes, and whose volume omits the closing ramp and auction entirely. Nothing in the row or in any tool warning marks it as truncated. The exposure is not bounded to 75 minutes: daily.py:849-853 deliberately keeps intraday 'as fallback' whenever today's EOD coverage is below MIN_LATEST_COVERAGE, so a failed or partial daily leaves the truncated candle standing as the authoritative latest bar (the 2026-08-31/09-01 daily failures in logs/ show this state does occur).
- **Recommendation:** Keep the stream running past the closing bell rather than stopping on the market-status flip: gate stop_intraday on ET wall clock >= 16:20 (close + feed delay + a margin), or have the client stop itself once every ticker's window watermark has passed 15:55 ET. Combined with the SIGINT fix in finding #1 (so shutdown()'s flush_all drain actually persists the final partial windows), this recovers all 78 windows. As a defensive measure, either have the live view expose the covered window (e.g. max(timestamp)) so callers can see how stale the synthetic candle is, or have the MCP tools attach a warning when the newest intraday bar is more than ~20 minutes behind the ET clock.
- **Evidence:** scheduler.log: 'Stopping intraday (PID …)' at 16:00:01/16:00:02 ET on all 13 recorded sessions. Observed write latency for every window today: median created_at - window_end = 16.1 min (min 16:04, e.g. 09:30 window first written 13:51:05 UTC = 09:51 ET), i.e. a minute bar for event time T arrives at wall clock T+15 min. Hence at 16:00 wall clock the newest event received is ~15:45, the 15:40 window has just become complete and the 15:45/15:50/15:55 windows have not arrived at all. The intraday logs confirm the last saved window on 09-01 and 09-02 was 15:35 (UTC 19:35). docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:139-141 and MAINTENANCE.md:142-144 describe the design as 'started at the open and killed at the close', which is incompatible with a 15-minute delayed source.
- **Impact:** Independently of Finding 1, the last 15-20 minutes of every regular session (the highest-volume period, including the closing auction) are structurally excluded from stock_prices_intraday, so the live daily candle, get_intraday_bars, and any consumer of the 15:45-15:55 bars are wrong every day until EOD replaces them; on early-close days the same applies to 12:45-13:00.
- **Recommendation:** Keep the stream running until at least close + 20 min (e.g. stop only when Polygon reports closed AND ET time >= 16:20, or on early-close days >= 13:20); the client already discards out-of-session minutes so running longer is safe. Record the intended stop time in the summary notification. Add a scheduler test/fixture for the stop condition.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Finding 33 is the symptom in stock_prices_intraday/stock_prices_live, finding 62 is the cause in scripts/market_scheduler.sh:687-693; one fix (keep streaming ~20 minutes past the close). Independent of the SIGKILL defect — the missing bars are never even emitted, so a working graceful shutdown would not recover them.
- **Verifier correction:** One refinement worth carrying into any fix: the two findings are genuinely independent, not redundant. Even with finding 0 fixed, `shutdown()`'s flush_all drain would recover only the partial 15:40 ET window -- the 15:45/15:50/15:55 windows are 15 minutes from arrival on the delayed feed at 16:00 ET and would still never exist. Recovering all 78 windows requires the stop-time change (>= 16:20 ET) AND the SIGINT fix together. Normal-day exposure is ~95 min (16:00 -> 17:35 ET), not ~75 min.
- **Verifier correction:** Two refinements, neither affecting the verdict:

1. Normal-day exposure is ~95 minutes, not the ~75 the finding's impact section implies. The 2026-09-02 cleanup ran at 21:35:19 UTC = 17:35 ET, so the truncated candle stood from 16:00 to 17:35 ET. Confirmed again today: still standing at 17:01 ET.

2. The stop-time change alone is not sufficient, and the two findings are independent rather than redundant. On the 15-minute-delayed feed, at 16:00 ET only the 15:40 window is anywhere near flushable; 15:45/15:50/15:55 are still ~15 minutes from arrival. Fixing finding #1 (SIGINT/flush_all drain) alone would recover at most the partial 15:40 window. Recovering all 78 windows requires both the stop-time gate (>= 16:20 ET, or a per-ticker watermark past 15:55 ET) AND the SIGINT fix.

Supporting detail for the recommendation: the truncation is not a code filter. websocket_client.py:45 sets REGULAR_SESSION_CLOSE = time(16, 0) and line 486 admits every event with 09:30 <= t < 16:00 ET, so the missing bars are purely never-received, not filtered out. The fix belongs in scripts/market_scheduler.sh:688-691, where stop_intraday fires on any non-'open' market status.
- **Verifier correction:** Three corrections to the finding's details, none of which change the verdict or severity:

1. SESSION COUNT: the finding cites "13 recorded sessions". scheduler.log is trimmed to the last 5000 lines by initialize_scheduler (currently 5012 lines, starting 2026-08-25), so only 8 "Stopping intraday" events survive — 7 at 16:00:01 ET, 1 at 16:00:02 ET. Still 100% of what is recorded; the claim is directionally right but the retained sample is 8, not 13.

2. SIZE OF THE GAP: the finding's title says minutes 15:45-15:59 can never be persisted. The observed loss is larger — 20 minutes, four windows (15:40, 15:45, 15:50, 15:55). The 15:40 window is also absent because its minutes had not yet been delivered at the 16:00:02 kill, even though the shutdown flush ran successfully. Confirmed on both 09-02 and 09-03: last persisted window is 15:35 on each.

3. HIGH/LOW NUANCE: the impact statement implies the whole OHLCV candle is wrong. Today AAPL's served high (330.81) and low (324.11) matched Polygon exactly, because the session extremes fell before 15:40. High and low are wrong only when an extreme lands in the excluded tail; close and volume are wrong every single day. Today's served AAPL close was 327.51 vs an actual 328.21.

Additional supporting detail the finding did not state: websocket_client.py defines REALTIME_WEBSOCKET_URL (line 23) but never assigns it — the comment at line 219 ("fallback to real-time if access granted") describes behavior that does not exist, so there is no configuration under which the feed is not delayed.

#### 10. get_dividend_yield_leaders and the screen_stocks dividend filter compare financial_ratios.dividend_yield (stored as a fraction) against a threshold documented and defaulted as a percent, so the default call excludes all ~1,000 real 2%+ payers and returns only a handful of distressed outliers. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions, mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:274 (AND fr.dividend_yield >= %(min_yield)s), schema at mcp_server/server.py:1528-1532`
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:274 (fr.dividend_yield >= %(min_yield)s, default 2.0 documented as '%' at server.py:1531); mcp_server/tools/screener.py:82 with server.py:1144 ('Dividend yield % [min, max]')`
- **Evidence:** financial_ratios.dividend_yield is a fraction: AAPL 0.003600 (true TTM yield 0.33%), KO 0.030900 (2.36%), MO 0.058400 (6.10%), VZ 0.057400 (5.57%), XOM 0.029900 (2.51%) — verified against sum(cash_amount) over the last 365 days / latest close for 13 known payers. mcp_server/charts/renderers/ratios.py:154 confirms the convention by formatting it as `x * 100`. The tool's schema says "Minimum dividend yield % (default: 2.0)". Replaying the tool SQL with min_yield=2.0 returns 7 rows: CMCT 7.1059, STRZ 5.3024, GIPR 3.8442, MAGN 2.9525, WHLR 2.8393, MAGN 2.4709, LRHC 2.0274 — i.e. yields of 200%-710%. Over the latest financial_ratios snapshot per ticker, 3 tickers pass `>= 2.0` while 1,013 pass `>= 0.02` (a genuine 2% threshold).
- **Impact:** The dividend-yield leaderboard is unusable at its default and at any sane user input: asking for '2% or better' returns 7 distressed micro-caps and silently omits all 1,013 real 2%+ payers (KO, XOM, VZ, MO, O, PFE, ...). A caller who compensates by passing min_yield=0.02 gets correct rows but a return payload whose dividend_yield values still read as 0.02 rather than 2.0.
- **Recommendation:** Divide at the boundary: filter on `fr.dividend_yield >= %(min_yield)s / 100.0` and return `fr.dividend_yield * 100 AS dividend_yield`, keeping the documented percent contract. Add a regression test asserting that min_yield=2.0 returns KO/XOM-class tickers.
- **Evidence:** SQL: latest financial_ratios.dividend_yield per ticker -> AAPL 0.003600, KO 0.030900, MO 0.058400, T 0.049400, VZ 0.057400 (fractions; median 0, p90 0.0462, max 3.8442). Running get_dividend_yield_leaders() with defaults returned 7 rows/6 tickers: CMCT 7.1059, STRZ 5.3024, GIPR 3.8442, MAGN 2.9525, WHLR 2.8393, MAGN 2.4709, LRHC 2.0274 (i.e. '710%' yields; last_dividend NULL for all). get_dividend_yield_leaders(min_yield=0.02, limit=200) -> 200 rows. screen_stocks(filters={'dividend_yield':[3,None]}) -> 1 row (GIPR 3.8442); filters={'dividend_yield':[0.03,None]} -> 500 rows including MO 0.0584, VZ 0.0574, T 0.0494.
- **Impact:** Every call to get_dividend_yield_leaders with the documented default (2.0 = '2%') serves a list of data-error tickers as 'yield leaders' and omits KO/MO/VZ/T etc.; any screen_stocks dividend_yield filter expressed in percent (as the schema instructs) silently returns nothing or garbage.
- **Recommendation:** Either store/serve dividend_yield in percent consistently or multiply by 100 in these two SQL expressions (fr.dividend_yield * 100) and in get_52week_extremes/get_company_details output; add a unit assertion test using KO (~3%).
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same unit mismatch, same one-line fix; round 2 additionally traced it into screener.py:82/server.py:1144. The tool's headline output is not merely imprecise, it is inverted — the correct answers are exactly the rows excluded.
- **Verifier correction:** Same defect, one extra detail the finding missed: the LEFT JOIN financial_ratios has no latest-snapshot restriction, so a ticker with several snapshots is returned once per snapshot — MAGN appears twice in the 7-row result. Fixing only the units would still leak duplicate rows.
- **Verifier correction:** Two refinements to the finding, both verified:

(a) The duplicate-snapshot defect the first verifier flagged is real and measurable. `LEFT JOIN financial_ratios fr ON c.ticker = fr.ticker` (corporate_actions.py:271) has no latest-snapshot restriction, unlike the sibling `latest_prices`/`latest_divs` CTEs and unlike mcp_server/tools/screener.py:163-171, which does use `DISTINCT ON (ticker) ... ORDER BY ticker, date DESC` on the same table. At the 0.02 threshold the join yields 2,072 rows for 1,079 distinct active tickers — roughly 2x inflation. It is visible even in the 7-row default output, where MAGN appears twice (2.952500 from the 2026-05-14 snapshot and 2.470900 from 2026-06-12). Fixing only the units would leave duplicate rows consuming the `limit` budget.

(b) The finding's claim that "a caller who compensates by passing min_yield=0.02 gets correct rows" is slightly optimistic. `get_dividend_yield_leaders(min_yield=0.02, limit=200)` returns 200 rows and still does not contain KO — partly legitimate (KO at 3.09% is not a top-200 yielder) but partly because stale duplicate snapshots occupy slots. The payload-unit half of the finding's impact statement is correct as written: returned `dividend_yield` values come back as 0.0309, not 3.09.

Recommendation stands: filter on `fr.dividend_yield >= %(min_yield)s / 100.0`, return `fr.dividend_yield * 100 AS dividend_yield`, and add a `latest_ratios` CTE with `DISTINCT ON (ticker) ... ORDER BY ticker, date DESC` mirroring screener.py:163-171.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed as stated. Two additions: (a) the same SQL at mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:271 does `LEFT JOIN financial_ratios fr ON c.ticker = fr.ticker` with no DISTINCT ON / date restriction, and financial_ratios holds 8171 rows over 4072 tickers, so any ticker with more than one snapshot is emitted once per snapshot — that is why the 6-ticker result has 7 rows (MAGN twice at 2.9525 and 2.4709). Fixing only the unit scaling leaves the duplicate-row defect. (b) The fraction convention also leaks unscaled through screen_stocks output (APH dividend_yield 0.004800) and get_52week_extremes(include_fundamentals=True), so a consumer reading the schema's '%' misreads those too. Severity lowered from critical to high: it is silent wrong output on one tool's default plus one screener filter's units, with no data loss or corruption.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed as stated, and the first verifier's duplicate-row addendum is confirmed and is worse than described. mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:271 does `LEFT JOIN financial_ratios fr ON c.ticker = fr.ticker` with no DISTINCT ON and no date restriction, while 3933 of 4072 tickers hold more than one snapshot. Two consequences beyond the unit bug: (a) one row is emitted per snapshot, which is why the 6-ticker result has 7 rows (MAGN at 2.9525 from 2026-05-14 and 2.4709 from 2026-06-12); (b) the query serves STALE snapshots as current — CMCT's headline 7.1059 is the 2026-02-13 row, whereas its latest 2026-06-12 value is 1.8638. Fixing only the *100 scaling would leave both the duplicate rows and the stale-snapshot selection in place, so the fix needs a DISTINCT ON (ticker) ... ORDER BY ticker, date DESC latest-snapshot CTE as well. The fraction convention also leaks unscaled through screen_stocks output and get_52week_extremes(include_fundamentals=True) (mcp_server/tools/screener.py:798,803) and get_company_details (mcp_server/tools/companies.py:108), so a consumer following the schema's '%' wording misreads those values too.

#### 11. The global execute_query wrapper caps every MCP response at MCP_MAX_ROWS=1000 with no truncation signal, so get_index_constituents returns 1,000 of 10,406 us_active tickers while reporting the full constituent_count, and get_intraday_bars silently drops the later tickers of a 13-20 ticker request. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/indices.py:543-550 (unbounded constituents query) with mcp_server/database.py execute_query wrapper 'SELECT * FROM (...) LIMIT MAX_ROWS' (MAX_ROWS default 1000, no env override in .env/run.sh)`
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:748-783 (WHERE rn <= limit ORDER BY ticker, timestamp; up to 20 tickers x limit 500) combined with mcp_server/database.py execute_query LIMIT 1000 wrapper`
- **Evidence:** In-process run: get_index_constituents('nasdaq_listed') -> constituent_count=4676, len(constituents)=1000; get_index_constituents('us_active') -> constituent_count=10406, len(constituents)=1000; sp500 -> 503/503. SQL: SELECT i.code, COUNT(ic.ticker) ... -> us_active 10406, nasdaq_listed 4676, russell1000 998.
- **Impact:** Callers iterating an index universe (e.g. to batch other tools) see a self-inconsistent payload and miss 79-90% of members with no warning.
- **Recommendation:** Page the constituents query (ORDER BY ticker with LIMIT/OFFSET or keyset) or raise MAX_ROWS for this tool; at minimum add a warning when len(constituents) < constituent_count.
- **Evidence:** Code path: execute_query wraps every query in 'SELECT * FROM (...) LIMIT 1000' (MAX_ROWS=1000, verified no override). A regular session has 78 five-minute bars (09:30-15:55); 13 tickers x 78 = 1014 > 1000, 20 tickers x 78 = 1560. Live run at 11:28 ET today: get_intraday_bars(tickers=[20 large caps], limit=500) returned 420 rows = 20 x 21 bars (not yet capped); intraday table today holds 116039 bars for 8795 tickers at bar_size 5. Because the outer ORDER BY is ticker, timestamp, truncation removes whole tickers from the end of the alphabet rather than trimming bars.
- **Impact:** Afternoon/EOD calls for a watchlist of 13-20 tickers silently omit the last tickers (e.g. WMT, XOM) or return partial sessions, contradicting the documented per-ticker limit.
- **Recommendation:** Bound tickers x limit to MAX_ROWS in the tool (raise ValueError or auto-lower limit with a warning), or stream per ticker; add a warning when len(result) == MAX_ROWS.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Two tools, one root cause (mcp_server/database.py:55/355) and one fix: signal or paginate truncation instead of silently clipping. Confirmed the cap and the absence of an env override. High: responses are internally self-contradictory (count says 10,406, payload has 1,000), which is worse than an explicit limit.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed as stated. Note the count comes from a separate GROUP BY query (indices.py:55-66) that is unaffected by the cap, which is exactly why the two fields disagree. Affects nasdaq_listed (79% of members dropped) and us_active (90% dropped); sp500, nasdaq100, dow30, russell1000 and mag7 are all under 1000 and return complete lists.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections, neither of which weakens the finding.

1) The cited location line numbers are wrong. mcp_server/tools/indices.py is only 200 lines; there is no line 543-550. The unbounded constituents query is at mcp_server/tools/indices.py:75-82, and get_index_constituents starts at line 39. The database wrapper citation is correct (mcp_server/database.py:369-372, MAX_ROWS at :55).

2) The truncation is MORE silent than the first verifier stated. They said database.py:408-409 "logs 'Database stream exceeded its SQL row cap' server-side only" — in fact that warning never fires here. The SQL wrapper already applies LIMIT 1000, so the stream yields exactly 1000 rows and terminates at normal EOF; the Python guard `if len(output) >= row_limit` is only evaluated while a row is in hand, and on the 1000th iteration len(output) is still 999. execute_query then returns a plain list with no truncation flag (database.py:420-424) and the server.py:2448-2450 handler passes the dict through unchanged. There is therefore no signal at all: none in the payload, none in the warnings array, and none in the logs.

Blast radius as stated: nasdaq_listed drops 3676 of 4676 members (79%) and us_active drops 9406 of 10406 (90%); sp500, nasdaq100, dow30, russell1000 and mag7 are all under 1000 and return complete lists.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed, and now worse than the finding's own evidence (that run was at 11:28 ET with only 21 bars per ticker and did not yet trip the cap). Truncation begins once tickers x bars exceeds 1000: at 63 bars/ticker it fits 15 tickers, and by the 16:00 close (78 bars) it fits only 12, so a 13-20 ticker watchlist loses whole tickers from the end of the alphabet for the entire afternoon.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed, with one correction that makes it slightly worse than both the finding and the first verifier state. Both credit database.py's row-cap `logger.warning("Database stream exceeded its SQL row cap")` as at least a server-side signal that the client never sees. It never fires at all: the SQL `LIMIT 1000` ends the stream at exactly 1000 rows, so the Python guard's `if len(output) >= row_limit` check is only evaluated when a 1001st row arrives, which never happens. An instrumented in-process run captured only "Executing query..." and "Query returned 1000 rows" from the mcp_server.database logger; row-cap warning fired: False. So the truncation is silent in the client response AND in the server log, leaving no signal anywhere. Also note the threshold is bar-count dependent rather than fixed: at the observed 74 bars/ticker the cap fits 13 full tickers, and at the 78-bar full session (09:30-15:55) it fits 12, so any 13-20 ticker watchlist loses whole tickers from the end of the alphabet through the afternoon. Files: /home/seed/code/sawa/mcp_server/tools/market_data.py (non-aggregate branch, `WHERE rn <= %(limit)s ORDER BY ticker, timestamp ASC`), /home/seed/code/sawa/mcp_server/database.py:55 and :369-372, /home/seed/code/sawa/mcp_server/server.py:2228-2242.

#### 12. financial_ratios.price, earnings_per_share and average_volume are frozen at snapshot time while stock_prices is continuously re-adjusted, so get_financial_ratios and get_stock_prices report prices for the same ticker on the same date that differ by up to ~800x, with nothing in either response naming the basis. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** split-basis
- **Reviewers:** fundamentals-tables, split-basis-inventory
- **Location:** `financial_ratios.price / earnings_per_share / average_volume (served by mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:190, :194)`
- **Location:** `financial_ratios.price / earnings_per_share / average_volume; served by mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:150-215 (get_financial_ratios)`
- **Evidence:** Joining financial_ratios to same-date stock_prices: 8,170 joinable rows, 264 off by >5%, 248 off by >1.8x across 163 tickers, and the ratios are exact split factors:
  ticker  date        fr.price   sp.close   fr.eps   fr.average_volume   30d adj avg volume   split
  CRWD    2026-06-12  682.80     170.70     -0.10    3,559,939           14,520,319           1:4 on 2026-07-02
  MNST    2026-06-12   92.83      46.415     2.08    5,801,842           11,778,120           1:2 on 2026-08-11
  DD      2026-06-12   48.26     144.78      0.24    3,830,097            1,283,138           3:1 on 2026-06-24
  KLAC    2026-02-13 1464.13     146.413    34.77    (eps 3.58 in the 2026-06-12 snapshot)     1:10 on 2026-06-12
Per-share and share-count columns are all stale by the split factor; market_cap and enterprise_value are absolute dollars and remain correct (KLAC 2026-02-13 market_cap 191.9B checks out), so the damage is confined to price / earnings_per_share / average_volume. grep -rn 'UPDATE financial_ratios|DELETE FROM financial_ratios' over *.py, *.sql and *.sh returns nothing — no repair path exists anywhere in the repo, and the split heal in sawa/split_adjust.py never references the table.
- **Impact:** get_financial_ratios serves a price series that disagrees with get_stock_prices for the same ticker and date by up to 100x (YHC ratio 0.0100), an EPS on a stale basis, and an average_volume on a stale share basis. get_company_details serves fr.market_cap as latest_market_cap (correct value, 83 days stale). The pure ratios (P/E, P/B, ROE, D/E) are internally consistent and unaffected.
- **Recommendation:** Either re-base price, earnings_per_share and average_volume by the cumulative split factor for splits with execution_date > financial_ratios.date whenever the split heal runs, or stop serving those three columns from financial_ratios and read price/volume from stock_prices at the snapshot date instead. Add a doctor check comparing fr.price against same-date stock_prices.close.
- **Evidence:** Joining financial_ratios to stock_prices on (ticker, date) and keeping pairs that differ by more than 10%: 257 rows across 168 tickers. Feeding each through the cumulative split factor for splits executed after that date, 254 of the 257 are explained exactly by split basis (|ln(ratio/factor)| < 0.05); only 1 is not.
  ticker  date        ratios_price  adjusted_close  ratio      split_factor
  YYAI    2026-02-13        1.0700        856.0000  0.001250   0.001250
  CDT     2026-02-13        1.0800        270.0000  0.004000   0.004000
  GDC     2026-06-12        0.1000         24.7500  0.004040   0.004000
  RCON    2026-06-12        0.5400        108.2600  0.004988   0.005000
The latest snapshot per ticker is stale for 94 tickers (88 off >=2x): 216 splits across 210 tickers have executed since the 2026-06-15 load, and the table's newest date is 2026-06-12. earnings_per_share and average_volume (a share count) drift with the same factor; the price_to_* ratios and dividend_yield do not, because numerator and denominator are same-date.
- **Impact:** Two sawa tools give a caller two prices for the same ticker on the same day that disagree by up to three orders of magnitude, with nothing in either response saying which basis it is on. Anything that divides across the two tables -- an EPS from financial_ratios over a close from stock_prices, or a share count against adjusted volume -- is wrong by the split factor for 168 tickers.
- **Recommendation:** Pick a basis and state it. Either re-base price/earnings_per_share/average_volume through SplitAdjuster.factor(ticker, date) in the split heal so the table matches stock_prices, or rename the served field (price_as_traded) and document the contract in docs/DATA_SOURCES.md, which today defines a basis only for stock_prices.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same defect and same missing repair path. Both reviewers rated medium; re-rated to high under the stated rubric — the `price` column is returned as a plain dated price field (confirmed at mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:180-193, no basis label), so two tools contradict each other by multiple split factors. The ratios themselves stay internally consistent, which is why the reviewers discounted it.
- **Verifier correction:** Real and correctly evidenced, but narrower than 'high' warrants: the blast radius is 163 tickers across a 30-date snapshot table of 8,171 rows (3.0% of joinable rows off by >1.8x), and only three columns — price, earnings_per_share, average_volume — are wrong. The derived ratios that are the tool's primary purpose (P/E, P/B, P/S, D/E, ROE, ROA, EV multiples) and market_cap/enterprise_value are all internally consistent and unaffected, and get_company_details reads only unaffected columns, so the finding's own impact paragraph already scopes it correctly. Medium, not high.
- **Verifier correction:** Blast radius is narrower than implied and worth stating: I grepped every consumer of these three columns and get_financial_ratios is the only one. mcp_server/tools/screener.py and companies.py read only fr.price_to_earnings and fr.dividend_yield, both basis-invariant, so the screener is unaffected. Line citation slip: market_data.py:190 is debt_to_equity, not price -- price is at :181; :194 (earnings_per_share as eps) and :200 (average_volume) are correct. Also note the affected set is essentially all reverse-split microcaps, where the discrepancy is loud (2-3 orders of magnitude) rather than subtle; the genuinely silent part is eps and average_volume, which drift by the split factor with no visible tell.

#### 13. stock_prices splices two different securities into one continuous series when a ticker is reassigned (≥71 tickers, e.g. FB, AKTS, GOLD, WOLF, DFNS), so every row-window indicator silently mixes the prior issuer's prices into the current one's. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** ticker-reuse
- **Reviewers:** prices, weekly-corporate-splits
- **Location:** `stock_prices (ticker DFNS, date < 2021-09-01)`
- **Location:** `stock_prices (ticker,date) series; e.g. FB, AKTS, GOLD, RNA, WW, WOLF, B, CALY`
- **Evidence:** The daily series is keyed on ticker alone, with no security-identity boundary. Detector (companies.list_date after existing price rows, >2x discontinuity at that boundary, no split explaining it) returns 71 tickers holding 25,071 prior-issuer rows.

Vivid case, ticker FB:
  SELECT date, round(close,2), volume FROM stock_prices WHERE ticker='FB' AND date IN ('2022-06-08','2025-06-26','2026-09-02');
   2022-06-08 | 195.65 | 22267154   <- Meta Platforms
   2025-06-26 |  39.91 |    36658   <- ProShares S&P 500 Dynamic Buffer ETF
   2026-09-02 |  45.03 |      331
companies.FB reads 'ProShares S&P 500 Dynamic Buffer ETF' (list_date 2025-06-24), yet 269 Meta rows remain in the same series.

Confirmed downstream corruption, AKTS (Akoustis Technologies, delisted at $0.0372 -> Aktis Oncology, relisted 2026-01-09 at $22.40):
  WITH last200 AS (SELECT date, close FROM stock_prices WHERE ticker='AKTS' ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT 200)
  SELECT count(*), min(date), round(avg(close),4), count(*) FILTER (WHERE close<1) FROM last200;
   200 | 2024-10-25 | 17.4870 | 37
  SELECT sma_200 FROM technical_indicators WHERE ticker='AKTS' AND date='2026-09-02';  -> 17.4870
The stored SMA-200 is exactly the mean of the last 200 rows, 37 of which are the delisted sub-penny company. True Aktis-only average since relisting: 21.4399 (off by ~19%).

GOLD (Barrick through 2025-05-08 -> Gold.com from 2025-12-02):
  SELECT low_52w FROM mv_52week_extremes WHERE ticker='GOLD' AND date='2026-09-02';  -> 16.9200
  SELECT round(min(low),2) FROM stock_prices WHERE ticker='GOLD' AND date > DATE '2026-09-02' - INTERVAL '52 weeks';  -> 28.83
16.92 is a Barrick price; it is served as Gold.com's 52-week low.

29 reassigned tickers have fewer than 252 post-boundary rows, and 18 of those carry a non-NULL sma_200 at 2026-09-02 that necessarily spans the identity boundary.
- **Impact:** Every row-window derived value (SMA-50/200, EMA, RSI, 52-week high/low, YTD and multi-month returns, volume averages, relative strength) is silently computed across two unrelated securities for at least 71 tickers, and 18 tickers are serving a contaminated sma_200 right now. Screeners and momentum tools rank these tickers on fabricated history; the AKTS SMA-200 is 19% wrong and the GOLD 52-week low is 41% below the true value. The count is a lower bound: the same defect exists for B (Barnes Group -> Barrick), CALY, SPYM, DXF and ARIS, which the list_date detector misses because the provider left list_date at the prior issuer's value.
- **Recommendation:** Give the price series a security-identity boundary rather than trusting the ticker. Cheapest correct fix: record a per-ticker `identity_start_date` (seeded from a detector combining a >N-day gap, a >2x unexplained discontinuity, and a composite_figi/cik change in companies) and have every window computation, plus mv_52week_extremes, clamp its lookback to it. Backfill the 71 detected tickers by moving pre-boundary rows into an archive table keyed by the prior identity, then recompute technical_indicators and refresh mv_52week_extremes for them. Add a doctor check that fails when a ticker's series contains an unexplained >2x step with no split record.
- **Evidence:** `SELECT min(date), max(date), count(*), count(*) FILTER (WHERE date<'2022-01-01'), count(*) FILTER (WHERE date BETWEEN '2022-01-01' AND '2026-02-08') FROM stock_prices WHERE ticker='DFNS'` -> 2021-02-18, 2026-09-02, 276, 133, 0. Closes: 2021-08-26 1606.25, next stored row 2026-02-09 298.75 (step 5.377), 2026-07-17 5.25 -> 2026-07-20 4.26 (1:125 applied). companies: 'T3 Defense Inc. Common Stock', list_date 2014-11-25. stock_splits DFNS: only 1:125 @2026-07-20. Provider did not serve 2021-08-27..2026-02-08 in the 09-02 refresh (horizon log earliest 2021-09-03 across tickers).
- **Impact:** 2021 rows describe a SPAC that used the symbol before, presented at $1,200-1,600 as T3 Defense history; 52-week/YTD/long-window tools over DFNS include them. The run stats will carry a permanent 'pre-horizon rows already sit on a different basis' entry for DFNS.
- **Recommendation:** Quarantine DFNS rows before 2026-02-09 (or mark ticker-reuse gaps) and add a gap-length check to the horizon scan so a >1-year hole is reported as ticker reuse rather than a basis mismatch.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Finding 46 is the DFNS instance of the general defect in 0 (133 rows of a prior SPAC scaled x125 by T3 Defense's reverse split, presented as $1,200-1,600 history) plus the observation that the new pre-horizon guard will re-report it forever. Severity high, not low: the contamination is unbounded and lands on the headline row-window values (52-week, YTD, SMA/EMA-200).
- **Verifier correction:** My independent detector (companies.list_date strictly inside an existing price series, >2x close discontinuity at the boundary, no stock_splits row within +/-10 days) returns 82 tickers holding 27,728 prior-issuer rows, not 71/25,071. Of those, 33 have fewer than 252 post-boundary rows and 19 (not 18) carry a non-NULL sma_200 at 2026-09-02 that necessarily spans the identity boundary. One evidence value is misquoted: stock_prices FB 2022-06-08 close is 196.64, not 195.65. Everything else, including the AKTS SMA-200 and GOLD 52-week low figures, matches exactly.
- **Verifier correction:** Detector counts: I get 79 tickers / 26,353 prior-issuer rows (finding: 71/25,071; verifier 1: 82/27,728). The exact count is detector-tuning-sensitive; the order of magnitude is stable.

ONE HEADLINE STATISTIC IS OVERSTATED. The claim "29 reassigned tickers have fewer than 252 post-boundary rows, and 18 of those carry a non-NULL sma_200 that necessarily spans the identity boundary" uses the wrong threshold: a 200-period SMA needs fewer than 200 post-boundary rows to be contaminated, not fewer than 252. Under the finding's own criterion I do reproduce 18 (and 32, not 29, with <252 post rows). But counting rows actually inside the trailing 200-row window at 2026-09-02, only 6 tickers are genuinely serving a contaminated sma_200 today:
  MCVT  88 prior rows in window  sma_200 16.5514 vs post-only 27.3169  (-39.4%)
  JAN   85                       17.0553 vs 27.4474                    (-37.9%)
  RNA   70                       33.7465 vs 13.0770                    (+158.1%)
  LIFE  50                       14.5854 vs 18.8601                    (-22.7%)
  AKTS  37                       17.4870 vs 21.4399                    (-18.4%)
  CONX   3                       11.3458 vs 11.3739                     (-0.2%)
RNA is a far more vivid case than the AKTS one the finding leads with: 158% wrong, mixing ~$72.80 prior-issuer prices into a $13 security. WOLF, VIA and CHAI were in the 18 but have zero prior-issuer rows in their current 200-row window, so their sma_200 is not contaminated right now.

THE 52-WEEK CONTAMINATION IS BROADER AND WORSE THAN STATED. 32 of the 79 have prior-issuer rows inside the trailing 252-row window, and mv_52week_extremes is serving grossly wrong lows at 2026-09-02 (served vs true post-boundary):
  AKTS 0.033 vs 14.72   CATG 0.010 vs 9.46   ACII 10.01 vs 24.35
  ESSC 6.72  vs 20.63   JAN  0.500 vs 22.76  CCOM 1.49  vs 23.34
  CLIM 9.77  vs 23.30   EYES 1.14  vs 23.90  FLXI 0.185 vs 47.93
  LIFE 1.395 vs 9.45    ALPS 0.14  vs 0.23
CCXI and CHAI additionally serve contaminated 52-week HIGHS (52.00 vs true 19.69; 25.122 vs 15.06). So the correct impact statement is "6 tickers serving a contaminated sma_200 and 32 serving contaminated 52-week extremes", not "18 serving a contaminated sma_200".

Minor: stock_prices FB 2022-06-08 close is 196.64, not 195.65 as quoted.
- **Verifier correction:** Three corrections.

(1) The stated impact is understated in the place that matters and overstated elsewhere. 52-week and YTD tools are NOT affected: only 143 rows exist since 2026-02-09, so those windows never reach 2021. What IS affected is technical_indicators, which is computed over a rolling row count and walks straight across the 1628-day gap: on 2026-09-02 DFNS has close 9.26 and sma_200 413.2568 (44x), and on 2026-02-09 close 298.75 with sma_50 1235.36. (Arithmetic checks out: ~57 rows at ~1250 plus ~143 rows at ~80 averages to ~413.) That is silently wrong data served by get_technical_indicators, get_latest_technical_indicators, screen_technical_indicators, detect_crossovers and get_multi_timeframe_alignment, plus long-range get_stock_prices.

(2) 'on every run' is wrong. weekly.py:595 and daily.py:487 pass only ca_stats['split_tickers'] (newly persisted splits), so DFNS does not enter the weekly/daily pipeline again. It reappears only in a full `sawa adjust-splits` with the default since = today-365 (i.e. until ~2027-07-20) or if a new DFNS split is recorded. The stats-noise half of the finding is therefore minor.

(3) It is a small class, not a one-off. Among the 229 tickers with a split in the last 365 days, five have a >365-day internal gap: DFNS (1628d), ICON (1074d), CRWG (1021d), BBBY (850d), WZRD (413d). WZRD is contaminated the same way -- close 11.766 vs sma_200 190.9277 on 2026-09-02. So the recommendation (a gap-length check that classifies a multi-year hole as ticker reuse rather than a basis mismatch, plus quarantining the pre-gap rows and recomputing TA) is right, and should also drive a doctor check, since none exists today.

#### 14. The economy dashboard chart reads the newest-first result set backwards, so it reports the oldest row as 'current', draws every sparkline right-to-left and inverts every trend arrow.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/renderers/economy.py:348,379,436 (latest = data[-1]); mcp_server/tools/economy.py:280-298`
- **Evidence:** v_economy_dashboard ends in ORDER BY d.date DESC (pg_get_viewdef) and get_economy_dashboard adds no ORDER BY of its own, so rows arrive newest-first. Verified: data[0].date = 2026-09-02, data[-1].date = 2026-08-20. All three renderers take latest = data[-1] and current = series[-1]. Rendered (r1.py/r5.py, colors off):
  JSON:  2026-09-02 10Y None | 2026-08-31 10Y 4.7500 | ... | 2026-08-20 10Y 4.6900
  NORMAL chart: "ECONOMY DASHBOARD | as of 2026-08-20" / "10Y Treasury  4.69% █▆▂▂▁▄▇▄ ▼ -1.3%"
  COMPACT chart: "ECONOMY | 10Y: 4.69% | Unemp: 4.1%"
  DETAILED chart: "As of 2026-08-20" / "10Y Treasury 4.69% ... ▼ -1.3%"
The newest yield in the same payload is 4.75% (2026-08-31); over the window the 10Y rose from 4.69 to 4.75 (+1.3%), and the chart says it fell 1.3%.
- **Impact:** get_economy_dashboard is the tool an assistant reaches for to state the current macro backdrop. Its chart states a stale "as of" date, a stale level for every indicator, and the wrong sign for every trend, while the JSON beside it holds the correct newest-first rows. Any narrative built from the chart ("yields are falling") is the opposite of the data.
- **Recommendation:** Either add ORDER BY date ASC to get_economy_dashboard's outer query (the view's own DESC is what the renderer trips on), or make the renderers order-independent: sort by the date key on entry to render_economy_dashboard instead of trusting positional order. A regression test that renders two rows with known dates and asserts the "as of" line would have caught this.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. The chart contradicts the JSON in the same response, and the tool exists precisely to state the current macro backdrop.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed as written, with a larger worst case than stated: at limit=100 (the documented max) the dashboard reports 'as of 2026-04-20' instead of 2026-09-03, 10Y 4.26% instead of 4.75%, and ▼ -10.3% instead of ▲ +11.5%. Root cause is the missing outer ORDER BY in mcp_server/tools/economy.py:283-300 (the view's own ORDER BY d.date DESC leaks through), not the renderers alone.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed as written. Two immaterial deviations from the finding's quoted evidence: (a) the specific dates/values in the evidence block (data[0]=2026-09-02, data[-1]=2026-08-20, 4.69%) came from a run one session earlier and shift by one trading day today — today the limit=10 window is 2026-09-03..2026-08-21 and the chart says "as of 2026-08-21 / 10Y 4.74%" against a true newest of 4.75% (2026-08-31); (b) at limit=10 the arrow renders ▶ rather than ▼ because the magnitude falls under the flat threshold, though the sign is still inverted (-0.2% vs +0.2%). The arrow inversion is unambiguous at the documented max limit=100, where the chart prints "as of 2026-04-20 / 10Y Treasury 4.26% ▼ -10.3% / Unemp: 4.3%" versus the correct ASC render "as of 2026-09-03 / 10Y Treasury 4.75% ▲ +11.5% / Unemployment 4.1%" — a 4.5-month-stale date, a 49bp level error, and a flipped sign on every indicator. Root cause is the missing outer ORDER BY at mcp_server/tools/economy.py:268-300 letting the view's own ORDER BY d.date DESC leak through, combined with the positional data[-1]/series[-1] assumption at mcp_server/charts/renderers/economy.py:348, 379, 436.

#### 15. The fundamentals chart labels annual statements 'Quarterly' and heads their columns Q4'25, because annual rows carry a non-NULL fiscal_quarter, presenting full-year revenue as one quarter's.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/renderers/fundamentals.py:109 and :209 (timeframe = "Quarterly" if latest.get("fiscal_quarter")), :227-233 and :320-326 (period headers)`
- **Evidence:** income_statements annual rows have fiscal_quarter populated: AAPL 2025-09-27 timeframe=annual, fiscal_year=2025, fiscal_quarter=4 (query over income_statements; 0 of 19,225 annual rows have a NULL fiscal_quarter). Rendered get_fundamentals('AAPL', timeframe='annual', limit=4) (r3.py):
  chart NORMAL header: "AAPL Fundamentals | Quarterly | Last 4 periods"
  chart DETAILED: "Metric                      Q4'25        Q4'24        Q4'23        Q4'22" / "Revenue                   $416.2B      $391.0B      $383.3B      $394.3B"
The JSON rows are annual: AAPL FY2025 revenue 416,161,000,000. AAPL's actual Q4 FY2025 revenue is 102.5B, so "Q4'25 $416.2B" is off by 4x under a label that names a quarter.
- **Impact:** Any user or assistant reading the chart for an annual request gets quarterly-labelled columns and a "Quarterly" banner over annual figures - a 4x error in the mental model of every income-statement and cash-flow line, with no warning anywhere in the response.
- **Recommendation:** Pass the requested timeframe into render_fundamentals_chart instead of inferring it, or infer from the row's own `timeframe` column (it is selected by the tool query for balance_sheets/cash_flows and can be added to income_statements); render "FY25" headers whenever the statement is annual.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. A 4x error in the reader's mental model of every income-statement and cash-flow line, with nothing in the chart to flag it.
- **Verifier correction:** Finding is accurate as written; no correction needed. Two minor additions from this pass: (1) the cash-flow detailed table also emits the same Q4'25..Q4'22 headers (confirmed in rendered output, not just by code reading at :320-326), and cash_flows rows likewise lack a `timeframe` key, so the recommended fix must cover both tables; (2) `trailing_twelve_months` rows also carry fiscal_quarter 1-4, but that timeframe is unreachable via get_fundamentals (validation at mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py:29-30 and the schema enum at mcp_server/server.py:563 allow only quarterly/annual), so the blast radius is annual requests only.

#### 16. load_prices writes every cached/staged symbol with no companies filter, so a ticker with no companies row hits stock_prices_ticker_fkey mid-load and the strict insert raises after earlier files were committed — the current cache reproduces it deterministically on the 31st file, and the fresh path discards the whole S3 download.

- **Theme:** coldstart-loader
- **Reviewers:** coldstart-loaders
- **Location:** `sawa/database/load.py:620-641 (load_prices, no valid_tickers) and :391-399 (_insert_rows strict raise); sawa/coldstart.py:1490-1572 (TemporaryDirectory staging, merge after load)`
- **Evidence:** Live constraint: `stock_prices_ticker_fkey FOREIGN KEY (ticker) REFERENCES companies(ticker) ON DELETE CASCADE`. Cache check: of the 525 files in data/prices, 6 tickers have no companies row (`{AMJB,CTRA,DTB,EP,HOLX,PFH}`); sorted(glob) order puts 30 files before AMJB.csv, each committed by _insert_rows(strict=True) (load.py:410-411), then AMJB's first INSERT raises psycopg ForeignKeyViolation -> `conn.rollback(); raise RuntimeError('Atomic insert into stock_prices failed')` -> propagates out of run_coldstart (coldstart.py:1973-1977 re-raises). Fresh path: download_prices receives `set(symbols)` (coldstart.py:1494-1502) while download_overviews treats a None overview as ordinary and non-fatal (coldstart.py:491-500, 'ordinary for many ETFs and delisted symbols'), so such symbols have S3 bars but no companies row; load_prices is called at :1546 inside `with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory(...)` and _merge_price_artifacts at :1567 is only reached on success, so the exception deletes the staged CSVs. docs/OPERATIONS.md prescribes `sawa coldstart --load-only` as the re-base remedy.
- **Impact:** A routine coldstart (any symbol with no ticker-details record) crashes at the price step after hours of S3 downloading, loses the download, and leaves a partially ingested price table; the documented --load-only remedy cannot complete against the present cache.
- **Recommendation:** Pass the companies ticker set into load_prices (valid_tickers) and treat non-company tickers as skipped-with-degraded-reason rather than strict failures, or pre-check FK eligibility in the existing pre-validation pass before the first write; move _merge_price_artifacts into a try/finally so a load failure never discards the staged download. Add a test with one cache file whose ticker is absent from companies.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. High because it loses hours of downloaded data, leaves a partially ingested price table, and the documented --load-only remedy fails the same way.
- **Verifier correction:** Accurate. Precise numbers: 6/525 cached tickers are orphans (AMJB, CTRA, DTB, EP, HOLX, PFH), AMJB.csv is the 31st file processed, and 30 per-file commits precede the raise.
- **Verifier correction:** Correct the reproduction details. The finding's mechanism is right, but the trigger file is not AMJB. In --load-only, sawa/coldstart.py:1210-1222 loads data/overviews/overviews_missing.csv into companies (upsert, strict) BEFORE load_prices at :1258, and that file contains AMJB, DTB, EP and PFH, so those four stop being orphans at load time. Of the 6 cache tickers with no companies row (AMJB, CTRA, DTB, EP, HOLX, PFH), only CTRA and HOLX are in neither overviews.csv nor overviews_missing.csv. CTRA.csv is at 0-based index 129 of sorted(glob) (the 130th of 525 files), so `sawa coldstart --load-only` against the present cache commits 129 files and then raises RuntimeError("Atomic insert into stock_prices failed") on CTRA; HOLX.csv (index 237) is the next one. Also worth adding to the evidence: the FK is non-deferrable (condeferrable=f), so it is caught by the per-row SAVEPOINT handler exactly as described; load_prices' pre-validation pass passes cleanly on all 525 files (verified by re-running it), so it always reaches the write loop; and get_tickers_from_csv_files (coldstart.py:531-597) scans only fundamentals and ratios, never data/prices, so the "[4/9] Validating company records" warning cannot surface price-cache orphans at all. In the fresh path, require_complete_persistence(expected_rows=int(overview_count)) at :1471-1474 is not a safety net because DownloadCount's int() is len(overviews) written, not len(symbols) requested.

#### 17. Seven tickers (MDXH, SQFT, ASPS, TPST, BTCS, SNES, OIO) have a stock_splits row whose ratio was back-applied to stock_prices even though no split occurred, leaving 3,759 daily bars and their indicators fabricated at 2x-10x the traded level.

- **Theme:** corporate-actions
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions
- **Location:** `stock_prices / stock_splits for MDXH 2023-11-14, SQFT 2022-01-24, ASPS 2025-04-03, TPST 2026-01-30, BTCS 2023-05-11, SNES 2022-09-01, OIO 2026-04-24`
- **Evidence:** Classifying all 1,851 splits by prev_close/post_close: 1,632 consistent (ratio ~1), 1 unadjusted (WTO, known), 7 where the stored series moves the exact OPPOSITE way from the recorded split. Cross-checked MDXH against Polygon as-traded (GET v2/aggs/ticker/MDXH/range/1/day/2023-11-10/2023-11-16?adjusted=false): actual closes 2.82 / 3.20 / 3.09 / 3.12 / 3.10 with volumes 51,060 / 219,311 / 39,289 — a completely continuous series with no split. stock_prices stores 2023-11-10 close 28.20 vol 5,106 and 2023-11-13 close 32.00 vol 21,931 against 2023-11-14 close 3.09 vol 39,289 — exactly 10x on price and exactly 1/10 on volume before the date, which is the arithmetic of the recorded `split_from=10, split_to=1` row applied to a non-event. Same signature at 2x for the five `split_from=1, split_to=2` cases (SQFT 18.95 -> 35.30 and flat thereafter at the new level with unchanged ~5k volume; ASPS 2.82 -> 6.58; TPST 1.405 -> 2.72; BTCS 0.74 -> 1.38; SNES 672 -> 1200). Affected: 3,759 stock_prices rows and 3,759 technical_indicators rows before those dates.
- **Impact:** Multi-year price history for these seven active tickers is fabricated at 2x-10x the traded level, with volume inversely wrong. Every derived indicator over those bars (SMA/EMA/RSI/ATR, 52-week extremes, volume profile, weekly/monthly candles, any return or drawdown computation spanning the date) is wrong, and today's split-basis repair would not catch it because the price series is internally consistent with the (bad) split row.
- **Recommendation:** Validate each split against the as-traded series before trusting it: for every stock_splits row, compare the as-traded close ratio across execution_date to split_to/split_from and quarantine or drop rows where the market shows no matching move. Add this as a doctor check (it is cheap: one lateral lookup per split) — the existing technical_indicators.post_split_recompute check at sawa/doctor.py:406-484 only verifies TA was recomputed, not that the split itself is real.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique, and the mirror image of the small-ratio group below (splits applied that never happened vs. splits recorded but never applied) — different fix, so kept separate.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections to the framing. (1) Census numbers: 2,171 splits, not 1,851; a plain 25% tolerance yields 1,819 continuous and 216 discontinuous, not 1,632/1/7, and the 1/ratio bucket has nine members (add GOAI 2025-02-11 and XRX 2026-02-09) — the finding's seven are a subset picked with a tighter tolerance. (2) Direction: only MDXH and OIO (both reverse-split rows) have pre-date bars ~2.4-10x too HIGH; the five 1->2 rows (SQFT, ASPS, TPST, BTCS, SNES) leave pre-date bars ~2x too LOW relative to the post-date basis, so 'fabricated at 2x-10x the traded level' holds only for the reverse cases. Only MDXH was cross-checked against as-traded data; for the five 1->2 cases the same signature admits a second reading (a real 1-for-2 reverse split recorded with split_from/split_to inverted and never adjusted for) — note each of those tickers has a genuine reverse split within weeks or months of the suspect row (ASPS 8->1 on 2025-05-28, SNES 20->1 on 2022-11-16, SQFT 10->1 on 2025-05-19, TPST 13->1 on 2025-04-09, BTCS 10->1 on 2021-08-17). Under either reading the basis break and the row counts are identical, so the recommendation (validate every split against the as-traded series and make it a doctor check) stands unchanged. Attribution is provider-side, not a sawa bug: MDXH's whole history was written in one coldstart fetch on 2026-02-17 19:11:33 and sawa requests adjusted=true (sawa/api/client.py:482), so Polygon's own adjusted aggregates carry the bad factor — the mirror image of the known WTO case.
- **Verifier correction:** Three corrections.

(1) OIO 2026-04-24 is a FALSE POSITIVE and should be dropped from the finding. Stored bars: 2026-04-23 close 7.05, then 2026-04-24 open 7.04, high 7.50, low 2.92, close 2.93, volume 243,691. The split-day OPEN continues the prior close exactly (ratio 0.9986) — there is no basis break at the boundary. The entire discontinuity happens intraday on 2026-04-24, a real -58% session, and the price keeps falling afterward (04-27 close 1.90, 04-28 1.71, current 1.71). The census flagged OIO only because prev_close/post_close compares the prior close to the split-day CLOSE across that crash, giving 2.406 — which is not even close to the claimed factor of 3. A spurious x3 applied to pre-04-24 bars would have left the split-day open at ~1/3 of the prior close (~2.35); a real-but-unadjusted 1-for-3 reverse would have left it at ~3x (~21). Neither is observed, so OIO's 32 pre-date bars are not off by 2.4x. Corrected scope: SIX tickers (MDXH, SQFT, ASPS, TPST, BTCS, SNES), 3,727 stock_prices rows and 3,727 technical_indicators rows, not seven / 3,759.

(2) Census numbers in the evidence are wrong: 2,171 splits, not 1,851; a 25% tolerance gives 1,819 continuous and 216 discontinuous, not 1,632/1/7. The "backwards factor" bucket has 8 members at a 0.80-1.25 band (adds XRX 2026-02-09, 2:3, open/prev 1.4197) with GOAI 2025-02-11 (4:1, open/prev 0.3308) just outside — so the seven are a tolerance-dependent subset, and at least two more rows show the same signature.

(3) Direction and cause. Only MDXH has pre-date bars too HIGH (10x); the five 1:2 rows leave pre-date bars ~2x too LOW relative to the post-date basis, so "fabricated at 2x-10x the traded level" is accurate only for MDXH. Only MDXH was pinned against as-traded data; for the five 1:2 cases the identical stored series is equally consistent with a real 1-for-2 reverse split recorded with split_from/split_to inverted and never adjusted for (each of those tickers has a genuine reverse split nearby: ASPS 8:1 on 2025-05-28, SNES 20:1 on 2022-11-16, SQFT 10:1 on 2025-05-19, TPST 13:1 on 2025-04-09, BTCS 10:1 on 2021-08-17). Attribution is provider-side, not a sawa bug: sawa requests adjusted=true (sawa/daily.py:206), the older stock_prices_backup snapshot carries the identical values, and none of the tickers is in stock_prices_unadjustable_archive.

#### 18. Seven tickers (AEG, AROW, IBM, LH, MMM, CZFS, LEN) still hold as-traded history before a recorded spin-off/stock-dividend that Polygon does apply in its adjusted feed, because every repair path filters small ratios out (f >= 1.2 OR f <= 0.8333).

- **Theme:** corporate-actions
- **Reviewers:** weekly-corporate-splits
- **Location:** `stock_prices (AEG <2021-08-26, AROW <2021-09-16, IBM <2021-11-04, LH <2023-07-03, MMM <2024-04-01, CZFS <2024-06-14, LEN <2025-01-21); docs/OPERATIONS.md stale-basis query filter`
- **Evidence:** Polygon GET /v2/aggs/ticker/IBM/range/1/day/2021-11-01/2021-11-05?adjusted=true -> 2021-11-03 c=121.5392, 2021-11-04 c=120.85. Stored: `SELECT date, close, created_at::date FROM stock_prices WHERE ticker='IBM' AND date BETWEEN '2021-11-01' AND '2021-11-05'` -> 2021-11-03 close 127.13000000 (= 121.5392 x 1.046, the recorded 1046:1000 factor), 2021-11-04 120.85 (matches), created_at 2026-02-12 (coldstart flat files). Scan over all 35 small-ratio splits with the code's own criterion: 11 show the raw step; 7 of them belong to tickers with no split in the trailing 365 days, so no refresh ever touched them: AEG 53:52 (71 stale rows), AROW 103:100 (146), IBM 1046:1000 (184), LH 1164:1000 (599), MMM 1196:1000 (786), CZFS 101:100 (576), LEN 1033:1000 (988) -- 3,350 rows. Their steps match the raw factor (MMM 0.8864 vs 1/1.196=0.8361; LH 0.8657 vs 0.8591; LEN 0.9407 vs 0.9681). The docs query and today's repair selection exclude f in (0.8333, 1.2).
- **Impact:** Pre-split history is 1-16% off the provider basis for large caps (MMM 16.4%, LH 14.1%, IBM 4.4%, LEN 3.2%), so returns, 52-week extremes and long SMAs/EMAs spanning those dates are wrong. The other four small-ratio tickers with the raw step (DVLT, FER, LILA, LILAK) are refreshed daily and therefore equal the provider basis; not counted here.
- **Recommendation:** Run `sawa adjust-splits --ticker X` for the seven (AEG's rows before 2021-08-26 are pre-horizon; the in-tail detector fires correctly for it, step 0.9889 vs raw 0.9811) and recompute TA. Drop the ratio filter from the docs/quarantine queries in favour of a tolerance test (|ln(step) - ln(1/f)| < |ln(step)| and |ln(step)-ln(1/f)| < 0.1), and add a one-off scan of every split older than the trailing year that never had a refresh.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Rated high on magnitude and profile: MMM is 16.4% off basis, LH 14.1%, IBM 4.4% across large-cap history that long SMAs/EMAs, 52-week extremes and multi-year returns all span. Related to but separate from the coldstart fractional-skip finding, which is a different code path and a different fix.
- **Verifier correction:** The confident core is IBM/LH/MMM/LEN (2,557 rows, 3.3-19.6% error) plus CZFS/AROW. AEG is weaker evidence: it has four recorded small-ratio splits and only the two 2021 ones show the raw step, so its 71 rows are not cleanly diagnosed by the same argument (and at ~$4.70 the absolute error is negligible).
- **Verifier correction:** Corrected scope: 4 tickers, not 7. Confirmed stale against Polygon's adjusted feed: IBM (184 rows before 2021-11-04, stored = 1.046x provider), LH (599 before 2023-07-03, 1.164x), MMM (786 before 2024-04-01, 1.196x), LEN (988 before 2025-01-21, 1.033x) = 2,557 rows. All four are 1000-denominator spin-off ratios sitting in the 2026-02-12 coldstart flat-file batch whose only split is older than the 365-day auto-detect window at sawa/split_adjust.py:380.

Removed: AROW (146 rows) and AEG (71 rows) are refuted — stored equals Polygon's adjusted close exactly on 2021-09-15, so Polygon does not apply their 103:100 / 53:52 stock-dividend factors and the stored history is already on the provider basis. CZFS (576 rows) is unestablished — its whole series came from the 2026-02-17 Polygon-fetch batch (same batch as AROW, proven correct), not coldstart, and a -0.895% day against a 1% factor is inside its normal daily range (-6.81% on 2024-06-10).
