Corrected mechanism: Polygon applies spin-off ratios recorded as 1000:xxxx (IBM, LH, MMM, LEN, and the correct control HON 1061:1000) but not the small integer stock-dividend ratios (AEG 52:53, AROW 100:103, GGB 20:21, ITUB 10:11, QGEN 36:35, UL 9:8 — all six match stored exactly).

Corrected recommendation: run `sawa adjust-splits --ticker` for IBM, LH, MMM, LEN only, and recompute TA. Do NOT simply drop the (f >= 1.2 OR f <= 0.8333) band in docs/OPERATIONS.md:286 for a pure log-tolerance test — that criterion already false-positives on genuine market moves (DVLT residual 0.159 vs its 1.017 factor; LILA/LILAK 0.19-0.20) and would re-base AEG/AROW/GGB/ITUB off the provider basis. Any replacement detector has to compare stored closes against the provider's own adjusted series rather than against the recorded ratio, plus a one-off scan of splits older than the trailing year that never had a refresh.

#### 19. stock_splits still holds ratios rounded to integers by the pre-guard loader (SF 2:1 for a true 1.5:1, SMX 5:1 and 2:1 for 4.88:1 and 2.285:1, AUUD 8:1 for 7.7:1, AKAN 4:1 for 4.5:1, FER 104:105), and get_split_adjuster, split_adjust and the restore script now compute re-bases from them.

- **Theme:** corporate-actions
- **Reviewers:** coldstart-loaders
- **Location:** `stock_splits.split_from / stock_splits.split_to (consumed by sawa/corporate_actions.py:72-84 get_split_adjuster, sawa/split_adjust.py get_unapplied_splits_in_range, scripts/restore_quarantined_prices.py:71-74)`
- **Evidence:** Polygon GET /v3/reference/splits (trailing year, 1000 rows) has 112 fractional-ratio records; 18 are for tracked tickers with stored prices and ALL 18 already have a stock_splits row with a rounded ratio: SF 2026-02-27 provider 1:1.5 stored 1:2 (created 2026-05-16); SMX 2026-02-17 provider 4.8828125:1 stored 5:1; SMX 2026-06-01 provider 2.285:1 stored 2:1; AUUD 2026-04-01 provider 7.7:1 stored 8:1; AKAN 2026-04-13 provider 4.5:1 stored 4:1; FER 2026-05-19 provider 103.5826:104.5826 stored 104:105; SCCO 1:1.012 and 1:1.01, TRI 1.015682:1, PHG 1:1.037, MOVE 1:1.358, JAGX 1:1.1, TR 1:1.03, SBS, METCB x2, FNRN, SNFCA all stored 1:1. `SELECT count(*) FROM stock_splits WHERE split_from = split_to` -> 32 rows (2025-05-30..2026-08-11, created 2026-02-26..2026-08-11). Guard history: `git log -S is_unrepresentable_split_ratio` -> introduced in 0781f4b; run_corporate_actions_update (sawa/corporate_actions.py:388-399) now skips fractional records instead of upserting them, so today's 2021 backfill neither corrected nor deleted the stale rows (load_splits upserts only rows it loads). The live REST series is fine (SF 78.48 -> 74.05 across the split, i.e. Polygon applied 1.5), so the wrong ratio only bites when something recomputes from stock_splits.
- **Impact:** SplitAdjuster.factor('SF', <2026-02-27>) = 2 instead of 1.5: any cached as-traded SF bar loaded by coldstart (fresh or --load-only) would be stored 25% too low; SMX factor 10 instead of 11.16 (12% error); AUUD/AKAN/FER similar; the 1:1 rows silently drop true 1-36% adjustments (TRI is in the current price cache with a 1:1 row). The same ratios drive split_adjust's pre-horizon 'unapplied split' step and restore_quarantined_prices' candidate re-basings (SMX is in that population).
- **Recommendation:** Repair the registry: delete or mark the 32 `split_from = split_to` rows and re-verify every stock_splits row against the provider for tickers with fractional records (extend the check across the whole 2021+ window, the 18 found are a lower bound from a 1000-row sample). Change run_corporate_actions_update so a skipped fractional record removes/flags any existing (ticker, execution_date) row instead of leaving it. Either widen split_from/split_to to NUMERIC so ratios are stored exactly (SplitAdjuster already uses Fraction) or add an `exact` flag and make SplitAdjuster/get_split_adjuster refuse inexact rows and 1:1 rows.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. High because the corrupted ratios are now inputs to three write paths: a re-base computed from SF's 2 instead of 1.5 stores prices 25% wrong, SMX 12% wrong. The 32 no-op 1:1 rows it also enumerates are tracked separately below because their fix (skip no-ops in the heal path) is different.
- **Verifier correction:** The finding's price-corruption impact is correct in mechanism and magnitude (SF factor 2 vs 1.5 = 25% low; SMX cumulative 10 vs 11.157 = 10.4% low) but is contingent on a full-universe coldstart. I re-ran SplitAdjuster.adjust_row over all 525 files in the current data/prices cache with the live registry: 58 tickers get re-based, and the ONLY mis-ratio ticker present is FER (442 rows, stored 105/104 vs true 104.5826/103.5826 = 0.004% error, negligible); TRI is present but its 1:1 row means its true 1.57% adjustment is silently skipped. SF, SMX, AUUD, AKAN, MOVE and JAGX are not in the current cache, so their 10-36% errors would only land on a fresh coldstart over the full ~10.4k universe. What is wrong TODAY, with no pipeline run, is the MCP-visible registry itself: get_stock_splits('SF') reports ratio 2:1 for a real 3:2, and get_recent_splits(days=30) reports a nonexistent SCCO 1:1 split.
- **Verifier correction:** Three corrections to the finding's supporting detail; the core claim stands unchanged.

(a) ROOT CAUSE IS POSTGRES, NOT A PYTHON `int(round(...))`. `git show 5f06bde^:sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py` shows StockSplit.from_polygon passed provider values straight through (`split_from=data["split_from"]`, `split_to=data["split_to"]`) with no coercion at all; the float then hit the INTEGER column and Postgres applied round-half-to-even on the float8->int4 cast. Verified live read-only: (1.5::float8)::int=2, (4.5::float8)::int=4, (7.7::float8)::int=8, (4.8828125::float8)::int=5, (2.285::float8)::int=2, (103.5826::float8)::int=104, (104.5826::float8)::int=105, (1.0085::float8)::int=1, (1.358::float8)::int=1 — every one of the six mis-stored ratios reproduced exactly, including the banker's-rounding tie 4.5->4. This matters for the fix: strict `_positive_integer` landed 2026-08-30 in 5f06bde and the skip guard in 0781f4b, and all 32 bad rows predate 5f06bde (newest created_at 2026-08-11), so NEW bad rows are already blocked. Only the historical rows need repair, plus the skip-should-delete change the finding recommends.

(b) THE restore_quarantined_prices EXPOSURE IS CURRENTLY NIL, NOT "SMX is in that population". stock_prices_unadjustable_archive now holds only ADTX(124) AEMD(77) CAPE(13) DLPN(23) EEIQ(1) FRSX(7) MDIA(99) PRFX(7) RGC(28) — SMX was restored by today's repair and none of the nine has a mis-ratio or 1:1 row.

(c) REACHABLE-TODAY PRICE IMPACT IS NARROW. data/prices holds 525 cached CSVs; of the mis-ratio tickers only FER.csv (443 lines, 2024-05-09 onward; stored 105/104 vs true 104.5826/103.5826 = 0.004% error, negligible) and TRI.csv (1257 lines from 2021-02-12; its 1:1 row silently skips a true 1.5682% re-base) are present. SF, SMX, AUUD, AKAN, MOVE, JAGX, PHG and SCCO are not cached, so their 1-36% errors only land on a fresh coldstart over the full ~10.4k universe. Separately, the 32 `split_from = split_to` rows cannot corrupt prices at all — SplitAdjuster gives them Fraction(1,1) and adjust_row returns the row untouched, and split_adjust's raw_split_jump_present computes raw_jump = -ln(1) = 0 and returns False — so they are silently-missing adjustments and wrong MCP output, not wrong arithmetic.

(d) Scope note on the 32: my provider page was truncated at 1000 records (next_url present) and reached back only to ~2026-02, so 12 of the 32 1:1 rows are provider-confirmed fabrications; the older 20 were not re-checked. A 1:1 "split" is a no-op by definition, so all 32 are bogus regardless of which fractional value each one flattened.

#### 20. The dividend loader clamps its fetch window to today and rejects any provider row outside it, so the dividends table contains zero future ex-dividend dates and get_ex_dividend_calendar / get_dividends(upcoming_only=True) can never return a row.

- **Theme:** dividends
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions
- **Location:** `sawa/corporate_actions.py:307 (end_date = date.today()), :427-429 (ex_dividend_date_lte=end_str), :438-446 (raise if any ex-date outside the window)`
- **Evidence:** SELECT count(*) FROM dividends WHERE ex_dividend_date >= CURRENT_DATE -> 0; max(ex_dividend_date) = 2026-09-02 with CURRENT_DATE = 2026-09-03. Forward-looking records demonstrably exist upstream and are partly stored: 827 rows have pay_date > CURRENT_DATE, max(pay_date) = 2027-06-01 — the same Polygon records carry announced future ex-dates that the `ex_dividend_date_lte=today` filter truncates. Replaying the tools: get_dividends(upcoming_only=True) -> `WHERE d.ex_dividend_date >= CURRENT_DATE` -> 0 rows; get_ex_dividend_calendar('2026-09-03','2026-10-03') -> 0 rows.
- **Impact:** get_ex_dividend_calendar (an explicitly forward-looking tool) and get_dividends(upcoming_only=True) can never return a row. Users asking 'which stocks go ex-dividend next week' get a confidently empty answer rather than an error, so the emptiness reads as 'no upcoming dividends' rather than 'this feed does not store the future'.
- **Recommendation:** Set the dividend end_date to e.g. today + 90 days (Polygon's declaration horizon) and widen the window guard at :438-446 accordingly, or drop the upper bound entirely for the dividends feed and keep it only for splits. Add a doctor check asserting count(*) FROM dividends WHERE ex_dividend_date > CURRENT_DATE > 0 on any weekday.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. A forward-looking tool that is structurally guaranteed to answer 'nothing' is a confident-wrong answer, not a gap.
- **Verifier correction:** The finding's inference that the 827 future pay_date rows prove future ex-dates exist upstream is weak (those rows all have past ex-dates with long-dated payments). The direct proof is the Polygon call above. Also note the upper bound is arguably deliberate for the splits feed — applying a not-yet-executed split would corrupt back-adjusted prices — so the fix should widen the window for dividends only, as the recommendation says.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections to the finding's evidence, neither of which changes the verdict:

1. The finding's central inference is WRONG: it claims the 827 rows with `pay_date > CURRENT_DATE` prove "the same Polygon records carry announced future ex-dates that the filter truncates". I checked those 827 rows directly — their ex-dates are min 2026-03-23, max 2026-09-02, i.e. ALL in the past. They are past ex-dates with long-dated payments and prove nothing about future ex-dates. Substitute the two proofs above (declaration_date lead of avg 100 / max 935 days across 44,704 rows; and the direct Polygon call returning 1000+ forward ex-dates with pagination). This matches the first verifier's correction, which I confirmed independently.

2. Location imprecision: `get_ex_dividend_calendar` and the `upcoming_only` filter are in mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py (:105-106 and :143/:175-176), NOT sawa/corporate_actions.py. The loader lines (:307, :427-429, :438-446) are correct as given for sawa/corporate_actions.py.

3. Endorsing the recommendation's scoping: the same window guard for SPLITS (sawa/corporate_actions.py:400-408) is arguably deliberate — persisting a not-yet-executed split would corrupt back-adjusted prices, since load_splits feeds the price/TA repair path via `stats["split_tickers"]`. Any fix must widen the window for the dividends feed only and leave the splits upper bound at today.

4. Strongest single regression test for the fix: `SELECT count(*) FROM dividends WHERE ex_dividend_date > created_at::date` is currently 0/45,228 and should become non-zero — a sharper doctor check than the proposed `ex_dividend_date > CURRENT_DATE > 0`, because it stays meaningful even if the pipeline has not run today.

#### 21. inflation.cpi_year_over_year has been NULL for every monthly release since 2025-05, so v_economy_dashboard.inflation_yoy and both MCP economy tools have served a NULL headline inflation rate for 15 months while the charts silently drop the series.

- **Theme:** economy-data
- **Reviewers:** economy-derived
- **Location:** `inflation.cpi_year_over_year; v_economy_dashboard.inflation_yoy; mcp_server/tools/economy.py:176 and :288`
- **Evidence:** SELECT count(*) tot, count(cpi), count(cpi_year_over_year), max(date) FILTER (WHERE cpi_year_over_year IS NOT NULL) FROM inflation;
 tot=65, cpi=64, cpi_year_over_year=50, last non-NULL = 2025-04-01.
Every row from 2025-05-01 through the latest release 2026-07-01 has cpi populated (332.813) and cpi_year_over_year NULL. The provider stopped sending the field — data/economy/inflation.csv (written 2026-09-02) has header `cpi,cpi_core,date,pce,pce_core,pce_spending` with no yoy column, and _load_economy_file maps only columns present in the CSV (sawa/database/load.py:880-893), so the column is simply never written.
The value is fully derivable from data already stored: SELECT a.date, round((a.cpi/b.cpi-1)*100,3) FROM inflation a JOIN inflation b ON b.date=a.date-interval '1 year' -> 2026-07-01: 3.316, 2026-06-01: 3.443, 2026-05-01: 4.180 (same scale as the stored history, e.g. 2025-04-01 = 2.311289).
SELECT * FROM v_economy_dashboard ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT 10 shows cpi = 332.813 and inflation_yoy blank on every one of the last 10 dates.
- **Impact:** get_economy_dashboard and get_economy_data('inflation') return inflation_yoy = null. mcp_server/charts/renderers/economy.py:239-243 and :356/:401/:452 silently drop the CPI-YoY series and the Fed-target comparison rather than erroring, so the dashboard renders as if inflation data simply does not exist. doctor only checks max(inflation.date), which is fresh, so nothing alerts.
- **Recommendation:** Compute cpi_year_over_year in the loader from cpi(t)/cpi(t-12)-1 whenever the provider omits it, backfill 2025-05-01..2026-07-01 (15 rows), and add a doctor check that the latest inflation row has a non-NULL cpi_year_over_year, not just a recent date.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. The single most-asked macro number is absent, and the renderer hides the absence rather than reporting it.
- **Verifier correction:** One correction to the remediation claim: the CPI-ratio derivation is approximate, not a faithful reconstruction of the historical series. Comparing derived vs stored where both exist — 2025-04-01 derived 2.333747 vs stored 2.311289; 2025-03-01 2.405585 vs 2.390725; 2024-11-01 2.714168 vs 2.749380 — the provider's historical values came from a different (unrounded or seasonally different) basis, so a straight cpi(t)/cpi(t-12)-1 backfill introduces a ~0.02-0.04pp discontinuity at the 2025-04/2025-05 seam. The derived figures quoted for 2026-07/06/05 (3.316 / 3.443 / 4.180) do reproduce exactly.
- **Verifier correction:** Three corrections/additions, none of which change the verdict.

(1) Factual overstatement in the evidence: "Every row from 2025-05-01 through the latest release 2026-07-01 has cpi populated" is not quite true -- 2025-10-01 has cpi, cpi_core AND cpi_year_over_year all NULL (only pce/pce_core are populated). So 14 of the 15 gap rows have cpi with NULL yoy; one row is missing cpi entirely, which is also why count(cpi)=64 rather than 65. The derivation is consequently impossible for 2025-10-01 and for 2026-10-01 once that release lands.

(2) I independently confirm verifier 1's correction that the cpi-ratio backfill is approximate, with the same numbers: where both exist, derived vs stored is 2025-04-01 2.333747 vs 2.311289, 2025-03-01 2.405585 vs 2.390725, 2025-02-01 2.814270 vs 2.821549, 2024-11-01 2.714168 vs 2.749380. A straight cpi(t)/cpi(t-12)-1 backfill leaves a ~0.02-0.04pp seam at the 2025-04/2025-05 boundary. Also worth noting the derived series is unusually jumpy (2026-02 2.40 -> 2026-03 3.34 -> 2026-04 3.77 -> 2026-05 4.18 -> 2026-06 3.44), which is a property of the stored CPI index itself and would become visible to users the moment it is backfilled.

(3) NEW, adjacent, not in the original finding: mcp_server/charts/renderers/economy.py takes `latest = data[-1]` at lines 112, 213, 348, 379 and 436, but mcp_server/tools/economy.py:288 issues `SELECT ... FROM v_economy_dashboard LIMIT %(limit)s` with no ORDER BY and the view returns newest-first. My live get_economy_dashboard(limit=6) returned rows 2026-09-02 down to 2026-08-26 and the chart header (renderer line 382) printed "as of 2026-08-26" -- i.e. "latest" is actually the OLDEST row in the window. The Fed 2% target block at :239-243 reads that same wrong row. This is masked today because inflation_yoy is NULL everywhere, but it would mis-source the headline and the target comparison immediately after the recommended backfill, so it should be fixed in the same pass.

#### 22. A single multi-ticker provider record discards a symbol's entire fundamentals download because the binding happens in a list comprehension inside the try block, and `sawa quarterly` still exits 0.

- **Theme:** fundamentals
- **Reviewers:** fundamentals-tables
- **Location:** `sawa/quarterly.py:86-98 (bound_rows comprehension inside try/except) with sawa/provider_downloads.py:167-168`
- **Evidence:** Polygon tags one filing with every ticker that shares the CIK/instrument, e.g. COO's annual income statements come back as tickers ['COO','DBI'] and every JPM row as tickers ['AMJ','JPM']. bind_provider_record raises ProviderError when the record's ticker set != {requested}. In sawa/quarterly.py:86 the binding runs inside a LIST COMPREHENSION, so the first bad record aborts the whole list and the except at :96 drops every row for that symbol. Replayed through the production client:
  $ uv run python -> PolygonClient.get_fundamentals('income-statements', ticker=sym, timeframe='annual'), then the same comprehension
  AAPL: 16 provider rows, 0 multi-ticker -> BOUND 16 rows
  COO:  24 provider rows, 5 multi-ticker -> WHOLE SYMBOL DROPPED (Provider returned a mismatched ticker identity)
  JPM:  16 provider rows, 16 multi-ticker -> WHOLE SYMBOL DROPPED
COO loses 19 perfectly good single-ticker rows because 5 siblings are co-tagged. The failure is not surfaced: DownloadStats.failed_feeds (sawa/provider_downloads.py:110-117) is non-empty only when EVERY symbol failed for a feed, so with AAPL succeeding run_quarterly appends 'fundamentals provider requests partially failed' to degraded_reasons but sets stats['success'] = not step_errors = True.
- **Impact:** Every dual-class, ETN-linked or co-tagged ticker (GOOGL/GOOG, BRK.A/BRK.B, JPM/AMJ, COO/DBI ...) is permanently excluded from fundamentals ingestion and will never recover, while `sawa quarterly` exits 0. get_fundamentals and v_latest_fundamentals return empty for those names forever.
- **Recommendation:** Bind per record, not per symbol: replace the comprehension with a loop that skips (and counts) individual rejected records, as sawa/add_symbol.py:270-280 already does. Better, treat a record whose ticker set CONTAINS the requested symbol as valid and bind it to the requested symbol — the provider is describing a shared filing, not returning the wrong company. Surface per-symbol reject counts as a degraded reason with the symbol list.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique (sawa/quarterly.py:86-98). Permanently excludes every dual-class / co-tagged ticker (GOOGL, BRK.B, JPM, COO) with a success exit code — silent, permanent, and self-concealing.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed as stated, with one correction to the recommendation: the second half — 'treat a record whose ticker set CONTAINS the requested symbol as valid and bind it to the requested symbol' — is actively harmful and would recreate finding #1. The live GET shows the 5 COO/DBI co-tagged records are Designer Brands filings (Jan/Feb fiscal year-ends, revenue 3,196,583,000 for 2022-01-29) that Polygon mislabels with COO; binding them to the requested symbol stores DBI's financials under COO. Only the first half of the recommendation is safe: per-record bind in a loop, skip and count rejects, and surface per-symbol reject counts as a degraded reason.
- **Verifier correction:** Two factual corrections to the write-up, neither of which weakens it:

1. Magnitude understated for JPM. With the production call shape (no timeframe filter), the live GET returns 100 income-statement records for JPM and ALL 100 are multi-ticker (69 ['AMJ','JPM']; the rest also carry JPMpC/JPMpD/JPMpJ/JPMpK/JPMpL/JPMpM). The finding's "16 provider rows, 16 multi-ticker" came from a timeframe='annual' probe; the unfiltered production call is worse.

2. "v_latest_fundamentals returns empty for those names" is inaccurate. sqlschema/06_views.sql:33 builds the view with LEFT JOIN LATERAL off companies, so JPM/GOOGL/BRK.B each still return one row — with every balance-sheet, cash-flow and income-statement column NULL. SELECT count(*) FROM v_latest_fundamentals WHERE ticker IN ('JPM','GOOGL','BRK.B') returns 3, not 0. get_fundamentals itself does return nothing for them.

Additional independent support for the round-1 verifier's caveat on the recommendation: income_statements ALREADY contains Designer Brands figures stored under COO — annual rows period_end 2022-01-29 (revenue 3,196,583,000), 2023-01-28 (3,315,428,000) and 2024-02-03 (3,074,976,000) sit beside genuine Cooper Oct-31 rows (2,922,500,000 / 3,308,400,000 / 3,593,200,000 / 3,895,400,000 / 4,092,400,000). So the "treat a record whose ticker set CONTAINS the requested symbol as valid" half of the recommendation would reproduce contamination that is already visible in the live table. Only the per-record loop that skips and counts rejects, plus surfacing per-symbol reject counts as a degraded reason (as sawa/add_symbol.py:270-280 already does), is safe.

#### 23. Fundamentals rows loaded before commit a55ca37 were bound to the provider's first co-tagged ticker instead of the requested symbol, so one issuer's financials are stored under another's ticker (COO shows Designer Brands) and 152 US large caps are absent entirely.

- **Theme:** fundamentals
- **Reviewers:** fundamentals-tables
- **Location:** `income_statements / balance_sheets / cash_flows (all rows created 2026-02-12..2026-06-15); loader was sawa/quarterly.py before commit a55ca37`
- **Evidence:** git show a55ca37^:sawa/quarterly.py shows the pre-fix binding: `record["tickers"] = record["tickers"][0] if record["tickers"] else ""` — the FIRST element of the provider array, not the requested symbol. load_csv_to_table then parses that first element (sawa/database/load.py:229-232). Consequences confirmed in the live DB and against the provider:
- COO: provider record period_end 2022-01-29, filing_date 2024-03-25, tickers ['COO','DBI'], revenue 3,196,583,000 (Designer Brands FY2021). Stored in income_statements under ticker='COO' with exactly those values. COO holds 72 income rows, DBI only 18; COO's 'annual' series interleaves Oct-FY (Cooper) and Jan/Feb-FY (Designer Brands) rows.
- JPM: every provider row is tickers ['AMJ','JPM'] -> first element 'AMJ' -> not in companies -> FK reject. income_statements has 0 rows for JPM.
- Same alphabetical-first pattern: GOOG 47 rows / GOOGL 0; BRK.A 47 rows / BRK.B 0.
- 26 tickers show two irreconcilable annual fiscal calendars (circular day-of-year distance > 21 days between annual period_ends).
- 152 active type='CS' companies with market_cap > $1B have zero income statements, including GOOGL ($4.15T), BRK.B ($1.08T), JPM ($946B), SCHW ($190B), CMCSA ($94B), CDNS ($93B), KMI ($72B), COR ($62B), XYZ ($49B).
- **Impact:** get_fundamentals, v_latest_fundamentals and the fundamentals chart renderer serve another company's revenue, net income, EPS and balance sheet under the wrong ticker (COO shows Designer Brands), while 152 US large caps including three of the ten biggest companies return no fundamentals at all. No repair path exists: grep for UPDATE/DELETE against these four tables across *.py/*.sql/*.sh returns nothing.
- **Recommendation:** Purge and re-ingest per ticker once finding #1 is fixed. As an interim integrity check, flag any ticker whose annual period_ends form more than one day-of-year cluster (>21 circular days apart) and quarantine those rows. Add a doctor check for tickers in companies with market_cap above a threshold and zero fundamentals rows.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique, and distinct from the loader defect above: the code was fixed, the stored data was not. Fix is a reload/repair of income_statements, balance_sheets and cash_flows.
- **Verifier correction:** Confirmed as stated. One scoping caveat: the 152 figure is an upper bound on damage attributable to this bug — a subset (TD, TTE, BMO, DB and other US-listed foreign private issuers) may have no Polygon financial statements at all regardless of binding. The unambiguous co-tag victims are GOOGL, BRK.B, JPM, SCHW, CMCSA, CDNS and peers, each of which has financial_ratios rows (proving the symbol was requested and answered) but zero rows in all three statement tables.
- **Verifier correction:** Two factual corrections and one amplification.

(1) Wrong fix commit. a55ca37 ("fix: validate provider responses and pagination", 2026-08-30 07:37) does NOT touch sawa/quarterly.py — `git show a55ca37 --name-only` lists 14 files, none of them quarterly.py. The binding fix landed two minutes later in 2bf3b63 ("fix: surface batch workflow failures", 2026-08-30 07:39), which replaced the first-element line with `bind_provider_record(record, symbol, output_field="tickers")` (sawa/provider_downloads.py:131-174; it raises ProviderError when any provider identity differs from the requested symbol). The evidence quote itself is correct because a55ca37^ and a55ca37 have identical quarterly.py.

(2) The mechanism is provable without the provider, and the "152 lost" cases are NOT ambiguous for the major names. The raw download CSVs from the Feb/Jun runs still exist under /home/seed/code/sawa/data/fundamentals/ and include a `cik` column. Grouping by CIK shows the financials for Schwab, Comcast, Cadence, Kinder Morgan, Cencora, Block, Prudential, JPMorgan, HEICO, Alphabet and Berkshire were all downloaded successfully and written under a stale/alternate first co-tag (SCH, CCZ, CDN, EP, ABC, SQ, PFH, AMJ/AMJB, HEI, GOOG, BRK.A). Verifier 1's caveat about foreign private issuers (TD, TTE, BMO, DB) is untested by me and may still apply to part of the 152, but for the top US names the loss is unambiguously caused by this bug.

(3) The wrong-values impact is broader than "COO shows Designer Brands". Two damage modes exist: (a) silent total loss when the donor ticker is absent from companies, and (b) cross-issuer contamination when the donor ticker is itself a live company. Mode (b) hits 7 tickers (ABX, AMBO, BATRA, COO, LIFE, VIA, VPG). The worst case is ABX: Abacus Global Management ($939M market cap, ~$59M quarterly revenue) has ALL FIVE of its annual income statements populated with Barrick Gold's numbers ($11.99B-$16.96B revenue, $2.0B-$5.0B net income), so get_fundamentals('ABX','annual') returns 100% another issuer's data with no correct rows to dilute it. VPG's 2021-2023 annual rows are Vishay Intertechnology's ($3.2-3.5B) against VPG's real ~$306M. Note v_latest_fundamentals happens to surface the correct issuer for COO/ABX/VPG because the most recent period belongs to the right company — the contamination is in the historical series that get_fundamentals and the chart renderer return.

#### 24. screen_by_indicators anchors on the whole-table MAX(date), so during the ~100-minute nightly TA write window it screens a partial universe and reports the truncated result as the market-wide screen (4 of 300 matches).

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** repositories-layer
- **Location:** `sawa/repositories/database.py:1219`
- **Evidence:** database.py:1219 builds the default date predicate as `conditions.append("date = (SELECT MAX(date) FROM technical_indicators)")` with no check that the max date is complete. The identical construct backs the MCP tool at mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:539. Measured live at 2026-09-03 21:35-21:44 UTC while the nightly daily run was writing TA:
  SELECT date, count(*) FROM technical_indicators WHERE date >= '2026-08-28' GROUP BY date;
  2026-08-28 10009 | 2026-08-31 10025 | 2026-09-01 10001 | 2026-09-02 10000 | 2026-09-03 169
So MAX(date) = 2026-09-03 with 169 of ~10,000 rows written. Then:
  rsi_14 >= 70 on MAX(date)  ->    4 tickers
  rsi_14 >= 70 on 2026-09-02 ->  300 tickers
Reproduced end-to-end through the repository (sawa.ta_query.screen_indicators, the only production consumer of this class):
  screen_indicators({'rsi_14': (70.0, None)}, limit=500)                    -> 7 rows, all dated 2026-09-03
  screen_indicators({'rsi_14': (70.0, None)}, target_date=date(2026,9,2))   -> 300 rows
The partial window is not brief: created_at on the 2026-09-03 rows runs 21:39:12 -> 21:44:42 at ~100 tickers/minute (85, 99, 110, 122, 102, 68 per minute), so filling ~10,000 tickers takes roughly 100 minutes every night, and longer after a ta-backfill or a split repair.
- **Impact:** For roughly 100 minutes every weeknight -- precisely the post-close window when screening is most likely -- `sawa ta-screen` and the MCP screen_technical_indicators tool return a near-empty result set (4 of 300 matches, a 98.7% silent loss) with no warning, no error and no indication that the anchor date is incomplete. ta_query.format_screen_results prints the truncated count as 'Screening Results (N matches)', so the caller reads it as a genuine market-wide answer. Any automated screen scheduled after the close is systematically wrong.
- **Recommendation:** Anchor on the last COMPLETE indicator date rather than the raw maximum: either pick the newest date whose row count is within a tolerance of the previous date's count, or gate on the daily run's own completion watermark (a run-state row) instead of MAX(date). At minimum, compare the candidate date's row count against the prior date's and attach an explicit 'partial date, N of M tickers computed' warning to the result so the truncation cannot be mistaken for a real screen. Apply the same fix to mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:539.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Kept at high: the failure window is exactly the post-close period when screening is most likely, and the wrong answer is indistinguishable from a real one.
- **Verifier correction:** Two numbers in the finding are overstated and should be corrected. (1) The partial window measured 35 min 30 s on 2026-09-03 (21:39:12 -> 22:14:42 UTC, ~281 tickers/min), not the claimed ~100 minutes at ~100 tickers/min -- the reviewer extrapolated from the first six minutes, which were slower because they covered the split-repair full-history recomputes. (2) The impact should say the partial set is alphabetically ordered, not a uniform sample, so the loss is not a clean 98.7% across all filters. The core claim -- MAX(date) anchors on an incomplete date for ~35 min every weeknight (17:39-18:15 ET), with no warning in either the CLI or MCP path -- stands.
- **Verifier correction:** Two numbers in the original finding are overstated and should be corrected; the core claim is unaffected. (1) WINDOW LENGTH: the partial window measured 35 min 30 s on 2026-09-03 (created_at 21:39:12.137 -> 22:14:42.768 UTC), not the claimed ~100 minutes. The reviewer extrapolated from the first six minutes (85, 99, 110, 122, 102, 92 tickers/min), which were slow because they covered the split-repair full-history recomputes; after a 7-minute gap the run sustained ~400 tickers/min (240, 364, 407, 422, 405, 407... per minute) through to completion. This confirms the first verifier's correction. (2) BASELINE: the loss should be measured against the true same-day answer (413 matches once 2026-09-03 completed), not the prior day's 300. On that basis the reviewer's sample instant represents roughly a 96% loss, and the loss decays continuously across the window (0.2% -> 4.4% -> 8.7% -> 52% -> 100% of truth at 21:39 / 21:44 / 21:52 / 22:00 / 22:14), so "a 98.7% silent loss" describes one instant near the very start rather than the whole window. (3) SHAPE OF THE TRUNCATION: the partial set is alphabetically ordered (after a handful of split-repair tickers), so the result is a systematically biased alphabetical head, not a uniform sample — the proportional loss will differ per filter rather than being a clean flat percentage. Everything else stands as written: both the CLI (sawa/cli.py:439, --date defaults to None) and the MCP tool (mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:539) anchor on the raw MAX(date), no completeness gate or run-state watermark exists anywhere on the path, and neither sawa/ta_query.py:187 nor mcp_server/server.py:2243-2260 attaches any warning that the anchor date is incomplete. The recommendation (anchor on the last complete indicator date, or attach an explicit "partial date, N of M tickers computed" warning, applied to both call sites) is the right fix.

#### 25. AsyncPolygonClient's default rate limiter is 5 requests per MINUTE, not the documented 5 per second, so get_live_prices_batch takes about nine minutes for the 50 tickers its own schema allows, with no server-side timeout.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** repositories-layer
- **Location:** `sawa/api/async_client.py:27`
- **Evidence:** async_client.py:27 declares `MAX_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE = 5` and async_client.py:109-112 builds the default limiter as `TokenBucket(rate=MAX_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE / 60.0, capacity=float(MAX_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE))` -- i.e. rate = 0.0833 req/s. Measured empirically:
  rate (req/s): 0.08333333333333333  => req/min: 5.0  capacity: 5.0
  acquire #1..#5 at t=0.00s ; #6 at t=12.01s ; #7 at t=24.01s ; #8 at t=36.00s
Both docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:69 ('Rate limiting: sawa/repositories/rate_limiter.py (default 5 req/s)') and docs/OPERATIONS.md:238 ('default 5 req/s for Polygon') state 5 per second, and the whole sync pipeline uses SyncRateLimiter(DEFAULT_API_RATE_LIMIT=5.0) which is 5 per second (sawa/utils/constants.py:10). No caller passes a rate_limiter argument: sawa/live.py:75 and :165 and sawa/scanner.py:99 all construct AsyncPolygonClient(api_key, logger). mcp_server/validation.py:61 caps get_live_prices_batch at 50 tickers and mcp_server/server.py:429-445 advertises maxItems 50; mcp_server/server.py has no per-tool timeout. 50 tickers = 5 free + 45 x 12s = 540s.
- **Impact:** MCP get_live_prices_batch, described to the model as a 'real-time batch query', blocks for up to ~9 minutes on a 50-ticker request and for ~2 minutes on a 15-ticker one, with no server-side timeout -- it either hangs the session or is killed by the client. `sawa scanner` over an index (scanner.py:99-105 fetches every constituent, then gathers get_ticker_details for every symbol at scanner.py:113-118) would need many hours. Conversely, because live.py builds a fresh AsyncPolygonClient (and therefore a fresh full bucket) on every call, the limiter provides no rate bound at all across calls: N rapid MCP calls each burst 5 requests immediately. The limiter is simultaneously far too slow within a request and absent between requests.
- **Recommendation:** Set the default to the documented per-second budget, e.g. `TokenBucket(rate=DEFAULT_API_RATE_LIMIT, capacity=DEFAULT_API_RATE_LIMIT)` reusing sawa.utils.constants.DEFAULT_API_RATE_LIMIT so sync and async paths cannot drift, and delete MAX_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE. Hoist the client (and its bucket) to module or process scope in live.py/scanner.py so the bound actually holds across calls instead of resetting per invocation.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique (sawa/api/async_client.py:27). A tool advertised as a real-time batch query effectively hangs the session — a functional break, not a performance nit.
- **Verifier correction:** One impact claim is wrong and should be dropped: there is no `sawa scanner` CLI command (grep -n scanner sawa/cli.py returns nothing), and sawa/scanner.py:scan_ytd_performance has no production caller -- the MCP scan_ytd_performance tool is served by the database-backed mcp_server/tools/scanner.py, not by the async client. The 'many hours over an index' scenario is therefore unreachable. The category should also be 'performance/availability', not 'wrong-values': the data returned is correct, it just arrives ~9 minutes late or not at all. Everything else, including the 12 s/request measurement and the get_live_prices_batch impact, is confirmed.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections, neither affecting the core finding. (1) The `sawa scanner` impact claim is unreachable as written: `grep -n scanner sawa/cli.py` exits 1 (no such CLI command), and the MCP scan_ytd_performance tool is served by the database-backed mcp_server/tools/scanner.py:249 (scan_ytd_performance_async), not by sawa/scanner.py. sawa/scanner.py:99's AsyncPolygonClient is reachable only as a lazy public export via sawa/__init__.py:37-40 and from tests/test_scanner_resilience.py -- a genuine latent hazard for a library consumer (scanner.py:99-105 fetches every index constituent, then :113-118 gathers get_ticker_details per symbol, so an S&P 500 scan would be ~1000 requests = ~3.3 hours), but not a live production path. Drop 'many hours over an index' as a current-impact claim; keep it as latent. (2) Category should be performance/availability, not wrong-values -- the returned data is correct, it just arrives ~9 minutes late or not at all. Everything else is confirmed, including the 12s/request measurement, the 8-ticker/36.01s end-to-end wall clock, the maxItems 50 and absent-timeout MCP impact, and the per-call fresh-bucket observation. The recommendation stands: use TokenBucket(rate=DEFAULT_API_RATE_LIMIT, capacity=DEFAULT_API_RATE_LIMIT) from sawa.utils.constants and delete MAX_REQUESTS_PER_MINUTE, and hoist the client to process scope so the bound holds across calls.

#### 26. earnings.eps_actual/eps_estimate are frozen on the pre-split basis for 109 tickers that split after the 2026-02-24 load while stock_prices is continuously re-adjusted, so every served P/E, earnings yield and EPS trend for those tickers is off by the split ratio (2x-25x).

- **Theme:** split-basis
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions
- **Location:** `earnings.eps_actual / scripts/populate_earnings.py:239-278 (no split invalidation), sawa/weekly.py:592-608 (split repair covers prices+TA only)`
- **Evidence:** Every earnings row was written in one batch on 2026-02-24 (SELECT date_trunc('day',created_at), count(*) FROM earnings GROUP BY 1 -> 2026-02-24 | 55848). 467 tickers have a split with execution_date > 2026-02-24; 109 of them have stored eps_actual, covering 1601 EPS rows. Concretely: BKNG split 1->25 on 2026-04-06; stock_prices BKNG 2026-04-02 close = 167.77 and 2026-09-02 close = 199.60 (post-split basis), but earnings BKNG 2026-02-18 eps_actual = 48.80 and 2025-10-28 = 99.50 (pre-split basis). TTM stored EPS = 228.51 against a 199.60 price -> naive P/E 0.9. Same pattern: KLAC (1->10 on 2026-06-12) eps 8.85 vs price 172.26 -> P/E 4.9; CVNA (1->5) -> 9.2; MNST (1->2) -> 25.2; CLMB (1->4) -> 5.0. grep over sawa/ and scripts/ shows no code path re-fetches or re-scales eps_* when a split lands (sawa/split_adjust.py and the daily/weekly split-heal touch stock_prices and technical_indicators only).
- **Impact:** get_earnings_history and get_earnings_calendar serve EPS numbers that are 2x-25x the correct split-adjusted value for 109 tickers including BKNG, KLAC, CRWD, CVNA, MNST, HON, DD, APH, POWL. Any P/E, earnings-yield or EPS-growth reasoning built on those rows is wrong by the split ratio, and the error is silent because both tables individually look self-consistent.
- **Recommendation:** Add a split-triggered earnings invalidation to the same hook that already repairs prices/TA: when run_corporate_actions_update returns split_tickers, either re-fetch those tickers' earnings (yfinance re-adjusts on fetch) or divide stored eps_estimate/eps_actual by the cumulative ratio for report_dates before execution_date. Until then, treat earnings.eps_* as as-reported and document the basis; a doctor check comparing max(stock_splits.execution_date) against earnings.updated_at per ticker would surface the drift.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Distinct from the earnings staleness group: even a fresh load would not restate history, and the fix is split invalidation in the earnings path (weekly.py:592-608 covers prices+TA only). Also distinct from the income_statements EPS defect — different table, different loader.
- **Verifier correction:** 115 tickers / 1,704 eps_actual rows are affected (not 109/1,601); 467 tickers split after the load, which matches. Restricting to rows whose report_date precedes the ticker's first post-load split gives the same 115/1,704. Some of the 115 have a net ratio of 1.0 (SNFCA, MOVE, TRI) and are unaffected in practice; the materially wrong ones run from 1.1x (LILA/LILAK) to 25x (BKNG).
- **Verifier correction:** Counts corrected: 115 tickers / 1,704 eps_actual rows affected, not 109/1,601 (the 467 post-load split tickers matches). Several of the 115 carry a net ratio of 1.0 and are unaffected in practice; the materially wrong ones span 1.2x (ATRO) to 25x (BKNG), and include reverse splits where the stored EPS is too LOW (HON 2:1 reverse -> stored TTM 10.67 should be 21.34; DD 3:1 reverse -> 3.70 should be 11.10), which the finding's "2x-25x too high" framing does not cover. NEW adjacent fact, not in the finding: max(report_date) is 2026-09-24 but zero rows with report_date > 2026-02-24 have an eps_actual (2,342 rows, all estimate-only) — roughly two quarters of actuals are missing outright, and since nothing re-runs the loader the split drift cannot self-heal. The recommendation's premise holds: no invalidation hook exists anywhere.

#### 27. The Hurst classification band (0.45/0.55) is narrower than the DFA estimator's own sampling error, so a universe of pure random walks would still be classified as trending or range-bound about 70% of the time.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/stock_character.py:283 (windows) + sawa/calculation/stock_character_config.py:8-9 (HURST_RANGE_THRESHOLD/HURST_TREND_THRESHOLD)`
- **Evidence:** Monte Carlo against the shipped code (300 replicates of i.i.d. N(0,1) 'returns' through sawa.calculation.hurst.compute_dfa): n=755 -> mean H=0.5036, sd=0.0464; n=503 -> 0.5044/0.0593; n=251 -> 0.5070/0.0746. The classifier's decision band is +/-0.05 around 0.5, i.e. 0.67 sd at the 1-year window. Feeding 500 simulated pure random walks through the exact _classify_hurst rule (756/504/252 nested windows) yields: range_bound 147 (21 of them 'unanimous', i.e. HIGH-confidence eligible), trending 176 (40 unanimous), boom_bust 28, dropped only 149 (30%). Live data agrees: within the 2026-09-02 range_bound cohort corr(hurst_1yr, hurst_3yr)=0.107 and corr(hurst_1yr,hurst_2yr)=0.249 even though the 1-year window is fully nested inside the others; and among 5,535 tickers with >=1200 bars since 2021 that were eligible in all six recent runs, only 1,280 of the 3,263 that appear at least once appear in all six - 61% flicker in and out of the published set week to week.
- **Impact:** The primary signal of the whole subsystem cannot separate its three regimes from estimator noise. Roughly two thirds of the ~3,180 rows published each week would be produced by a pure random walk, and membership of the 'classified' set is not reproducible across weeks. Every downstream artifact (baseline, flags, scorecard) inherits a label that carries little information.
- **Recommendation:** Either widen the decision band to the measured sampling error (e.g. require |H-0.5| > 2*sd(H) for the shortest window, ~0.15) or replace the point-estimate test with a bootstrap/surrogate test: shuffle the returns, recompute DFA, and accept a classification only when the observed H falls outside the surrogate distribution. Report the per-window standard error alongside each stored hurst_* value so consumers can see it.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. The primary signal of the whole subsystem cannot separate its three regimes from estimator noise, so roughly two thirds of the ~3,180 published rows carry no information.
- **Verifier correction:** Two evidence items are overstated. (a) End-to-end, not 'two thirds': running the full classify_stock (Hurst + secondary confirmation) on 300 pure geometric random walks of 1,394 bars publishes 115/300 = 38.3% (83 range_bound, 32 trending, 12 of them HIGH), not ~67%. The 70-73% figure is the Hurst stage alone; ADX/R2 confirmation removes about half of it. (b) The cited Hurst window correlations are conditioned on having been classified, which truncates them; unconditionally over all 3,180 rows of run 2026-09-02 corr(hurst_1yr,hurst_3yr)=0.4197 and corr(hurst_1yr,hurst_2yr)=0.5865. The stronger evidence is the 40.1% six-run stability and the failure of the spec's own validation names. Severity stays high: the primary signal's decision band is inside its own sampling error, the published label set is not reproducible week to week, and the 'leaning' relaxation in _classify_hurst is a deviation from the documented HIGH/MEDIUM rule that widens the noise admission.
- **Verifier correction:** Two corrections to the finding's stated numbers, plus four new pieces of evidence.

CORRECTIONS
(a) The "roughly two thirds of the ~3,180 rows published each week would be produced by a pure random walk" impact statement conflates two stages. 76.4% is the Hurst-gate pass rate; the *published* rate end-to-end through `classify_stock` is 41.4% for zero-drift walks (rising to 53-59% once realistic drift is added, because drift makes the OLS R2 trending confirmation fire). This matches the first verifier's 38.3%. The impact should read: a pure random walk is published roughly 40-60% of the time depending on drift, and ~3.4% of the time at HIGH confidence.
(b) The cited window correlations (0.107 / 0.249) are conditioned on having been classified, which truncates them; unconditionally over all 3,180 rows they are 0.4197 / 0.5865. But the correct comparison is not to 1.0 — it is to what pure noise produces under the *same* selection, which I measured at -0.21 / 0.03 (range_bound-selected) and 0.25 / 0.53 (all-selected). Live is in the same regime as noise, which is the actual point.
(c) Minor: the config threshold lines are `stock_character_config.py:7-8`, not 8-9.

NEW EVIDENCE (all reproduced this session)
1. The range_bound secondary confirmation is inert. Over 300 simulated pure random walks, mean full-history ADX(20) is 16.93 (max 22.26): `ADX_RANGE_MAX = 25` passes 100.0% and the `< 25*0.8` HIGH gate passes 97%. Not one range_bound candidate was ever filtered. Since range_bound is 2,333 of 3,180 published rows, the "secondary confirmation" that is supposed to guard the dominant class provides no discrimination at all — this is arguably a separate, equally serious defect and strengthens the recommendation.
2. Distribution-level proof that the published Hurst values carry no dispersion beyond noise: the cross-sectional sd of live published hurst_1yr is 0.0875 versus 0.0921 for gate-selected pure random walks (3yr: 0.0628 vs 0.0515; 2yr: 0.0737 vs 0.0643). The live spread is not larger than the noise spread. The only live/simulated difference is a mean shift (live 0.4667 vs sim 0.5125 at 1yr), which is why range_bound dominates the output — a level effect, not a discriminating one.
3. The week-to-week flicker is attributable to the band edge, not to a varying input universe. Across the six runs 2026-07-27..2026-09-02 (5,418 tickers with >=1200 bars), 3,174 appear at least once and 1,238 (39.0%) in all six. Sorting by appearance count gives a clean monotonic gradient in max|H-0.5| measured on 2026-09-02: 0.0664 (1 of 6) -> 0.0713 (3 of 6) -> 0.0787 (5 of 6) -> 0.1086 (all 6), with 78% of the one-run group inside 0.075 of 0.5 against 25% of the stable group. Counter-nuance to state honestly: conditional on being present in two consecutive runs, the label itself is stable (279 of 14,177 consecutive pairs flip = 2.0%). The instability is membership, not direction.
4. The shipped classifier violates its own written spec, and the deviation is visible in the stored table. `docs/STOCK_CHARACTER.md:60-86` requires all three windows to clear the threshold for both HIGH and MEDIUM; the `_classify_hurst` "leaning" branch at `sawa/calculation/stock_character.py:120-129` admits a stock on one window. Of 3,101 range_bound/trending rows on 2026-09-02, only 416 meet the documented rule, and 1,177 have a window on the opposite side of 0.5 from their assigned label (TSLA range_bound with hurst_3yr=0.5102/hurst_2yr=0.5126; KO trending with hurst_2yr=hurst_1yr=0.4986; PG range_bound with hurst_1yr=0.5087). Separately, `unanimous` is computed over non-NaN windows only, so 90 of 374 HIGH rows reached HIGH on two windows with hurst_3yr NULL — worth folding into the fix.

Recommendation stands, with two additions: re-tighten `_classify_hurst` to the documented all-three-windows rule (or amend the doc deliberately), and require `unanimous` to mean all three windows present and agreeing rather than all non-NaN windows. Re-derive `ADX_RANGE_MAX` against a null distribution before relying on it as confirmation.

#### 28. DECORRELATION_SPY divides a 10-day correlation by the dispersion of a 90-day correlation, so its '2 standard deviation' test fires on 22-52% of all windows and flags 40% of the universe every run.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/stock_character_detect.py:262-283`
- **Evidence:** The flag computes deviation = |corr(last 10 daily log returns vs SPY) - baseline.spy_corr_90d_mean| / baseline.spy_corr_90d_std and fires above 2.0, where both baseline moments come from the rolling *90-day* correlation series (stock_character_baseline.py:_compute_benchmark_correlation with CORRELATION_WINDOW=90). Recomputed on the real series (2021-02-12..2026-09-01): PFE std(90d rolling corr)=0.186 but std(10d corr)=0.359; XOM 0.267 vs 0.418; TJX 0.198 vs 0.363; D 0.214 vs 0.378; JPST 0.120 vs 0.345. Applying the live rule to every historical 10-day window flags 31.4% (PFE), 21.8% (XOM), 24.4% (TJX), 29.7% (D) and 51.7% (JPST) of them. Live: DECORRELATION_SPY is the most common flag in run 2026-09-02, 1,276 rows out of 3,180 classified tickers (40%). XOM's stored spy_corr_recent is -0.6955 vs a baseline of 0.2301 - a value entirely inside the sampling noise of a 10-observation correlation.
- **Impact:** The single most frequent 'atypical behaviour' signal in the system is a normal outcome, not an anomaly. Any ranking by flag_count is dominated by it, and 'macro driver detached' (the spec's interpretation) is asserted for two fifths of the universe every week.
- **Recommendation:** Compare like with like: either compute the baseline mean/std over the rolling 10-day correlation series, or convert both to Fisher z and test against the analytic standard error 1/sqrt(n-3) of the 10-day estimate. Alternatively lengthen the recent window to 90 days so it matches the baseline statistic.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique, and distinct from the ATR- and volume-baseline miscalibrations below (different statistic, different fix). The most frequent 'atypical behaviour' signal in the system is a normal outcome.
- **Verifier correction:** Location is stock_character_detect.py:255-283 (the block starts at the '# 11. DECORRELATION_SPY' comment on line 255, not 262). Minor additional detail the finding did not note: the baseline correlation series is built from simple returns (_align_returns) while recent_corr uses log returns, an inconsistency that is immaterial next to the window mismatch.
- **Verifier correction:** Location: the block is stock_character_detect.py:255-284 (starts at the '# 11. DECORRELATION_SPY' comment on line 255), not 262-283. Two observed refinements: (1) the baseline correlation series is built from simple returns (_align_returns, stock_character_baseline.py:179) while recent_corr uses log returns - immaterial next to the window mismatch; (2) the stored scorecard field spy_corr_recent is computed by a second, slightly different helper (stock_character_scorecard.py:37 _compute_spy_corr_recent, which slices prices[-window:] = 10 closes -> 9 returns, vs the flag's 11 closes -> 10 returns), so the stored spy_corr_recent and the flag's own recent_corr can differ slightly (XOM: stored -0.6955 implies dev 3.480, flag value 3.485271). New quantitative evidence beyond the original: on 30 randomly sampled classified tickers the live rule fires on 30.0% of windows on average versus 4.1% for the same 2-sigma test against the like-for-like 10-day dispersion, confirming the excess is the window mismatch itself. Also observed: the flag has zero test coverage (no occurrence of DECORRELATION anywhere under tests/), and the 40% share is stable across the last eight weekly runs (35.3%-42.9%).

### Medium (48)

#### 29. sawa doctor has no check for any of the failure modes this audit found — intraday session emptiness, schema drift/unapplied migrations, stale split basis, collapsed volume, earnings freshness, cumulative-indicator corruption, delisted-but-active companies, backup age, or any database operational metric (bloat, vacuum age, locks, connections, disk). *(filed independently by 5 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** monitoring
- **Reviewers:** db-health-concurrency, intraday-live, ops-scripts-monitoring, scheduling-and-doctor-gaps, volume-and-archive
- **Location:** `sawa/doctor.py (16 checks, all data-domain)`
- **Location:** `sawa/doctor.py (whole file; _required_tables_checks:104-145 defines the only table inventory)`
- **Location:** `sawa/doctor.py:105`
- **Location:** `sawa/doctor.py:105-146 (table-existence only), 403-409 (60-day post-split window), 493-612 (no stock_prices_intraday query); scripts/market_scheduler.sh:612-663 (summary has no bar counts)`
- **Location:** `sawa/doctor.py:363-377`
- **Evidence:** `grep -in 'intraday' sawa/doctor.py` returns nothing. _required_tables_checks (doctor.py:105-116) lists companies, stock_prices, technical_indicators, news_articles, market_internals, stock_prices_live, mv_52week_extremes — stock_prices_intraday is absent, and there is no freshness, row-count, session-completeness or bar-count assertion anywhere in the module. The scheduler only runs `sawa doctor --job daily|weekly`, i.e. after the market is closed and after cleanup_today_intraday_data has already emptied the table, so even an added check would have to be scheduled differently. Empirically the table is currently correct, which is exactly why the 74-of-78 truncation (finding #2) has persisted across at least 13 observed sessions with no alert.
- **Evidence:** _required_tables_checks only calls to_regclass per table name; no column/constraint/type assertions, so a migration left unapplied (as 44-46 were until 2026-09-01) passes while the live schema today shows they exist (stock_prices_ohlcv_complete CHECK, stock_prices_intraday_pkey(ticker,timestamp,bar_size_minutes), dividends_normalized_identity_uidx). No doctor check or scheduler summary references stock_prices_intraday counts (build_daily_summary prints only start/stop times). post_split_recompute limits to execution_date >= max(date)-60. The stale-basis SQL documented in OPERATIONS.md:276-287 is manual only; run today it returns 2 tickers (WTO 2026-02-17, HUIZ 2024-12-09) while `sawa doctor --job all` reports 46 passed / 0 failed.
- **Evidence:** The complete set of doctor check names in sawa/doctor.py is: companies.active_count, companies.attribute_completeness, stock_prices.expected_universe, stock_prices.latest_date, stock_prices.total_tickers, stock_prices.total_rows, stock_prices.latest_coverage, stock_prices.latest_rows, stock_prices.latest_ohlcv_sanity, technical_indicators.post_split_recompute, technical_indicators.latest_date, technical_indicators.coverage, news_articles.recent, mv_52week_extremes.freshness, stock_character_classification.latest_run, stock_character_classification.coverage -- every one is a row-count or freshness assertion about domain data. `grep -rn 'pg_stat_user_tables|n_dead_tup|pg_stat_activity|pg_database_size|max_connections|VACUUM|autovacuum' sawa/ mcp_server/ scripts/` returns only two read-only hits inside mcp_server/tools/schema.py (the describe_table tool). `grep -rn backup sawa/ scripts/ mcp…
- **Evidence:** sawa/doctor.py:363-377 is the only volume health check in the codebase (grep 'volume' sawa/doctor.py returns one hit, line 374): `WHERE date = (SELECT MAX(date) FROM stock_prices) AND (... OR volume IS NULL OR volume < 0)`. It is scoped to one date and treats 0 as valid. sqlschema/44_ohlcv_completeness.sql's stock_prices_ohlcv_sane CHECK reads `volume >= 0`. sawa/database/load.py:620-645 applies split_adjuster.adjust_row as a row_transform and counts only rows persisted, never rows whose volume collapsed. Consequence measured: 79,909 zero-volume rows and 16,248 provably-traded zero-volume rows have accumulated since 2021-02-18 with no check, alert or log line. Confirmed the damage is invisible from the latest date: on 2026-09-02, of 819 deep-reverse-ticker TA rows, 0 have volume_sma_20=0 and 0 have dollar_volume_sma_20=0.
- **Evidence:** grep of sawa/doctor.py returns 0 occurrences for each of: 'earnings', 'obv', 'vwap', 'backup', 'delisted', 'index_constituents', 'intraday'/'stock_prices_intraday', 'migration'. Live state each would have caught: earnings has MAX(created_at)=2026-02-24 over 55,848 rows while report_date runs to 2026-09-24 (1,993 already-reported quarters still carry NULL eps_actual) and scripts/populate_earnings.py has no trigger; obv/vwap are window-relative for ~419k rows; the newest DB backup is 102 days old; companies.active is never set false (10,430 active, ~280 dead). The doctor's only 'is this table alive' checks for the corporate-actions domain are stock_splits.readable / dividends.readable (doctor.py:690-700), which merely run COUNT(*) at WARN severity and pass on any non-error.
- **Impact:** The entire intraday/live surface — the thing every MCP price tool reads during market hours — has zero monitoring. A session that streams nothing (bad key, subscription rejected, socket wedged), that covers only 500 of 10,430 tickers, or that stops at 15:35 ET every day, produces no warning from doctor and no error notification from the scheduler. get_data_status reports latest_timestamp and row_count but is a read tool nobody runs on a schedule, and it asserts nothing.
- **Recommendation:** Add an intraday job mode to doctor (`--job intraday`) run by the scheduler while the market is open: assert max(timestamp) is within ~25 minutes of the ET clock, assert the distinct-ticker count is within a tolerance of the previous session's, and assert the bar count for a fixed liquid basket (SPY/QQQ/AAPL/MSFT/NVDA) equals the number of elapsed windows. Additionally assert at 16:30 ET that a 15:55 ET window exists for that basket — that single check would have caught finding #2 on day one.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Five reviewers filed overlapping lists of missing checks (35 intraday; 82 intraday/migrations/split basis; 145 collapsed volume, noting the CHECK constraint permits volume>=0 and doctor only inspects the latest trading date; 158 earnings/cumulative/backup/delisted; 142 the entire operational-health family). One owner, one work item: extend doctor's check inventory. Kept as one group rather than eight because the individual checks are trivial next to the decision to cover these domains at all. The specific data defects they would have caught are ranked separately above.

#### 30. The daily split self-heal window is widened to the oldest stale per-symbol watermark and load_splits reports unchanged upserts as newly persisted, so every run re-fetches five years of history and full-recomputes TA for ~628 tickers whose splits are up to a year old, as one all-or-nothing batch. *(filed independently by 3 reviewers)*

- **Reviewers:** daily-pipeline, ops-scripts-monitoring, weekly-corporate-splits

Indicators calculated: 516762 ... Time: 5.5 minutes' (heal span 21:35:19-21:44:38 = ~9 min/day). Same on 09-01 (629 tickers). SQL: SELECT COUNT(DISTINCT …

- **Evidence:** logs/daily_20260902_210002.log: line 10 'Extending repair windows for 277 ticker(s) with stale per-symbol watermarks; oldest is 2025-09-12'; 10690 GET /v3/reference/splits?execution_date.gte=2025-09-12&...; 10695 'Loaded 763 splits'; 10697 'Re-adjusting prices for 628 split ticker(s)...'; 10699 'Re-fetching adjusted prices from 2021-01-04 to 2026-09-02'; 11440 'Inserted 495000/498132 records'; 11442 'Recomputing TA for 628 split ticker(s)...'; 12243 '[daily] complete in 4448.0s' (prices done 21:35 UTC, self-heal 21:35→22:14 = 39 of 74 min). Same on 2026-09-01 (629 tickers, 'Loaded 764 splits'). 11,071 Polygon GETs for the run vs 10,442 on a no-new-data Sunday. DB: active tickers by last stored price date → 3 at 2025-09-12..26, 55 at 2025-10..2026-05, 317 at 2026-06..08 (375 stale watermarks drive oldest_start back a year). Docstring of _heal_splits_in_window says it should catch 'splits …
- **Impact:** Every daily run (7 days/week) re-fetches ~500k adjusted bars and rewrites ~500k stock_prices rows plus ~517k technical_indicators rows for tickers whose splits are up to a year old, adding ~9 minutes and heavy dead-tuple churn; the same-day heal that the design exists for depends on Polygon serving 628 complete 5-year histories every evening, and a single unrelated ticker's malformed/missing rows (09-01) cancels the re-base for the tickers that split that day. The set of stale tickers is permanent (nothing deactivates delisted tickers), so the window and batch size grow monotonically.
- **Recommendation:** Decouple the split lookup from stale watermarks: use last_price_date - PRICE_REPAIR_OVERLAP_DAYS for the universe-wide lookup, and run a second run_corporate_actions_update(tickers=stale_symbols, start_date=their watermark) only for the widened tickers (the function already accepts tickers=). In refresh_split_adjusted_prices, fail per ticker (skip/report incomplete tickers, adjust the rest) instead of returning error for the whole batch. Add a deactivation or quarantine path for tickers with no provider rows for N days so watermarks stop widening.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Three reviewers, one defect with two contributing causes (the watermark-derived split_heal_start_date at daily.py:644-648 and the over-broad persisted_tickers at corporate_actions.py:116); finding 43 names both. Severity medium, not high (one reviewer): the values written are correct, the costs are ~9-40 extra minutes per run, ~500k needless rewrites six times a week, dead-tuple bloat and an all-or-nothing failure surface.

#### 31. A single malformed provider dividend record aborts the whole corporate-actions transaction because parsing happens in an unguarded list comprehension, discarding that run's splits — and therefore the split price/TA heal — for as long as the record stays in the trailing-year window. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** corporate-actions
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions, weekly-corporate-splits
- **Location:** `sawa/corporate_actions.py:437 (dividends = [Dividend.from_polygon(d) ...], no per-record isolation), sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:173-182 and 190-194`
- **Location:** `sawa/corporate_actions.py:437 (dividends = [Dividend.from_polygon(d) for d in raw_dividends]); sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:83-90, 173-182`
- **Evidence:** Exercised the parser directly: Dividend.from_polygon with cash_amount=0.0 raises `ValueError: cash_amount must be a positive finite NUMERIC(10,4) value`; cash_amount=0.00004 (quantizes to 0) raises the same; frequency in {3, 6, 26, 365} raises `frequency must be one of 0, 1, 2, 4, 12, 24, 52 when provided`. Such records demonstrably occur for TRACKED tickers: dividends id=146508 is ARTY (iShares Future AI & Tech ETF, active=true, 517 price rows) ex 2025-06-16 with cash_amount = 0.0000, stored before the `positive=True` guard was added in commit 5f06bde (2026-08-30). The list comprehension has no per-record guard, unlike the splits path immediately above it, which explicitly escapes the analogous case via is_unrepresentable_split_ratio (:388-399). The raised ValueError propagates out of run_corporate_actions_update; sawa/weekly.py:578-588 catches it as a step failure, and because splits a…
- **Evidence:** Live: `SELECT ticker, ex_dividend_date, cash_amount, dividend_type, created_at FROM dividends WHERE cash_amount <= 0` -> ARTY 2025-06-16 0.0000 CD created 2026-05-16, i.e. Polygon does emit zero-amount CD records for tracked tickers; `positive=True` was added in 5f06bde (2026-08-30) so that record would now raise ValueError. The list comprehension at corporate_actions.py:437 has no try/except; the exception leaves the `with psycopg.connect` block (rollback) and run_corporate_actions_update, so splits staged at :412 are lost too (tests/test_corporate_actions_truthfulness.py::test_later_dividend_error_rolls_back_staged_split_transaction encodes this atomicity). Fractional split ratios received the tolerant treatment (is_unrepresentable_split_ratio, :388-399) but dividends did not. The 09-01 04:52 daily shows the same class for splits: "Daily split self-heal failed: ValueError: split_to mus…
- **Impact:** One sub-cent or zero-amount distribution, or one unlisted frequency value, from any of ~10,400 tracked tickers turns the weekly corporate-actions step into a total no-op — no dividends AND no splits — and because split_tickers comes back empty the downstream price/TA split repair also does not run. The failure is recorded as a degraded step rather than as data loss.
- **Recommendation:** Give dividends the same per-record escape the splits path has: wrap each Dividend.from_polygon in try/except ValueError, count the rejects into stats (so the run is visibly degraded) and continue with the rest. Same for the StockSplit comprehension, which still aborts on a zero/negative/non-numeric ratio that is_unrepresentable_split_ratio deliberately does not cover.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same defect at sawa/corporate_actions.py:437 from two reviewers, both low. Re-rated medium: one sub-cent distribution from any of 10,400 tickers turns the weekly corporate-actions step into a total no-op for up to 365 days, blocking split recording and the heal chain — a much larger blast radius than the dropped dividend itself.

#### 32. The nightly refresh of mv_52week_extremes uses the non-CONCURRENT form although the required unique index exists and the schema file prescribes CONCURRENTLY, holding ACCESS EXCLUSIVE for 6m21s and blocking every MCP call that joins the view. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** db-locking
- **Reviewers:** daily-pipeline, db-health-concurrency
- **Location:** `sawa/daily.py:411`
- **Evidence:** daily.py:411 cur.execute("REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW mv_52week_extremes"). sqlschema/14_52week_extremes.sql:8 '-- REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY mv_52week_extremes;', :25-27 '-- Unique index required for CONCURRENTLY refresh' idx_mv_52week_extremes_ticker_date, :33-35 COMMENT '... Refresh after daily data load with REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY.' Live pg_indexes confirms the unique index exists. logs/daily_20260902_210002.log:12099-12100 'Refreshing 52-week extremes materialized view (2026-09-01 -> 2026-09-02)...' 21:44:38 -> 'Refreshed' 21:50:59. Readers: mcp_server/tools/screener.py:421, 776, 846 JOIN mv_52week_extremes. mv size 892 MB.
- **Evidence:** sqlschema/14_52week_extremes.sql:8 documents the intended refresh as `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY mv_52week_extremes;`, lines 26-27 create `idx_mv_52week_extremes_ticker_date` under the comment '-- Unique index required for CONCURRENTLY refresh', and the matview's own COMMENT says 'Refresh after daily data load with REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY'. pg_indexes confirms the index is live and UNIQUE, so the CONCURRENTLY precondition is satisfied today. sawa/daily.py:411 nevertheless executes plain `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW mv_52week_extremes`, which takes ACCESS EXCLUSIVE. Scale of the rebuild: pg_total_relation_size = 893 MB (550 MB heap + 343 MB of index), 10,041,418 rows, n_tup_ins 30,092,805 over 3 lifetime refreshes; EXPLAIN of the defining query gives cost 1,015,291 for a WindowAgg over an Index Scan on stock_prices_pkey (round 1 measured 6m21s in the run log). The…
- **Impact:** A plain REFRESH takes an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock, so MCP screener queries that join mv_52week_extremes block (or time out) for ~6 minutes every evening at ~17:44-17:51 ET; inconsistent with the documented refresh method.
- **Recommendation:** Use REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW CONCURRENTLY mv_52week_extremes (unique index already present), and add a regression test asserting the CONCURRENTLY keyword in the executed SQL.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Identical finding at sawa/daily.py:411 from two reviewers, both medium. One-word fix with a measured 6-minute nightly outage on the screener path.

#### 33. mv_52week_extremes uses a 252-ROW window instead of a 365-day window, so for any ticker with missing sessions the served '52-week' high/low reaches arbitrarily far back (FISV's is from 2023-04-25). *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** derived-views
- **Reviewers:** economy-derived, prices
- **Location:** `mv_52week_extremes; sqlschema/14_52week_extremes.sql:14-22`
- **Location:** `sqlschema/14_52week_extremes.sql:17,21 (ROWS BETWEEN 251 PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW); consumed at mcp_server/tools/screener.py:846`
- **Evidence:** The definition is a row-count window, while the COMMENT and the name promise a time window:
    MAX(high) OVER (PARTITION BY ticker ORDER BY date ROWS BETWEEN 251 PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW) AS high_52w
    COMMENT ... 'Pre-computed 52-week high/low per ticker using 252-trading-day window.'
252 rows equals 52 weeks only when a ticker trades every session. 963 tickers have interior gaps over the last 2 years (35,742 missing trading days), so their windows overshoot.

How far the 252nd-most-recent row actually sits, across all tickers with >=252 rows:
  WITH last_rows AS (SELECT ticker, date, row_number() OVER (PARTITION BY ticker ORDER BY date DESC) rn FROM stock_prices WHERE date >= '2023-01-01')
  SELECT count(*), count(*) FILTER (WHERE window_start < DATE '2025-09-02' - INTERVAL '30 days'),
         count(*) FILTER (WHERE window_start < DATE '2025-09-02' - INTERVAL '90 days'), min(windo…
- **Evidence:** SELECT 'FISV mv', high_52w, low_52w FROM mv_52week_extremes WHERE ticker='FISV' AND date='2026-09-02' -> 122.39 / 47.04.
SELECT max(high), min(low) FROM stock_prices WHERE ticker='FISV' AND date > '2026-09-02'::date-365 AND date <= '2026-09-02' -> 70.41 / 47.04.
The 122.39 high is dated 2023-04-25 — 1,254 days before the row it labels. Cause: FISV has 107 rows in 2023, 0 in 2024 and 35 in 2025, so 252 rows span 3.4 years.
Universe-wide, measuring how far back the 252nd-previous row lies at each ticker's latest date: 141 tickers span >400 calendar days and 71 span >500 (worst: WLTH 1280d, FISV 1254d, TWLV 1237d, BBBY 1214d).
Downstream effect on the screener's high_52w_pct: FISV close 51.98 reports -54.94% off its '52-week high' instead of the true -21.67%.
- **Impact:** 52-week high/low is served wrong for hundreds of tickers, and the error is unbounded — it grows with the number of missing sessions rather than being capped. Anything built on it is affected: 52-week-extreme screens, percent-off-high / percent-above-low ratios, breakout detection and range-position scoring. The row window is also what carries prior-issuer prices into the current security's extremes in finding #1, since a gap at a reassignment boundary is exactly the case where the window overshoots most.
- **Recommendation:** Replace the row window with a date window: `RANGE BETWEEN INTERVAL '52 weeks' PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW` over `ORDER BY date`, which Postgres supports for date-ordered windows and which makes the definition match both its name and its COMMENT. Refresh the matview after the change and reconcile it against a direct min/max over the same date window for a sample of gapped tickers.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same defect at sqlschema/14_52week_extremes.sql:14-22; the two reviewers wrote 251 PRECEDING and 252-row for the same window. Distinct fix from the staleness group — this one is in the view definition. Error is unbounded but concentrated in illiquid and post-halt names (~141 tickers).

#### 34. Price rows revised outside the daily 14-day overlap never trigger TA recomputation, because pending_ta_recompute_from is derived only from rows the daily fetch itself inserted, leaving 13,269 volume_sma_20 rows across 4,454 tickers and 503 sma_20 rows inconsistent with the prices they are derived from. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** indicators, ta-engine
- **Location:** `sawa/daily.py:787-806 (pending_ta_recompute_from is derived only from rows the daily fetch itself inserted) and sawa/daily.py:1063-1079`
- **Location:** `technical_indicators.volume_sma_20 / technical_indicators.sma_20 (sawa/daily.py:806 - recompute_from is only ever the 14-day fetch window start)`
- **Evidence:** Full-history exact recompute of volume_sma_20 (AVG(volume) over 19 preceding + current rows, 9,833,851 rows checked): 13,269 rows differ by more than 1 unit across 4,454 tickers; 3,029 rows across 2,833 tickers differ by more than 1%. By month, bad rows / tickers: 2026-06 10/4 | 2026-05 2,831/2,825 | 2025-11 10/1 | 2025-04 5/2 | 2023-08 15/1 | 2023-05 19/1 | 2021-07 68/4 | 2021-06 44/4.
Full-history exact recompute of sma_20 (9,834,058 rows): 503 rows differ by >0.05%, 497 tickers; 496 of those are all on 2026-05-15. Examples on 2026-05-15 (exact vs stored): GCTS 1.4590 vs 1.4420 (1.17%), QUCY 0.7603 vs 0.7675 (0.95%), ASTX 27.4860 vs 27.3150 (0.62%), COE 26.2005 vs 26.0380 (0.62%).
Single-ticker walk-through, CHNR: 2026-06-02 exact volume_sma_20 18,501.6 vs stored 18,496; 2026-06-03 exact 44,554.7 vs stored 37,187 (16.5% low), which also makes stored volume_ratio 10.698287 instead of 54…
- **Evidence:** OBV recurrence breaks outside the two residue bands (2026-04-01..05-14 and >=2026-08-17): USO 31 broken steps 2026-04-02..2026-08-17 (max_err 90,794,375), PDBC 31, DBC 31, COPX 30, UNG 30, DBA 30, COMT 29, FAST 6, COKE 6, plus ~20 microcaps with 3-5 each. Detail for USO: 2026-03-26 stored obv_step 42,398,638 vs stored volume 42,398,600 (close direction correct, volume differs by 38); 2026-04-09 step 44,210,723 vs volume 44,214,800; price row created_at 2026-05-16 16:30, TA row created_at 2026-05-16 21:05 - the stock_prices.volume was revised after the TA row was written and no recompute followed. The USO 2026-04-01..04-10 price rows are unique (no duplicates).
- **Impact:** Historical volume_sma_20 / volume_ratio / sma_20 served by get_technical_indicators do not match the prices in the same response, so a user (or a backtest) reconstructing an indicator from stock_prices gets a different answer than the stored column. Errors up to 16.5% on volume_sma_20.
- **Recommendation:** Track which (ticker, date) price rows an upsert actually CHANGED (compare old vs new in the ON CONFLICT, or use `RETURNING (xmax <> 0)` plus a value diff) and feed the earliest genuinely changed date into pending_ta_recompute_from instead of the fetch-window start. Add a periodic doctor check that exact-recomputes volume_sma_20 (integer, so no float tolerance needed) over a rolling year and flags mismatches.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same defect at sawa/daily.py:787-806; reviewers rated low and medium. Medium is right — the individual numeric errors are small, but the structural consequence is that any writer other than the daily fetch (weekly refresh, adjust-splits, restore scripts) leaves TA silently stale.

#### 35. talib.BBANDS suffers float64 catastrophic cancellation for tickers whose adjusted history spans many orders of magnitude, storing negative bb_lower, 24,000% bb_width_pct or bands collapsed onto the middle — and a full recompute reproduces it, so ta-backfill cannot fix it. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** indicators, ta-engine
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:238 (talib.BBANDS over the full close array)`
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:238-242 (talib.BBANDS sliding-sum variance) -> technical_indicators.bb_upper/bb_lower/bb_width_pct`
- **Evidence:** Exact NUMERIC recompute (AVG(close) +/- 2*STDDEV_POP(close) over 20 preceding rows) vs stored, at every ticker's latest date (10,273 tickers, 20-bar windows): 10 tickers wrong, max absolute error 1941.13. On 2026-09-02:
  SMX  close 16.1123 exact [14.8028, 17.5034] stored bb_upper 1958.6327 bb_lower -1926.3265 bb_width_pct 24050.844865
  WHLR close  0.5254 exact [ 0.6089,  2.1806] stored bb_upper  522.9070 bb_lower  -520.1175 bb_width_pct 74780.841499
  XXII close  2.3400 exact [ 2.4823,  4.9540] stored bb_upper   34.8315 bb_lower   -27.3952 bb_width_pct  1673.592125
  VCIG close  1.8300 exact [ 1.5944,  5.1216] stored bb_upper/middle/lower all 3.3580, bb_width_pct 0.000000
  CETX close  2.5200 exact [ 2.4491,  3.1329] stored bb_upper/middle/lower all 2.7910, bb_width_pct 0.000000
  ADTX close  0.0072 exact [-0.0002,  0.0089] stored bb_upper/middle/lower all 0.0044, bb_width_pct 0.000000…
- **Evidence:** SQL exact 20-bar STDDEV_POP(close) from stock_prices vs stddev implied by stored bands on 2026-09-02: WHLR exact sma20 1.3948 sd 0.3929 exact bb_upper 2.1806, stored bb_upper 522.9070 (implied sd 260.76), stored bb_lower -520.1175, bb_width_pct 74780.84; SMX exact sd 0.6751 exact upper 17.5034, stored 1958.6327 (implied sd 971.24), bb_width_pct 24050.84; XXII exact sd 0.6179 exact upper 4.9540, stored 34.8315, bb_width_pct 1673.59; control MGN exact 5.3002 == stored 5.3002. ta_compare.py shows WHLR stored == full recompute from current prices (created_at 2026-09-03, i.e. today's recompute). Price span max(close)/min(close): WHLR 7.32e10 (2021-03-01 close 24,530,688,000 vs 0.5254 now), SMX 8.30e9, XXII 5.37e8. talib's TA_INT_VAR keeps running sum and sum-of-squares over the whole array; after values ~1e10 the residual rounding error dwarfs the true 20-bar variance. On 2026-09-02, 3 rows h…
- **Impact:** mcp_server/tools/momentum.py:117-122 derives squeeze_on directly from stored bb_upper/bb_lower vs the Keltner channel. Tickers whose bands collapse (bb_upper == bb_lower) report a permanent, maximally-tight squeeze and will top any squeeze scan; tickers with +/-1926 bands report squeeze_on = false forever. get_technical_indicators serves bb_lower values that are negative for a positive-priced stock.
- **Recommendation:** Do not feed the raw multi-order-of-magnitude array to talib.BBANDS. Either compute bb_middle/bb_upper/bb_lower from a numerically stable rolling mean/variance (Welford, or numpy stride tricks over the 20-bar window in float64 after subtracting the window mean), or normalise the close array by its last value before calling BBANDS and rescale. Additionally, add a post-hoc guard in validate_indicator: reject bb_lower > bb_upper, bb_upper == bb_lower when the 20-bar close range is non-zero, and bb_width_pct above a sane ceiling.
