- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same defect at ta_engine.py:238; round 2 added the worked example (WHLR: stored ±521 bands for a $0.53 stock whose exact 20-day stddev is 0.39) and the crucial point that the value is not merely stale. Downstream effect on the squeeze screener is the same in both.

#### 36. get_dividend_yield_leaders joins financial_ratios without selecting the latest snapshot, so tickers appear two or three times with different yields, the ordering mixes current and months-old values, and the LIMIT is consumed by duplicates. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions, mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:271 (LEFT JOIN financial_ratios fr ON c.ticker = fr.ticker with no date restriction) vs the DISTINCT ON LATEST_RATIOS_CTE used in screener.py:161`
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:271 (LEFT JOIN financial_ratios fr ON c.ticker = fr.ticker)`
- **Evidence:** financial_ratios holds one row per (ticker, date) snapshot: 139 tickers with 1 row, 3,767 with 2, 166 with 3 (AAPL has snapshots for 2026-02-10, 2026-02-13 and 2026-06-12 with dividend_yield 0.0039 / 0.0041 / 0.0036). The tool's query has no `DISTINCT ON` and no date predicate, so it fans out. Replaying it verbatim: 7 rows returned for 6 distinct tickers, with MAGN appearing twice at dividend_yield 2.9525 and 2.4709. Sibling code avoids this — get_dividend_yield_leaders' own latest_prices and latest_divs CTEs both use DISTINCT ON.
- **Evidence:** SQL: financial_ratios has 8171 rows for 4072 tickers; 3933 tickers have 2-3 snapshots. Default tool output already shows MAGN twice (2.9525 @2026-05-14 and 2.4709 @2026-06-12). With the unit bug corrected (>= 0.02, limit 200) the top-200 rows contain only 117 distinct tickers and 98 rows come from non-latest snapshots.
- **Impact:** Duplicate companies in the leaderboard, and the ordering mixes current and months-old yields so a ticker can outrank a peer purely because an older snapshot had a higher yield. At limit=50 the caller can receive as few as ~17 distinct companies.
- **Recommendation:** Wrap financial_ratios in a `latest_ratios AS (SELECT DISTINCT ON (ticker) ticker, dividend_yield FROM financial_ratios ORDER BY ticker, date DESC)` CTE mirroring the two CTEs already in the same query, and join that. The same unfiltered join appears in other tools (mcp_server/tools/companies.py:108, screener.py:798) and is worth auditing on the same pass.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Identical finding at corporate_actions.py:271 from two reviewers, both medium; the fix is the DISTINCT ON latest_ratios CTE already used in screener.py:161. Separate from the fraction/percent unit bug in the same query — two independent fixes.

#### 37. The technical_indicators and stock_character coverage checks count all-time DISTINCT tickers instead of tickers present in the latest run, so a half-completed run passes and the reported coverage (5,802) has no relation to the latest run (3,180). *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** monitoring
- **Reviewers:** scheduling-and-doctor-gaps, stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/doctor.py:506 (technical_indicators) and sawa/doctor.py:659 (stock_character_classification)`
- **Location:** `sawa/doctor.py:656-686`
- **Evidence:** The query is `SELECT MAX(run_date), COUNT(DISTINCT ticker) FROM stock_character_classification` with no run_date predicate. Live: COUNT(DISTINCT ticker) over the whole table = 5,802, while the latest run (2026-09-02) holds 3,180 rows; active companies = 10,430 and min_coverage defaults to 0.85 (sawa/doctor.py:746). The check therefore reports 'stock character covers 5802/10430 active tickers' - an 82% overstatement of what the latest run actually produced - and because it is a WARN it never fails either way. Since latest_run only looks at MAX(run_date), a run that wrote a handful of rows would pass freshness (FAIL severity) and still report 5,802 coverage.
- **Evidence:** doctor.py:504-509 runs `SELECT MAX(date), COUNT(DISTINCT ticker) FROM technical_indicators` and feeds that count to `technical_indicators.coverage` (>=85% of active companies, FAIL) while `technical_indicators.latest_date` only needs MAX(date) within 4 days — one single row for today satisfies it. Observed live at 2026-09-03 22:10 UTC, mid-daily-run: technical_indicators had 8,203 distinct tickers on 2026-09-03 versus 9,981 in stock_prices (1,778 missing), yet MAX(date)=2026-09-03 (PASS) and all-time COUNT(DISTINCT ticker)=10,428 against 10,430 active companies = 99.98% (PASS). After the run finished the gap closed to 9,981/9,981 — but a TA step that died at 82% would have produced an identical, all-green doctor report. Same pattern at :659 for stock character: all-time distinct = 5,802 while the latest run_date 2026-09-02 classified only 3,180 tickers, so the check reports 5,802/10,430 …
- **Impact:** The only monitoring the subsystem has cannot detect a partially failed run. Coverage would keep reporting the historical union even if the batch degraded to a few dozen tickers, and the reported number is the one recorded in operator notes as the system's coverage.
- **Recommendation:** Scope the count to the latest run: `SELECT run_date, COUNT(*) FROM stock_character_classification WHERE run_date = (SELECT MAX(run_date) ...)`, and compare it against the previous run's count so a sudden drop is caught.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** One reviewer filed the stock_character instance (low), one filed both tables (medium). Same query defect, same fix. Medium: these are the FAIL-severity checks meant to make the scheduler retry a broken daily, and they are structurally incapable of detecting a partial run.

#### 38. The quarterly domain has neither a scheduled loader nor a reachable health check: nothing runs `sawa quarterly` (so the whole June-2026 reporting season is missing) and no scheduled path invokes doctor --job quarterly, the only FAIL-severity guard on fundamentals freshness. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** scheduler
- **Reviewers:** fundamentals-tables, scheduling-and-doctor-gaps
- **Location:** `crontab / scripts/market_scheduler.sh (no quarterly entry, no scripts/quarterly.sh); doctor gate at sawa/doctor.py:706-738`
- **Location:** `sawa/doctor.py:788 (job gate) and scripts/market_scheduler.sh:112-128 (run_doctor)`
- **Evidence:** crontab -l has one sawa entry (*/15 market_scheduler.sh); grep -rn 'quarterly' scripts/ matches only an unrelated comment in populate_earnings.py; there is no scripts/quarterly.sh next to daily.sh/weekly.sh/coldstart.sh; ls logs/ | grep -c quarterly = 0 (36 log files present). docs/OPERATIONS.md:60 nonetheless lists `sawa quarterly | Fundamentals + financial ratios | Quarterly`.
Result in the DB (today 2026-09-03):
  income_statements quarterly: max(period_end)=2026-05-10, max(filing_date)=2026-06-11, rows with period_end >= 2026-05-11 = 0
  of 4,572 tickers, 4,504 have a latest quarterly period_end before 2026-05-01 and 0 have any June-2026 quarter
  financial_ratios: max(date)=2026-06-12 (83 days stale); the provider serves a current snapshot (live ratios call for AAPL returns date 2026-09-02)
sawa/doctor.py:706-738 checks MAX(period_end) against a 210-day window, so income_statements …
- **Evidence:** run_doctor_on_connection gates the fundamentals checks: `if job in {"all", "quarterly", "coldstart"}: checks.extend(_quarterly_checks(conn, today=today))` (doctor.py:788). Every automated invocation passes a different job: market_scheduler.sh calls `run_doctor daily` (line 597) and `run_doctor weekly` (line 533), which run `sawa doctor --job "$job"` (line 116); scripts/daily.sh:20 and scripts/weekly.sh:20 do the same. grep over crontab, scripts/*.sh and systemd timers finds no other doctor invocation, and `grep -c quarterly scripts/market_scheduler.sh` = 0 — `sawa quarterly` itself has no trigger either (no quarterly_*.log has ever been produced: logs/ contains only intraday/daily/doctor/weekly runs). Live consequence: financial_ratios MAX(date)=2026-06-12, balance_sheets/income_statements/cash_flows MAX(period_end)=2026-05-10, i.e. the whole Q2-2026 reporting season is missing and no au…
- **Impact:** Every 10-Q filed July-August 2026 is missing from all four fundamentals tables. get_fundamentals, v_latest_fundamentals and the screener's latest_ratios CTE serve a full quarter of stale financials with no warning, and the automated health check is structurally incapable of noticing for another four months.
- **Recommendation:** Add a quarterly job to scripts/market_scheduler.sh (or a scripts/quarterly.sh + cron entry) and tighten the doctor gate: check per-ticker freshness (e.g. share of tickers whose latest period_end is within ~135 days of today) rather than a global MAX over a 210-day window, and check financial_ratios.max(date) against a much shorter window since the provider snapshots it daily.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Two reviewers found the loader gap (low) and the doctor gap (medium); both are closed by the same fix — add a quarterly slot to scripts/market_scheduler.sh that runs the loader and the matching doctor job. Medium: a full quarter of stale financials served with no warning until January 2027.

#### 39. Failures before monitored_run (missing env in cli, or the scheduler's .env allowlist parse) produce no notification because the scheduler assumes any exit < 126 was self-reported, and the dead-man's-switch heartbeat is inert because neither heartbeat URL is configured. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** scheduler
- **Reviewers:** ops-scripts-monitoring, scheduling-and-doctor-gaps
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:100-109, 251-258; sawa/cli.py:167-172, 298-303, 939-941`
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:136-138 (`heartbeat()` returns 0 when the URL is empty), call sites :530, :535, :547, :588, :593, :606`
- **Evidence:** cli.py:167-172 returns 1 for missing POLYGON_API_KEY/DATABASE_URL before monitored_run (no notifier call); market_scheduler.sh:104-108 'Skipping duplicate alert' for exit_code < 126; setup_env (251-258) raises SystemExit for any .env key outside the hard-coded allowlist and returns 1, which under set -e exits main before notify/intraday/daily. .env key names present: DATABASE_URL, FRED_API_KEY, NTFY_TOPIC, PG*, POLYGON_* — no SAWA_HEARTBEAT_URL/SAWA_WEEKLY_HEARTBEAT_URL; grep HEARTBEAT docs/*.md .env.example → no hits, so the dead-man switch notify.py:31-32 relies on is unarmed.
- **Evidence:** heartbeat() begins `local url="$1" ...; [ -z "$url" ] && return 0` and every call site passes `"${SAWA_HEARTBEAT_URL:-}"` / `"${SAWA_WEEKLY_HEARTBEAT_URL:-}"`. Checking .env for those keys: SAWA_HEARTBEAT_URL=ABSENT, SAWA_WEEKLY_HEARTBEAT_URL=ABSENT (only NTFY_TOPIC is set). So all six heartbeat calls are no-ops. Every alert this deployment can emit is a push from inside a job that actually ran (`sawa notify` / monitored_run ntfy POSTs); nothing observes absence. Corroborating live evidence that absence is a real failure mode here: the weekly DB backup has failed silently for 14 weeks with output going only to a log file, and a failing daily aborts the scheduler tick before any further logging (see the set -e finding).
- **Impact:** Adding one new variable to .env (or losing a key) silently stops intraday, daily and weekly on every tick with only a cron.log line; no push, no heartbeat miss.
- **Recommendation:** Have the scheduler alert when a job exits non-zero without emitting its '[job] starting' marker (or make cli send a notification before returning 1); make unknown .env keys a logged skip rather than a fatal parse error; document and configure SAWA_HEARTBEAT_URL in OPERATIONS.md and .env.example.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Two reviewers describing one hole from two ends: 81 names both the pre-monitored gap and the unconfigured heartbeat, 157 documents the inert heartbeat() call sites. One fix restores the outer safety net. Adding a single variable to .env silently stops intraday, daily and weekly on every tick with only a cron.log line.

#### 40. drop_all_tables enumerates pg_tables rather than the schema manifest, so a documented destructive rebuild would silently destroy tables outside sqlschema — the stock_prices_unadjustable_archive (sole copy of 379 quarantined rows, defined only inside a script) and an external project's three turbulence_* tables written daily by cron. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** schema-drift
- **Reviewers:** schema-drift, volume-and-archive
- **Location:** `sawa/database/schema.py:262-296 and scripts/quarantine_unadjustable_prices.py:104`
- **Location:** `sawa/database/schema.py:drop_all_tables (SELECT tablename FROM pg_tables WHERE schemaname='public' ... DROP TABLE ... CASCADE); live tables turbulence_composite_scores, turbulence_regime_classifications, turbulence_volatility_metrics`
- **Evidence:** Live relations not defined in any sqlschema file: turbulence_composite_scores (1040 rows, date 2022-07-13..2026-09-02), turbulence_regime_classifications (1391 rows, 2021-02-19..2026-09-02), turbulence_volatility_metrics (0 rows); `select max(created_at) from turbulence_composite_scores` -> 2026-09-02 22:24:56 UTC (written yesterday). No reference in sawa/, mcp_server/, scripts/ or docs/ (grep -rn 'turbulence_' -> none). Writer found outside the repo: /home/seed/code/turbulence/src/turbulence/db_upload.py; crontab: `15 21 * * 1-5 /home/seed/code/turbulence/scripts/generate_chart.sh`. drop_all_tables enumerates every table in public and drops it with CASCADE; docs/MAINTENANCE.md:80-86 describes --drop-existing as wiping 'public tables' without mentioning that another application's tables live there.
- **Evidence:** The table is created ad hoc at scripts/quarantine_unadjustable_prices.py:104 `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS stock_prices_unadjustable_archive (...)`; grep across sqlschema/*.sql returns no definition, and sawa/database/schema.py:24-54 EXPECTED_TABLES does not list it (nor stock_prices_backup), so sawa/database/schema.py:337-339 verify_tables would not notice its absence. sawa/database/schema.py:275-291 drop_all_tables selects `tablename FROM pg_tables WHERE schemaname='public'` and issues `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS public.<t> CASCADE` for every row — it is a whitelist-free sweep, reached from sawa/cli.py:136 `drop_tables=args.drop_existing or schema_only`. Nothing in sqlschema/ then recreates either table. Live contents at risk: SELECT ticker,count(*),min(date),max(date),max(archived_at) FROM stock_prices_unadjustable_archive GROUP BY ticker -> 379 rows / 9 tickers (ADTX 124, MDIA 99, AEMD 77, R…
- **Impact:** A destructive sawa rebuild (documented for 'schema rebuilds') would silently delete another application's production data; conversely the external writer can add objects that sawa's verification and backups do not know about. The coupling is undocumented on the sawa side.
- **Recommendation:** Document the shared-database arrangement in docs/OPERATIONS.md/MAINTENANCE.md; make drop_all_tables drop only tables sawa owns (EXPECTED_TABLES + its own archive tables) or refuse when unknown tables are present without an explicit flag; longer term move the external project to its own schema/database.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Two reviewers found two victims of the same mechanism (sawa/database/schema.py drop_all_tables) with one fix: restrict the drop to EXPECTED_TABLES and add the archive to the manifest. Rated medium (one reviewer said low): a single documented command permanently destroys the only copy of deliberately quarantined data and another application's production data.

#### 41. MCP tools pass raw sic_description in a field named `sector` instead of routing through the GICS mapper with its 'Unclassified' fallback, so get_sector_performance silently drops 55% of the priced universe, list_sectors emits a NULL-named sector, and `sector` means two different taxonomies depending on which tool produced it (get_volume_leaders filters on GICS but reports SIC). *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** taxonomy
- **Reviewers:** companies-indices
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/movers.py:256 vs movers.py:282; mcp_server/tools/companies.py:97 and :182; mcp_server/tools/indices.py:157; mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:263`
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/sectors.py:51 and mcp_server/tools/sectors.py:111`
- **Evidence:** Both call sites pass `c.sic_description` as get_gics_sector's third argument rather than the function's own default: sectors.py:51 `get_gics_sector(c.ticker, c.sic_code, c.sic_description) as sector` and sectors.py:111 the same expression, which sectors.py:157 then guards with `AND {sector_expr} IS NOT NULL`. get_gics_sector ends in `RETURN COALESCE(v_sector, p_sic_desc)` (sqlschema/38_gics_function_v2.sql), so for the 5,678 active tickers with sic_code IS NULL and no override the result is SQL NULL, not a string. Running the exact list_sectors(gics) query: SELECT get_gics_sector(c.ticker,c.sic_code,c.sic_description) AS sector, COUNT(DISTINCT c.ticker) FROM companies c WHERE c.active GROUP BY sector ORDER BY 2 DESC -> the top row is an empty/NULL sector with 5,678 stocks, ahead of Financials (1,129). Running the get_sector_performance predicate: SELECT count(*), count(*) FILTER (WHERE g…
- **Evidence:** `grep -rn 'as sector' mcp_server/` shows 14 sites using `get_gics_sector(c.ticker, c.sic_code, c.sic_description) as sector` and 5 using `c.sic_description as sector`. The worst is get_volume_leaders: movers.py:256 filters `AND get_gics_sector(...) ILIKE %(sector)s` while movers.py:282 selects `c.sic_description as sector`. Reproducing that tool's shape: SELECT c.ticker, c.sic_description AS sector_returned, get_gics_sector(c.ticker,c.sic_code,c.sic_description) AS sector_filtered_on FROM companies c JOIN stock_prices p ON p.ticker=c.ticker AND p.date='2026-09-02' WHERE c.active AND get_gics_sector(...) ILIKE '%Information Technology%' ORDER BY p.volume*p.close DESC LIMIT 5 -> NVDA/MU/AVGO come back with sector_returned = 'SEMICONDUCTORS & RELATED DEVICES', DELL with 'ELECTRONIC COMPUTERS', SNDK with 'COMPUTER STORAGE DEVICES' — never the string the caller filtered by. Same split inside …
- **Impact:** get_sector_performance omits 5,490 of 10,000 priced tickers with no warning — the caller cannot tell an empty sector from an excluded one, and any breadth reading derived from it is computed on 45% of the universe. list_sectors returns a row whose `sector` key is null, which its own docstring promises is a "Sector name"; JSON consumers see `"sector": null` as the largest category. The two tools therefore disagree about the same expression, and both disagree with the documented intent.
- **Recommendation:** Pass `COALESCE(c.sic_description, 'Unclassified')` (or simply omit the third argument and let the function default apply) at both call sites, and drop the `IS NOT NULL` guard in get_sector_performance so the bucket is reported rather than discarded. That makes both tools match the behaviour the overhaul doc describes.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Confirmed both tools default to taxonomy='gics' and use get_gics_sector there but bare c.sic_description on the sic branch (mcp_server/tools/sectors.py:48-51, 110-115). One fix — send every sector-labelled field through the mapper — resolves the silent 55% drop, the NULL name and the cross-tool inconsistency, so the two reviewers' findings are one group.

#### 42. companies.active is never set false and delisted_utc is never populated, so ~250-280 delisted or halted tickers stay in the active universe and ~273 of them in index constituent lists. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** universe-coverage
- **Reviewers:** companies-indices, prices
- **Location:** `companies.active / companies.delisted_utc; demotion absent from sawa/weekly.py:70-76`
- **Location:** `companies.active / companies.delisted_utc; sawa/database/load.py:436 (COMPANY_COLUMNS); sawa/weekly.py:69-93`
- **Evidence:**   SELECT active, count(*) FROM companies GROUP BY 1;      -> t | 10430   (no false rows at all)
  SELECT count(*) FROM companies WHERE delisted_utc IS NOT NULL;  -> 0
grep across sawa/ and scripts/ finds no code that ever writes active=false or delisted_utc. The one place the signal is available is deliberately discarded — sawa/weekly.py:70-76 comments that a None from get_ticker_details is 'the provider's documented "no details for this ticker" answer ... ordinary for many ETFs and delisted symbols' and swallows it rather than demoting the row.

  -- active companies with no price in the last 10 trading days
  SELECT count(*) FROM companies c WHERE c.active AND EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM stock_prices p WHERE p.ticker=c.ticker)
    AND NOT EXISTS(SELECT 1 FROM stock_prices p WHERE p.ticker=c.ticker AND p.date >= DATE '2026-08-19');
   -> 280      (oldest last price: OST 2025-09-12, ~12 months …
- **Evidence:** SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE delisted_utc IS NOT NULL), count(*) FILTER (WHERE NOT active) FROM companies -> 0 and 0, out of 10,430 rows. Yet: WITH lastp AS (SELECT ticker, max(date) d FROM stock_prices GROUP BY ticker) SELECT count(*) FROM companies c LEFT JOIN lastp l USING(ticker) WHERE c.active AND (l.d IS NULL OR l.d < DATE '2026-08-03') -> 248 (240 of them are index constituents; 14 have not traded since 2025; 2 have no price row at all: SVA, USPE). Mechanism, confirmed end to end: SELECT (updated_at::date = '2026-09-02') AS refreshed, count(*), count(*) FILTER (stale 30d) FROM companies GROUP BY 1 -> refreshed=true: 10,128 rows / 13 stale; refreshed=false: 302 rows / 235 stale / 0 priced on 2026-09-02. The 302 skipped rows are exactly the ones Polygon no longer knows: GET /v3/reference/tickers/BK returns {"status":"NOT_FOUND"}. sawa/weekly.py:74-82 treats that None as an ordinary…
- **Impact:** The 'active universe' is permanently inflated with securities that no longer trade. Market-breadth, sector-performance and index-constituent queries count 273 non-trading tickers, and the S&P 500 constituent list is simultaneously wrong in both directions — it holds 5 dead entries and (per finding #2) is missing their replacements. Coverage ratios are computed against an inflated denominator, so price coverage reads ~96% on a perfect day and drifts down silently as more tickers die. The daily pipeline also keeps requesting all 280 every night.
- **Recommendation:** Treat a None from get_ticker_details at sawa/weekly.py:70-76 as the demotion signal it is: set active=false and stamp delisted_utc (from the provider's delisted_utc when present, else the ticker's last price date) instead of discarding it. Keep the historical rows — docs/TA_EXPANSION_PLAN.md:867 already specifies 'stop fetching new rows for inactive tickers but keep historical rows', which is the right behaviour and simply is not implemented. Prune demoted tickers from index_constituents, and add a doctor check that fails when an active company has had no price for more than ~10 trading days.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same defect (no demotion step in sawa/weekly.py:69-93); the counts differ (248 vs 280) because the reviewers used different delisting evidence. One rated high, one medium; medium is right — ~2.5% of the universe, affecting breadth and constituent counts but not serving wrong values for real securities. Distinct fix from the missing-new-listings defect below.

#### 43. Sparkline._sample plots a fixed stride that never reaches the end of the series, so a 252-day price chart silently omits its six most recent sessions and re-normalises the y-axis to the surviving subset, hiding the true high and low.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/core/sparkline.py:141-152 (_sample), used at sparkline.py:55-62`
- **Evidence:** _sample uses idx = int(i * len/width) for i in range(width), so the highest index reached is int((width-1)*len/width) < len-1; min/max for normalisation are computed at sparkline.py:60-62 AFTER sampling. Rendered BIAF, get_stock_prices('BIAF','2025-09-02','2026-09-02',limit=252) -> 252 rows, sparkline_width 40 (r2.py):
  chart: "Price | $111.15 ▇█▅▃...▁▁ $9.75 | ▼ -91.2%"
  plotted indices [0,6,12]...[233,239,245]; last plotted date = 2026-08-25 close 5.19; dropped tail = 2026-08-26,27,28,31, 2026-09-01, 2026-09-02
  sparkline min/max = 5.19/114.705 vs true min/max = 4.56/157.68
BIAF rallied 5.19 -> 9.75 (+88%) in exactly the six dropped sessions; that move is absent from the plotted bars while the label and trend beside it include it. RARE behaves identically (last plotted 2026-08-25). The effect is not limited to long ranges: whenever len(values) > width the final bar is dropped (a ~60…
- **Impact:** Every price sparkline ends up to six trading days stale and is scaled to a window that excludes the series extremes, so the visual trend contradicts the numbers printed on the same line, and a recent gap, crash or squeeze is invisible on the chart that is supposed to show it. Also affects economy time series (100 points into 40 chars drops the last two observations).
- **Recommendation:** Bucket-average or bucket-min/max the series into `width` bins instead of stride-picking, and always force the final bucket to include the last observation; normalise against the full series' min/max, not the sampled subset, so the extremes stay on-scale.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Distinct mechanism and fix from the NULL-compaction defect in the same file: this one drops the tail, that one closes gaps.

#### 44. Job openings are stored in thousands but the two economy renderers treat the column as a raw count, printing '0.0M' on one chart and '7.3K' on the other for 7.27 million openings.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/renderers/economy.py:331 (current / 1_000_000) and economy.py:456 (format_large_number)`
- **Evidence:** labor_market.job_openings for 2026-07-01 is 7271.00 (JOLTS level in thousands, from Polygon /fed/v1/labor-market per docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:248; 7,271 literal openings nationwide alongside a 4.1% unemployment rate is impossible). Rendered (r1.py):
  JSON: {'date': 2026-07-01, ..., 'job_openings': Decimal('7271.00')}
  labor market chart: "Job Openings          0.0M █▆▃▁▅▃▃▇▇▆▅ ▼ -5.1%"
  dashboard DETAILED chart (r5.py): "Job Openings               7.3K"
The suffix "M" at economy.py:319 asserts millions and the /1e6 at :331 asserts the column is a unit count; neither matches the stored scale.
- **Impact:** The single labor-demand number on both economy charts is wrong by a factor of 1000 in two different directions (0.0M vs 7.3K), while the JSON shows 7271. A reader of the chart cannot recover the real figure from either rendering.
- **Recommendation:** Multiply by 1000 once at the read boundary (or store the level in units) and format with format_large_number; drop the ad-hoc suffix=='M' branch. Same fix at both call sites.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Wrong by a factor of 1000 in two different directions, and the real figure is recoverable from neither rendering.

#### 45. The ratios chart carries the last non-NULL value of each metric forward and presents it as the current value for the header's end date, so a company whose latest P/E is NULL is shown with a stale P/E.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/renderers/ratios.py:57-58 (get_series drops NULLs per key) and :64 ("current": series[-1])`
- **Evidence:** 3,639 of 8,171 financial_ratios rows have a NULL price_to_earnings. Rendered get_financial_ratios('ACHC','2020-01-01','2026-09-03') (r4.py):
  JSON: 2026-02-13 pe 14.1300, eps 1.1900 | 2026-06-12 pe None, pb 1.1600, roe -0.5653, eps -12.0400
  chart: "ACHC Financial Ratios | 2026-02-13 → 2026-06-12" / "P/E Ratio   |       14.1"
The chart's own header says the series ends 2026-06-12, where P/E is NULL because the company swung to a -$12.04 EPS loss; the 14.1 shown is the 2026-02-13 value. The same carry-forward applies to every key, and each key's series is filtered independently, so different rows of one chart can be current as of different dates.
- **Impact:** A metric that is undefined (loss-making company, missing snapshot) is displayed as a live valuation with no marker, directly contradicting the null in the JSON row for the same date. Screening or commentary based on the chart will treat a loss-maker as trading at 14x earnings.
- **Recommendation:** Take "current" from the last row of `data` (the newest date), not from the last non-NULL of a per-key compaction, and render "--" when that row's value is NULL; if carry-forward is wanted, label it with the date it came from.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Directly contradicts the null in the JSON row for the same date, with no marker.

#### 46. NULL observations are dropped and the gap closed rather than broken, so sibling series stacked in one chart end up on different time axes and no longer line up by date.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/core/sparkline.py:52 (clean_values filter); callers economy.py:323, :163, :224, :283, :389, :401, :413, :460`
- **Evidence:** labor_market 2025-10-01 has NULL unemployment_rate and NULL labor_force_participation_rate (1 of 65 rows for each), with avg_hourly_earnings and job_openings populated. Rendered get_economy_data('labor_market','2025-09-01','2026-09-03') -> 11 monthly rows (r1.py):
  "Unemployment          4.1% ▆█▆▄▆▄▄▄▂▁ ▼ -6.8%"   (10 bars)
  "Participation        61.4% ██▇▅▄▄▃▃▁▁ ▼ -1.8%"   (10 bars)
  "Hourly Earnings     $37.62 ▁▂▃▃▄▅▅▆▇▇█ ▲ +2.5%"  (11 bars)
  "Job Openings          0.0M █▆▃▁▅▃▃▇▇▆▅ ▼ -5.1%"  (11 bars)
Bar 2 of the unemployment row is 2025-11-01 while bar 2 of the earnings row is 2025-10-01; the rows are visually presented as a single aligned grid over one "2025-09-01 → 2026-07-01" header.
- **Impact:** Cross-series reading of the chart ("unemployment fell while earnings rose in month X") is off by one period for any series with a gap, and the compaction is invisible - no marker, no warning, and the trend % is computed over a different span for each row.
- **Recommendation:** Render a break character (or repeat the previous level with a distinct glyph) for None instead of removing the point, so every series in one chart keeps the same length and index-to-date mapping; alternatively pass a shared date index into the sparkline.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. The cross-series reading the economy charts exist to support is off by one period for any series with a gap, invisibly.

#### 47. If COLUMNS is present in the server's environment, every chart collapses to a one-line COMPACT summary and a 12-line ANSI 'TERMINAL TOO NARROW' modal is injected into the tool response's warnings array.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/config.py:59-67 (get_width via shutil.get_terminal_size), core/layout.py:96-99 (auto-downgrade), core/modal.py:8-119, wired at mcp_server/server.py:159-163 and 2622-2623`
- **Evidence:** Reproduced: `COLUMNS=80 uv run python -c "...get_chart_config(); get_layout(c); check_width_and_warn(...)"` prints "width 80 detail ChartDetail.COMPACT spark 25" and "warning? True" followed by the boxed "⚠  TERMINAL TOO NARROW" modal with a 24-bit colour escape (\x1b[38;2;255;179;71m). Without COLUMNS the pipe path falls back to 100 and NORMAL. The COMPACT price chart drops the date range, volume, high/low and the OHLC summary entirely (prices.py:114-149).
- **Impact:** The MCP server has no terminal; a client that exports COLUMNS (tmux, a wrapper script, some launchers) loses most of every chart's content and gets a resize instruction rendered with raw ANSI in a JSON warnings array intended for the model.
- **Recommendation:** Do not auto-detect terminal size in a stdio server: default to the configured width (MCP_CHART_WIDTH / config file) and never emit the width modal unless the caller explicitly asked for a narrower layout.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. The MCP server has no terminal; any client that exports COLUMNS loses most of every chart and receives raw ANSI in JSON.

#### 48. --load-only warns and continues when cached fundamentals/ratios reference tickers with no companies row, but the strict eligibility filter then raises ValueError, so the run crashes after prices were committed; the current cache has 80 such tickers.

- **Theme:** coldstart-loader
- **Reviewers:** coldstart-loaders
- **Location:** `sawa/coldstart.py:1226-1239 (warn-and-continue) vs sawa/database/load.py:274-279 (strict raise) reached via load_fundamentals/_load_fundamentals_file:796-804 and load_ratios:680-688`
- **Evidence:** coldstart.py:1231-1239 logs 'Found N cached-data tickers with no company row ... run a download workflow' and proceeds to load_fundamentals(conn, fundamentals_dir, logger, valid_tickers) and load_ratios(..., valid_tickers). Both call load_csv_to_table(strict=True, valid_tickers=...), where `effective_strict and skipped_rows` -> `raise ValueError('Rejected {skipped}/{source} {table} source row(s) during eligibility filtering')` (load.py:274-279). Live check of the cache: 4748 distinct tickers in data/fundamentals/*.csv + data/ratios/ratios.csv, 80 absent from companies (AADI, ABC, ABDW, ACRX, ACST, ADES, AGLE, AJX, ALCC, ...). The exception escapes run_coldstart (:1973-1977), after companies and prices were already committed. test_strict_ratio_loader_rejects_filtered_rows_before_writing documents the strict behaviour for fresh artifacts, but no test covers the offline path's warning.
- **Impact:** The offline load never reaches ratios/economy for a cache produced by an earlier, larger universe; the operator sees a stack trace instead of the degraded outcome the warning promises.
- **Recommendation:** In load-only/skip-downloads mode either pass strict=False for cached fundamentals/ratios and record skipped rows as a degraded reason, or perform the ticker check before any write and fail fast with a clear message. Add a test for the offline path with one unknown ticker.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Same warn-versus-raise inconsistency family as the load_prices FK crash but a different code path and a different fix; the operator gets a stack trace instead of the degraded outcome the warning promises.

#### 49. refresh_split_registry reports success while run_corporate_actions_update drops tracked fractional-ratio splits that Polygon does apply to REST bars, so flat-file bars for those tickers are loaded on a mixed basis and the outcome hides the skip count.

- **Theme:** coldstart-loader
- **Reviewers:** coldstart-loaders
- **Location:** `sawa/coldstart.py:218-263 (refresh_split_registry outcome omits splits_fractional_skipped) and sawa/corporate_actions.py:384-399 (fractional records skipped for tracked tickers)`
- **Evidence:** corporate_actions.py:387-399: after _tracked_only, records with non-integer split_from/split_to are excluded and only logged ('Skipped N split(s) with non-integer share ratios'); stats['splits_fractional_skipped'] is set but refresh_split_registry copies only success/splits_fetched/splits_loaded/errors (coldstart.py:248-255) and coldstart never adds a degraded reason. Weekly logs 2026-09-02 02:21 and 03:33: 'Found 1755 splits', 'Ignored 934 split(s) for untracked tickers', 'Skipped 38 split(s) with non-integer share ratios' (38 tracked tickers in a one-year window; 224 in the 2026-09-01 runs). Polygon sample (trailing year, 1000 rows): 112 fractional records, 18 for tracked tickers with stored history (SF 1:1.5, SCCO 1:1.012, TRI 1.015682:1, PHG 1:1.037, MOVE 1:1.358, AUUD 7.7:1, AKAN 4.5:1, SMX 2.285:1, ...). Polygon's REST series does apply them: SF closes 78.48 (2026-02-26) -> 74.05 (…
- **Impact:** Every coldstart universe that contains a ticker with a fractional ratio change (ADR ratio changes, reorganisations, 3:2-style splits Polygon encodes as 1.5) is loaded on a mixed basis for that ticker without any degraded signal.
- **Recommendation:** Surface splits_fractional_skipped (and the tickers) in refresh_split_registry's outcome and add a degraded reason; store exact ratios (NUMERIC columns or a Fraction-friendly representation) so SplitAdjuster can apply them, or exclude such tickers' flat-file bars from the load and fetch them via REST like add-symbol does.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Shares the 'fractional/small ratios ignored' theme with the AEG/IBM/MMM group but is a different code path (coldstart registry + corporate_actions.py:384-399) with its own fix — surface splits_fractional_skipped and stop dropping the records.

#### 50. The pre-horizon continuity guard cannot see a tail that is off by any ratio inside (0.34, 3), so RUSHA/RUSHB's 1.5x-stale pre-2021-09-02 rows are reported as 'already adjusted' on every run instead of being re-based.

- **Theme:** corporate-actions
- **Reviewers:** weekly-corporate-splits
- **Location:** `sawa/split_adjust.py:43 (BASIS_CONTINUITY_BOUNDS), sawa/split_adjust.py:227-229 and 257-259 (plan_pre_horizon_rebases)`
- **Evidence:** Live: `SELECT date, open, close FROM stock_prices WHERE ticker='RUSHB' AND date BETWEEN '2021-08-27' AND '2021-09-08'` -> 2021-09-01 close 29.13330000, 2021-09-02 open 19.34670000 close 19.39550000 (step 1.502); stock_splits RUSHB: 3:2 @2023-08-29, 3:2 @2026-09-01. RUSHA identical (29.3467 -> 19.5555, 1.5007). 137 pre-horizon rows each. Generalized detector (horizon step vs product of last k post-tail splits within 3%) returns exactly RUSHA and RUSHB. Origin: logs/daily_20260902_210002.log:11341 "18630 stored date(s) predate the provider's available history (earliest 2021-09-03) and keep their existing basis; the adjustable range is re-based" (HEAD code upserted the window only). Reproduction with the working-tree code: stored window rows == fetched (factor 1), tail close 100.4 vs boundary 50 (2x) -> plan_pre_horizon_rebases returns ([], ['X'], {}) i.e. already_adjusted; only at 4x does …
- **Impact:** 274 rows (RUSHA+RUSHB) are served 1.5x too high before 2021-09-02 and their SMA/EMA-200 are contaminated into 2022; the new stats bucket them under pre_horizon_dates_already_adjusted, so the run reports success. Every common forward split ratio (2:1, 3:1, 3:2, 5:4, 4:3) and 1:2 reverse falls inside the guard; only >=4:1 / <=1:3 are caught. scripts/quarantine_unadjustable_prices.py uses the same (0.34, 3) bounds and is equally blind, so today's repair also missed RUSHA/RUSHB.
- **Recommendation:** Do not infer 'same basis' from the stored window: compute the expected tail factor from stock_splits (products of the last k recorded splits executing after the tail's last date, k=1..n) and compare tail_close / fetched boundary close against 1 and against each product; re-base when it matches a product != 1, treat ~1 as adjusted, and skip+report anything else. Add a test with a 2:1 tail-vs-window discontinuity. Immediately fix RUSHA/RUSHB (rows < 2021-09-02 divided by 1.5, volume x1.5, then recompute TA) and re-run the horizon scan with the new detector before Saturday's weekly.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique, in the uncommitted sawa/split_adjust.py that is explicitly in scope. The guard's bounds make the most common split ratios exactly the ones it cannot detect, and the new stats bucket the miss under a success label.

#### 51. raw_split_jump_present has no ratio floor or tolerance and compares close-to-close, so a 21:20 stock dividend fires on a -2.4% day and a correctly adjusted series with a genuine spike (HUIZ) is classified as unapplied.

- **Theme:** corporate-actions
- **Reviewers:** weekly-corporate-splits
- **Location:** `sawa/split_adjust.py:133-139 (raw_split_jump_present), 246-256 (in-tail plans appended without any cross-check)`
- **Evidence:** Thresholds from the formula abs(step-raw) < abs(step): ratio 21/20 fires when next/prev < 0.9759 (-2.4%), 11/10 at -4.7%, 5/4 at -10.6%, 3/2 at -18.4%, 2 at -29.3%, 1/2 at +41.4%. Live provider-served HUIZ (re-fetched today): 2024-12-06 close 2.70040000, 2024-12-09 open 2.67000000 (continuous, i.e. the 1:5 is applied) but close 6.06000000; the code's criterion on closes: |ln(6.06/2.7004) - ln 5| = 0.80 < 0.808 -> True. Scan of all 2,139 non-trivial splits with the exact criterion: 13 fire today, 11 of them small-ratio (0.8333..1.2) which docs/OPERATIONS.md's query deliberately excludes with `f >= 1.2 OR f <= 0.8333` while the code does not. For Saturday's run 0 of the 17 in-range candidates fire, so no immediate damage.
- **Impact:** A false positive multiplies every row before the execution date by the split ratio inside the committed refresh transaction with no post-check, silently corrupting the tail; because every daily/weekly run re-plans (finding 3) any borderline case is re-tried until it fires. Exposure grows as the provider horizon moves forward past more recorded splits (IBM's 1046:1000 enters the tail range in ~2 months).
- **Recommendation:** Use the execution-date open (or a median of a few bars on each side) against the previous close, require the measured step to be within a tolerance of the raw ratio (e.g. |ln(step)-ln(1/f)| <= 0.1) rather than merely 'closer than zero', skip |ln f| below a floor unless the match is tight, and after the UPDATE verify the step across the split is now ~1 before commit. Add tests for 21:20 and for the HUIZ-style spike.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique, in the uncommitted split_adjust.py. Re-rated from low to medium: a false positive multiplies every row before the execution date inside the committed refresh transaction with no post-check, so the failure mode is silent corruption of a good tail — and every run re-plans it.

#### 52. When the split self-heal fails, daily still runs the incremental TA step over the now mixed-basis price series for the freshly split tickers, persists those indicators, exits 0 and marks the day done.

- **Theme:** daily-pipeline
- **Reviewers:** daily-pipeline
- **Location:** `sawa/daily.py:907-924 (heal exception swallowed), sawa/daily.py:1051-1122 (TA step has no knowledge of heal failure), sawa/daily.py:1315-1321 (only prices_error is fatal)`
- **Evidence:** Code: _heal_splits_in_window raises RuntimeError -> caught at daily.py:921-924 into stats['split_heal_error'] only; ta_recompute_from_by_ticker (filled at daily.py:801-806 from the 14-day overlap upsert) still drives the TA step, which for every split ticker computes indicators from recompute_from-1280d over history where only the last 14 days are on the post-split basis; stats['success'] = not fatal_reasons ignores split_heal_error. Observed 2026-09-01: logs/daily_20260901_210002.log:11343 'Daily split self-heal failed ... (0/629 tickers)' at 21:37:28; then 'Calculating technical indicators...' and 11461 'Calculated 120677 indicator records' at 22:06:59; 11481-11487 'DAILY UPDATE COMPLETE (DEGRADED) ... DEGRADED: split adjustment/TA self-heal failed'. scheduler.log 8996 '[2026-09-01 18:07:12 ET] Daily completed: Latest prices: 2026-09-01'; ~/.sawa/scheduler/daily_done_2026-09-01 exists.…
- **Impact:** For ~24 hours the MCP served SMA/EMA/Bollinger/ATR values for the day's split tickers computed over a series that mixes pre- and post-split bases (e.g. SMA-200 for a 12:1 reverse split ~11x too high), while the job reported success and the scheduler stopped retrying. Self-corrects only if the next day's heal succeeds.
- **Recommendation:** When the heal fails, either exclude the affected split_tickers from the incremental TA step for that run (leave their TA at the previous day rather than persisting mixed-basis rows) or treat heal failure for tickers that split inside the window as fatal so the scheduler retries. At minimum record the affected tickers in stats and the DEGRADED notification.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique, and independent of the heal-window defect: this is the swallowed exception at daily.py:907-924 plus the fatal-error gate at :1315-1321. Serves an SMA-200 ~11x too high for a 12:1 reverse split for ~24 hours while reporting success.

#### 53. Default autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor 0.2 lets stock_prices accumulate 1.88M dead tuples (18.7%) and go 17 days unvacuumed, decaying the visibility map to 71.2% and costing 2.23M random heap fetches on a single full-universe read.

- **Theme:** db-tuning
- **Reviewers:** db-health-concurrency
- **Location:** `pg_settings.autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor (default 0.2) / stock_prices, technical_indicators`
- **Evidence:** pg_settings: autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor=0.2, autovacuum_vacuum_threshold=50, both source='default'; pg_class.reloptions is NULL for every table in public (no per-table override). Computed trigger: stock_prices needs 50+0.2*10,050,067 = 2,010,063 dead tuples, technical_indicators 2,003,721. pg_stat_user_tables now: stock_prices n_live_tup 10,050,067 / n_dead_tup 1,882,376 (18.7%), last_autovacuum 2026-08-17 22:58 = 16 days 23:04 ago, autovacuum_count 5, vacuum_count 0, n_tup_upd 9,315,543 of which only 2,996,403 (32%) HOT; technical_indicators 10,020,642 / 1,579,531 (15.8%), last_autovacuum 2026-09-02 03:44. pg_class visibility map: stock_prices relallvisible 87,266 / relpages 122,608 = 71.2%; technical_indicators 261,355 / 363,472 = 71.9%. Measured cost -- EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) on `WITH latest AS (SELECT DISTINCT ON (ticker) ticker,date,close FROM stock_prices ORDER BY ticker, d…
- **Impact:** Every index-only scan over the price or indicator history pays 20-25% extra random heap I/O, so the MCP tools that walk the whole universe (get_index_with_prices, screen_stocks, get_market_breadth, get_ytd_returns) burn a large slice of their 30 s statement budget on vacuum debt rather than work. It also makes the nightly job slower: the ~500k-row price rewrite and the 628-ticker TA delete/insert are appended to a heap that is one-fifth dead, and read-only queries end up doing the writeback. Because the threshold is proportional, it never improves as the tables grow -- at 20M rows stock_prices
- **Recommendation:** Set per-table storage parameters on the two 10M-row tables, e.g. `ALTER TABLE stock_prices SET (autovacuum_vacuum_scale_factor=0.01, autovacuum_analyze_scale_factor=0.02, autovacuum_vacuum_cost_limit=2000, fillfactor=90)` and the same on technical_indicators, so autovacuum fires around 100k dead tuples instead of 2M and the update-heavy price loader gets more HOT updates. Run a one-off `VACUUM (ANALYZE)` on both after the change. Add a doctor check on n_dead_tup/n_live_tup and age(last_autovacuum).
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Per-table autovacuum settings are the fix; separate from the server memory defaults below.

#### 54. The server runs on postgres:18 image defaults — shared_buffers 128 MB, work_mem 4 MB, effective_cache_size 4 GB — on a 23 GiB host with a 7.2 GB database, giving a 90.3% cache hit rate, 2.06 TB read from the OS and 69 GB of temp-file spill.

- **Theme:** db-tuning
- **Reviewers:** db-health-concurrency
- **Location:** `/var/lib/postgresql/18/docker/postgresql.conf (container stocksdb) / pg_settings`
- **Evidence:** The container's postgresql.conf is the unmodified image default: `shared_buffers = 128MB`, `max_connections = 100`, `max_wal_size = 1GB`, `min_wal_size = 80MB` and nothing else. Every other memory GUC reports source='default' in pg_settings: work_mem 4096 kB, maintenance_work_mem 65536 kB, effective_cache_size 524288*8kB = 4 GB, random_page_cost 4, default_statistics_target 100. postgresql.auto.conf contains no ALTER SYSTEM overrides and pg_db_role_setting is empty. Host capacity: `free -h` shows 23 GiB total with 15 GiB in buff/cache and 21 GiB available; `nproc` = 4; the quadlet /home/seed/.config/containers/systemd/stocksdb.container sets no memory limit. Database size 7234 MB (all public relations 9385 MB), so shared_buffers covers 1.8% of it. Resulting counters from pg_stat_database for 'postgres' since 2026-06-04: blks_read 270,676,032 (2.06 TB) vs blks_hit 2,511,396,793 -> cache_h…
- **Impact:** Nothing stays cached between queries, so the read path re-reads from the page cache (and the WAL/heap writeback path competes with it) on every call; effective_cache_size=4 GB also makes the planner under-value index paths on a machine that really has ~16 GiB of cache. work_mem=4MB forces external merge sorts for the screener and window-function shapes, which is where the 69 GB of temp bytes comes from. This is the largest single lever on the 3-second full-universe query times and on the nightly job's runtime, and it silently caps headroom as the tables grow.
- **Recommendation:** Set, via a mounted conf or ALTER SYSTEM plus a restart: shared_buffers 6GB, effective_cache_size 16GB, work_mem 32MB (with max_connections 100 the worst case is bounded well under RAM), maintenance_work_mem 1GB, random_page_cost 1.1 (SSD), default_statistics_target 200. Re-check pg_stat_database cache_hit_pct and temp_bytes afterwards. Pin the settings in the quadlet or a versioned postgresql.conf so a container recreate does not silently revert to image defaults again.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Different fix from autovacuum tuning; also the reason several read-path inefficiencies in this audit hurt more than they otherwise would.

#### 55. 138 duplicate fiscal-period groups exist across income_statements, balance_sheets and cash_flows because the primary key is period_end-based while the loader's completeness check counts rows rather than identities, so get_fundamentals(timeframe='annual') can return two different FY2022 rows for one ticker.

- **Theme:** fundamentals
- **Reviewers:** fundamentals-tables
- **Location:** `income_statements / balance_sheets / cash_flows; check at sawa/database/load.py:104-125 (require_complete_persistence) and upsert at sawa/database/load.py:353-360`
- **Evidence:** Grouping by (ticker, fiscal_year, fiscal_quarter, timeframe): income_statements 58 groups / 116 rows, cash_flows 45 / 90, balance_sheets 35 / 70. Example — COO annual, both labelled fiscal_year 2022 fiscal_quarter 4:
  period_end 2022-01-29 filing_date 2024-03-25 revenue 3,196,583,000 diluted_eps 2.00
  period_end 2022-10-31 filing_date 2024-12-06 revenue 3,308,400,000 diluted_eps 1.94
The PK is (ticker, period_end, timeframe), so two rows for one fiscal period never conflict. require_complete_persistence only compares source_rows / inserted / expected_rows; _insert_rows counts every successful execute, and an ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE that silently overwrites an existing row still counts as 1, so a CSV containing two rows for the same PK passes every assertion while only the LAST one survives — and the upsert at load.py:353-360 is `col = EXCLUDED.col` with no filing_date precedence, so 'las…
- **Impact:** get_fundamentals(timeframe='annual', limit=20) returns two 'FY2022' rows for the same ticker with different revenue, and any fiscal-period-keyed consumer double-counts. The loader cannot detect this class of problem at all, so a same-run PK collision silently keeps an arbitrary row.
- **Recommendation:** Have require_complete_persistence assert that the CSV contains no duplicate primary keys (count distinct PK tuples == source_rows) and raise otherwise. Add an ORDER BY filing_date so that when a collision does occur the newest filing is written last. Add a doctor check for duplicate (ticker, fiscal_year, fiscal_quarter, timeframe) groups.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Distinct from the wrong-ticker binding defect: this is a key-design problem that the loader is structurally unable to detect.

#### 56. 2,675 income-statement rows are empty shells that store cost_of_revenue and ebitda as 0.00 while every other line is NULL, and those zeros are served as real values.

- **Theme:** fundamentals
- **Reviewers:** fundamentals-tables
- **Location:** `income_statements (timeframe='quarterly'); served by mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py:82-140 and mcp_server/charts/renderers/fundamentals.py:282-284`
- **Evidence:** SELECT over income_statements WHERE timeframe='quarterly': 8,383 of 70,232 rows have revenue NULL; of those, 8,262 still carry cost_of_revenue = 0.00 and 2,675 (787 tickers) carry ebitda = 0.00. Sample rows (period_end 2026-03-31): NGG, LSPD, JHX, HIVE all have revenue NULL, gross_profit NULL, operating_income NULL, net_income NULL — but cost_of_revenue 0.00 and ebitda 0.00. Overall ebitda is NULL in 0.0% of quarterly rows and exactly 0 in 3.9%, i.e. the missing-value marker for that column is a literal zero, not NULL. mcp_server/charts/renderers/fundamentals.py:282-284 compounds this on the balance-sheet chart with `latest.get("total_assets", 0) or 0`, drawing a NULL balance sheet as a zero one.
- **Impact:** get_fundamentals returns 'ebitda: 0.00' and 'cost_of_revenue: 0.00' for 2,675 periods where the true values are unknown, which reads as a real business fact (zero EBITDA) rather than missing data, and any EV/EBITDA or margin computation downstream divides by or compares against a fabricated zero.
- **Recommendation:** At the storage boundary in _load_fundamentals_file, treat a row whose revenue and net income are both absent as unusable and normalise its remaining 0.00 markers to NULL, or add a loader rule that a 0 in cost_of_revenue/ebitda accompanied by NULL revenue is stored as NULL. Remove the `or 0` coercion in the chart renderer.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. 'ebitda: 0.00' reads as a business fact rather than missing data, and any EV/EBITDA or margin computed from it is wrong rather than absent.

#### 57. The daily incremental TA block overwrites the trailing ~14 days of every ticker's series with bounded-window values, undoing the full-history split recompute performed earlier in the same run and re-injecting the exact EMA discontinuity that _heal_splits_in_window exists to remove.

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** indicators
- **Location:** `sawa/daily.py:806 (pending_ta_recompute_from) -> sawa/daily.py:1075, 1106 (bounded window + _ta_rows_to_persist), vs sawa/daily.py:914 (_heal_splits_in_window full recompute)`
- **Evidence:** Code path: daily.py:800-806 sets pending_ta_recompute_from[ticker] = the earliest price date actually fetched, i.e. the start of the 14-day repair overlap. daily.py:1067 then computes recompute_from = _effective_ta_recompute_from(last_ta, repair_from) = min(repair_from, last_ta+1d) = repair_from; daily.py:1075 sets price_start = recompute_from - 1280 calendar days (get_required_lookback_days() == 1280, verified by running it); daily.py:1106 persists every row with date >= recompute_from. _heal_splits_in_window (daily.py:914, calling recompute_ta_for_tickers at daily.py:511) runs EARLIER in the same run and writes a correct full-history series, which the later block then partially overwrites.
Data evidence from the 2026-09-02 run: logs/daily_20260902_210002.log:2879 shows `GET .../aggs/ticker/EJH/range/1/day/2026-08-18/2026-09-02`, and line 11443 shows `Recomputing technical indicators fo…
- **Impact:** 23% of the tickers whose TA was just fully recomputed for a split come out of the same daily run with a hard discontinuity in ema_100/ema_200 at the 14-day boundary — the exact defect _heal_splits_in_window's docstring says it exists to prevent. Crossover/trend tools reading ema_200 see a phantom 40x gap with no corresponding price move.
- **Recommendation:** Have the split-heal step record the tickers it fully recomputed and skip them in the incremental TA block for that run (or set their recompute_from to None so the strictly-incremental path applies). More generally, when recompute_from is older than last_ta, fetch prices from the ticker's first date rather than recompute_from - 1280 days, so an overwrite is always full-history-consistent.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Opposite direction to the 'revisions outside the window are never recomputed' defect below — here good full-history values are clobbered by bounded-window ones — so a separate fix (order the steps, or exclude just-healed tickers from the incremental pass).

#### 58. EMA-200/100/50, RSI-21 and ADX rows for 2026-05-15..2026-06-15 still carry sliding-window seed drift from the pre-2026-06-13 short warm-up and were never recomputed; ~1,500 tickers/day are off by >0.1% and some by >5%.

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** ta-engine
- **Location:** `technical_indicators.ema_200 (also ema_100, ema_50, rsi_21, adx_14) for dates 2026-05-15..2026-06-15; producer sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:334-362 (EMA_WARMUP_MULTIPLIER introduced 9be3ee8 2026-06-13)`
- **Evidence:** EMA-200 recurrence violation |ema[d] - (ema[d-1] + (close-ema[d-1])*2/201)| / ema[d-1] by date over stored rows: avg 0.028-0.030 bp and 0 tickers >10 bp for every date 2026-04-16..05-14; 2026-05-15 avg 189.6 bp, p99 2121 bp, 3,693 tickers >10 bp; 05-18 avg 92 bp (4,856 >10 bp); 05-19..06-12 avg 5.5-6.1 bp with 1,421-1,650 tickers/day >10 bp; 06-15 avg 511.7 bp (7,486 >10 bp); from 06-16 avg 0.09-0.26 bp; from 08-19 avg 0.031-0.034 bp, 0 >10 bp. Full recompute vs stored: SYNA 2026-06-12 ema_200 stored 84.9649 vs full 89.6744 (-5.3%); FCFS 2026-06-09 177.8201 vs 182.6205; AAPL 2026-06-03 264.8766 vs 263.2749; SCHL 2026-05-15 31.6447 vs 32.6535; ema_100 AAPL 2026-05-15 266.4180 vs 266.9819; rsi_21 diffs up to 0.00135, adx_14 up to 0.000144 in the same window. Additionally 234 tickers have sma_200/ema_200 NULL for 2026-05-15..2026-06-12 despite >=200 prior bars (e.g. NSYS 1365 bars, 219 NULL…
- **Impact:** Trend/crossover analyses (price vs EMA-200, EMA-50/200 alignment, get_multi_timeframe_alignment, detect_crossovers) over mid-May to mid-June 2026 use EMA values off by up to several percent for hundreds of tickers, and report 'insufficient data' NULLs for 234 tickers that had ample history. Rows before 05-15 and after 06-15 are fine, so the series has two visible discontinuities.
- **Recommendation:** Same remedy as finding 1: one full `sawa ta-backfill`. Consider a doctor check that the stored EMA-200 recurrence residual stays below ~1 bp on sampled tickers, which would have flagged both the 05-15 and 06-15 boundaries.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Same shape as the OBV/VWAP group (a landed code fix with no historical backfill) but different columns, different cause and a different date range, so a separate repair.

#### 59. No early-close or holiday calendar exists anywhere in the intraday path: REGULAR_SESSION_CLOSE, the live view and the MCP session filters are hard-coded to 16:00 and market_hours.is_market_open() knows no holidays.

- **Theme:** intraday-stream
- **Reviewers:** intraday-live-code
- **Location:** `sawa/api/websocket_client.py:44-45,486; sawa/utils/market_hours.py:66-84; sqlschema/45_intraday_bar_size_identity.sql:339-340,400-401; mcp_server/tools/market_data.py:736-737,767-768; scripts/market_scheduler.sh:338-357 (time-based fallback)`
- **Evidence:** grep -rn 'holiday|early_close|early close|13:00|half.day|EARLY_CLOSE' sawa/ returns only unrelated comments (daily.py:771,778, s3.py, ta_engine.py); nothing models 13:00 closes (2026-11-27, 2026-12-24 etc.). The Polygon minute stream is the same aggregate series as REST: the 2026-09-02 AAPL 1-minute response contains 845 bars spanning 04:00-20:00 ET, i.e. after-hours minutes are delivered and only the client's fixed 09:30-16:00 filter keeps them out. The scheduler's primary path uses Polygon /v1/marketstatus/now (which does know early closes) but its fallback (:338-357) and the MCP hint (server.py:2648) use weekday+time only. Today the scheduler's immediate stop at 'closed' (Findings 1/2) masks the problem; the recommended fix for Finding 2 (run past close) would expose it.
- **Impact:** On an early-close day, once the stream runs past 13:00, post-close prints 13:00-16:00 would be folded into 'regular-session' 5-minute bars and into the live daily candle's high/low/close/volume (wrong OHLCV served by get_intraday_bars / stock_prices_live for the whole afternoon). is_market_open() also reports 'market open' on weekday holidays, mis-firing the MCP hint.
- **Recommendation:** Introduce a small NYSE calendar (holidays + 13:00 early closes) in sawa/utils/market_hours.py and use it for the session close in the websocket client (per market date), in is_market_open(), and expose the session close to the view/MCP filters (e.g. a session_close column or a calendar table the view joins). Add tests for an early-close date.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique, and latent today only because the stream is stopped early; once the stop-timing defect is fixed, post-close prints on an early-close day would be folded into regular-session bars and into the live daily candle's OHLCV.

#### 60. get_index_with_prices recomputes a latest-price row for all 9,240 tickers on every call, reading 8.9M index entries and ~1M buffer pages to return 8 rows.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** companies-indices
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/indices.py:144-152`
- **Evidence:** The `latest_prices` CTE is `SELECT DISTINCT ON (ticker) ticker, date, close, volume FROM stock_prices ORDER BY ticker, date DESC` with no date filter and no correlation to the requested index, so it is fully materialized before the join. EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS) of the tool's exact query for code='mag7': `Index Scan using idx_stock_prices_ticker_date on stock_prices (actual rows=8896290) Buffers: shared hit=886419 read=160505` feeding a Materialize that the outer nested loop rescans 8 times; total `Buffers: shared hit=886431 read=161839`, `Execution Time: 3016.331 ms`, `actual rows=8`. The same unbounded DISTINCT ON pattern appears in mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:245-253.
- **Impact:** An 8-row lookup reads 8.9M index entries and touches over a million buffer pages, evicting the shared buffer cache that the price and indicator tools depend on. The cost is identical for mag7 and us_active, and it grows with total history rather than with the requested index. A `get_index_with_prices('us_active')` call has the same profile. The tool also reads `stock_prices` rather than `stock_prices_live`, so during market hours it returns EOD closes while sibling tools return live ones.
- **Recommendation:** Restrict the CTE to the constituents actually requested and to a bounded date window — join index_constituents/indices inside the CTE and add `WHERE date >= (SELECT max(date) FROM stock_prices) - INTERVAL '10 days'` — or use the same `get_price_date_refs()` bind-parameter pattern the other tools adopted after the stock_prices_live drift fix. Switch the source to stock_prices_live for consistency with the rest of the tool surface.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Results are correct, so not high; kept at medium rather than low because it evicts the shared buffer cache that every price and indicator tool depends on, on a server configured with 128 MB shared_buffers.

#### 61. get_market_breadth counts tickers with NULL sma_50/sma_200 as 'below' the moving average while keeping them in the denominator, biasing pct_above_200dma down by roughly 5 points.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/movers.py:375-376 (CASE WHEN p_now.close > ti.sma_200 THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) and :403-406 (SUM/COUNT(*))`
- **Evidence:** SQL on 2026-09-02 (active tickers with both price rows): total 9948, sma_200 NULL 875, sma_50 NULL 3; above_200 = 5154. Tool output (in-process get_market_breadth()): above_200dma 5154, total 9948, pct_above_200dma 51.8. Correct share among tickers that have an SMA-200: 5154/9073 = 56.8%.
- **Impact:** Breadth percentages are biased downward by ~5 points on the 200-day measure (more when many recent listings exist); consumers comparing to standard breadth thresholds get a wrong regime read.
- **Recommendation:** Use COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE ti.sma_200 IS NOT NULL) as the denominator for pct_above_200dma (and likewise for sma_50), and expose the count of tickers lacking the SMA.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Systematic one-directional bias in a number read against standard regime thresholds.

#### 62. All YTD returns use the close of the first trading day of the year as the base instead of the prior year-end close, omitting the first session's move, and the convention is documented nowhere.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/_dates.py:44-45 (ytd_start = MIN(date) >= DATE_TRUNC('year')), screener.py:524 (get_ytd_returns), scanner.py:331/246-255 (scan_ytd_performance start=Jan 1)`
- **Evidence:** SQL: SPY close 2025-12-31 = 681.92, 2026-01-02 = 683.17. _dates refs live: ytd_start = 2026-01-02. Every YTD figure (get_top_movers period=ytd, get_sector_performance return_ytd, screen_stocks change_ytd, get_ytd_returns, scan_ytd_performance) is therefore price/683.17-1 rather than price/681.92-1 (SPY base differs by 0.18%; single stocks by their Jan-2 move).
- **Impact:** YTD numbers systematically differ from the standard definition used by brokers/index providers; for stocks with a large Jan-2 move the discrepancy is material. Not documented anywhere in docs/ as a deliberate convention.
- **Recommendation:** Define ytd_start as MAX(date) < DATE_TRUNC('year', today) (last trading day of prior year) in _dates.py and the two other sites, or document the first-trading-day convention explicitly in tool descriptions.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Reviewer rated high; re-rated medium under the stated rubric — this is a convention choice whose typical error is one session's move (usually well under 1%), though it is systematic across get_ytd_returns, screen_stocks and scan_ytd_performance and materially wrong for stocks with a large Jan-2 gap.

#### 63. get_momentum_indicators and get_squeeze_indicators use 'N PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW' windows, so every N-period indicator (Stochastic %K/%D, Williams %R, ADX smoothing, squeeze momentum) is computed over N+1 bars.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/momentum.py:324-340 (stoch_k/williams 14 PRECEDING), :356-367 and :428-432 (adx_period 14 PRECEDING), :409-412 and :437-442 (stoch_d 3 PRECEDING), :129-135 (mom_period 12 PRECEDING)`
- **Evidence:** SQL on AAPL: COUNT(*) OVER (ROWS BETWEEN 14 PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW) = 15. 2026-09-02: 15-row lowest low 302.05 vs 14-row 302.939 -> stoch_k 86.94 (tool) vs 86.49 (true 14-period); 2026-09-01: 91.88 vs 91.41. Over the last 250 AAPL sessions the 15-row and 14-row high/low windows differ on 77 days.
- **Impact:** Reported %K/%D, Williams %R, +DI/-DI/ADX and squeeze momentum deviate from their stated periods (STOCHASTIC_K_PERIOD=14 etc.) on roughly a third of days; overbought/oversold signals near the 20/80 thresholds can flip.
- **Recommendation:** Use ROWS BETWEEN (period - 1) PRECEDING AND CURRENT ROW (bind period-1) for all rolling windows; add a test asserting a 14-row window against a hand-computed value.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Off-by-one against the stated periods on roughly a third of days, enough to flip overbought/oversold signals near the 20/80 thresholds.

#### 64. During the trading session, live-anchored tools join only tickers that already have an intraday bar today, silently omitting 1,186 of 10,000 EOD tickers with no fallback to their last EOD row.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/movers.py:128-129, sectors.py:151, screener.py:447/544-545/843, screener.py:643-646 (JOIN stock_prices_live p_now ON p_now.date = %(latest)s where latest = today once any intraday bar exists)`
- **Evidence:** SQL at 11:28 ET 2026-09-03: _dates latest = 2026-09-03; stock_prices_live rows for today = 8818; stock_prices rows for 2026-09-02 = 10000; tickers with an EOD row on 09-02 but no intraday bar today = 1186 (7 with market_cap > $1B, 0 in sp500/russell1000). These tickers are absent from get_top_movers/screen_stocks/get_sector_performance/get_52week_extremes/get_ytd_returns/detect_crossovers results until they print a trade.
- **Impact:** Intraday screens and sector aggregates cover a shrinking universe of mostly illiquid names with no warning; get_ytd_returns for such a ticker returns nothing during the session.
- **Recommendation:** Fall back to the last EOD row for tickers lacking a live row (COALESCE via LEFT JOIN on prev_day) or add a warning with the count of tickers excluded when latest = today.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Intraday screens and sector aggregates quietly cover a shrinking, liquidity-biased universe; get_ytd_returns returns nothing at all for an affected ticker.

#### 65. The scheduler retries a failed daily or weekly on every tick with no attempt cap or backoff, and each failed daily emits three separate pushes, producing ~7 full-universe Polygon fetches and ~21 notifications in one evening that the operator ends by hand-editing state files.

- **Theme:** scheduler
- **Reviewers:** ops-scripts-monitoring
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:700-707 and 716-723 (no attempt counter); sawa/daily.py:888, 1334 and sawa/utils/monitoring.py:126-141 (three failure pushes)`
- **Evidence:** ~/.sawa/scheduler/scheduler.log 8531-8576: 'Starting sawa daily' at 17:00, 18:00, 19:00, 20:00, 21:00, 22:00, 23:00 ET on 2026-08-31, each 'ERROR: sawa daily failed (exit 1)' after ~57 min, the last 'exit 143' (killed). Lines 9001-9073: weekly ran 18:07, 19:15, 20:30 (exit 143), 21:30, 22:45 ET on 2026-09-01 then 'sawa doctor --job weekly failed'; weekly_done_2026-W36 mtime 2026-09-02 04:51 UTC (touched by hand, no 'Weekly completed' line). grep -c 'HTTP Request: POST https://ntfy' logs/daily_20260831_210002.log = 3 (price fetch failed + completed DEGRADED + FAILED); current code still sends all three when prices_error is set (daily.py:888, 1334; monitoring.py:126).
- **Impact:** A systemic provider failure yields ~7 full-universe Polygon fetches and ~21 pushes in one evening, blocks the weekly for the day (set -e exit after run_daily), and forces manual state edits; a failed weekly re-runs 10k overview requests hourly.
- **Recommendation:** Keep a per-day attempt counter (e.g. daily_attempts_YYYY-MM-DD) with a max of 2-3 tries and exponential spacing; send one consolidated failure push per run (drop the DEGRADED push when the run is already fatal).
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Distinct fix from the set -e abort below (retry policy vs. error isolation between jobs), though the two compound each other during an outage.

#### 66. A failing `sawa daily` aborts the whole scheduler tick under `set -e`, so the weekly job (economy series, overviews, news, corporate actions/splits, stock character) is never attempted while the daily is failing.

- **Theme:** scheduler
- **Reviewers:** scheduling-and-doctor-gaps
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:705 (bare `run_daily` in the else branch, with `set -euo pipefail` at :14)`
- **Evidence:** main() runs `else\n    run_daily\n    action_taken=true\nfi` (:704-706) and only afterwards evaluates the weekly block (:709-722). run_daily returns 1 whenever `sawa daily` or the follow-up doctor fails (:586-594). A command in an if/else BODY is not exempt from `set -e`, so the shell exits immediately. Verified with a minimal repro (bash -euo pipefail, function returning 1 in an else branch, followed by another call): only 'in f' printed, 'g ran' never ran, exit=1. Live proof: on Monday 2026-08-31 — the first day of ISO week 2026-W36, so the weekly was due — scheduler.log shows 7 consecutive evening ticks (17:00, 18:00, 19:00, 20:00, 21:00, 22:00, 23:00 ET) each logging 'Starting sawa daily...' then 'ERROR: sawa daily failed (exit 1)' and nothing else: no 'Weekly:' line, no 'No action needed'. The W36 weekly only ran the next evening (2026-09-01 18:07 ET) after the daily finally succeed…
- **Impact:** Whenever the daily job is broken — which is exactly when data is already degrading — the weekly job (economy series, company overviews, news, corporate actions/splits, stock character) is silently skipped for the rest of the day, with no notification saying the weekly was skipped. A daily outage spanning a Monday-to-Monday boundary would push corporate actions and split ingestion out by a full week while the operator only sees 'Daily FAILED' alerts. It also suppresses the tick's final logging, making the scheduler log misleading about what it decided.
- **Recommendation:** Make the failure non-fatal to the tick: `run_daily || daily_failed=1` (or `if ! run_daily; then ...; fi`), so the weekly block is still evaluated, and let the exit status be aggregated at the end of main(). Consider running the weekly block before the daily block, since the weekly is the lower-frequency, higher-latency job.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Error isolation between scheduled jobs; the weekly is skipped exactly when data is already degrading.

#### 67. dividends.cash_amount is never re-based on a split, so 693 stored per-share dividends across 157 tickers sit on a basis the adjusted price series has abandoned, and three MCP tools print them next to split-adjusted prices.

- **Theme:** split-basis
- **Reviewers:** split-basis-inventory
- **Location:** `dividends.cash_amount (served by mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:122, :171, :266)`
- **Evidence:** Nothing re-bases the column: grep for UPDATE against dividends over all *.py/*.sql returns nothing, and sawa/split_adjust.py's only UPDATE is line 274 against stock_prices.
Scale: joining each dividend to the splits executed after its ex-date, `SELECT count(*), count(DISTINCT ticker) ...` gives 693 rows / 157 tickers on a superseded basis, 623 of them off by >=2x; 622 rows / 140 tickers have ex-dates since 2024-01-01, i.e. inside the served price window.
BKNG (25:1 on 2026-04-06), dividend joined to the adjusted close on its own ex-date:
  2026-03-06  cash_amount 10.5000  close 182.0172  -> 5.77% single payment
  2026-06-05  cash_amount  0.4200  close 165.8400  -> 0.25% single payment
A 23x artificial dividend cut that never happened. KLAC (10:1): 2.30 -> 0.23. COKE (10:1): 2.50 -> 0.25.
Served wrong today by get_dividend_yield_leaders, which selects the latest CD dividend and the latest…
- **Impact:** Any dividend history read next to the price series shows phantom cuts or raises of up to 25x at each split date; trailing-yield arithmetic done from get_dividends output is wrong by the split ratio for 140 tickers; get_dividend_yield_leaders prints a last_dividend/price pair that cannot be divided for 39 active tickers.
- **Recommendation:** Apply SplitAdjuster.factor(ticker, ex_dividend_date) to cash_amount at read time, or re-base the column in the same transaction as the price heal in sawa/split_adjust.py. Storing the as-declared amount is defensible only if a second adjusted column is served alongside it; serving one number that silently changes basis mid-series is not.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Kept at medium rather than high (unlike financial_ratios.price and earnings EPS): the stored amount is a correct historical as-paid fact; the defect is that it is served unlabelled next to an adjusted price series, producing phantom cuts of up to 25x and wrong trailing-yield arithmetic.

#### 68. Every secondary confirmation and every baseline is computed over the full ~5.5-year price history while the Hurst classification uses 1-3 year windows, so 38% of 'trending' names fail the documented R-squared gate on the 3-year window the spec prescribes.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/stock_character.py:318-320 (_confirm_range_bound / _confirm_trending receive the untruncated array); sawa/calculation/stock_character_baseline.py:compute_baseline`
- **Evidence:** classify_stock computes Hurst on the last 756/504/252 bars (stock_character.py:283) but passes the *entire* array to the confirmation step, and compute_baseline uses the entire array for ATR, ADX, OLS, range percentiles, volume profile, correlations and SMA adherence. Ticker histories now run from 2021-02-12, i.e. 1,394 bars (~5.5 years), not the ~756 the spec assumes ('Compute per classified stock over the full available history (3yr)', docs/STOCK_CHARACTER.md:127). Recomputed OLS on log close for the same tickers: XOM R2 = 0.7565 over the full history (the stored value, which is what let it pass the 0.70 gate) but 0.5042 over the last 756 bars; PFE 0.7174 vs 0.3837; D 0.2360 vs 0.8470; JPST 0.0945 vs 0.4829. Universe-wide, using Postgres regr_r2 over the last 756 bars for the 706 trending tickers with regression_r2 stored in run 2026-09-02: 269 (38%) fall below 0.70, average R2 drops f…
- **Impact:** Classification and confirmation disagree about what period they describe. A stock whose recent 3 years are a random walk is confirmed as 'trending' by a 5.5-year regression, and range_high/range_low/price_percentile/atr_baseline all describe a window the label does not.
- **Recommendation:** Truncate the price array to WINDOW_FULL (756 bars) once, at the top of classify_stock/compute_baseline, and pass that slice to every stage; or make the window explicit in config and record it in the tables so the label's horizon is unambiguous.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Classification and confirmation describe different periods, so range_high/range_low/price_percentile/atr_baseline do not describe the regime they are attached to.

#### 69. Compounded reverse-split adjusted history is consumed uncritically by the stock_character batch, producing HIGH-confidence 'trending' labels for sub-$3 microcaps and baselines such as an $11.1 billion ATR.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/stock_character_batch.py:57 (unfiltered SELECT from stock_prices) -> stock_character_baseline.py:429 (atr_baseline = mean of full-history ATR)`
- **Evidence:** Run 2026-09-02: ADTX is stored as trending/HIGH with atr_baseline = 11,149,559,043.7129 and current_price = 0.0079; SMX trending/HIGH atr_baseline 642,803,025.09 at price 16.75; EJH 24,597,176.50 at 1.22; XXII 13,158,645.42 at 2.61; VCIG 4,781,156.20 at 1.86. PPCB is range_bound/HIGH with range_high = 33,621,840.0000 and current_price 1.13 (flags COMPRESSION, EXTREMUM_LOW). 55 of the 3,180 baseline rows have atr_baseline > $1,000 and 19 have range_high > $10,000. The cause is visible in stock_prices: max(close) is 1,484,006,400,000 for ADTX, 106,879,258,342.89 for SMX, 38,465,280,000 for WHLR, 1,256,407,200 for XXII - the reverse-split-adjusted early history. A monotone 1e12 -> 0.008 decay is a perfect log-linear 'trend', which is why these names pass R2 > 0.70 with HIGH confidence. ADTX is in fact the highest-volume HIGH-confidence name in the whole scorecard (volume_sma20 = 19,165,320)…
- **Impact:** The top of the ranked output - the artifact the spec exists to produce - is occupied by delisted/quarantined penny stocks whose 'trend' is a split-adjustment ramp, and the stored baselines are numerically meaningless (an ATR three orders of magnitude larger than the world's largest share price).
- **Recommendation:** Reject or truncate a ticker's history where max(close)/min(close) exceeds a sanity bound (say 10^4), or start the window at the last point where the adjusted series is representable; at minimum refuse to classify a ticker whose atr_baseline exceeds, say, 10x its current price, and record the reason.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique to this subsystem (sawa/stock_character_batch.py:57 selects from stock_prices unfiltered). Related in cause to the split-adjustment-basis findings but fixed here by filtering or normalising the input series.
