#### 70. Nothing reads stock_character_classification/_baseline/_flags/_scorecard: the subsystem writes 81 MB and ~3,200 rows per weekly run into tables with no consumer, and a failed run still escalates to a doctor FAIL.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/database/stock_character.py:408-644 (six read helpers, zero callers)`
- **Evidence:** Repo-wide grep for 'stock_character' outside the producer modules returns only sawa/doctor.py (freshness + coverage check), sawa/cli.py and sawa/weekly.py (both invoke the batch), sqlschema, docs and tests. mcp_server/ has zero matches. information_schema.view_table_usage where table_name LIKE 'stock_character%' returns 0 rows (no view or matview reads them). No entry in scripts/ and no crontab entry other than market_scheduler.sh. No sibling repo under /home/seed/code matches. get_latest_classification, get_latest_baseline, get_latest_scorecard, get_flags_for_ticker, get_ranked_alerts and get_prices_with_benchmarks each appear exactly once - at their own definition. Table sizes: classification 11 MB, baseline 30 MB, flags 24 MB, scorecard 16 MB (81 MB total, 19 run_dates retained, no pruning). docs/STOCK_CHARACTER.md:222-236 specifies a ranked CSV/JSON alert list that does not exist. Th…
- **Impact:** A weekly multi-process batch over ~10,000 tickers, plus 81 MB of storage growing by ~4 MB a week, produces output that is never read; and a failed run escalates to a FAIL exit code in doctor for an artifact nobody consumes. The only feedback loop that would have surfaced the calibration defects below - somebody looking at the output - does not exist.
- **Recommendation:** Decide the subsystem's fate explicitly: either expose it (an MCP tool over get_ranked_alerts plus the spec's CSV/JSON export, which would immediately expose the flag-rate problems), or stop scheduling it in weekly and retain only the code. If kept, add run_date retention pruning and correct the misleading doctor comment.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique (six read helpers in sawa/database/stock_character.py, zero callers). Frames the severity of every other stock_character finding — nothing is currently served — but is itself worth a decision: wire it up or retire it.

#### 71. COMPRESSION / EXPANSION / VOL_SPIKE compare a 10-day dollar ATR against a five-year mean dollar ATR, so they measure price-level drift rather than volatility regime — a stock that has merely risen is chronically flagged 'expansion'.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/stock_character_scorecard.py:168 and sawa/calculation/stock_character_detect.py:139-159`
- **Evidence:** atr_ratio = recent_atr(10 bars, in dollars) / baseline.atr_baseline (mean ATR(14) over the full ~5.5-year history, in dollars). For the 2,730 tickers in run 2026-09-02 with both an atr_ratio and a price on 2023-09-01 and 2026-09-01, corr(atr_ratio, ln(price growth over 3 years)) = 0.529. The 323 tickers that at least doubled average atr_ratio 1.982 - just above the EXPANSION/VOL_SPIKE gate of 1.80 - while the 522 that at least halved average 0.180, far below the COMPRESSION gate of 0.60. The price-normalised statistic the spec asks for is already computed and stored (atr_pct_baseline, stock_character_baseline.py:433-435) and is never used by any flag.
- **Impact:** A stock that has merely risen is chronically flagged 'volatility expansion / possible breakout' and one that has fallen is chronically flagged 'coiling'. 513 COMPRESSION + 186 EXPANSION + 46 VOL_SPIKE flags in the latest run, and the scorecard's atr_ratio column carries the same distortion.
- **Recommendation:** Divide the recent ATR by the current close and compare against atr_pct_baseline (already stored), or compute the baseline ATR over a trailing window comparable to the recent one rather than the full history.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Same miscalibration pattern as the volume flags below (short window vs. a non-stationary five-year mean) but a different statistic and a different fix (normalise ATR as a percentage of price), so kept separate.

#### 72. SMA touch-and-bounce adherence counts a move straight through the moving average as a 'bounce' because the confirmation uses an absolute distance, roughly doubling the ratio that gates AT_150SMA/AT_200SMA.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/stock_character_baseline.py:375`
- **Evidence:** _compute_sma_adherence records a bounce when abs(close[i+10] - sma[i]) / sma[i] >= SMA_BOUNCE_MIN_MOVE_PCT (2%) - the sign is discarded, so a price that touches the 200-SMA and then falls 2% *through* it scores identically to one that rebounds 2% away. Recomputing both definitions on the real series (directional = the move must be on the side the price approached from): PFE SMA200 0.6429 (stored, and above the 0.60 gate) vs 0.3929 directional; PFE SMA150 0.5833 vs 0.3056; XOM SMA150 0.7812 vs 0.3750; XOM SMA200 0.6667 vs 0.5000; TJX SMA200 0.8889 vs 0.6111; D SMA150 0.5946 vs 0.4054. Live effect: 1,371 of 2,333 range_bound rows in run 2026-09-02 clear the 0.60 SMA_ADHERENCE_MIN_RATIO gate on sma_200_adherence_ratio (mean 0.5872 range_bound, 0.7378 trending), which is the sole precondition for emitting AT_200SMA (114 rows) and AT_150SMA (144 rows).
- **Impact:** sma_150/200_adherence_ratio does not measure what its name and the spec say ('backtest touch-and-bounce ratio'); it is close to 'did the stock move 2% in ten days', which is almost always true. The AT_150SMA/AT_200SMA flags are therefore emitted for stocks with no demonstrated SMA respect.
- **Recommendation:** Require the confirming move to be signed: record which side of the SMA the price approached from (e.g. close a few bars before the touch) and count a bounce only when close[i+bounce_days] is at least bounce_min_pct on that same side.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique (stock_character_baseline.py:375). The stored column measures approximately 'did the stock move 2% in ten days', not what its name and the spec claim.

#### 73. VOLUME_SPIKE / VOLUME_DROUGHT compare a 10-day average against a five-year mean volume that is systematically 29% below current levels, so 39% of the universe carries a volume flag.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/stock_character_baseline.py:474-478 (volume_sma20 = mean of the rolling 20-day volume over the whole history) and sawa/calculation/stock_character_detect.py:288-306`
- **Evidence:** The column is named volume_sma20 and the spec calls it 'baseline average volume', but it is the mean of the 20-day rolling volume over the full ~5.5-year history. Comparing it against each ticker's actual average volume over the trailing year (2025-09-01..2026-09-01) for the 3,180 rows of run 2026-09-02: median recent/baseline = 1.288, with 639 tickers above 2.0 and 193 below 0.5 - i.e. for 639 names the ordinary current volume already exceeds the VOLUME_SPIKE_RATIO gate of 2.0 before any spike occurs. Live flag counts: VOLUME_SPIKE 584 and VOLUME_DROUGHT 671, together 1,255 of 3,180 tickers (39%).
- **Impact:** A secular change in turnover is reported as a recurring anomaly. Combined with DECORRELATION_SPY, the two miscalibrated common flags account for 2,531 of the 6,829 flag rows in the latest run, and only 216 of 3,180 scorecards are flag-free.
- **Recommendation:** Use a trailing baseline of comparable age (e.g. the 60- or 250-day median volume) rather than the full-history mean, or de-trend volume before the comparison.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Together with DECORRELATION_SPY it accounts for 2,531 of the 6,829 flag rows in the latest run — a secular turnover change reported as a recurring anomaly.

#### 74. The weekly universe refresh iterates only over tickers already in companies, so no new listing has entered the database since 2026-05-25 — roughly 500 IPOs, spin-offs, new ETFs and ticker changes are absent.

- **Theme:** universe-coverage
- **Reviewers:** prices
- **Location:** `sawa/weekly.py:50 (download_overviews) fed by sawa/database/connection.py:104-107`
- **Evidence:** download_overviews(client, symbols, ...) loops `for i, symbol in enumerate(symbols, 1): client.get_ticker_details(symbol)` — `symbols` comes from get_active_tickers, which is literally `SELECT ticker FROM companies WHERE active = true` (sawa/database/connection.py:107). The refresh is closed over the current universe and can never discover a ticker not already present.

Observed consequences:
  SELECT max(created_at) FROM companies;  -> 2026-05-25 22:50:39
  SELECT date_trunc('month',created_at)::date, count(*) FROM companies WHERE created_at >= '2025-09-01' GROUP BY 1;
   2026-02-01 | 4837
   2026-03-01 |    4
   2026-04-01 |   26
   2026-05-01 | 5563
  (nothing in June, July, August or September 2026)

Meanwhile rows ARE being updated daily, so the job is running and appears healthy:
  SELECT date_trunc('day',updated_at)::date, count(*) FROM companies WHERE updated_at >= '2026-08-25' G…
- **Impact:** Every IPO, spin-off, new ETF and ticker change since late May 2026 is absent from the database — about 500 securities over ~3.5 months, growing ~130/month. They cannot be screened, charted or alerted on, and a ticker change silently orphans the series (BK stops on 2026-05-20 and no successor row was ever created). Combined with finding #3 the daily universe is shrinking monotonically: avg tickers/day fell from 10,273 in May 2026 to 10,001 in September 2026 with no additions offsetting the losses. The failure is invisible because the job updates 10,128 existing rows every night and reports succ
- **Recommendation:** Make the refresh discover the universe rather than re-read it: call the Polygon ticker-list endpoint (sawa/utils/symbols.py already builds `exchange=..., active=true, type IN (CS,ETF,ADRC)` queries) and upsert the full result, instead of looping get_ticker_details over get_active_tickers. Add a doctor check that fails when max(companies.created_at) is older than ~14 days or when zero new tickers appeared in the last month, so this cannot regress silently again.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Same table as the delisting defect but the opposite direction and an independent fix (seed the refresh from the provider's ticker list rather than from the DB).

#### 75. All seven index constituent sets are 110 days stale; nothing schedules a refresh and no check reports the staleness, so us_active — the universe behind every index= filter — misses recent listings and retains dead ones.

- **Theme:** universe-coverage
- **Reviewers:** companies-indices
- **Location:** `indices.last_updated / index_constituents; sawa/coldstart.py:790 (populate_index_constituents), sawa/cli.py:562 (cmd_index_update)`
- **Evidence:** SELECT i.code, count(ic.ticker), i.last_updated, max(ic.added_at) FROM indices i LEFT JOIN index_constituents ic ON ic.index_id=i.id GROUP BY 1,3 -> every one of the 7 indices has last_updated = max(added_at) = 2026-05-16 16:39 (sp500 503, nasdaq_listed 4676, us_active 10406, nasdaq100 101, dow30 30, mag7 8, russell1000 998). That is 110 days before today. populate_index_constituents is called from only two places (`grep -rn populate_index_constituents`): coldstart (sawa/coldstart.py:1936) and `sawa index-update` (sawa/cli.py:578); `grep -n 'sawa |index-update' scripts/daily.sh scripts/weekly.sh scripts/market_scheduler.sh` shows the schedulers run only daily/weekly/intraday/doctor, never index-update. `grep -rn index sawa/doctor.py | wc -l` -> 0, so no health check covers it. docs/MAINTENANCE.md:325-330 presents index-update purely as a manual follow-up to add-symbol, with no cadence. C…
- **Impact:** us_active is the universe behind every `index=` filter in the MCP tools (mcp_server/tools/_index_filter.py), so GLD/SLV/IAU and 21 other tradeable symbols are invisible to any index-filtered screen or scan. sp500-scoped results carry constituents that no longer trade and omit any additions made since May. `list_indices` and `get_index_constituents` report last_updated honestly, but nothing acts on it.
- **Recommendation:** Run `sawa index-update` on a schedule (weekly is enough — Wikipedia sources change slowly, Polygon's us_active does not) by adding it to scripts/weekly.sh or market_scheduler.sh, and add a doctor check that warns when max(indices.last_updated) is older than ~14 days. Backfilling now would also pull the 15 companies created 2026-05-25 and the 9 pre-existing ones that Polygon has since dropped into the correct memberships.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Reviewer rated high; re-rated medium — the drift is real and unmonitored, but 110 days of S&P 500 membership change and ~24 missing tradeable symbols out of 10k is a coverage error, not a wrong-value error.

#### 76. detect_volume_anomalies manufactures hundreds of fabricated anomalies out of rounding noise on reverse-split tickers (366 events for LBGJ) and returns them with no warning.

- **Theme:** volume-integrity
- **Reviewers:** volume-and-archive
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/volume_analysis.py:254-282`
- **Evidence:** Live call detect_volume_anomalies(ticker='LBGJ', lookback_days=252) -> summary {spike_count:44, drop_count:143, divergence_count:179, bearish:62, bullish:75, climactic_events:42}, warnings:[]. The underlying numbers are pure quantization: first spikes are 2025-09-04 volume=3 vs volume_ma_20=1 ratio 5.45; 2025-09-08 volume=3 ma=1 ratio 4.0; 2025-09-09 volume=6 ma=1 ratio 6.67; first drops are 2025-09-16 volume=0 ma=2 ratio 0. Code path: mcp_server/tools/volume_analysis.py:254 `vol_ma = sum(volumes[i-20:i]) / 20`, :255 `if vol_ma == 0: continue`, :257 `vol_ratio = volumes[i] / vol_ma` — the only guard is against an exactly-zero window, so a window averaging 0.55 shares yields ratios of 2-7 that clear the default threshold_multiplier=2.0.
- **Impact:** A user asking 'has LBGJ had unusual volume?' gets 366 fabricated events presented as spikes, drops, divergences and climactic reversals, with no indication that the entire window's volumes are single-digit rounding artefacts. The same applies to every one of the 81 affected tickers whose collapsed span falls inside the lookback.
- **Recommendation:** Add a floor to the tool: when the 20-day mean volume is below a few hundred shares, emit a warning and suppress the anomaly classification rather than dividing single-digit integers, and flag windows that span a recorded split.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. A downstream consequence of the collapsed-volume defect but a separate fix — the tool needs a degenerate-volume guard even after the underlying data is repaired, because the historical window will always contain reconstructed values.

### Low (46)

#### 77. No REST backfill into stock_prices_intraday exists, so the recovery path that docs/DATA_SOURCES.md, docs/OPERATIONS.md and three code sites promise for lost intraday bars is not implemented and every intraday loss is permanent. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

- **Theme:** intraday-stream
- **Reviewers:** intraday-live, intraday-live-code
- **Location:** `docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:74`
- **Location:** `docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:74,240; docs/OPERATIONS.md:196; sawa/api/client.py:37 ('aggregates-intraday' endpoint, unused); sawa/api/websocket_client.py:633-636; sawa/intraday.py:78-80`
- **Evidence:** docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:74 lists `/v2/aggs/ticker/{t}/range/{m}/{ts}/...` -> `stock_prices_intraday` (REST fallback) | `intraday` (rare; usually WS) | `sawa/database/intraday_load.py`, and :240 repeats 'REST /v2/aggs/.../{m}/{ts}/... for backfill'. websocket_client.py:_enforce_buffer_limit logs 'DATA LOSS: ... Recover the missing interval from historical data.' and sawa/intraday.py:81-84 reports 'data loss: N unpersisted intraday bar(s) were dropped; historical recovery is required'. But `grep -rn 'load_intraday_bars' --include=*.py sawa/ mcp_server/ scripts/` returns only its definition in sawa/database/intraday_load.py:20 and a single caller, sawa/api/websocket_client.py:755 (_write_bars_to_db), reached only from the websocket path. `sawa/cli.py` exposes no intraday-backfill subcommand.
- **Evidence:** grep -rn 'aggregates-intraday' sawa/ mcp_server/ tests/ -> only its definition at client.py:37; grep for timespan='minute'/'hour' or range/1/minute in sawa/ and mcp_server/ -> none; the only get_aggregates callers (sawa/live.py:76, async_client.py:351) use the default timespan='day'. DATA_SOURCES.md:74 lists '/v2/aggs/ticker/{t}/range/{m}/{ts}/... -> stock_prices_intraday (REST fallback) … sawa/database/intraday_load.py' and :240 'REST … for backfill'. OPERATIONS.md:196 says 'Intraday prices | (ticker, timestamp) | Upsert' while the live PK (pg_constraint) and migration 45 are (ticker, timestamp, bar_size_minutes). websocket_client.py:633-636 logs 'Recover the missing interval from historical data' and intraday.py:79-80 'historical recovery is required' although no such tool exists.
- **Impact:** Any intraday bar not captured live is permanently unrecoverable: the retry-buffer overflow, the SIGKILL-at-close aggregator loss (finding #1), and the structurally missing 15:40-16:00 ET windows (finding #2) all have no remediation path, despite the code telling the operator to perform one. An operator following the log instruction has no tool to run.
- **Recommendation:** Either implement the documented REST backfill — a `sawa intraday --backfill YYYY-MM-DD` that pulls `/v2/aggs/ticker/{t}/range/5/minute/{d}/{d}`, normalizes volume via normalize_provider_volume, and feeds load_intraday_bars with source_minute_count/mask=full so it wins the superset upsert — or delete the claim from docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:74,240 and reword the two log messages to say the interval is unrecoverable.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Same finding from two reviewers, both low. The severity of the loss itself is carried by the two intraday data-loss groups; what is filed here is the missing remedy plus the docs that promise it (including a stale (ticker, timestamp) identity description).

#### 78. A 376 MB stock_prices_backup table (4.68M rows, 2021-02-12..2026-02-25) sits undocumented in the production database outside the schema manifest — a 2026-02-26 pre-migration snapshot that holds non-zero volume for 8,125 of the destroyed bars but whose wholesale restore would reintroduce a stale split basis and violate the ticker foreign key. *(filed independently by 2 reviewers)*

Zero references in the repo (grep 'stock_prices_backup' across the tree and `git log --all -S` both return nothing) and it is absent from EXPECTED_TABLES (sawa/database/schema.py:24-54). Basis proven: on 2025-06-02, 3,821 of 4,127 common rows match stock_prices exactly and 306 differ, and for every differing ticker backup_close/current_close equals the inverse of the post-2026-02-26 split f…

#### 79. The price chart's OHLC summary reports the window's first CLOSE under the label 'Open:', and the detailed view's volatility uses that same wrong base.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/renderers/prices.py:209 (summary), :276 (detailed table), :288-290 (volatility)`
- **Evidence:** prices.py:54 sets first_close = closes[0] and lines 209/276 print it as "Open:"; data[0]['open'] is never read. Rendered BIAF (r2.py):
  JSON first row: date 2025-09-03, open 105.30000000, close 111.15000000
  chart: "Open: $111.15 | High: $202.50 | Low: $4.56 | Close: $9.75"
High and Low on the same line ARE taken from the true intraday high/low columns, so the line reads as a genuine period OHLC in which only "Open" is fabricated (5.6% off here). prices.py:288 then computes "Volatility: ...% (price range as % of open)" against the same close.
- **Impact:** The one OHLC line a user reads off the chart is internally inconsistent - three of its four values come from the OHLC columns and the fourth comes from the close series - and the JSON alongside carries the correct open, so chart and data disagree on the same field.
- **Recommendation:** Use data[0]['open'] for the Open cell (falling back to the first close only when open is missing), and use it as the volatility denominator.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Three of the four values come from the OHLC columns and the fourth from the close series, while the JSON alongside carries the correct open.

#### 80. The volume row of the price chart prints min and max in the visual position where the price row prints first and last, so the two lines use the same grammar for different quantities.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/renderers/prices.py:194 (price line: first/last) vs :199-203 (volume line: min/max)`
- **Evidence:** Rendered BIAF (r2.py):
  "Price | $111.15 ▇█▅... $9.75 | ▼ -91.2%"      <- $111.15 is the FIRST close, $9.75 the LAST
  "Vol   | 214.0 ▁▁▁... 46.4M | Avg: 616.1K"        <- 214.0 is min(volumes), 46.4M is max(volumes)
The volume series neither starts at 214 nor ends at 46.4M (46.4M is the spike day mid-series).
- **Impact:** The volume line reads as "volume went from 214 shares to 46.4M shares over the period", which is not what the data says; nothing on the line distinguishes the two conventions.
- **Recommendation:** Either print first/last for volume as well, or prefix the labels ("min 214.0 ... max 46.4M") so the two rows are not read with the same grammar.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Reads as 'volume went from 214 to 46.4M shares over the period', which is not what the data says.

#### 81. The economy dashboard's trend column mixes percentage-point differences and relative percent changes, rendering both with a % sign and applying one 0.5 threshold to two incompatible scales.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render
- **Location:** `mcp_server/charts/renderers/economy.py:393 (relative %) vs :405 and :418 (raw difference), all fed to TrendIndicator.render(is_percent=True)`
- **Evidence:** economy.py:393 computes change = ((series[-1]-series[0])/series[0]*100) for the treasury rows, while :405 and :418 compute change = current - series[0] for CPI YoY and unemployment; TrendIndicator.render (widgets/trend.py:62) formats every one as f"{value:.1f}%". Rendered NORMAL dashboard (r1.py) shows "10Y Treasury 4.69% ... ▼ -1.3%" (relative) next to "Unemployment 4.1% ... ▶ 0.0%" (percentage points). economy.py:466 in the DETAILED renderer uses the relative form for the very same unemployment row, so the two detail levels disagree about what the number means.
- **Impact:** A 0.3pp move in unemployment and a 0.3% relative move in a yield print identically; the neutral/positive/negative threshold of 0.5 is applied to two incompatible scales, so trend arrows are assigned inconsistently across rows and across detail levels.
- **Recommendation:** Pick one convention per indicator kind (percentage points for rates, relative % for levels), record it in the metric table, and pass is_percent/suffix accordingly so the column is self-describing.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Trend arrows are assigned inconsistently across the indicators in a single table.

#### 82. get_economy_data accepts indicator_type 'market_internals' but the chart dispatcher has no renderer for it, so the tool's chart field is the literal string 'Unknown indicator type: market_internals'.

- **Theme:** charts
- **Reviewers:** charts-render

Rendered (r4.py): get_economy_data('market_internals','2026-06-01','2026-09-03') returned 68 rows and render_economy_chart(...,'market_internals') returned exactly 'Unknown indicator type: market_internals'. The render did not raise, so server.py's try/except does not suppress it - the string is placed in the response's chart field.
- **Impact:** The one economy series most likely to be asked about intraday (VIX / VIX3M / HY spread) returns an error sentence where clients expect display text, and the failure is invisible in the logs because nothing raised.
- **Recommendation:** Add a market_internals renderer (VIX, VIX3M, term structure, HY spread), or return None for unknown types so the field is null rather than an error string masquerading as a chart.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. The VIX/VIX3M/HY-spread series returns an error sentence where clients expect display text, and nothing raises, so the failure is invisible in the logs.

#### 84. ta_query drops 8 of the 28 stored technical indicators, so `sawa ta` can never display SMA-200, EMA-200, ADX-14 or the dollar-volume/BB-width columns even though the screener accepts them as filters.

Anyone screening on sma_200 gets matches they cannot inspect.

#### 85. ta_query drives its async repository through asyncio.get_event_loop().run_until_complete(), which already warns on Python 3.12 and is scheduled to become a RuntimeError, and leaks the loop on every invocation.

- **Theme:** cli
- **Reviewers:** repositories-layer
- **Location:** `sawa/ta_query.py:33`
- **Evidence:** ta_query.py:33, :65 and :92 all call `asyncio.get_event_loop().run_until_complete(_fetch())` from synchronous CLI code with no running loop. Reproduced on the project's interpreter (Python 3.12.3):
  $ uv run --no-sync python -W error::DeprecationWarning -c "from sawa.ta_query import get_latest_indicators; print(get_latest_indicators('AAPL'))"
  File "/home/seed/code/sawa/sawa/ta_query.py", line 33, in get_latest_indicators
    result = asyncio.get_event_loop().run_until_complete(_fetch())
  File "/usr/lib/python3.12/asyncio/events.py", line 697, in get_event_loop
    warnings.warn('There is no current event loop', ...)
  DeprecationWarning: There is no current event loop
Without -W error the calls succeed today. Every other synchronous entry point in the codebase uses asyncio.run() instead -- cli.py:493, :535, :615 all call `asyncio.run(repo....)`. ta_query.py is the only module still o…
- **Impact:** `sawa ta` and `sawa ta-screen` will stop working on a Python upgrade past the removal, with a RuntimeError rather than a graceful message. The implicitly-created loop is also never closed, so each invocation leaks a loop object and its selector for the life of the process.
- **Recommendation:** Replace all three call sites with `asyncio.run(...)`, matching what cli.py already does for the index commands.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Latent breakage on a Python upgrade, plus a per-invocation loop leak.

#### 86. _merge_price_artifacts appends staging rows without de-duplication, so re-running coldstart over an overlapping window grows the load-only cache with duplicate (symbol, date) rows — every file in the live cache already carries a duplicated 2026-02-11 row.

- **Theme:** coldstart-loader
- **Reviewers:** coldstart-loaders
- **Location:** `sawa/coldstart.py:392-405 (_merge_price_artifacts)`
- **Evidence:** _merge_price_artifacts copies a new file or appends all non-header lines of the staging file to the existing cache file; there is no date/key check. Live cache: all 525 files in data/prices have exactly one duplicated date (`cut -d, -f1 | sort | uniq -d` -> 2026-02-11 in every file), e.g. data/prices/AAPL.csv lines 1256-1257 are byte-identical '2026-02-11,AAPL,274.695,275.5,280.18,274.45,51931283'. load_prices then counts both as source_rows/eligible_rows and upserts the same key twice (harmless for the table, misleading in prices_persistence stats).
- **Impact:** Cache size and --load-only time grow linearly with each re-run of the same window; persistence stats over-report rows; a future change that stops upserting (or that treats duplicate keys as errors) would break the offline load.
- **Recommendation:** Merge by key: read existing dates for the symbol and append only new ones, or rebuild the cache file from staging when the staging window covers it. Add a test that merging the same staging twice leaves one row per date.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Harmless while the loader upserts, but it inflates cache size, load time and persistence statistics, and would break the offline path if duplicate keys ever became errors.

#### 87. Thirty-two 1:1 no-op rows in stock_splits are served as splits and needlessly trigger the post-split price refetch and full TA recompute for large caps such as SCCO and ITUB.

- **Theme:** corporate-actions
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions
- **Location:** `stock_splits WHERE split_to = split_from (SCCO x4, METCB x4, SNFCA x2, NGG x2, SBS x2, PHG, TRI, ITUB, CBSH, RYN, ...); sawa/corporate_actions.py:416 + sawa/weekly.py:592-608`
- **Evidence:** SELECT count(*) FROM stock_splits WHERE split_to = split_from -> 32, spanning 2025-05-30..2026-08-11. Replaying get_recent_splits(days=30) returns 'SCCO 2026-08-11 1:1' alongside real splits. StockSplit.from_polygon accepts them (is_unrepresentable_split_ratio({'split_from':1,'split_to':1}) is False) and StockSplit(1,1).multiplier == 1.0. Every such row lands in split_result.persisted_tickers -> stats['split_tickers'] (sawa/corporate_actions.py:416), which sawa/weekly.py:592-608 and sawa/daily.py:486-500 use to re-fetch the full adjusted history and fully recompute technical_indicators for that ticker.
- **Impact:** Cosmetic in reads (a '1:1 split' is meaningless to a user asking what split recently), and wasteful in the pipeline: a no-op row costs a full history refetch plus a complete TA recompute for a large-cap like SCCO or ITUB, up to four times a year each.
- **Recommendation:** Skip split_to == split_from at parse time (treat it like the fractional-ratio case: count it, log it, do not persist) or at minimum exclude it from persisted_tickers so it never triggers the repair path, and filter it out of get_recent_splits/get_stock_splits.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Kept separate from the corrupted-ratio group that also enumerates these rows: the fix here is to skip no-ops in the read and heal paths, which is independent of correcting the rounded ratios. Its waste impact overlaps the split-heal churn group.

#### 88. The scheduler runs the full daily job on Saturdays and Sundays with no trading-day gate, so the 10.4k-symbol sweep, the full-universe TA recompute and the split heal execute twice per weekend with no new data to load.

- **Theme:** daily-pipeline
- **Reviewers:** daily-pipeline
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:695-706 (run_daily when status!=open and hour>=17, no weekday/holiday check); sawa/daily.py:671-678`
- **Evidence:** scheduler.log 7468-7471 '[2026-08-29 17:00:01 ET] Starting sawa daily... [17:57:35 ET] Daily completed: Latest prices: 2026-08-28' and 7964-8000 same on Sunday 08-30 (~57 min each); ~/.sawa/scheduler/daily_done_2026-08-29 and daily_done_2026-08-30 exist. daily.py:593-595 always sets start_date = last_price_date-14d, so should_fetch_prices (daily.py:671) is always true and the 'Prices already up to date.' branch (daily.py:676-678) is unreachable; a weekend run therefore re-fetches ~120k bars, re-upserts them, recomputes 14 days of TA for ~10.4k tickers and runs the 628-ticker heal (see high finding).
- **Impact:** Roughly two extra hours of Polygon API calls and ~240k needless stock_prices rewrites plus full-universe TA rewrites per weekend; idempotent (values unchanged) but adds table bloat and provider load, and any provider hiccup on a weekend produces DEGRADED/FAILED alerts for a day with no market data.
- **Recommendation:** In market_scheduler.sh skip run_daily when the ET date is not a trading day (Polygon market-status API is already queried; or check stock_prices MAX(date) == previous trading day), or make run_daily a no-op when no trading day falls in (last_price_date, end_date].
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Idempotent in values, so low; costs ~2 hours of provider calls and ~240k needless rewrites per weekend and enlarges the window in which a provider hiccup can damage good data.

#### 89. Every write path runs with statement_timeout, lock_timeout and idle_in_transaction_session_timeout all at 0, so one stuck pipeline transaction holds its locks forever and can freeze autovacuum cluster-wide with nothing to detect it.

- **Theme:** db-tuning
- **Reviewers:** db-health-concurrency
- **Location:** `pg_settings (boot_val 0 for all three) / sawa/database/connection.py:71`
- **Evidence:** pg_settings reset_val and boot_val are 0 for statement_timeout, lock_timeout, idle_in_transaction_session_timeout, idle_session_timeout and transaction_timeout (the 180000 I see for statement_timeout has source='client', i.e. my own PGOPTIONS). `SELECT * FROM pg_db_role_setting` returns zero rows, so there is no database- or role-level default either. A grep for statement_timeout / lock_timeout / idle_in_transaction across sawa/, mcp_server/ and scripts/ finds exactly one setter, mcp_server/database.py:386 `SET LOCAL statement_timeout = {30s}`, which applies only to MCP reads. sawa/database/connection.py:71 (`return psycopg.connect(**conn_params)`) -- the single connection factory for daily, weekly, coldstart, intraday and ta-backfill -- passes no options string. Confirmed the reverse direction is safe: mcp_server/database.py:245-256 hands out pooled connections through psycopg_pool's co…
- **Impact:** A pipeline transaction that stalls (blocked socket to Polygon inside an open transaction, a lock wait, a hung worker) will (a) hold ACCESS EXCLUSIVE indefinitely once daily.py:411 starts REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW, stalling every reader of mv_52week_extremes behind it, (b) pin the global xmin horizon so autovacuum cannot remove dead tuples in *any* table -- on top of the 1.88M/1.58M backlog in finding 2 -- and (c) occupy a connection slot. pg_replication_slots and pg_prepared_xacts are empty today so nothing is stuck right now, but there is no timeout, no alert and no doctor check that would su
- **Recommendation:** Set cluster defaults: `ALTER DATABASE postgres SET idle_in_transaction_session_timeout = '5min'` and `SET lock_timeout = '30s'`, and give the batch role a generous but finite `statement_timeout` (e.g. 30min) with an explicit higher `SET LOCAL` around the known-long steps (matview refresh, full TA recompute). Add a doctor check for backends with xact_start older than a few minutes or state='idle in transaction'.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique and latent — no stuck transaction was observed — but it is the mechanism that would turn the ACCESS EXCLUSIVE matview refresh into an indefinite outage. Kept at the reviewer's low because it is a hardening gap rather than an active defect.

#### 90. Two dividends carry dividend_type 'recurring', outside the documented CD/SC/LT/ST domain, hiding them from every type-filtered read and from the latest_divs CTE in get_dividend_yield_leaders.

- **Theme:** dividends
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions
- **Location:** `dividends id 121692 (BBUC 2026-03-23) and id 127197 (ELME 2026-01-08); loader at sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:200 (dividend_type=data.get("dividend_type") — unvalidated)`
- **Evidence:** SELECT dividend_type, count(*) FROM dividends GROUP BY 1 -> CD 44854, SC 372, recurring 2. sqlschema/18_corporate_actions.sql documents the domain as 'CD=cash, SC=special cash, LT=long-term cap gain, ST=short-term cap gain' and Dividend.from_polygon validates frequency against an allowlist but passes dividend_type through untouched. The two rows are material: ELME 2026-01-08 cash_amount 14.6700 (its only 2026 distribution; its CD rows are 0.1800), BBUC 2026-03-23 cash_amount 0.6250 against a 0.0625 regular quarterly. Because the unique index is `(ticker, ex_dividend_date, COALESCE(dividend_type,''))`, a later correctly-typed refresh of the same ex-date inserts a second row instead of updating these.
- **Impact:** get_dividends(dividend_type='CD') and the latest_divs CTE inside get_dividend_yield_leaders (WHERE dividend_type = 'CD', mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:256) both skip these rows, so ELME's most recent and by far largest distribution is invisible and its 'last dividend' reads as 0.1800 from 2025-09-17. The rows are also duplication-prone under replay.
- **Recommendation:** Validate dividend_type in Dividend.from_polygon against {CD, SC, LT, ST, None} and either map or reject anything else with a logged counter, so provider vocabulary drift surfaces at ingest instead of silently partitioning the table. Repair the two existing rows (both look like special/large distributions -> SC).
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Two rows, but it demonstrates that dividend_type is stored unvalidated (sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:200).

#### 91. get_dividends labels frequency 52 (weekly, 5,900 rows) and 24 (semi-monthly, 138 rows) as 'Unknown' even though commit 0781f4b declared them legitimate income-ETF schedules.

- **Theme:** dividends
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:125-132 (CASE d.frequency ... ELSE 'Unknown')`
- **Evidence:** SQL: SELECT frequency, COUNT(*) FROM dividends GROUP BY 1 -> 12:19530, 4:17001, 52:5900, 2:1119, 1:857, 0:683, 24:138. git show 0781f4b: 'Dividend frequency 52 (weekly) and 24 (semi-monthly) are ordinary income-ETF schedules, not corruption.'
- **Impact:** 13% of dividend rows are presented with an 'Unknown' frequency label, misleading consumers about payout cadence for weekly/semi-monthly payers.
- **Recommendation:** Add WHEN 52 THEN 'Weekly' WHEN 24 THEN 'Semi-Monthly' to the CASE (and mirror in any yield annualisation).
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Presentation-only, but 13% of dividend rows carry a misleading cadence label.

#### 92. Schema documentation is out of date: sqlschema/README.md lacks migrations 47/48, misplaces stock_prices_live and mis-describes 29; 00_setup.sql claims 22 tables; sawa/daily.py's docstring still cites NUMERIC(20,8).

- **Theme:** docs
- **Reviewers:** schema-drift
- **Location:** `sqlschema/README.md (file table ends at 46; row 21 says '5-min bars + view stock_prices_live'; row 29 says 'drop legacy VIX rows'); sqlschema/00_setup.sql:32,56; sawa/daily.py:290`
- **Evidence:** grep -n '47_\|48_' sqlschema/README.md -> no matches although both files exist and are in REQUIRED_SCHEMA_FILENAMES. README row for 21 attributes the stock_prices_live view to 21 (it is defined in 22/24/33/40/42/45). README row 29: 'Migration: drop legacy VIX rows from stock_prices / companies' but file 29 body says 'Historical cleanup intentionally omitted ... non-destructive'. 00_setup.sql: '-- Expected tables (22 total)' vs 30 in EXPECTED_TABLES / 38 live. sawa/daily.py:290: 'after rounding to the stored NUMERIC(20,8) precision' while stock_prices is NUMERIC(24,8) and is_valid_daily_ohlcv uses MAX_STORABLE_PRICE=1e16.
- **Impact:** Operators following the catalogue will misjudge what a migration does and which precision the loader enforces.
- **Recommendation:** Update README rows for 21/29 and add 47/48; refresh 00_setup.sql expected lists (or drop them in favour of EXPECTED_TABLES); fix the daily.py docstring.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Operators following the catalogue will misjudge what a migration does and which precision the loader enforces.

#### 93. Documentation states the stock_character tables are computed from technical_indicators, financial_ratios and fundamentals, but the batch reads only stock_prices and re-derives ATR/ADX/SMA itself.

- **Theme:** docs
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:222 and :257, sqlschema/README.md:36`
- **Evidence:** docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:222 lists the sources for the four stock_character_* tables as '`stock_prices`, `technical_indicators`, `financial_ratios`, fundamentals', :257 repeats 'computed locally from stock_prices + technical_indicators + fundamentals', and sqlschema/README.md:36 says the same. The only queries the batch issues are `SELECT date, open, high, low, close, volume FROM stock_prices WHERE ticker = %s ORDER BY date ASC` (sawa/stock_character_batch.py:57 for each ticker and :179 for the SPY/GLD/TLT benchmarks). Neither technical_indicators nor any fundamentals table is imported or queried anywhere under sawa/calculation/stock_character*.py.
- **Impact:** An operator debugging a bad classification, or planning a schema change, is told to look at three tables that have no bearing on the output - and would not know that the subsystem re-derives ATR/ADX/SMA itself rather than reusing technical_indicators (where they are computed with different, Wilder-smoothed definitions).
- **Recommendation:** Correct both files to name stock_prices as the sole input, and note that ATR/ADX/SMA are recomputed in-module and deliberately differ from technical_indicators.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Points an operator debugging a bad classification at three tables that have no bearing on the output.

#### 94. Docs describe the scheduler as running weekly on Saturdays under a weekday-only cron and give stale state/lock advice; the actual behaviour is the first closed evening of the ISO week (Mondays) with an every-day cron.

- **Theme:** docs
- **Reviewers:** ops-scripts-monitoring
- **Location:** `docs/OPERATIONS.md:152-157,167-168; docs/MAINTENANCE.md:150-163,168,171,311,380-381; scripts/weekly.sh:4; scripts/daily.sh:4`
- **Evidence:** market_scheduler.sh:7-10 header and live `crontab -l` use `*/15 * * * *` (every day); lines 709-723 run weekly on any closed evening >= 17:00 ET once per ISO week; ~/.sawa/scheduler/weekly_done_2026-W27..W35 were all created on Mondays 23:11-23:17 UTC. OPERATIONS.md:153 'weekly on Saturdays', :156 cron '1-5', :168 and weekly.sh:4 'Saturday, matching market_scheduler.sh'; MAINTENANCE.md:150 'weekdays', :163 'If Saturday and weekly hasn't run'. MAINTENANCE.md:168 lists intraday.log as state but the scheduler no longer writes it (market_scheduler.sh:428-431; file is 0 bytes from Jun 4). MAINTENANCE.md:380-381 tells operators to rm scheduler.lock for a 'stale lock' — flock is released on process exit, and deleting the file while a run holds it lets a second instance lock a new inode and overlap. MAINTENANCE.md:311 marks FRED_API_KEY required 'yes' while OPERATIONS.md:29 and daily.py:1295 tre…
- **Impact:** An operator following the docs installs a weekday-only cron and expects Saturday weeklies; the lock advice can cause overlapping schedulers.
- **Recommendation:** Rewrite the Scheduling sections to match the script (every-day cron, first closed evening per ISO week), remove intraday.log, replace the rm-lock advice with `flock -n` / pid inspection, reconcile FRED requiredness and the daily.sh cron example.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. An operator following docs/OPERATIONS.md and docs/MAINTENANCE.md installs the wrong cron and can end up with overlapping schedulers.

#### 95. fiscal_year and fiscal_quarter are NULL for 100% of the 55,848 earnings rows, yet both are returned by two MCP tools and carry a dead index.

- **Theme:** earnings-data
- **Reviewers:** corporate-actions
- **Location:** `earnings.fiscal_year / earnings.fiscal_quarter; scripts/populate_earnings.py:151-164; mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:332-334, 367-368`
- **Evidence:** SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE fiscal_year IS NULL), count(*) FILTER (WHERE fiscal_quarter IS NULL) FROM earnings -> 55848, 55848 out of 55848. The populate script that derives them (derive_fiscal_period, and the fiscal_quarter/fiscal_year columns in upsert_earnings) was introduced in commit 0900481 dated 2026-06-14, whereas every stored row was created 2026-02-24 — the fix exists but has never been run. get_earnings_calendar (mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py:332-334) and get_earnings_history (:367-368) both SELECT these columns, and sqlschema/18_corporate_actions.sql creates idx_earnings_fiscal on (fiscal_year, fiscal_quarter).
- **Impact:** Every earnings response carries two always-null documented fields, so a caller cannot group results by reporting period and may infer the period is unknown rather than unloaded. idx_earnings_fiscal is dead weight on an 55k-row table.
- **Recommendation:** Backfill in place — the derivation is purely a function of report_date: `UPDATE earnings SET fiscal_quarter = 'Q' || EXTRACT(QUARTER FROM report_date), fiscal_year = EXTRACT(YEAR FROM report_date) WHERE fiscal_quarter IS NULL` — which also gives the next populate_earnings run something to COALESCE against. Note the derived value is the calendar quarter of the report, not the issuer's fiscal quarter, as the docstring at populate_earnings.py:151-158 says; the column name oversells it.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Distinct from the earnings staleness group — these columns were never populated by the loader at all — and distinct from the income_statements fiscal_quarter defect that mislabels the fundamentals chart.

#### 96. `sawa add-symbol` refreshes only 5 of 21 financial_ratios columns on conflict and never updates a restated fundamentals row, diverging from the shared loader.

- **Theme:** fundamentals
- **Reviewers:** fundamentals-tables
- **Location:** `sawa/add_symbol.py:150-156 (ratios ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE) and sawa/add_symbol.py:303-309 (fundamentals ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING)`
- **Evidence:** sawa/add_symbol.py:150-156 upserts financial_ratios with `ON CONFLICT (ticker, date) DO UPDATE SET average_volume, market_cap, price, price_to_earnings, dividend_yield` — 5 of the 21 non-key columns. The other 16 (earnings_per_share, free_cash_flow, enterprise_value, return_on_equity, return_on_assets, debt_to_equity, price_to_book, price_to_sales, ev_to_ebitda, ...) keep whatever was written first. sawa/add_symbol.py:303-309 inserts fundamentals with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`, so a corrected or restated period is never written. The shared loader takes the opposite position: sawa/database/load.py:353-360 builds `col = EXCLUDED.col` for every non-key column.
- **Impact:** Re-running `sawa add-symbol` for an existing ticker (a documented recovery step) silently leaves 16 ratio columns and all fundamentals on their original values, so a corrected provider snapshot for the same date never lands. Two code paths write the same tables with contradictory conflict semantics.
- **Recommendation:** Make add_symbol use the same full-column upsert as sawa/database/load.py, or route it through load_ratios/_load_fundamentals_file so there is one conflict policy for these tables.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Re-running add-symbol is a documented recovery step that silently does not recover corrected provider snapshots.

#### 97. The fundamentals loader silently drops any CSV column it cannot name-match to a DB column, and no check would notice a provider field rename.

- **Theme:** fundamentals
- **Reviewers:** fundamentals-tables
- **Location:** `sawa/database/load.py:757-782 (_load_fundamentals_file column_mapping)`
- **Evidence:** _load_fundamentals_file reads the CSV header, normalises each name (lower, spaces and hyphens to underscores) and keeps it ONLY if the normalised name is already a column of the target table; anything else is dropped with no warning and no count. The only downstream assertion, require_complete_persistence (sawa/database/load.py:104-125), compares row counts (source_rows, eligible_rows, inserted) and never inspects which columns were populated, so a provider renaming e.g. `ebitda` to `ebitda_ttm` would write every row with that column NULL and the run would report fully_persisted=True. I verified this has not happened yet: pg_stats null_frac for every column of all four tables shows no column at 1.00 (worst are commitments_and_contingencies 0.89 and other_cash_adjustments 0.98, both legitimately sparse).
- **Impact:** Latent silent-failure class on the exact axis that has bitten this pipeline before (provider drift in commits 0781f4b and a55ca37). A renamed or newly-nested provider field would silently null out a served column while every counter reports success.
- **Recommendation:** Log and count the unmapped CSV headers, and have require_complete_persistence fail when a column that was non-NULL in the previous load becomes 100% NULL in the new one (or simply when any CSV header fails to map). A per-column non-NULL coverage assertion for a small set of critical columns (revenue, total_assets, net_cash_from_operating_activities) would catch it cheaply.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique and latent, but on the exact axis (provider drift) that produced commits 0781f4b and a55ca37; a renamed field would null out a served column while every count still looked right.

#### 98. The 1,280-calendar-day TA warm-up leaves the ta-lib EMA seed carrying most of the weight for tickers whose adjusted history spans orders of magnitude, so stored ema_100/ema_200 differ from the true full-history EMA by up to 97%.

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** indicators
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:334-362 (EMA_WARMUP_MULTIPLIER / get_required_lookback_days) -> technical_indicators.ema_100, technical_indicators.ema_200`
- **Evidence:** Independent no-talib EMA (seeded with the SMA of the first N bars over the FULL stored history, then the exact alpha recurrence) vs stored, latest date 2026-09-02, for the 30 tickers with the largest max(close)/min(close) ratio:
  EJH  stored ema_200   460.2051  independent 18248.5461  (97.5% off)
  ALLR stored ema_200    25.4839  independent   187.8840  (86.4% off)
  PPCB stored ema_200   642.6904  independent  2669.8119  (75.9% off)
  REVB stored ema_200    13.3637  independent    29.5823  (54.8% off)
  EDBL stored ema_200   786.5209  independent  1485.2613  (47.0% off)
  RNAZ stored ema_200   153.9890  independent   204.3104  (24.6% off)
  CNSP stored ema_200    94.2811  independent   121.0941  (22.1% off)
  APVO stored ema_200   860.4131  independent   915.3679  ( 6.0% off)
EJH ema_100 is also off (stored 5.3966 vs 5.5862). The same 30-ticker run shows sma_20/50/200, ema_12/26, rsi_1…
- **Impact:** ema_200 (a headline trend indicator, used by screen_technical_indicators, detect_crossovers and get_multi_timeframe_alignment) is off by up to two orders of magnitude for these tickers, and the value changes completely if anyone runs `sawa ta-backfill` — the published series is not reproducible from the documented definition.
- **Recommendation:** Size the warm-up in TRADING BARS from stock_prices rather than calendar days (e.g. take the last max(4*200, N) rows via ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT), and additionally seed the long EMAs from the previously stored ema value for the bar before the window (an EMA seed, unlike a fresh SMA seed, makes the incremental result identical to a full recompute). At minimum, document that ema_100/ema_200 are window-approximations and exclude tickers with extreme price-range ratios.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Kept apart from the 'EMA is meaningless for compounded reverse-split tickers' finding: this is about the computation window (stored value vs. true value), that one is about the adjustment basis making the true value itself uninformative.

#### 99. EMA columns for compounded-reverse-split tickers are dominated by the astronomically large adjusted early history (WHLR ema_200 = 5,273,912 at a close of 0.53), making EMA-based tools meaningless for 24+ tickers.

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** ta-engine
- **Location:** `technical_indicators.ema_200 / ema_100 (sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:212-216) for tickers with max(close)/min(close) > ~1e6`
- **Evidence:** On 2026-09-02, 66 rows have ema_200 > 5*sma_200 and 24 have ema_200 > 20*sma_200 (for a sane series EMA-200 and SMA-200 are within a small factor). Top: ADTX close 0.0072, sma_200 123.86, ema_200 20,434,028 (ratio 164,981), ema_100 24,238; WHLR close 0.5254, sma_200 620.26, ema_200 5,273,912.80, ema_100 8,162.65; SMX close 16.11, sma_200 4,179, ema_200 17,579,077; VCIG ratio 1021; XXII ratio 1002; CDT ema_200 46,767 at close 0.91. Python check: talib EMA-200 on WHLR == exact SMA-seeded recurrence, i.e. the value is the mathematically correct EMA of the stored series - the residual weight (199/201)^1184 ~ 7e-6 applied to 2021 closes of ~2.5e10 still contributes millions. 63 rows have ema_200 > 100*close.
- **Impact:** Price-vs-EMA-200, EMA stack alignment and MACD-derived signals for these tickers are pure artifacts of the adjustment basis; screens like 'price above EMA-200' silently exclude them and 'distance from EMA' ranks them as extreme outliers.
- **Recommendation:** Treat EMA/MACD as undefined when the ticker's price span within the loaded history exceeds a threshold (e.g. NULL the EMA family when max/min close > 1e4), or compute EMA-family indicators only over a bounded bar window (e.g. the last 4x period bars, as the incremental path effectively does) rather than the full 5-year series. Document the limitation in technical_indicator_metadata.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Not fixable by a longer warm-up — the fix is to exclude or renormalise these series — so separate from the warm-up finding.

#### 100. get_required_lookback_days is calendar-based and assumes daily bars, so sparse or relisted tickers get fewer than 200 bars in the daily window and store NULL sma_200/ema_200 where a full recompute yields values; the two code paths disagree and the 14-day overwrite flips them.

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** ta-engine
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:353-362 (get_required_lookback_days) and sawa/daily.py:1074-1079 (price_start = recompute_from/last_ta - timedelta(days=lookback_days))`
- **Evidence:** On 2026-09-02, TA rows with sma_200 IS NULL while the ticker has >=200 bars: VIVO 549 bars total, 119 in the 1280-day window; CCXI 548/127; ACH 496/168; CLIM 492/121; HCIC 480/78; DSAC 472/116; AMCI 461/183; GIW 434/189; TPAY 430/137; DOO 416/185; EYES 409/83; ATC 395/79; JATT 293/84; MRLN 290/118; APXT 278/184 (15 shown, LIMIT 15). Across tickers with a 2026-09-02 TA row: 53 have <400 bars in the window despite >=400 total, 79 have <800 despite >=800 (EMA-200 warm-up shorter than the 4x design). The 234-ticker excess-NULL residue in finding 2 is the same mechanism with the older shorter window. A full ta-backfill (get_prices_for_ticker with no start_date) computes sma_200/ema_200 for these dates; the next daily run's recompute_from overwrite (daily.py:800-806, 1106-1110) would set them back to NULL.
- **Impact:** Stored sma_200/ema_200 (and EMA warm-up quality) for ~50-80 thinly traded or relisted tickers depend on which code path last wrote the row rather than on the data; a backfill and the following daily run disagree, producing non-idempotent series.
- **Recommendation:** Fetch the warm-up by bar count instead of calendar days (e.g. SELECT ... WHERE ticker=%s AND date < recompute_from ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT max_period*EMA_WARMUP_MULTIPLIER, then the rows >= recompute_from), or fall back to full history when the calendar window returns fewer than max_period*EMA_WARMUP_MULTIPLIER bars.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Same function as the warm-up finding but a different failure mode (bar count vs. seed weight) needing a different change (count bars, not calendar days).

#### 101. 522 price rows across seven commodity ETFs (DBC, COPX, DBA, UNG, USO, COMT, PDBC) have no technical_indicators row at all: each series is truncated at the front, so the oldest 31-92 bars are missing and the next 19 rows carry NULL sma_20 despite 20 preceding price bars.

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** indicators
- **Location:** `technical_indicators (DBC, COPX, DBA, UNG, USO, COMT, PDBC)`
- **Evidence:** LEFT JOIN of stock_prices to technical_indicators over the full tables: 522 price rows across 7 tickers have no TA row (0 rows in the reverse direction).
  DBC/COPX/DBA/UNG/USO: 92 missing each, prices start 2021-01-04 but TA starts 2021-05-17 (1,423 price rows vs 1,331 TA rows)
  COMT/PDBC:            31 missing each, prices start 2021-01-04 but TA starts 2021-02-18 (1,423 vs 1,392)
Knock-on effect: a full-history check for rows with a complete 20-bar price window but NULL sma_20 returns exactly 19 such rows for each of these 7 tickers (UNG/COPX/USO/DBA/DBC 2021-05-17..2021-06-11, PDBC/COMT 2021-02-18..2021-03-16) — the truncated series restarts its own warm-up. These same 7 tickers are also the 2021-2025 tail of the obv/vwap mismatch in finding 1 (their cumulative series never includes the dropped bars).
- **Impact:** Charts and screens over 2021 H1 silently skip these 7 ETFs, their earliest 19 indicator rows are NULL for no visible reason, and their entire OBV/VWAP series is offset by the dropped bars' contribution for all 1,331 remaining rows.
- **Recommendation:** Run recompute_ta_for_tickers for these 7 tickers (it deletes and rebuilds from the first price row). Then add a doctor check for `count(stock_prices) - count(technical_indicators)` per ticker, which is a one-index-scan invariant that would have caught this.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique — an oldest-N truncation, distinct from the 'revisions never recomputed' group even though the same seven ETFs appear in both. Their whole OBV/VWAP series is offset by the dropped bars' contribution.

#### 102. technical_indicator_metadata.min_periods_required is wrong for 5 of 28 indicators, understating the warm-up by one bar for rsi_14/rsi_21/atr_14/adx_14 and by eight bars for macd_line.

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** indicators
- **Location:** `technical_indicator_metadata.min_periods_required (rsi_14, rsi_21, atr_14, adx_14, macd_line)`
- **Evidence:** Empirical first non-NULL row ordinal for AAPL (contiguous series, TA starts on the first price row, row_number() over date):
  rsi_14 first at bar 15 (metadata says 14), rsi_21 first at bar 22 (metadata 21), atr_14 first at bar 15 (metadata 14), adx_14 first at bar 28 (metadata 27), macd_line first at bar 34 (metadata 26), macd_signal first at bar 34 (metadata 35).
sma_5=5, sma_200=200, ema_12=12, ema_200=200, bb_upper=20, obv=1, vwap=1, volume_sma_20=20 all match.
The engine's own MIN_PERIODS (sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:37-64) is right about adx_14 (28, with the correct comment "First TA-Lib ADX is at zero-based index 2 * 14 - 1") but the DB metadata says 27; the engine and metadata are both wrong about macd_line (26 vs the actual 34). Metadata is otherwise perfectly aligned: all 28 rows map to real columns, and every indicator column has exactly one metadata row.
- **Impact:** list_technical_indicators serves these numbers to callers deciding how much history to request; a caller that trusts min_periods_required = 26 for macd_line will get NULLs for 8 more bars than expected. Purely informational — MIN_PERIODS is used only via max() in get_required_lookback_days (ta_engine.py:360), so the calculation itself is unaffected.
- **Recommendation:** Correct the five metadata rows (rsi_14 -> 15, rsi_21 -> 22, atr_14 -> 15, adx_14 -> 28, macd_line -> 34) and the engine's MIN_PERIODS['macd_line'], and add a test that asserts the metadata table matches ta_engine.MIN_PERIODS.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique and purely informational — list_technical_indicators serves these numbers to callers deciding how much history to request.

#### 103. `sawa ta-backfill --estimate-only` writes technical indicators for the 10 sampled tickers as a side effect of estimating runtime.

- **Theme:** indicators-ta
- **Reviewers:** ta-engine
- **Location:** `sawa/ta_backfill.py:147-149 (estimate_backfill_time calls _init_worker then _process_ticker, which calls load_technical_indicators with commit=True)`
- **Evidence:** estimate_backfill_time(): `_init_worker(database_url)`; `results = [_process_ticker(t) for t in test_tickers]`. _process_ticker (lines 42-117) fetches prices, calculates, and calls load_technical_indicators(conn, indicators, log, commit=not _replace_existing) -> commit=True. run_ta_backfill(estimate_only=True) at lines 257-273 returns before the dry_run check, so there is no guard. The docstring says 'If True, only run time estimation'.
- **Impact:** An operator asking for an estimate on a read-only-intended maintenance window mutates up to 10 tickers' full TA series (benign values, but unexpected writes; also a rollback trap if the estimate is run against a DB mid-migration).
- **Recommendation:** Pass a write=False flag into _process_ticker for estimation (skip load_technical_indicators and just time fetch+calc), or run the estimate inside a transaction that is rolled back.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Benign values, but an unexpected write from a read-only-intended flag, and a rollback trap if run against a database mid-migration.

#### 104. The websocket minute-bar validator still rejects fractional provider volume — the exact drift that broke the daily loader in commit 0781f4b and that Polygon already emits on aggregates.

- **Theme:** intraday-stream
- **Reviewers:** intraday-live
- **Location:** `sawa/api/websocket_client.py:457`
- **Evidence:** websocket_client.py:457 rejects a minute event when `not float(numeric_fields['v']).is_integer()`, routing it to :463 'Ignoring malformed OHLCV minute bar' + invalid_minute_events and dropping the minute entirely. Commit 0781f4b ('Polygon now returns fractional share volume on aggregates, which the daily validator rejected as malformed. 114,481 of 120,700 rows were dropped...') fixed exactly this on the REST/daily path by adding sawa/domain/price_validation.normalize_provider_volume (docstring: 'Polygon reports fractional share volume on aggregate bars ... Round half-up to whole shares at the provider boundary'), but the websocket path was not migrated and does not import it. My one Polygon GET confirms the provider is emitting fractional volume on aggregate bars today: `/v2/aggs/ticker/AAPL/range/5/minute/2026-09-02/2026-09-02` returned v values such as 1560459.244462 and 18443708.92195…
- **Impact:** If Polygon extends fractional volume to the AM websocket channel as it already has on aggregates, every minute event carrying a fractional volume is silently discarded. Affected windows would either vanish or be persisted with an understated volume and an under-populated source_minute_mask, which then loses the superset upsert comparison and can never be corrected. The failure is counted only in invalid_minute_events, which per finding #1 is never reported because record_stream_outcome() never runs — so the symptom would be a quiet, ticker-specific degradation of the live candle rather than a 
- **Recommendation:** Replace the `is_integer()` rejection at websocket_client.py:457 with the same provider-boundary normalization the daily path uses: `volume = normalize_provider_volume(bar_data.get('v'))`, reject only on None, and keep the strict integral contract for the value handed to load_intraday_bars. That restores a single definition of 'what a provider volume is' across both ingest paths.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique and latent, but on a provider axis that has already caused one outage; if Polygon extends fractional volume to the AM channel, affected windows vanish silently.

#### 105. validate_index_code accepts any lowercase-shaped string, so a nonexistent or mistyped index code yields an empty result set instead of an error across every index-filtered tool.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** companies-indices
- **Location:** `mcp_server/validation.py:245-270; mcp_server/tools/_index_filter.py:60-68`
- **Evidence:** validate_index_code (mcp_server/validation.py:264-268) only checks `_INDEX_CODE_RE` — "lowercase letters, digits, or underscores" — and never consults the indices table; docs/INDEX_AND_SECTOR_OVERHAUL_2026-05-16.md:183-185 states this is deliberate so new codes work without a server release. build_index_filter then emits `AND c.ticker IN (SELECT ic.ticker FROM index_constituents ic JOIN indices i ON ic.index_id=i.id WHERE i.code = %(index)s)`, which matches nothing for an unknown code. Confirmed against the live table: the only 7 codes are sp500, nasdaq_listed, us_active, nasdaq100, dow30, mag7, russell1000 — so a plausible-looking `russell2000` or `sp600` (russell2000 is explicitly deferred per sqlschema/39_russell1000_index.sql:15-18) passes validation and produces zero rows. By contrast get_index_constituents queries indices directly and does return `{"error": "Index not found: ..."}`…
- **Impact:** A typo or a reasonable guess at an index code is indistinguishable from "no stocks matched your criteria" across every index-filtered tool (screen_stocks, get_top_movers, get_volume_leaders, list_companies, the sector tools, corporate actions). The caller gets a confident empty answer rather than a correctable error.
- **Recommendation:** Have build_index_filter (or validate_index_code) resolve the code against the indices table once per request and raise the same "Index not found" error get_index_constituents already returns, listing the valid codes. That keeps the release-free extensibility — new rows in `indices` still work immediately — while making unknown codes fail loudly.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. A typo is indistinguishable from 'no stocks matched your criteria'.

#### 106. get_ytd_returns inner-joins the start-date row, silently dropping 685 active tickers (2026 listings and others) that have no price on the YTD start date.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/screener.py:544 (JOIN stock_prices_live p_start ... p_start.date = %(start_dt)s)`
- **Evidence:** SQL: active tickers priced on 2026-09-02 with no row on 2026-01-02 -> 685. A caller passing such a ticker in `tickers` gets no row and no warning. By contrast scan_ytd_performance (scanner.py:246-255) uses a LATERAL first row >= start_date, so the two YTD tools disagree on coverage.
- **Impact:** Requested tickers vanish from the result set; batch callers may misreport 'no data' or compute averages over a biased subset.
- **Recommendation:** Use LATERAL 'first close >= start' (as scanner.py does) or LEFT JOIN and return ytd_pct NULL with the actual start date used; emit a warning listing tickers without a start row.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique and separate from the YTD base-date convention: this is a join shape, that is a date choice.

#### 107. get_volume_profile and calculate_support_resistance_levels fetch only int(lookback*1.5) calendar days, so they compute over fewer bars than requested about 15-17% of the time and the 1-2 day profile fails outright on Mondays and after holidays.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/volume_analysis.py:52 and :65 (start_date = today - int(lookback_days*1.5); rows[-lookback_days:]); mcp_server/tools/support_resistance.py:38 and :53`
- **Evidence:** SQL simulation over the last 261 weekdays using SPY dates, counting rows with date >= D - int(L*1.5) AND date < D (pre-EOD state): L=1 -> 55 days return 0 rows ('No price data found'); L=2 -> 108 days short (6 zero); L=3 -> 26 short; L=5 -> 11 short; L=10 -> 24 short; L=20 -> 44 short; L=30 -> 37 short. Including the current day's EOD row (post-load) still leaves L=1 short on 3 days and L=2 on 9 days.
- **Impact:** Volume profile/value area and cluster/volume S&R levels are computed over fewer bars than the caller asked for (e.g. 17-19 instead of 20) roughly 15-17% of the time, and the 1-2 day profile fails outright on Mondays/after holidays; only the 'trading_days' field reveals it.
- **Recommendation:** Fetch with the inner DESC/LIMIT pattern already used by get_stock_prices (ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT lookback) instead of a calendar-day estimate, or use a >=2.0x buffer plus 5 days.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Same one-line fix pattern in two files (volume_analysis.py:52/65 and support_resistance.py:38/53), filed as one finding.

#### 108. get_fundamentals derives free_cash_flow as OCF + capex assuming capex is negative, but 2,257 cash_flows rows carry a positive purchase_of_property_plant_and_equipment, inflating FCF by 2x|capex| for those periods.

- **Theme:** mcp-read-path
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py:736-737 (net_cash_from_operating_activities + purchase_of_property_plant_and_equipment) vs cash_flows.purchase_of_property_plant_and_equipment sign`
- **Evidence:** SQL: 140831 rows with capex; 137289 negative, 2257 positive. DE quarterly capex alternates sign: 2026-05-03 +237,000,000 (OCF 1,932,000,000 -> tool FCF 2,169M vs 1,695M if the outflow were negative), 2026-02-01 -688M, 2025-11-02 -1,367M, 2025-07-27 -2,306M, 2025-04-27 +236M. Other positive rows: FCEL 2026-04-30 +504K, WSBC 2026-03-31 +1.8M, EXEL TTM +12.5M.
- **Impact:** For ~1.6% of periods (including large caps like DE) the reported free_cash_flow is overstated; quarter-over-quarter FCF series for those tickers oscillate spuriously.
- **Recommendation:** Use OCF - ABS(capex) (capex is an outflow by definition) or flag/normalise positive capex at load time; add a doctor check for positive capex.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. ~1.6% of periods, but it makes quarter-over-quarter FCF series oscillate spuriously for large caps such as DE.

#### 109. MCP failure accounting counts caller argument-validation errors as tool failures, so three consecutive bad-argument calls fire a 'Sawa MCP: <tool> failing' NTFY alert.

- **Theme:** monitoring
- **Reviewers:** mcp-read-path
- **Location:** `mcp_server/server.py:2074 (validate_tool_arguments inside call_tool raises ValueError) and :2851-2870 (_handle_call_tool 'except Exception' -> _record_call_outcome_safely(success=False)); mcp_server/monitoring.py:record_call_outcome`
- **Evidence:** Code path: any ValueError raised by validate_tool_arguments (bad ticker format, limit too large, invalid date) or by _validate_protocol_arguments propagates to _handle_call_tool's generic except and increments the per-tool consecutive-failure counter identically to a database/runtime error; only MCPError (unknown tool) is exempt. tests/test_mcp_monitoring.py:143-162 (test_third_failure_fires_alert) uses error=ValueError('bad input') as the counted failure, so tests encode this behaviour rather than distinguishing it.
- **Impact:** User typos produce phone alerts and reset genuine failure streaks, diluting the signal the alert is meant to carry (module docstring: 'a given tool fails N times in a row').
- **Recommendation:** Classify validation ValueErrors (raised before the tool body runs) as is_error results that do not increment the failure counter, or count them under a separate key; keep DB/runtime exceptions on the alerting path.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. User typos produce phone alerts and reset genuine failure streaks, diluting the signal the alert exists to carry.

#### 110. The doctor FAILED notification does not name the failing check, and the documented claim that warnings are included in notifications is false under the scheduler.

- **Theme:** monitoring
- **Reviewers:** ops-scripts-monitoring
- **Location:** `sawa/utils/monitoring.py:50-56, 126-141; sawa/doctor.py:845-854; scripts/market_scheduler.sh:269; docs/OPERATIONS.md:186-187`
- **Evidence:** _format_stats renders list values as '<key>: N items'; run_doctor puts per-check results under stats['results'] (a list) with no failed/warned name lists, so the push body is 'checks: 27, passed: 24, warnings: 2, failed: 1, job: weekly, results: 27 items' (scheduler.log 9071-9075 on 2026-09-01 23:51 ET; the failing check was only in logs/doctor_20260902_035132.log:30). The scheduler exports SAWA_NOTIFY_SUCCESS=0, so the doctor success push that would carry a warnings count is never sent; OPERATIONS.md:186-187 states warnings 'are printed and included in notifications'.
- **Impact:** Operator must open server logs to learn which check failed; WARN-level findings never reach the phone.
- **Recommendation:** Add stats['failed_checks'] and stats['warned_checks'] (names + one-line message) to the doctor summary and render them; fix the OPERATIONS.md sentence.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Forces a log dive to learn which check failed, and WARN-level findings never reach the phone at all.

#### 111. The doctor check for mv_52week_extremes compares only the matview's MAX(date) to stock_prices' MAX(date) and is WARN-only, so a matview holding wrong values is certified fresh.

- **Theme:** monitoring
- **Reviewers:** scheduling-and-doctor-gaps
- **Location:** `sawa/doctor.py:583-610 (check `mv_52week_extremes.freshness`, severity="warn" at :606)`
- **Evidence:** The check reads `SELECT (SELECT MAX(date) FROM stock_prices), (SELECT MAX(date) FROM mv_52week_extremes)` and passes when the two are equal and the matview date is not in the future. The matview's date column is copied from the underlying prices, so any historical price rewrite that does not advance the latest date leaves both MAX(date) values identical while the stored high_52w/low_52w are computed from the pre-rewrite basis — the exact state round 1 observed this morning (APH high_52w served as 178.52 against a true 89.26) with both tables at MAX(date)=2026-09-02. After tonight's daily run refreshed the matview I recomputed the 252-row extremes for the 12 re-adjusted tickers (ANET APH CMG DECK DELL ETR GE SRE WRB WSM HUIZ) and all now match exactly (APH 89.26 = 89.26), confirming the values had been stale while the date check would have passed. Severity is "warn", so even a genuine dat…
- **Impact:** The doctor is supposed to be the backstop for the daily refresh gate, but it validates only a timestamp that is structurally guaranteed to match, so it can never detect content staleness in a view served by get_52week_extremes and the MCP screening tools. A one-day-late refresh also only WARNs, and warnings are not included in the scheduler's notification.
- **Recommendation:** Replace or supplement the date comparison with a content probe: recompute the 52-week high/low for a small deterministic sample of tickers (including any ticker with a split in the last year) and FAIL when the matview disagrees by more than a small tolerance. Promote a date mismatch to FAIL once the refresh path is reliable.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. It is the intended backstop for the refresh gate that is currently serving APH's 52-week high 2.2x too high, and it validates a timestamp that is structurally guaranteed to match. Kept low as a check defect; the data damage it fails to catch is ranked high separately.

#### 112. Log growth is unbounded outside per-run rotation (cron.log never trimmed, logs/ never pruned, ~130 MB/day of intraday DEBUG) and every extended-hours tick writes a false 'Polygon API unreachable' warning pair.

- **Theme:** ops-hygiene
- **Reviewers:** ops-scripts-monitoring
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:57-60 (trims scheduler.log only), 335-339 (extended-hours handling); sawa/utils/logging.py:26-27,132 (per-run rotation, file always DEBUG)`
- **Evidence:** ~/.sawa/scheduler/cron.log: 90,150 lines / 5.7 MB, first line 2026-02-26, no trim path; $PROJECT_DIR/logs: 305 MB across 33 files for 5 days, intraday_20260831_133003.log + .1-.4 = 128 MB for one session; grep for -delete/unlink/mtime in sawa/ and scripts/ finds only state-file cleanup, so old run files are never removed by code. cron.log contains 4,601 'WARN: Polygon API returned unexpected status: extended-hours' each followed by 'WARN: Polygon API unreachable, using time-based fallback' although the API answered; scheduler.log shows the pair on every pre/post-market tick (e.g. 2026-09-03 08:15-09:15 ET).
- **Impact:** Disk fills slowly and predictably; the fallback path (no early-close awareness) is taken for ~9 hours every day and real outages are indistinguishable from normal extended-hours responses in the log.
- **Recommendation:** Treat exchanges.nyse == 'extended-hours' as closed without warning; add `find logs -mtime +14 -delete` (or logrotate) for $PROJECT_DIR/logs and cron.log; drop intraday file logging to INFO.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Slow predictable disk fill plus ~9 hours a day of false warnings that make real outages indistinguishable from normal extended-hours responses.

#### 113. The scheduler's daily summary never reports 'Prices inserted' because its awk pattern expects 'Inserted N price' while the daily logs 'Inserted N records'.

- **Theme:** scheduler
- **Reviewers:** ops-scripts-monitoring
- **Location:** `scripts/market_scheduler.sh:572-580 vs sawa/daily.py:362, 882`
- **Evidence:** awk regex `/Inserted [0-9,]+ price/`; actual log lines: '  Inserted 5000/120618 records', '  Inserted 120618 records' (logs/daily_20260902_210002.log:10658, 10684). Every 'Daily completed:' summary in scheduler.log (e.g. 9493-9495) contains only 'Latest prices', 'Intraday ran', 'Daily' lines — no 'Prices inserted'.
- **Impact:** The operator's nightly push lacks the one volume metric it was built to carry; a run that inserted 0 rows looks identical to a normal one.
- **Recommendation:** Match `Inserted [0-9,]+ records$` on the final summary line (or have daily print a stable `prices_inserted=` marker) and add a test.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. The nightly push is missing the one volume metric it was built to carry, so a run that inserted 0 rows looks normal.

#### 114. rrg_relative_strength and rrg_rotation_signals (207k rows each, 65 MB) exist live with no writer in sawa or any sibling repo, while docs/TA_EXPANSION_PLAN.md states they are 'Not in DB'.

- **Theme:** schema-drift
- **Reviewers:** schema-drift
- **Location:** `live tables public.rrg_relative_strength, public.rrg_rotation_signals; docs/TA_EXPANSION_PLAN.md:43`
- **Evidence:** `select min(date), max(date), count(*), count(distinct ticker), max(created_at) from rrg_relative_strength` -> 2021-06-10 | 2026-03-06 | 207104 | 249 | 2026-03-07 03:55:02. Tables have their own PKs/indexes (idx_rrg_rs_benchmark, idx_rrg_signals_crossing ...). grep -rl rrg_ in /home/seed/code (py/sql/md) finds only design docs in everything-os; no INSERT anywhere. docs/TA_EXPANSION_PLAN.md:43: '"rrg_relative_strength / rrg_rotation_signals already in DB" | **Not in DB.**'
- **Impact:** Orphaned, six-months-stale tables (65 MB) that contradict the design doc; a future plan step could either collide with or wrongly trust them.
- **Recommendation:** Decide ownership: either add them to sqlschema + EXPECTED_TABLES with a writer, or drop them and correct TA_EXPANSION_PLAN.md.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Orphaned six-month-stale tables that contradict the design doc a future plan step would trust.

#### 115. A no-drop schema replay is far from metadata-only: files 11/17/30 rebuild 11 BRIN indexes that 41 immediately drops, and 24/42/48 each drop and fully recompute the 892 MB mv_52week_extremes, so every 'idempotent' replay performs hours of rewrites under ACCESS EXCLUSIVE locks.

- **Theme:** schema-drift
- **Reviewers:** schema-drift
- **Location:** `sqlschema/11_technical_indicators.sql:44-48, 17_extended_sma.sql:15-17, 30_add_adx_and_extended_indicators.sql:16-18 (CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS ... USING BRIN) vs 41_dashboard_spine_and_integrity.sql:129-139 (DROP INDEX IF EXISTS x11); 24:9-11/119-131, 42:26-29/109-127, 48:66-70/117-126 (DROP/CREATE MATERIALIZED VIEW + REFRESH)`
- **Evidence:** Live: pg_indexes shows none of idx_ta_rsi_14, idx_ta_rsi_21, idx_ta_atr_14, idx_ta_macd_line, idx_ta_volume_ratio, idx_ta_sma_100/150/200, idx_ta_adx_14, idx_ta_bb_width_pct, idx_ta_dollar_volume_sma_20 exist (41 dropped them).
