Because they do not exist, `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` in 11/17/30 will build each of them (full heap scan of technical_indicators, 3653 MB, reltuples 10,002,580) on every replay before 41 drops them again. mv_52week_extremes is 892 MB over 10,034,177 rows and is dropped/recreated unconditionally by 24, 42 and 48 (48 additionally REFRESHes). README.md and docs/MAINTENANCE.md:354 describe the replay as the routine, idempotent way to apply any SQL change.
- **Impact:** Each replay of the schema set costs many full-table scans and rebuilds while holding ACCESS EXCLUSIVE locks on stock_prices/technical_indicators dependents; the MCP server and daily pipeline are blocked for the duration, and a statement_timeout or a failure late in the set discards all of the work. This turns a routine 'I changed an SQL file' upgrade into an outage window.
- **Recommendation:** Guard index creation in 11/17/30 with a check that 41 has not retired them (or delete the CREATE INDEX lines from the earlier files since the final state is what matters), and make 24/42/48 conditional so the matview is only dropped/recreated when the column type actually changes (check atttypmod first). Add a 'replay is a no-op on an up-to-date schema' test using a schema-only database.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Separate from the narrowing-migration failure: fixing the overflow would expose this cost rather than remove it.

#### 116. 80% of published stock_character classifications are accepted on a single Hurst window while another window says 'random walk', contradicting the spec's requirement that all three windows agree.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/stock_character.py:109-127 (_classify_hurst 'leaning' branch)`
- **Evidence:** docs/STOCK_CHARACTER.md:60-84 states 'Require consistency across all three windows before accepting classification', 'Range-bound: < 0.45 all three windows', 'Trending: > 0.55 all three windows', and defines MEDIUM as 'all three windows agree, secondary confirmation marginal'. The code instead returns (class, unanimous=False) whenever some windows show a signal and the rest sit in [0.45,0.55], and only downgrades the confidence to MEDIUM. In run 2026-09-02: 1,960 of the 2,065 range_bound/MEDIUM rows and 594 of the 662 trending/MEDIUM rows are leaning - 2,554 of 3,180 rows (80%). Cohort means confirm it: the trending class averages hurst_3yr = 0.5478, below its own 0.55 gate; the range_bound class averages hurst_3yr = 0.4687, above its 0.45 gate. Example: XOM is stored as trending with 0.4861 / 0.5047 / 0.5761 - two of three windows in the random-walk band and the longest one on the mean-…
- **Impact:** The publication gate the spec relies on to keep the output list small and clean is not applied, which is the main reason 3,180 names are published instead of the 'clean list of 30-100' the spec targets. A 'MEDIUM' label conflates 'all windows agree but the secondary test was marginal' with 'one window out of three showed anything at all'.
- **Recommendation:** Either enforce the documented rule (require all valid windows on the same side; drop leaners) or update docs/STOCK_CHARACTER.md and add a stored column distinguishing unanimous from leaning so a consumer can filter. The current state has the code and its build spec disagreeing on the system's central gate.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. The publication gate that was supposed to keep the output to 30-100 clean names is why 3,180 are published instead.

#### 117. The latest stock_character run is stamped run_date 2026-09-02 but was computed on 2026-09-01 ET from prices through 2026-09-01, a residue of the UTC-vs-market-date bug fixed after it was written.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `stock_character_classification.run_date = 2026-09-02 (all four stock_character_* tables)`
- **Evidence:** SELECT min/max(created_at AT TIME ZONE 'America/New_York') for run_date 2026-09-02 returns 2026-09-01 20:08:31 .. 2026-09-01 22:31:20 - the rows were written during the ET evening of 2026-09-01, so get_market_date() would have returned 2026-09-01. The data confirms the vintage: for the 3,084 tickers with a 2026-09-01 bar, scorecard.current_price equals the 2026-09-01 close in 3,083 cases, while only 127 of 3,180 match the 2026-09-02 close. The fix that replaced date.today() with get_market_date() (sawa/stock_character_batch.py:207-211) landed in commit 0781f4b at 2026-09-02 04:56 UTC - after these rows were written. The database therefore holds two run_dates (2026-09-01 and 2026-09-02) for what is essentially the same computation over the same prices (3,172 tickers in common, 2 character changes).
- **Impact:** The run that any consumer or the doctor would treat as 'latest' is labelled with a market date one trading day ahead of the prices it describes, and the run_date key no longer identifies the data vintage. Historical run_dates written before 0781f4b are affected the same way whenever the batch finished after 20:00 ET.
- **Recommendation:** Delete or re-stamp the 2026-09-02 row set (it duplicates 2026-09-01), and consider deriving run_date from MAX(date) of the prices actually used rather than from a wall clock, so the stamp is a property of the data.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. The run key no longer identifies the data vintage; one stale run, fixed forward but not corrected in place.

#### 118. HVN and LVN are fixed quantile slices of the price axis rather than detected nodes, so AT_HVN and IN_LVN fire mechanically and 162 tickers are simultaneously 'at a high-volume node' and 'in an air pocket'.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/stock_character_baseline.py:262-278 and sawa/calculation/stock_character_detect.py:161-186`
- **Evidence:** _compute_volume_profile takes the 70th and 20th percentiles of the non-empty bucket volumes, so it always returns the top 15 of 50 buckets as HVN and the bottom 10 as LVN regardless of whether any real node exists (live: mean 14.50 HVN levels, max 17; mean 9.75 LVN levels, max 11, for all 2,333 range_bound rows). The detector then fires AT_HVN or IN_LVN when the price is within HVN_PROXIMITY_PCT (1%) of *any* such bucket midpoint. Measured on the real profiles, the +/-1% neighbourhood of the 15 HVN levels covers 25% (PFE), 23% (TJX) and 30% (D) of the entire price axis; for JPST - a near-constant NAV ETF whose 50 buckets are 0.017 wide, 0.03% of price - it covers 100%, and JPST's stored scorecard duly carries both AT_HVN and IN_LVN. Live run 2026-09-02: AT_HVN 928 and IN_LVN 589 of 2,333 range_bound tickers, and 162 tickers carry both flags at once (scorecard at_hvn AND in_lvn = 162).
- **Impact:** Two of the spec's headline setups ('consolidating at a major volume node' and 'price in an air pocket - fast move likely') are asserted for 40% and 25% of range-bound names respectively, and are asserted simultaneously - a logical contradiction - for 162 of them.
- **Recommendation:** Select HVN/LVN as local extrema of the smoothed volume-at-price profile (peaks/valleys with a prominence threshold) instead of fixed quantile slices, cap the number returned, and express the proximity test in bucket widths (or in ATR) rather than a fixed 1% of price so it degrades sensibly for low-volatility instruments.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Two headline setups asserted for 40% and 25% of range-bound names, and asserted simultaneously for 162 of them — self-contradictory output.

#### 119. typical_cycle_days is an artifact of the hard-coded extrema order parameter, not a per-stock statistic: 93% of values fall in 18-23 days with a standard deviation of 1.5.

- **Theme:** stock-character
- **Reviewers:** stock-character-calc
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/stock_character_baseline.py:283-311 (_compute_typical_cycle_days, order=10)`
- **Evidence:** The detector marks bar i as an extreme when it is the max or min of the +/-10 bar window and then enforces a minimum spacing of `order` bars, so the mean spacing is pinned near 2*order regardless of the series. Live, run 2026-09-02, all 2,333 range_bound rows: min 15.50, max 32.40, mean 20.54, stddev 1.474, 5th/95th percentile 18.3/22.9, and 2,172 (93%) fall between 18 and 23. My offline recomputation gives 21.3 (PFE), 20.0 (TJX), 19.5 (D), 20.2 (JPST) for four very different instruments including a near-constant-NAV bond ETF.
- **Impact:** A column advertised as 'avg days between range extremes' carries no per-stock information; anything that ranked or filtered on it would be ranking noise. Stored for 2,333 tickers every week.
- **Recommendation:** Estimate the cycle from the dominant period of the detrended series (FFT/periodogram or autocorrelation peak), or make `order` adaptive; failing that, drop the column rather than publish a constant.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. A column advertised as 'avg days between range extremes' that carries no per-stock information, stored weekly for 2,333 tickers.

#### 120. TA tests contain no known-answer checks for any indicator and no incremental-vs-full convergence test for EMA/RSI/ADX, and the DB seed query is exercised only through a fake cursor, so the OBV/VWAP and EMA-seed regressions shipped with a green suite.

- **Theme:** testing
- **Reviewers:** ta-engine
- **Location:** `tests/calculation/test_ta_engine.py:294-404; tests/test_ta_incremental.py:16-57 (_SeedConnection fake); tests/test_ta_persistence_atomicity.py (psycopg.connect monkeypatched)`
- **Evidence:** test_ta_engine.py asserts only structure: results non-None after warm-up, RSI within 0..100, OBV equals talib.OBV, seeded VWAP/OBV equals full at 6 boundaries on a synthetic 96-bar series. No test compares SMA/EMA/RSI/MACD/BB/ATR/ADX to reference values; no test that a seeded window of max_period*EMA_WARMUP_MULTIPLIER bars reproduces full-history EMA-200/RSI/ADX within storage precision (TestGetRequiredLookbackDays only checks the constant >= 600). test_ta_incremental.py:44-57 verifies get_cumulative_indicator_seed builds the expected SQL string and unpacks a hard-coded row; the SQL itself never runs. Independent verification in this review (RSI/ATR/EMA/BB manual == talib to 1e-6; seeded 880-bar window == full to <=0.0051 on 23 real tickers) found the math correct, so this is a coverage gap rather than a math defect.
- **Impact:** Formula or seeding regressions (like the two that produced findings 1-2) can ship with a green test suite; the only production signal is a full recompute diff, which nobody runs routinely.
- **Recommendation:** Add (a) known-answer tests for each indicator against hand-computed values on a short fixture, (b) a convergence test asserting a seeded window of the configured lookback reproduces full-history EMA-200/RSI-21/ADX-14 within 1e-3 on a long synthetic series, and (c) an integration test (or doctor check) that runs get_cumulative_indicator_seed against Postgres and compares with the engine's unseeded full computation.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Low as a defect, but it is the reason two of this audit's high-severity indicator findings existed undetected for months.

#### 121. volume_sma_20 is stored as BIGINT while volume_ratio is computed from the unrounded float average, so 14,428 rows are served that contradict themselves — a 20-day average volume of 0 next to a volume ratio of 6.67.

- **Theme:** volume-integrity
- **Reviewers:** volume-and-archive
- **Location:** `sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:264-270`
- **Evidence:** sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:264 `volume_sma_20 = talib.SMA(volumes, timeperiod=20)` (float64); :268-270 `volume_ratio = np.where(volume_sma_20 > 0, volumes / volume_sma_20, np.nan)` uses that float; :316 stores `volume_sma_20=_to_int(...)`, rounding it to the BIGINT column. Live consequence: SELECT ticker,date,volume,volume_sma_20,volume_ratio FROM technical_indicators JOIN stock_prices USING (ticker,date) WHERE ticker='XXII' AND date BETWEEN '2024-09-03' AND '2024-09-06' -> 2024-09-03 volume=1, volume_sma_20=0, volume_ratio=6.666667; 2024-09-05 volume=1, volume_sma_20=0, volume_ratio=5.000000. Also ACON 2022-10-07: volume=1, volume_sma_20=0, volume_ratio=2.000000. A consumer recomputing volume/volume_sma_20 from the two stored columns divides by zero; the guard `volume_sma_20 > 0` protects the float that is never stored, not the integer that is.
- **Impact:** Any consumer that reads both columns (the MCP indicator tools return both side by side) gets mutually inconsistent numbers, and the divide-by-zero guard is applied to a value different from the one persisted. 14,428 rows are in this state.
- **Recommendation:** Store volume_sma_20 as NUMERIC (as dollar_volume_sma_20 already is), or derive volume_ratio from the same rounded integer that gets persisted so the stored triple is internally consistent.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. Surfaces because of the collapsed-volume defect but is an independent storage/derivation mismatch at ta_engine.py:264-270 that would persist after that repair.

#### 122. get_volume_profile and get_advanced_volume_indicators mix magnitudes across a reverse split inside one window, producing a point of control at a price the stock never traded and OBV/CMF verdicts driven entirely by the final bars.

- **Theme:** volume-integrity
- **Reviewers:** volume-and-archive
- **Location:** `mcp_server/tools/volume_analysis.py:76-113 and :338-478`
- **Evidence:** Live call get_volume_profile(ticker='LBGJ', lookback_days=120, price_bins=10) -> price_low 2.1, price_high 2200 (a 1,047x range in one profile), poc {price: 112, volume: 37,519,415}, value_area_low 2.1 / value_area_high 221.89, first bin holding 98.8% of all volume, warnings:[]. LBGJ's last close is ~$2, so the reported 'point of control' of $112 is a split-adjusted price level at which no share ever changed hands. Live call get_advanced_volume_indicators(ticker='WHLR', lookback_days=252) -> the returned series runs from 2025-09-04 (close 12528, volume 57) to 2026-09-03 (close 0.5119, volume 858,928); latest obv -57,097,878, cmf_20 -0.4911, vwap 15.62 against a 0.51 close, and the emitted signals read 'OBV falling with price - confirms downtrend' and 'Price (0.51) below VWAP (15.62) - bearish'. The 2025 half of that OBV series moves in units of tens while the 2026 half moves in units of …
- **Impact:** Both tools present split-adjustment artefacts as tradeable structure: a POC and value area at prices that never existed, and a VWAP/OBV/CMF verdict that is arithmetically dominated by the post-split bars. Neither response carries a warning that the window spans a split (grep for 'split' in mcp_server/tools/volume_analysis.py and movers.py returns nothing).
- **Recommendation:** Detect a recorded split inside the requested window and either warn explicitly or restrict the analysis to the post-split segment; a 1,000x price range inside one volume profile should be refused rather than binned.
- **Consolidation / re-rating:** Unique. A window-composition problem in the read path, separate from the stored-volume destruction and from the anomaly-detector's fabricated events.

## Coverage gaps the critic identified after round one

Each of these became a round-two audit; the findings above include their results.

- **fundamentals (balance_sheets / income_statements / cash_flows / financial_ratios)** — 146 MB and ~408k rows across four tables covering 4,570 tickers were owned by no dimension. Every reviewer wrote them off as "quarterly snapshots, known/accepted", which covered only their staleness — not their contents. Nothing checked duplicate (ticker, period_end, timeframe) rows, restatement handling via filing_date, the 66,744 trailing_twelve_months rows in cash_flows and 66,954 in income_statements that would triple-count in any unfiltered aggregate, NULL density per column, or whether v_latest_fundamentals picks the right period. The one filed finding (capex sign in FCF) is a symptom found from the read path, not an audit of the tables.
- **financial_ratios split basis (new defect, same class as the confirmed earnings finding)** — financial_ratios stores price, earnings_per_share, market_cap, free_cash_flow and enterprise_value frozen at snapshot time, while stock_prices is continuously re-adjusted for splits. No dimension checked it: the corporate-actions reviewer found the identical freeze in earnings.eps_actual and stopped there; the mcp-read-path reviewer used financial_ratios but only questioned dividend_yield's units.
- **quarterly pipeline scheduling** — `sawa quarterly` is documented in docs/OPERATIONS.md:60 as a quarterly job but has no slot in scripts/market_scheduler.sh and no crontab entry — crontab holds only market_scheduler.sh, the turbulence chart job and a Postgres backup. It is manual-only, exactly like scripts/populate_earnings.py, which the ops dimension did flag. Nobody noticed the quarterly job has the same defect.
- **backups / disaster recovery** — No dimension checked whether the database is backed up. It is not. /home/seed/scripts/backup_postgres.sh runs weekly from cron (Sun 01:00) and has failed 14 consecutive times with "permission denied while trying to connect to the docker API at unix:///var/run/docker.sock" — the script still targets docker while Postgres was moved to rootless podman. The last successful backup is postgres_backup_20260524_010001.tar, 102 days ago.
- **PostgreSQL table health (bloat / autovacuum)** — No reviewer queried pg_stat_user_tables. stock_prices carries 1,481,329 dead tuples (14.8% of live rows) with last_autovacuum 2026-08-17 — 17 days ago; technical_indicators carries 1,062,485 (10.6%), last autovacuumed 2026-09-02. Neither table has ever had a manual VACUUM (vacuum_count = 0).
- **cross-table split-basis inventory** — Four dimensions each independently found a stale-split-basis defect in the one table they owned (earnings eps, mv_52week_extremes, companies.market_cap — noticed and dismissed as provider timing, and financial_ratios which I found above). Nobody built the inventory: which stored columns anywhere in the schema hold a price- or share-denominated value that a split invalidates, and which of them have a repair path.
- **stock_character (calculation correctness and consumers)** — Two dimensions checked stock_character cross-table row integrity — and it is genuinely clean — but nobody validated the calculation: sawa/calculation/stock_character.py, _detect.py, _scorecard.py, _baseline.py and hurst.py, plus the batch driver, produce 61k classifications, 61k scorecards, 61k baselines and 137k flags nightly with no known-answer test and no reviewer. Separately, grep shows no MCP tool and no CLI reads any of the four tables: readers exist only in sawa/database/stock_character.py and sawa/doctor.py.
- **blast radius of the volume-collapse defect** — The split-adjustment volume rounding defect is filed at medium severity with no measurement of how many bars it destroyed. The prices dimension separately cleared 28,800 zero-volume bars as genuine ETF no-trade days, but that sweep was limited to bars since 2024-09-03 with open=high=low=close, which excludes this class entirely.
- **sawa/repositories/ layer** — 3,200 lines (base.py 544, database.py 1494, factory.py 369, polygon_prices.py 348, cache.py 203, rate_limiter.py 171) that no dimension reviewed. The mcp-read-path reviewer read only database.py:1091-1247 as a TA consumer. This is live code, not legacy: SyncRateLimiter is imported by daily.py, weekly.py, quarterly.py, coldstart.py, corporate_actions.py, split_adjust.py and add_symbol.py — every Polygon-calling path in the system — and DatabaseIndexRepository backs three cli index commands.
- **mcp_server/charts/** — The chart subsystem (core, widgets, themes and four renderers — economy.py 474, fundamentals.py 357, prices.py 292, ratios.py 276) was essentially unreviewed. Every MCP tool returns a `chart` alongside its data; the audit inspected ratios.py:154 (the x100 yield display) and the prices.py render path only.
- **test suite execution** — Nine of eleven dimensions explicitly declined to run pytest ("skipped for time"); the other two ran subsets of 88 and 154 tests. Nobody ran the whole suite, including against the uncommitted split-basis work that is about to be committed.
- **concurrency and connection budget** — The interaction between MCP reads and the nightly write transaction was inferred from lock semantics but never observed. Nobody sampled pg_locks or pg_stat_activity during a daily run, and nobody compared max_connections against the sum of the MCP pool, the TA backfill worker pool (one connection per ticker per worker), the intraday loader and the external turbulence cron that shares this database.
- **stock_prices_backup provenance** — The ops dimension correctly flagged a 376 MB / 4.68M-row public.stock_prices_backup absent from sqlschema, EXPECTED_TABLES and docs, but nobody established what it is, what price basis it holds, or whether restoring from it would be safe.

## What each reviewer covered

Negative results matter as much as findings: this records what was checked and came back clean.

### prices (data)

All queries run read-only against the live DB. Checks performed, including those that found nothing:

CLEAN (no defect found):
- Future-dated rows: `SELECT count(*) FROM stock_prices WHERE date > CURRENT_DATE` = 0; rows after 2026-09-02 = 0.
- Weekend rows: `extract(dow from date) IN (0,6)` = 0 rows.
- Holiday rows: LEFT JOIN of stock_prices against a 57-date VALUES list of NYSE holidays 2021-2026 (incl. 2025-01-09 Carter mourning, Good Fridays, 2026-07-03 observed) returned 0 rows on any holiday.
- OHLC ordering beyond the CHECK: open>high = 0, open<low = 0, close>high = 0, close<low = 0 across all 10.03M rows. (The CHECK only enforces high>=low, so this is data quality, not constraint-enforced; a constraint covering open/close within [low,high] is absent but currently unviolated.)
- Duplicate (ticker,date): 0 (PK-enforced). Price tickers absent from companies: 0 (FK-enforced). Inactive companies holding prices: 0.
- created_at anomalies: NULL = 0; created_at::date < date = 0; created_at in the future = 0.
- Precision/rounding: sampled `TABLESAMPLE SYSTEM (2)` over 2025-01..2026-09, bucketing trailing-zero-stripped decimal places by quarter. Stable across every quarter (~77% <=2dp, ~9% 3dp, ~18% 4dp, ~0% >=5dp). No sign of a mixed provider basis shift.
- stock_prices_live view: definition dumped via pg_get_viewdef. Value parity vs base table for all rows since 2026-06-01 = 0 mismatches; base rows since 2026-08-01 not visible in the view = 0. Sampled 10 tickers: view_rows = base_rows + 1 in every case, the extra row being today's `intraday` branch for 2026-09-03; market_clock resolves correctly to 2026-09-03 ET. No defect.
- Split-adjustment coherence (the requested sample check, run exhaustively rather than on 30 tickers): 1,271 splits with execution_date >= 2024-09-03, measuring close(first bar on/after exec) / close(last bar before). 1,122 step within [0.8,1.25] i.e. correctly adjusted; only 17 step outside [0.5,2.0]. Direction convention confirmed via NVDA 2024-06-10 (split_from=1, split_to=10) and code at sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:128 — share multiplier is split_to/split_from. No split shows a step equal to its own ratio at any large ratio except WTO 2026-02-17 (already known/provider-side). Split adjustment is broadly sound.
- Latest-date coverage per exchange (active companies, LATERAL max(date)): XNAS 4390/4669 current, ARCX 2377/2429, XNYS 1939/1999, BATS 1275/1313, OTC Link 12/12, XASE 7/8.
- Flat zero-volume bars: 28,800 rows since 2024-09-03 with open=high=low=close and volume=0. 28,754 of them (1,113 tickers) are `type='ETF'`. Inspected PMNV (26.155-26.245, daily volume 6-111 shares): these are genuine no-trade days on micro-liquidity ETFs, faithfully stored provider data. NOT reported as a defect. (Only 559 repeat the prior close, so they are not carry-forwards.)
- Unexplained large discontinuities: 244 events / 201 tickers since 2024-09-03 with a >4x or <0.25x move on consecutive trading days and no split within +/-5 days. I chased the most extreme (PAVS 2025-11-28 126,000 -> 2025-12-01 9,360) and cross-checked against Polygon `v3/reference/splits?ticker=PAVS`: Polygon returns exactly the 4 splits already stored (2022-10-11, 2025-12-18, 2026-03-31, 2026-06-29). Rows on both sides share created_at 2026-02-17, i.e. one coherent fetch. Concluded genuine provider data, NOT a missing-split defect. Not reported.
- BK cross-check: `v1/open-close/BK/2026-09-02` and `v3/reference/tickers/BK` both return NOT_FOUND, proving BK's absence is provider-side delisting/rename, not a dropped fetch by sawa. This turned what looked like a critical missing-data finding into finding #3 (universe not retired). Two small Polygon GETs total.

PARTIALLY COVERED / NOT REACHED:
- Per-ticker gap analysis: 963 tickers have interior gaps over the last 2 years (35,742 missing trading days) against a 502-day calendar derived from the distinct-date set. After excluding tickers flagged by the list_date reassignment detector, 897 remain with 29,758 missing days, and 13 of those have avg volume >= 1M with >20 missing days (CALY, SPYM, GOLD, CAPS, DXF, ARIS, B, WBUY, BMNR, SHAZ, SNYR, VIVK, MRNO). I inspected these and they are overwhelmingly the SAME ticker-reassignment class as finding #1, just missed by the list_date detector because Polygon left list_date at the prior issuer's value (e.g. B is Barrick since 2025-05-09 but list_date=1985-02-01 from Barnes Group; CALY is named "Callaway Golf" but the series changes identity 2024-10-09 -> 2026-01-16). I did NOT build a second detector for that variant, so finding #1's 71-ticker / 25,071-row count is a LOWER BOUND. The residual illiquid-ticker gaps are consistent with genuine no-trade days and I did not attempt to separate them further.
- I did not verify per-row OHLC values against Polygon beyond the two GETs above; per-row price accuracy is largely unaudited.
- I reviewed the uncommitted sawa/split_adjust.py diff (pre-horizon re-base) and sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py SplitAdjuster for the volume issue in finding #4, but did not review the coldstart.py, database/load.py, or scripts/restore_quarantined_prices.py portions of the working tree.
- technical_indicators and mv_52week_extremes were queried only as downstream evidence for price defects; I did not audit them as a dimension.
- Dividends were not checked at all (out of dimension).
- Not reported but noted: `stock_prices_ohlcv_sane` permits volume = 0 and does not constrain open/close into [low,high]; both would be cheap guards.

### corporate-actions (data)

CODE READ (full): sawa/corporate_actions.py, sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py (incl. the uncommitted SplitAdjuster), scripts/populate_earnings.py, mcp_server/tools/corporate_actions.py, mcp_server/server.py tool schemas for these tools, sawa/weekly.py:575-610 + sawa/daily.py:460-505 call sites, sqlschema/18,19,20,46, sawa/doctor.py split checks, git log/blame for populate_earnings + `positive=True`. Reviewed `git diff sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py` (SplitAdjuster) — factor()/adjust_row() direction and volume scaling verified correct by hand (reverse 1:10 -> price x10, volume /10); no defect found there beyond the data it consumes.

SPLITS — CHECKED: row count 2,171 (2021-01-04..2026-09-03); ratio distribution; absurd ratios (CRWG 40000:1, PPCB 60000:1 — real reverse splits on sub-penny shells, left alone); duplicate/overlapping splits (UNIQUE(ticker,execution_date) enforced live; 7 pairs <=30 days apart, all plausible serial reverse splits); splits for tickers with no prices = 0; splits for inactive companies = 0; 1:1 no-op splits = 32 (reported); price-vs-split continuity for all 1,851 splits with meaningful ratios and adjacent surrounding bars — 1,632 consistent, 1 "unadjusted" (WTO, known), 7 inverted (reported), 211 "other" spot-checked and dominated by genuine penny-stock volatility; inverse check for MISSING splits (near-integer one-day jumps in liquid names with no split row within +/-2 days) — top candidates INHD, QMMM, SPRB, STI, DFNS, BIRD, MLTX, WOLF, QURE, PRAX all inspected with OHLCV windows and all show 10-300x volume explosions -> genuine squeeze/news/reorg events, NOT missing splits, so no finding.

DIVIDENDS — CHECKED: 45,228 rows, 2025-02-28..2026-09-02; type distribution (CD 44,854 / SC 372 / 'recurring' 2 — reported); frequency distribution; duplicates by (ticker, ex_date) = 73 pairs, every one a legitimate CD+SC pair on the same date (ACNB, ARI, BKE, CME, ...) so the composite identity key is behaving; NULL/zero/negative amounts (1 zero — ARTY, reported; 0 negative); tiny amounts (8 rows <0.0005); pay_date < ex_date = 22 rows — all large specials (NLOP 6.75, SVA 55.00, AD 23.00) matching NYSE due-bill convention where the ex-date follows the payable date, so NOT a defect; record_date < ex_date = 29 rows and declaration_date > ex_date = 0; ex-dates on non-trading days = 2 (HSHP on MLK Day 2026-01-19 and Juneteenth 2026-06-19) — 2 rows, left as noise; cash_amount vs cum-dividend close (last close strictly before ex-date) — 56 over 50%, 30 over 100%, every one a fund liquidating/termination distribution (ARKD, CUSD, DVX* series) with no subsequent bar, so provider-correct; annualized yield >200% using frequency — all option-income ETFs (QDTE, XDTE, YBTC) with year-end capital-gain distributions; frequency vs observed cadence for 2,764 tickers with >=6 payments — only 7 mismatches (reported); coverage for 25 known large-cap payers (AAPL MSFT JNJ KO PG XOM JPM CVX PEP MRK ABBV HD WMT MCD CSCO VZ T PFE IBM UNH CAT MMM O NVDA AVGO) — all 4/4 quarterly (O 12/12), no gaps; dividends for inactive companies = 0; dividend_identity_conflicts = 0 rows, table shape matches migration 46 exactly and the live unique index dividends_normalized_identity_uidx matches the loader's ON CONFLICT target with the legacy 3-column constraint correctly dropped (no schema drift).

EARNINGS — CHECKED: 55,848 rows / 2,792 tickers, 1999-04-22..2026-09-24; all rows created in one batch on 2026-02-24 (reported); duplicates blocked by UNIQUE(ticker,report_date) live; near-duplicate same-quarter pairs within 12 days = 69, of which 15 are surviving phantom estimates (12 inside and 3 outside the 7-day PHANTOM_RECONCILE_DAYS window) — small enough that I left it out of the findings; report_date sanity — 101 weekend report dates (0.18%), 1 ticker with earnings but no prices; surprise_pct vs (actual-estimate)/|estimate|*100 — 36,558/51,664 match at percent scale, 0 at fraction scale, and the 15,106 "mismatches" are all explained by eps_estimate/eps_actual being stored rounded to 2dp while surprise_pct comes from unrounded inputs (ratios cluster 0.75-1.25), so the SCALE IS CORRECT and this is not a defect; eps scale across historical splits — NVDA (10:1 2024-06-10), TSLA (3:1 2022-08-25) and AAPL histories are all internally back-adjusted by yfinance, i.e. consistent WITHIN the table (the defect is that the basis is frozen — see finding 1); future report dates without estimates = 1; revenue_actual 100% NULL (column unused, no consumer, not reported).

CROSS-TABLE / TOOL REPLAYS — CHECKED: replayed get_dividends(upcoming_only), get_ex_dividend_calendar, get_earnings_calendar(upcoming_only), get_recent_splits(30) and get_dividend_yield_leaders(min_yield=2.0) as literal SQL; financial_ratios cardinality per ticker (139 tickers 1 row, 3,767 tickers 2 rows, 166 tickers 3 rows) and dividend_yield unit verified against 13 known payers plus mcp_server/charts/renderers/ratios.py:154 which multiplies by 100 to display.

ONE EXTERNAL CALL USED: a single Polygon GET (v2/aggs/MDXH 2023-11-10..16, adjusted=false) to establish ground truth for finding 5.

NOT COVERED / COULD NOT CHECK: (a) I did not cross-check the remaining 6 mis-adjusted split tickers against Polygon (only MDXH) — the budget allowed one call, so their diagnosis rests on internal mutual inconsistency plus the MDXH pattern; (b) I could not determine whether the bogus MDXH-class split rows originate in Polygon's reference feed or were introduced locally, because both paths produce the identical arithmetic — resolving that needs a v3/reference/splits call per ticker; (c) `dividends` only holds 18 months of history (2025-02-28 onward) so I could not audit dividend continuity across the full 5-year price window; (d) I did not run pytest, and did not audit LT/ST dividend types (none present); (e) the 211 "other" split/price discontinuities were sampled (top 40 by magnitude), not exhaustively adjudicated — a few of those may be additional instances of finding 5.

### indicators (data)

All DB work was done read-only via `PGOPTIONS='-c default_transaction_read_only=on'`. Independent recomputes were done two ways: (a) exact NUMERIC window functions in SQL, (b) a pure-Python reimplementation (no ta-lib) of SMA/EMA/Wilder-RSI/Wilder-ATR/Wilder-ADX/OBV in /tmp/claude-1002/-home-seed-code-sawa/c398cd5c-7d46-4752-bc25-f3632715aed9/scratchpad/{indep.py,adx.py,recompare.py,bbtest.py}.

CODE READ: sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py (full), sawa/database/ta_load.py (full), sawa/domain/technical_indicators.py, sawa/daily.py (lines 55-100, 454-520, 690-930, 1000-1140), sawa/ta_backfill.py (recompute_ta_for_tickers / run_ta_backfill), mcp_server/tools/momentum.py (squeeze query), git log -S on cumulative_seed, logs/daily_20260902_210002.log (lines 2879, 10698, 11083, 11443).

- Independent (no-talib) SMA-20/50/200, EMA-12/26/200, RSI-14/21 (Wilder), ATR-14 (Wilder), OBV vs stored, latest 5 dates, for AAPL MSFT NVDA JPM XOM CMG ANET KLAC GE SMX WHLR XXII VCIG JAGX ADTX: 0 differences (EJH only in ema_200 — finding 4).

NOT COVERED / LIMITS:

### companies-indices (data)

All queries run read-only against the live DB (2026-09-03, latest stored daily date 2026-09-02). One Polygon GET used, for BK ticker-details.

CHECKS THAT FOUND NOTHING WRONG
- companies normalization: `SELECT count(*) FILTER (WHERE ticker <> upper(ticker)), FILTER (WHERE ticker <> btrim(ticker)), FILTER (WHERE ticker ~ '[^A-Z0-9.\-]'), FILTER (WHERE name IS NULL OR btrim(name)=''), FILTER (WHERE name <> btrim(name))` over 10,430 rows -> all zero. `validate_ticker` (sawa/utils/symbols.py:38-54) uppercases+strips at the boundary.
- companies duplicates: `GROUP BY upper(btrim(ticker)) HAVING count(*)>1` -> 0 rows. Dot/dash suffix collision probe (`replace(replace(ticker,'.',''),'-','')` self-join) -> 0 rows. 24 dotted tickers (BRK.A/BRK.B style), no dashed forms, no aliasing.
- companies with prices but active=false: 0 (there are no inactive rows at all — see finding 1).
- future/invalid list_date: 0 rows with `list_date > CURRENT_DATE`; 53 rows with NULL list_date (benign).
- index_constituents duplicates: impossible, PK (index_id,ticker); confirmed no dup normalized tickers.
- constituents referencing missing companies: impossible, FK `index_constituents_ticker_fkey`; verified 0.
- cross-index containment: sp500/nasdaq100/russell1000 members all present in us_active (0 misses); dow30 ⊂ sp500 (0 misses). sp500 \ russell1000 = 6 (ERIE NXPI PSKY SATS STX TEL) — consistent with Russell's annual June reconstitution and the Wikipedia source, not treated as a defect.
- per-index counts vs expectations: sp500=503, nasdaq100=101, dow30=30, mag7=8, russell1000=998, nasdaq_listed=4676, us_active=10406. All inside MINIMUM/MAXIMUM_INDEX_SOURCE_COUNTS (sawa/coldstart.py:87-107) and match the expected values documented in docs/INDEX_AND_SECTOR_OVERHAUL_2026-05-16.md:222-226 (which states mag7=8 by design — GOOG+GOOGL — so the "7 vs 8" discrepancy is documented intent, not a bug).
- sector resolution for the 15 named tickers (AAPL MSFT NVDA JPM XOM UNH PG KO CAT BA NEE AMT PLD SPG T): every one resolves to the correct GICS sector via sic_gics_mapping; none needs an override; no wrong mappings found.
- sic_gics_mapping completeness: 0 distinct sic_codes present in companies lack a mapping row, so no company falls through to the raw-SIC-description branch of get_gics_sector (`sic_desc_leak = 0` in the per-type census). sp500/nasdaq100/dow30 sector coverage is 100% real-GICS (503/503, 101/101, 30/30).
- gics_overrides quality: 351 rows (345 yfinance + 6 manual seed). 0 rows carry a sector outside the 11 GICS sectors; 0 rows shadow a ticker that already has a usable sic_code (so no override/mapping conflicts). Migration 37's seed function + trigger and migration 38's lookup order (overrides -> sic_gics_mapping -> sic_desc) match the live function body I dumped.
- sp500 market_cap/shares plausibility: of the 498 sp500 members priced on 2026-09-02, 497 have `market_cap / (close * weighted_shares_outstanding)` in [0.7, 1.4]; 0 null, 0 non-positive, 0 under $1B, 0 off by 1e3/1e6 in either direction. The single outlier is APH at ratio 1.981 — explained by its 1:2 split with execution_date 2026-09-03 (today): prices and market_cap are on the post-split basis while Polygon's weighted_shares_outstanding is still pre-split. Provider timing, self-healing, not reported.
- doctor's price-completeness denominator (sawa/doctor.py:154-187) prefers `recent_daily_baseline` over `active_companies`, so the dead-ticker population in finding 1 is invisible to `sawa doctor` — noted as part of that finding rather than separately.

CHECKS THAT PRODUCED FINDINGS (details in findings)
- active vs delisted_utc; active companies with no/stale prices (finding 1).
- index_constituents freshness / scheduling / monitoring (finding 2).
- list_sectors vs get_sector_performance vs the DB function (findings 3, 4).
- get_index_with_prices cost and taxonomy (findings 4, 5).

NOT COVERED / COULD NOT CHECK
- Whether the stored sp500/nasdaq100/russell1000 membership lists match the *actual* index membership as of 2026-09-03. That requires re-scraping Wikipedia, which is outside the allowed tool set here, so finding 2 is evidenced by staleness plus one provably-dead member (BK) rather than by a full membership diff. Names such as MRVL/SNOW/NET being absent from the stored sp500 list were noticed but NOT reported, because I cannot verify 2026 index membership from the DB alone.

- I did not review the uncommitted split-adjustment working-tree changes (SplitAdjuster, split_adjust.py, restore_quarantined_prices.py) — `git diff` shows they touch no companies/indices/GICS code path (the only companies-related hunk in sawa/coldstart.py is an import line), so they are out of this dimension.

### intraday-live (data)

CHECKS PERFORMED (all queries run read-only against the live DB on 2026-09-03 ~18:00-18:30 UTC / 14:00-14:30 ET, mid-session).

CLEAN — nothing wrong found:
1. Table shape/type. `information_schema.columns` for stock_prices_intraday: `timestamp` is `timestamp with time zone` (migration 40's tz repair is applied), open/high/low/close NUMERIC, volume BIGINT, bar_size_minutes/source_minute_count/source_minute_mask all NOT NULL with physical NOT NULL metadata (migration 44 validated). PK = (ticker, timestamp, bar_size_minutes) per migration 45; the legacy 2-column unique index is gone. Indexes idx_intraday_date (ET-date expression), idx_intraday_timestamp, idx_intraday_ticker_timestamp, idx_intraday_ticker_size_timestamp all present.
2. Contents. 293,326 rows / 9,312 tickers at first census (304,751 by the end of the audit), all with bar_size_minutes=5. min(timestamp)=2026-09-03 13:30:00+00, i.e. exactly 09:30:00 ET under EDT — the 09:30 ET bar is stored as expected. `SELECT (timestamp AT TIME ZONE 'America/New_York')::date, count(*) GROUP BY 1` returns a single row (2026-09-03).
3. Bar alignment. `WHERE EXTRACT(EPOCH FROM ((ts AT TIME ZONE 'America/New_York') - (date + TIME '09:30:00')))::bigint % 300 <> 0` → 0 rows. Every bar sits on the 5-minute grid anchored at the 09:30 ET session open (not on a UTC/hour floor).
4. Session bounds. 0 rows with ET time < 09:30 or >= 16:00. `_aggregate_bar` (websocket_client.py:483) drops out-of-session minutes before they reach the aggregator.
5. OHLC sanity (migration 43). 0 rows failing `open>0 AND high>0 AND low>0 AND close>0 AND high>=low AND volume>=0`; also 0 rows with `high < GREATEST(open,low,close)` or `low > LEAST(open,high,close)` (the stricter Python guard in intraday_load.py:78-81, which the DB does not enforce); 0 negative volumes.
6. Duplicates. PK forbids exact duplicates; `GROUP BY ticker, timestamp HAVING count(*)>1` → 0, so no same-timestamp collisions across bar sizes either.
7. Per-ticker completeness for liquid names. AAPL/MSFT/NVDA/TSLA/AMZN/GOOGL/META/SPY/QQQ/AMD/INTC/F/BAC/PLTR/SOFI each had 53/53 available windows with source_minute_count=5 on every bar. Collection is healthy intra-session.
8. source_minute_count/mask semantics (migration 45). 104,505 bars have mask=31/count=5; the count<5 population (~64%) is spread evenly across the session (58%-70% per window), NOT concentrated at open/close — this is the expected consequence of Polygon AM emitting a minute bar only when a trade occurs, confirmed by mega-caps showing 100% count=5. Mask bit 0 = first minute of the window (`1 << ((event_time-bar_start)//60)`), consistent with the DB popcount check. `stock_prices_intraday_lineage_consistent` and `..._source_minute_mask_check` are both VALIDATED.
9. Write-path fidelity end-to-end. Exported all 304,751 stored bars and compared against the last-logged snapshot of every (ticker, window) parsed out of logs/intraday_20260903_133002.log{,.1}: 304,751 matched, 0 missing in DB, 0 volume mismatches, 0 source_minute_count mismatches. The 6,646 "close" differences are all artifacts of the log's %.2f formatting (e.g. WDH 0.9950 logged as 0.99). The mask-superset upsert in load_intraday_bars is not corrupting or dropping revisions.
10. stock_prices_live definition vs. schema file. `pg_get_viewdef` matches sqlschema/45 exactly (coverage CTE + DISTINCT ON resolution choice + three UNION ALL arms). No view drift — unlike the pre-40 incident.
11. View coherence / no double counting. For today: 9,315 rows all `data_source='intraday'`, 0 tickers appearing twice, 0 stock_prices rows for 2026-09-03 (so the NOT EXISTS arm-exclusion is untested but correct by construction), 0 stock_prices rows dated in the future (which the view would silently drop). open/close inside [low,high] for all 9,315 synthetic candles. Date attribution is ET throughout.
12. Extreme movers in the live view are real market events, not defects: NCT (-88.7%) is an in-progress crash with LULD-halt-shaped gaps and 16M shares; VSTD/CHPT/SPWR/GIPR all check out against stock_splits (no split has an ex-date of 2026-09-03).
13. Cross-check against the provider. One Polygon GET (`/v2/aggs/ticker/AAPL/range/5/minute/2026-09-02/2026-09-02`) confirmed our stored/logged bars: session open 326.865 (ours 326.87), high 328.40 (ours 328.40), low 323.53 (ours 323.53), 09:30 window volume 1,560,459 vs. our 1,560,345 (0.007%, late-trade corrections). Bar boundaries and values are right. (This response also returned only 45 regular-session bars totalling 18.4M shares — fewer bars and less volume than our own websocket capture of the same day — so I did NOT use it as a volume reference; it is evidently truncated on this plan.)
14. Subscription coverage. 1,104 of 10,430 active tickers have no bar today, but they are uniformly distributed across the alphabet (~10% in every first-letter bucket), so this is not a truncated subscribe message; 1,084 of them traded <100k shares on 2026-09-02. Three exceptions (APGE 4.9M, CSHP 1.8M, POLE 1.0M on 09-02) have zero bars and zero log lines today; APGE's price has been pinned at ~135.0 for a week (merger-shaped) and CSHP/POLE are an ETF and a SPAC whose 09-02 volume was a one-off, so I could not establish a defect without further API calls. Flagging as unresolved rather than as a finding.
15. get_volume_leaders / screen_stocks TA-date mismatch. technical_indicators has 0 rows for 2026-09-03 while get_price_date_refs()['latest'] is 2026-09-03 during the session. I checked whether tools join TA on the live date: movers.get_volume_leaders deliberately uses get_eod_date_refs() (movers.py:242) and screener.screen_stocks uses `dr.latest_ta` (screener.py:408). Both handle it correctly — no finding.
16. MCP tools behave coherently: get_latest_price('AAPL') and get_intraday_bars(aggregate=true) return the identical candle (O 324.87 H 330.81 L 324.11 C 327.24 V 20,289,920, 55 bars) and get_latest_price emits a "market is currently open" warning. get_intraday_bars for a past date returns [] with an honest "No intraday data found" warning.
17. Housekeeping checks with nothing to report: 0 inactive companies hold intraday rows; created_at values are all from today; 0 warnings or errors in either of today's intraday log files; no "Rejecting minute for finalized window" or "late event" rejections in any session log.

NOT COVERED / LIMITS:
- No prior-day intraday data exists anywhere in the DB, so all historical assertions rest on logs/intraday_2026090{1,2,3}_133002.log*, not on stored rows.
- I could not measure exactly how much volume the missing 15:40-16:00 ET window carries, because the one permitted provider GET came back truncated. The close-error statistics in the findings are the reliable measurement.
- Half-day / early-close (13:00 ET) sessions were not observed in the available logs; the truncation finding should apply there identically but is untested.
- I did not exercise the 1/15/30/60-minute bar sizes — only bar_size_minutes=5 has ever been written, so the migration-45 multi-resolution arbitration in stock_prices_live is dead code in practice and untested against live data.
- I did not run pytest.

ADDITIONAL MINOR ITEMS not raised as findings: `sawa/utils/cleanup_intraday.py:31` parses INTRADAY_RETENTION_DAYS with a bare int() outside its try block (a malformed env var is an uncaught traceback); `sawa/live.py:70,155` use `date.today()` (UTC) rather than get_market_date() for the Polygon end bound, which is only an upper bound and harmless; stock_prices_intraday.created_at is `timestamp without time zone` while every other timestamp on the table is tz-aware.

### daily-pipeline (pipeline)

CHECKS PERFORMED (code traced end to end: sawa/daily.py, sawa/utils/market_hours.py, sawa/utils/dates.py, sawa/domain/price_validation.py, sawa/database/ta_load.py, sawa/split_adjust.py [working tree], sawa/corporate_actions.py, sawa/ta_backfill.py, sawa/database/news.py, sawa/database/intraday_load.py, sawa/api/client.py get/get_paginated/get_trading_days, sawa/cli.py cmd_daily, sawa/utils/monitoring.py monitored_run, scripts/market_scheduler.sh, tests/test_daily.py; logs/daily_20260901_210002.log, logs/daily_20260902_210002.log, logs/daily_20260830_210002.log, ~/.sawa/scheduler/scheduler.log).

1. Date window: end_date=get_market_date() (ET) minus 1 day before 17:00 ET; is_after_market_close() returns True on weekends so end_date==market date on Sat/Sun/holidays (see low finding). start_date = last_price_date-14d then min() with per-symbol watermarks (daily.py:593-662). Holiday reasoning (Labor Day 2026-09-07): fetch returns through 09-04, coverage_date=max(trading_days)=09-04, guard OK; only the spurious EOD-coverage warning fires. Catch-up after outage exercised 08-31 (7 hourly retries, known). UTC-vs-ET: only fetch-window bounds use date.today() (corporate-actions end_date, FRED window, news) - harmless per the 2026-09-02 memory rule. timestamp_to_date(UTC) on Polygon ET-midnight ms yields the same calendar date in EST and EDT.
2. Provider validation: normalize_provider_volume rounds half-up then is_valid_daily_ohlcv enforces integral/range/OHLC relations; rows outside [per-symbol start, end] rejected and counted (daily.py:235-237); tested by test_fetch_* cases. OK.
3. Coverage guard + rollback (daily.py:808-880): coverage computed inside the uncommitted txn; prices_error -> rollback of prices AND the intraday cleanup; single commit; insert_prices(commit=False) rolls back on exception; test_low_persisted_fraction_on_trading_day_is_fatal covers it. Upsert overwrites unconditionally (provider-authoritative; no evidence of a bad valid-looking bar overwriting good data).
4. Incremental TA: ran scratch script ta_drift.py (read-only) for AAPL MSFT NVDA KLAC FEED IESC MNST SFBS APH XOM BRK.B SMX MGN RUSHA TSLA: full-history TA vs daily-style window (lookback 1280 calendar days + cumulative seed, recompute_from=last-14d) vs STORED rows on 2026-09-02 -> zero indicator differences >0.05%. SQL: stored sma_200 vs AVG of last 200 closes for 9056 tickers -> 0 off by >0.1%. TA/price alignment: 2026-09-02 10000/10000 rows, 2026-09-01 10001/10001; 0 active price rows missing TA in last 30d; 0 orphan TA rows in last 30d. EMA-200 drift fix confirmed effective.
5. Split heal: traced daily.py:454-519 + split_adjust.refresh_split_adjusted_prices + ta_backfill.recompute_ta_for_tickers. Price continuity at 70 splits since 2026-08-10: close step across execution date 0.70-1.48 (all adjusted). Working-tree pre-horizon re-base interplay with a daily re-run: after the first upsert the probe rows equal the provider, factor snaps to 1 -> already_adjusted; get_unapplied_splits_in_range sees no raw jump -> idempotent. NOTE production still runs HEAD's split_adjust (09-02 log prints the HEAD message 'keep their existing basis; the adjustable range is re-based'), so the working-tree logic has not run in the scheduler yet. Heal ticker list 09-01 vs 09-02 differs only by PSTV (Polygon split feed variance).
6. News: get_paginated wraps every failure into ProviderError (client.py:200-360), so daily's (httpx.RequestError, ProviderError, psycopg.Error) filter is adequate (prior 'news exception gap' closed). news_articles max published 2026-09-02 20:50Z; 4031 articles loaded 09-02.
7. Market internals: FRED window UTC-bounded (harmless); merge_cboe_internals fills only holes; market_internals max 2026-09-02, vix 09-02, hy_spread 09-01 (FRED T+1, expected).
8. Intraday cleanup: same txn as EOD; 09-02 run deleted 417,910 intraday rows where EOD arrived; table now holds only 2026-09-03 (75,520 rows, 8461 tickers). cleanup_old_intraday_data(7d) OK.
9. Degraded vs success: stats.success False only for prices_error (or news_error in news-only); cmd_daily returns 1; monitored_run sends FAILED for exception or success False; scheduler also notifies (documented belt+suspenders) and retries each 15-min tick until daily_done (known). Degraded runs exit 0 and mark daily_done (see medium finding). tests/test_daily.py: 28 passed (conftest strips NTFY_TOPIC; no real notifications).
10. mv_52week_extremes fresh (2026-09-02); doctor threshold 0.85 matches MIN_LATEST_COVERAGE.

Universe: companies has 10,430 active / 0 inactive; weekly download_overviews skips tickers whose details return None ('ordinary for delisted symbols', weekly.py:70-75), and no code path writes active=false -> stale watermarks are permanent (280 active tickers with last price < 2026-08-19; 67 < 90d; oldest OST 2025-09-12; USPE/SVA have no prices at all).

### weekly-corporate-splits (pipeline)

CHECKED (code): sawa/split_adjust.py (working tree + `git show HEAD:sawa/split_adjust.py`), sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py, sawa/corporate_actions.py, sawa/weekly.py:574-640, sawa/quarterly.py:200-400, sawa/daily.py:454-520 and 566-660 (split heal call site), sawa/cli.py:684-745 (adjust-splits), sawa/database/load.py diff (row_transform), sawa/coldstart.py diff (refresh_split_registry), sawa/ta_backfill.py:171-220, scripts/restore_quarantined_prices.py (full), tests/test_split_adjust.py, tests/domain/test_split_adjuster.py, tests/test_corporate_actions_truthfulness.py, tests/test_restore_quarantined_prices.py (names), docs diff (OPERATIONS/MAINTENANCE/DATA_SOURCES).

CHECKED (live DB, read-only) with no defect found:
- `\d dividends`: unique expression index `dividends_normalized_identity_uidx (ticker, ex_dividend_date, COALESCE(dividend_type,''::character varying))` matches the ON CONFLICT clause in load_dividends exactly; `SELECT ... GROUP BY ticker, ex_dividend_date, COALESCE(dividend_type,'') HAVING count(*)>1` -> 0 rows; 0 NULL dividend_type; 0 future ex-dates; weekly 09-02 loaded 35,406 dividends. Frequencies present: {0,1,2,4,12,24,52} all inside the allowlist.
- `\d stock_splits` / `\d stock_prices`: types as expected (NUMERIC(24,8), bigint volume, CHECK constraints). stock_splits: 2171 rows, 0 future execution dates, 0 splits for inactive/unknown tickers, 0 splits for tickers without prices; 32 rows are 1:1 (26 in the trailing year across 19 tickers).
- Window semantics: `execution_date_lte=today` excludes announced-not-executed splits; they are picked up by the daily heal / next weekly once executed. The out-of-window guard (corporate_actions.py:400-408) is consistent with the request window.
- Savepoint handling: only ForeignKeyViolation is isolated per row; any other DB error propagates, the transaction is rolled back by the connection context manager and the weekly records the step failure (weekly.py:634-638). Tests confirm rollback-on-error and single commit.
- Transaction boundary: insert_prices(commit=False) + rebase_rows_before_date + one commit (split_adjust.py:565-600); rollback on exception or row-count mismatch. Covered by test_refresh_rebases_the_pre_horizon_tail_in_the_upsert_transaction / ..._rolls_back_when_the_rebase_touches_an_unexpected_row_count.
- expected_rows consistency: boundary plan expects len(tail_dates) where tail = stored dates < min(fetched) and the UPDATE hits date < horizon; fetched rows are all >= horizon so the upsert cannot change the count. execution_date == horizon edge composes correctly.
- Plan composition: two UPDATEs on nested row sets are multiplicative and commutative; prices are rounded twice at 8 dp (negligible), volume rounded twice to integer (can differ by 1 share from a single rounding, e.g. 7*3/2->11, *2 -> 22 vs 21). Not reported as a finding.
- SplitAdjuster: factor() uses execution_date > on (bar on the execution date is on the new basis) matching Polygon semantics; quantize to PRICE_SCALE 1e-8 HALF_UP, volume HALF_UP to integer; range checks; prec=60 context. Polygon adjusted values observed with <=4 dp (IBM 2021-11-03 c=121.5392), so 8-dp storage is a superset. Polygon volume is fractional (v=6177174.966) and normalize_provider_volume handles it in fetch_prices_via_api.
- Rolling-horizon idempotency: for a tail already re-based, the next run measures factor ~1 at the boundary and finds no in-tail raw step; Python reproduction confirms the already_adjusted path; weekly re-runs are upsert-idempotent.
- Weekly chain failure isolation: refresh failure raises before TA recompute; splits/dividends already committed so the next run retries; recompute_ta_for_tickers uses per-ticker DELETE+replace transactions.
- Today's repair: prices vs technical_indicators row counts and max dates match for ANET APH CMG DECK DELL ETR GE SRE WRB WSM HUIZ WTO (WTO last price 2026-07-01, known).
- Horizon-boundary scan for the 661 weekly-refresh tickers: 245 have pre-2021-09-02 rows (26,023 rows); only 4 have a >25% step at the boundary: RUSHA/RUSHB (finding 1), ANY (genuine +42% day on 2021-09-02: open 482.50 continuous with prior close 464.10; guard behaves correctly), DFNS (finding 6).
- Simulated `get_unapplied_splits_in_range` for Saturday's run (splits in (min stored date, 2021-09-09] for recent-split tickers, code's exact criterion): 17 candidates, 0 would fire, so no imminent false re-base.
- restore_quarantined_prices.py: INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING + DELETE with exact count check and single commit; BOUNDARY_BOUNDS (0.75,1.3334) would refuse a 1.5x tail as-is; 9 tests exist. No defect found.
- quarterly.py: no split/dividend logic; stats/degraded/step_errors semantics consistent with weekly's pattern. Not reviewed further.

OBSERVATIONS not raised as findings: dividend_type 'recurring' appears on 2 rows (ELME 2026-01-08, BBUC 2026-03-23), a provider value outside the documented CD/SC/LT/ST set (schema comment drift; consumers filtering dividend_type='CD' miss them). HUIZ 2024-12-09 is used as evidence in finding 4 (provider-served, split applied, close spike makes the heuristic fire). Polygon serves 1706-1755 splits/year of which ~53% are for untracked tickers and 38 are fractional fund reorganizations (skipped correctly).

UNTESTED FAILURE MODES identified: (a) tail on a stale basis while the provider window rows are already on the provider basis with a ratio inside (0.34, 3) -- the only discontinuity test uses 40x; (b) raw_split_jump_present with small ratios (21:20, 11:10) or a genuine large move on the execution date; (c) get_unapplied_splits_in_range's SQL is only ever mocked; (d) actual execution of two composed PreHorizonRebase plans for one ticker (ordering/rounding) is only asserted via mocked rowcounts; (e) a tracked-ticker dividend/split record that fails parsing aborting the batch -- tests assert the parser raises and that a dividend-phase error rolls back splits, but none asserts a tolerant path; (f) SplitAdjuster.adjust_row raising inside load_csv_to_table (strict file load fails) has no test; (g) stats semantics of pre_horizon_dates_rebased for in-tail-only plans.

NOT CHECKED / LIMITS: pytest was not run; one Polygon GET was used (IBM 2021-11-01..05 adjusted) -- whether Polygon applies the LILA/LILAK/DVLT/FER small-ratio splits was not verified (those tickers are refreshed daily so their stored rows equal the provider basis regardless); sawa/daily.py `_symbol_repair_start_dates` internals were not reviewed beyond the call site; coldstart split-registry flow was read but not executed; intraday and MCP consumers out of scope.

### coldstart-loaders (pipeline)

CHECKED (files read in full): sawa/coldstart.py, sawa/database/load.py, sawa/database/loader.py, sawa/api/s3.py, sawa/provider_downloads.py, sawa/add_symbol.py, sawa/corporate_actions.py, sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py (incl. new SplitAdjuster), sawa/split_adjust.py diff, sawa/database/schema.py, scripts/restore_quarantined_prices.py, tests/test_coldstart_safety.py, tests/test_loader_persistence.py, tests/domain/test_split_adjuster.py, docs diffs, sqlschema/README.md, sqlschema 02/17/18/24/30/31/33/42/45/46/47/48. Ran pytest on the 5 relevant suites: 88 passed at the working tree.

Basis verification: S3 flat files are as-traded — cache data/prices/NVDA.csv 2021-02-12 close 598.45 vs live stock_prices 14.96125000 (=598.45/40 exactly), high 15.29113750 (=611.6455/40), volume 373477720 (=9336943*40); AAPL (no split) identical in cache and DB. So SplitAdjuster's math matches Polygon's REST basis. add-symbol backfill uses REST (`sawa/daily.py:206` params adjusted=true via fetch_prices_via_api -> insert_prices), so it stores the split-adjusted basis, consistent with stock_prices; it does not record the new ticker's splits in stock_splits (only weekly's trailing-year window will), noted but not a loader defect. Polygon client get_splits goes through get_paginated (follows next_url), so the whole-window registry fetch is not truncated at 1000.

Fresh path trace: refresh_split_registry runs in its own connection after load_companies committed (coldstart conn idle) — no snapshot issue; on failure prices are not loaded, run is fatal, cache is still merged (as designed); stats['prices_loaded'] is simply never set in that branch (cosmetic). Row_transform values: Decimal (8dp quantized) and int volume flow into _insert_rows -> psycopg natively; factor==1 rows stay strings (psycopg sends as unknown-typed text, the pre-existing behaviour). CSV validation runs on the as-traded row before the transform (load.py:590-618); adjusted rows are not re-validated (see finding on partial commits). Load-only with duplicates: upsert makes identical duplicate rows harmless; source_rows double-counts (finding, low). Tickers that split AFTER the cache was written: get_split_adjuster reads every stock_splits row, so a later-recorded split is applied to the older cached bars and the upsert reproduces the REST basis — correct when the registry is complete and exact (see stock_splits findings); load-only also overwrites any REST-side provider corrections with the cached day-of values (inherent, not reported).

Registry filter: run_corporate_actions_update filters splits to companies.active=true; live DB has 10430 companies, all active, 0 inactive/NULL, so the filter is currently moot. stock_splits: 2171 rows, 2021-01-04..2026-09-03, 1447 tickers (backfill confirmed).

Live raw-jump check (docs' own detection query, run via per-split index lookups): only HUIZ 2024-12-09 (1:5, step 2.24x, closes 2.70->6.06->4.09->3.10->2.70, volume 19,635 -> 4,953,615) and WTO 2026-02-17 (known). HUIZ was re-fetched adjusted from Polygon today, so this is provider-served data; the volume spike suggests a genuine move rather than a mis-adjustment — reported here as ambiguous, not as a finding.

Schema replay: grepped all 4x (and earlier) migrations for unguarded ALTER/CREATE/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE. 45 UPDATEs are WHERE ... IS NULL (idempotent); 46 DELETE is the README-documented exception with archive copy; 47/48 are widen-only (no-op on replay); 42/24 drop-and-recreate views (fine). Only 24, 31, 42 narrow columns on replay against the current live types (finding). No CONCURRENTLY or ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE, so single-transaction execution is fine.

Index constituents: floors/ceilings, eligible-coverage and preservation checks, savepoint rollback traced; live counts sp500 503, nasdaq100 101, dow30 30, mag7 8, russell1000 998, nasdaq_listed 4676, us_active 10406. Only companies-present tickers are inserted (by design). Economy: download_economy/_load_economy_file identity mapping and market_internals COALESCE path traced; nothing wrong found. Companies loader: COMPANY_COLUMNS upserts active from the overview CSV ('True'/'False' strings coerced by Postgres); add_symbol.insert_company's ON CONFLICT does not refresh `active` (pre-existing, low, not reported).

External call: one Polygon GET /v3/reference/splits (trailing year, limit 1000; 1000 of ~1755 returned) used to cross-check fractional-ratio splits against stock_splits.

Test gaps observed: no test of the fresh-path branch where refresh_split_registry fails (load_prices must be skipped, _merge_price_artifacts still called, run fatal); no test that a row_transform/FK failure on file N does not leave files 1..N-1 committed or lose the staging dir; no test of _merge_price_artifacts re-run behaviour; no test that SplitAdjuster rejects 1:1 or rounded ratios.

NOT CHECKED: sawa/database/loader.py CLI (legacy standalone loader — read, no defects found, not exercised); the full Polygon fractional-split population beyond the 1000-row sample (so the 18 stale registry rows found are a lower bound); actual execution of `sawa coldstart --no-drop` (read-only session — the narrowing failure was proven by casting the live max value to NUMERIC(20,8), which raises numeric field overflow); the S3 flat files themselves (no S3 call made).

### ta-engine (pipeline)

CHECKLIST AND WHAT WAS DONE (all DB access read-only via PGOPTIONS default_transaction_read_only; Python via `uv run --no-sync` with the same option; no pipeline commands run, no writes).

1. Indicator definitions vs standard formulas (sawa/calculation/ta_engine.py:204-269). Verified independently in Python on AAPL full history (1395 bars): Wilder RSI-14 manual 60.857372 == talib; Wilder ATR-14 manual 7.428318 == talib; EMA-12 with SMA seed / alpha 2/13 manual 316.721447 == talib; EMA-200 SMA-seed 285.7802 == talib (first-value seed 285.7803); BB 20/2 population stddev 324.3341/299.7019 == talib; talib MACD first index 33 (=34 bars), signal same; ADX first index 27 (=28 bars); macd_line-(ema12-ema26) = 0.0 at last bar. OBV: manual recurrence with talib first-bar convention (test_unseeded_obv_matches_talib_first_volume_convention covers). VWAP: cumulative typical-price VWAP since series start (documented as "cumulative" in metadata; non-standard for daily bars but consistent with intent). bb_width_pct, volume_ratio (today/SMA20 incl. today), dollar_volume_sma_20 = SMA(close*volume,20): match metadata descriptions. Stored-row identities on 2026-09-02 (10,000 rows): max|bb_middle-sma_20|=0.0000; max|macd_hist-(line-signal)|=0.0001; max|macd_line-(ema_12-ema_26)|=0.0010 (avg 0.000025), 0 rows >0.01. NO FORMULA DEFECT FOUND.
   Minor hygiene (not filed): MIN_PERIODS['macd_signal']=35 and metadata min_periods_required=35 vs talib actual 34 bars; MIN_PERIODS['adx_14']=28 vs metadata 27 (talib first value is the 28th bar). MIN_PERIODS is only used via max() in get_required_lookback_days, so harmless.

Parent connection is closed before Pool creation (no inherited sockets). One new connection per ticker per worker and row-by-row cur.execute (no executemany) - inefficiency only, not filed. ### intraday-live-code (pipeline)

CHECKLIST AND WHAT WAS DONE (all DB access read-only; one Polygon GET used: AAPL 1-minute aggregates for 2026-09-02).

Code read in full: sawa/intraday.py, sawa/live.py, sawa/api/websocket_client.py (995 lines), sawa/database/intraday_load.py, sawa/utils/cleanup_intraday.py, sawa/utils/market_hours.py, sqlschema/21,40,43,45, mcp_server/tools/market_data.py (get_intraday_bars, get_latest_price, get_stock_prices, get_live_price_async), mcp_server/tools/_dates.py, scripts/market_scheduler.sh, sawa/daily.py:600-880 (EOD gating + intraday cleanup), sawa/utils/monitoring.py (monitored_run), sawa/cli.py cmd_intraday, tests/test_intraday_bar_identity.py, tests/test_live.py, test list of tests/test_websocket_resilience.py. Working-tree diff (git diff --stat) touches only split/coldstart files, none of the intraday/live code; no intraday-relevant lines in the diff.

1. 1-min -> 5-min aggregation / bucket boundaries: traced _aggregate_bar (websocket_client.py:401-586). Exercised in Python for bar_size 5 and 60 on EST (2026-01-15), EDT (2026-07-15) and the DST-start Sunday (2026-03-08): 09:29 rejected, 09:30/09:34 -> window 09:30 mask 0b10001, 09:35 -> new window, 12:29 -> 12:25 (bit 4), 15:59 -> 15:55 (bit 4), 16:00/16:01 rejected; 60-min windows anchor to 09:30 (09:30, 11:30 bit 59, 12:30, 15:30 bit 29). Correct. session_anchor via .replace() on ZoneInfo-aware datetime is DST-safe (no transition inside 09:30-16:00). OK.
2. Stored data quality (all 110,921 bars for 2026-09-03 as of 11:24 ET): misaligned minutes 0, non-zero seconds 0, out-of-session rows 0, OHLC violations 0, zero-volume 0. AAPL/MSFT/SPY/NVDA/TSLA/AMZN/QQQ/GOOGL: 20 bars each, all 5/5 minutes, contiguous 09:30..11:05, open of each bar ~= prior close. 61% of all bars have <5 source minutes with masks spread across all 31 patterns; these are thin tickers with no trades in some minutes (Polygon emits AM only for traded minutes) - expected, not a defect.
3. Flush watermark / late bars: complete = watermark >= bar_end (inclusive edge correct since minute W+5 belongs to next window); finalized after +15 min; finalized_through rejects bar_start <= cutoff. Write latency measured from created_at: median 16.1 min after window end for every window (15-min feed delay + ~1 min), max 45 min = 15-min delay + 30-min STREAM_STALL for thin tickers (by design). Defect found: out-of-session events return before the watermark update, so the last window of the day never completes via watermark (Finding 3, demonstrated in Python).
4. source_minute_count/mask: mask bit = (minute - bar_start)//60, count = bit_count; loader validates 1<=count<=bar_size, mask in (0, 1<<bar_size), popcount==count; DB CHECKs stock_prices_intraday_source_minute_mask_check / _lineage_consistent / _source_minute_count_check are all present and convalidated=t in the live DB (pg_constraint). OK.
5. Upsert / duplicate suppression: CASE overwrites only when incoming mask is a superset (same-mask correction and superset overwrite; equal masks overwrite even if high/volume decrease, as intended). Verified with SELECT over VALUES: (00111,11000)->f, (00111,00111)->t, (00111,11111)->t, (11111,00111)->f. Disjoint partial after restart is discarded (Finding 5). In-memory _buffer_snapshot/_restore_failed_batch use the same superset rule; consistent.
6. Timezone: timestamps stored TIMESTAMPTZ (live column type confirmed), bar_start computed in UTC; view/MCP/cleanup use AT TIME ZONE 'America/New_York'; expression index idx_intraday_date present. DB TimeZone=Etc/UTC. created_at is naive UTC (CURRENT_TIMESTAMP in UTC session) - fine. Live view in DB matches migration 45 text (pg_get_viewdef). OK.
7. Reconnect: in-memory aggregator/buffer survive reconnects; backoff resets after connect+subscribe. Today and yesterday ran on a single PID each (scheduler log), no reconnects observed. Polygon delayed feed does not replay; a process restart within 15 min of open loses nothing thanks to the delay. OK.
8. Holiday / early close: market_hours.py has no holiday or early-close calendar (grep for holiday/early_close/13:00 in sawa/ found none); is_market_open() is only used for an MCP hint (server.py:2648). Scheduler relies on Polygon /v1/marketstatus/now (handles holidays) with a time-based fallback that also lacks holidays. WS client / view / MCP hard-code 16:00 (Finding 6).
9. Cleanup policy: cleanup_old_intraday_data deletes timestamp < now-7d (cannot touch today); cleanup_today_intraday_data runs inside the daily price transaction only when end_date == market date, no fetch_errors and coverage >= MIN_LATEST_COVERAGE, per-ticker EXISTS on stock_prices (daily.py:832-850). Effect: table only ever holds today's bars (verified: only 2026-09-03 present); INTRADAY_RETENTION_DAYS/7-day retention is effectively moot but harmless. sawa.utils.cleanup_intraday is not in crontab (daily does the cleanup). OK.
10. REST live path (sawa/live.py): get_aggregates default adjusted=True (async_client.py:120,144) -> adjusted basis; timestamp_to_date uses UTC date, correct for Polygon daily t (04:00/05:00 UTC); end_date=date.today() (UTC) is harmless as an upper bound; batch failures surfaced per ticker; div-by-zero guarded. No defect. Docs claim a REST intraday fallback that does not exist in code (Finding 7).
11. Live view / synthetic daily bar: open=first bar open, high/low=max/min, close=last bar close, volume=sum over one chosen resolution; NOT EXISTS on stock_prices switches to EOD. Sample: AAPL 2026-09-03 intraday O 324.87 H 330.69 L 324.11 C 330.045 V 11,013,895 matches the stored 5-min bars. 8790 intraday rows / 0 eod rows for today. 16:00-minute exclusion is Finding 4.
12. Session stop / shutdown (the big one): scheduler.log shows 13/13 stops since 2026-08-20 ended in "did not exit gracefully, sending SIGKILL"; intraday logs for 09-01 and 09-02 contain zero "Shutting down"/"Shutdown complete"/"Interrupted" lines and end with a normal "Saved ... 19:10 - 19:35" at 20:00:08/20:00:10 UTC, 6-8 s AFTER the SIGINT. Empirical test (scratchpad/sigtest.sh, same non-interactive `set -euo pipefail` + `cmd ... 9>&- &` shape as market_scheduler.sh:431): child SIGINT disposition = SIG_IGN, asyncio would not install its handler, child survives kill -INT (Finding 1). Stop timing vs 15-min delay is Finding 2. Polygon AAPL 1-min 2026-09-02: through-15:39 aggregate C 325.11 / V 24.95M vs official EOD 324.96 / 33.59M; 15:59 bar c=325.00 v=531k; 16:00 bar o=324.96 (== official close) v=988k.
13. Loader throughput: single-row execute loop, ~4,176 rows in ~2 s from today's log (~2k rows/s); a full-universe final flush (~10k tickers x several partial windows) is tens of seconds - relevant to the 60 s INTRADAY_STOP_TIMEOUT once SIGINT is fixed (folded into Finding 1 recommendation, not a separate finding).
14. Doctor: sawa/doctor.py has 0 references to intraday; nothing detects the truncated sessions (folded into Finding 1).
15. Test gaps: test_websocket_resilience covers minute correction, horizon finalization, delayed-feed non-flush, 60-min anchoring, session rollover, buffer/loss telemetry, handshake timeouts, shutdown-by-direct-call; no test that the last regular-session window completes/flushes after after-hours events, no test for signal-driven shutdown (SIGINT/SIGTERM), no early-close test, no mid-window restart/merge test, no DST-day test. test_intraday_bar_identity only string-matches the SQL (never exercises the CASE against Postgres). test_live has no test for the adjusted flag or holiday-gap windows (days=1 on a Tuesday after a Monday holiday returns "No data" since start=end-2d).
NOT CHECKED: behaviour of the realtime endpoint (only delayed is used), actual bar-for-bar comparison of stored 5-min bars vs Polygon REST 5-min aggregates (single external call budget spent on the closing-auction check), actual early-close-day data (none retained), pytest run (not needed for evidence).
