6. Code vs schema: scratch script parsed every INSERT INTO (12 target tables), UPDATE ... SET (stock_prices, indices) and 610 alias-qualified column references in sawa/, mcp_server/, scripts/ (working tree incl. uncommitted SplitAdjuster/coldstart/load/split_adjust/restore script) against live columns: zero missing tables or columns. grep for stale names (revenue_estimate, vix_close, nasdaq5000/in_nasdaq5000, load_*_from_csv): only comments and the MCP legacy-alias map. Python bounds vs live precision: MAX_STORABLE_PRICE=1e16 & PRICE_SCALE=1e-8 (sawa/domain/price_validation.py:15) == stock_prices NUMERIC(24,8) OK; intraday MAX_PRICE_EXCLUSIVE=1e12 (sawa/database/intraday_load.py:14, sawa/api/websocket_client.py:48) == NUMERIC(20,8) OK; _MAX_NUMERIC_10_4=1e6/scale 1e-4 (sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:15) == dividends.cash_amount & earnings eps NUMERIC(10,4) OK (live max cash_amount 90.0, eps 414,683.63, surprise_pct 109,750 all fit); _MAX_MARKET_INTERNAL_VALUE=9999.9999 (sawa/database/load.py:38) == NUMERIC(8,4) OK; TA writer has no Python bound and relies on the DB error (ta_load.py:100-108) — live maxima bb_upper 1.19e12, sma_5 1.06e12 fit NUMERIC(20,4). load.py:778/882 and add_symbol.py:259 query information_schema.columns without a table_schema filter — harmless today (only public exists).

7. Seeds: technical_indicator_metadata 28 rows == 11+17+30; indices codes sp500, nasdaq_listed, us_active, nasdaq100, dow30, mag7, russell1000; sic_gics_mapping 380 rows; gics_overrides 6 manual + 345 yfinance.

8. Migration special handling reviewed: 46 LOCK TABLE + archive/DELETE (needs one transaction — runner provides it; idempotent on replay); 45 PK swap (ACCESS EXCLUSIVE; idempotent); 40/42/48 view drop order; 41/44 full-table scans on replay (NOT EXISTS checks) — acceptable; VALIDATE/SET NOT NULL on already-valid objects are no-ops.

NOT CHECKED / LIMITS: could not execute any migration against a scratch database (read-only session) — replay behaviour is inferred from PostgreSQL typmod-coercion semantics plus the direct cast test shown in finding 1; did not verify the turbulence project's schema files; did not diff docs/DATA_SOURCES.md in depth (grep only); did not review mcp_server tool SQL for unqualified SELECT column names beyond the alias-qualified scan.

### fundamentals-tables (gap)

SCOPE COVERED: balance_sheets (88,991 rows), income_statements (156,411), cash_flows (154,811), financial_ratios (8,171 / 4,072 tickers), v_latest_fundamentals, v_company_summary, sawa/quarterly.py, sawa/database/load.py, sawa/add_symbol.py, sawa/repositories/database.py, sawa/doctor.py, mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py, market_data.py, companies.py, screener.py, corporate_actions.py, mcp_server/charts/renderers/fundamentals.py.

CHECKS THAT FOUND A DEFECT (see findings): multi-ticker provider records collapsing whole-symbol downloads; historical tickers[0] misattribution; income_statements split-basis flip-flop; financial_ratios split-basis freeze; unscheduled `sawa quarterly` + missing June-2026 season; duplicate fiscal-period identities; zero-for-NULL shell rows; unreachable TTM rows; add_symbol partial-column upsert; silent column-mapping drop.

CHECKS THAT FOUND NOTHING WRONG (with the query/file used):
- Duplicate (ticker, period_end, timeframe): impossible — live PK confirmed on all three tables (pg_constraint query), all three PK columns NOT NULL. Zero exact duplicates.
- TTM double-counting by consumers: I enumerated every reader. sawa/repositories/database.py:281/344/404 all bind `timeframe = %s`; mcp_server/tools/fundamentals.py binds `timeframe = %(timeframe)s` on all three sub-queries; v_latest_fundamentals filters `timeframe='quarterly'` in all three LATERALs; pg_views/pg_matviews scan found only v_latest_fundamentals and v_company_summary over these tables; doctor.py only does MAX()/COUNT(). NO consumer aggregates without a timeframe filter, so no double- or triple-counting exists. (The exposure is the opposite problem — see the "TTM unreachable" finding.)
- TTM arithmetic validity: for the top-40 caps, ttm revenue == sum of trailing 4 quarterly revenues to 0.00% for every ticker. TTM rows are internally correct.
- Balance-sheet identity assets = liabilities + equity on the 30 largest caps: max deviation 0.10% (WMT, noncontrolling interest rounding); 27 of 30 are exact to the cent.
- gross_profit = revenue - cost_of_revenue on the 30 largest caps: gap is exactly 0.00 for every non-NULL row.
- basic vs diluted: only 179/70,227 quarterly rows have diluted_shares < basic_shares and 458 have diluted EPS > basic EPS with positive income (0.25%/0.65%) — provider noise, not a systematic defect.
- EBITDA plausibility: ebitda < operating_income in 1,241/67,445 quarterly rows (1.8%), consistent with impairment/other-income treatment; not reported.
- 100% gross-margin artifact (revenue>0, cost_of_revenue=0, gross_profit=revenue): 7,905 rows / 716 tickers, but the large-cap instances are Visa and Mastercard, which genuinely report no COGS line. Not a defect.
- Silent provider schema drift via column mapping: I checked pg_stats null_frac for every column of all four tables. No column is 100% NULL; the highest are commitments_and_contingencies 0.89, other_cash_adjustments 0.98, noncontrolling_interest 0.69 — all legitimately sparse. Column mapping is currently working (the risk is reported as a low finding).
- financial_ratios coverage 4,072/10,430 active companies looks alarming but breaks down as CS 3,948/5,042 (78%) vs ETF 89/4,986 — the provider simply has no ratios for ETFs. Not a defect.
- financial_ratios.market_cap / enterprise_value under splits: these are absolute-dollar quantities and are NOT corrupted by the re-adjustment (KLAC 2026-02-13 market_cap 191.9B is correct); only price / earnings_per_share / average_volume are per-share or per-share-count and therefore stale. Verified before reporting.
- "Latest fundamentals" selection: v_latest_fundamentals and get_fundamentals both order by period_end DESC within a timeframe; for AAPL/AVGO/COO/KR the row returned is the genuinely latest period. Selection logic is correct (the COO row it returns is COO's own, not the contaminating issuer's).
- Restatement handling via filing_date: traced load.py:353-360 — the upsert is `col = EXCLUDED.col` on PK conflict with no filing_date comparison, so within one CSV the LAST row wins, not the newest filing. I could not turn this into a defect: the provider returns exactly one row per (ticker, period_end, timeframe), carrying the filing_date of the most recent filing that reported the period (verified on COO's annual series — 2024-10-31 and 2025-10-31 both carry filing_date 2025-12-05, the FY2025 10-K). Restatement precedence is therefore provider-resolved today and the missing ordering is latent, not active. Reported only as part of the row-count-only completeness finding.
- Capex sign convention: 2,257 positive purchase_of_property_plant_and_equipment rows exist and get_fundamentals derives FCF as OCF + capex — this is already a confirmed round-1 finding (mcp-read-path), so NOT re-reported. I did confirm the counts: annual 17,519 neg / 112 pos, quarterly 58,556 neg / 1,314 pos, TTM 61,214 neg / 831 pos.
- net_income tie between income_statements and cash_flows: 4,131/66,772 quarterly pairs (6.19%) disagree by >$0.01. I did not report this — the two lines legitimately differ (consolidated vs. attributable-to-parent, discontinued operations), and I could not separate that from a genuine defect without per-filing ground truth.

NOT CHECKED / LIMITS:
- I could not enumerate how many of the 10,430 requested symbols carry multi-ticker provider records without ~10k API calls. The 152-ticker figure in finding #2 is a DB-side lower bound (US common stock >$1B with zero income statements), and I confirmed the mechanism on exactly three symbols (AAPL clean, COO 5/24 multi, JPM 16/16 multi).
- I did not attempt to identify the true owner of every contaminated row; only COO->Designer Brands and JPM->AMJ were positively identified.
- I did not test the coldstart fundamentals path end to end (sawa/coldstart.py:546-548), only the shared loader it calls.
- ADR/foreign-private-issuer tickers (TSM, HSBC, NVO, ...) with no fundamentals are excluded from my impact counts as a likely provider gap, not a pipeline defect.
- I made 6 small Polygon GETs (income-statements COO/JPM, balance-sheets AAPL, ratios AAPL, plus a 3-endpoint key-shape probe and a 3-symbol production-path replay) rather than the single one suggested. They were necessary to separate provider-side from code-side causes; I made an incorrect inference from `r.get('ticker')` on the first pass (the key is absent, not null) and the extra probe corrected it before reporting.

### split-basis-inventory (gap)

METHOD. Enumerated every numeric column in the live schema via information_schema (filtered on price/share/cap/value/amount name patterns), cross-checked against sqlschema/*.sql and sawa/database/schema.py's EXPECTED_TABLES manifest, then for each table asked three questions: (a) does a split invalidate it, (b) does any code path re-base it, (c) is it wrong right now. Repair-path question answered definitively by grep: `grep -rn "UPDATE dividends|UPDATE earnings|UPDATE income_statements|UPDATE financial_ratios|UPDATE companies|UPDATE stock_character|UPDATE trader_cards"` over *.py and *.sql returns ONE hit, and it is scripts/backfill_sic_codes.py:183 (sic_code). sawa/split_adjust.py contains exactly one UPDATE (line 274, stock_prices). SplitAdjuster.factor(ticker, on) in sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:402 is a generic per-ticker cumulative-ratio helper but is applied only to price bars in load_prices. So the answer to (b) is NO for every non-price table in the database.

TABLE-BY-TABLE VERDICT (split invalidates? / repaired by anything? / wrong now?):
- stock_prices open/high/low/close/volume — YES / YES (sawa/split_adjust.py:274, called from daily.py:496 and weekly.py:602) / basis correct (round-1 issues stand).
- technical_indicators (all 28 indicator columns) — YES / YES (recompute_ta_for_tickers after every heal) / basis correct.
- mv_52week_extremes.high_52w/low_52w — YES / partially (daily.py:375 refresh gated on max(date) only). Systemic count I measured: of 513 tickers that split in 2026, 15 have a stale stored 52-week high and 7 are off by >50%: DFNS 1661.25 stored vs 335.00 true (4.96x), LMFA 128.50 vs 35.75 (3.59x), ORIS 75.20 vs 31.20, APH 178.52 vs 89.26 (2.00x), APM 44.70 vs 25.90, GIBO 165.00 vs 97.00, VYNE 79.75 vs 46.95. Round 1 reported APH and the gate, so not re-reported; the 15/7 counts are the new part.
- stock_prices_intraday open/high/low/close/volume — YES / NO. Retention is 7 days (daily.py:868, cleanup_old_intraday_data). stock_prices_live (view def read in full) unions only today's bars, so the live candle is safe; get_intraday_bars can serve up to 7 sessions across a split with no re-basing. NOT currently wrong: the table holds only 2026-09-03 13:30–19:35 UTC (419,253 rows), and today's only split (APH 1:2) was already healed — APH intraday 79.33-82.00 against a post-split stored close of 80.04. No finding, no evidence of present harm.
- stock_prices_backup (4.68M rows, frozen 2026-02-25, NUMERIC(12,4)) — YES / NO. `grep -rn stock_prices_backup` over *.py/*.sql/*.md/*.sh returns zero hits, so it is unreferenced dead weight on a 6-month-old basis; it would be a wrong-basis restore source. Round 1 already reported it as undocumented.
- stock_prices_unadjustable_archive (379 rows, 9 tickers ADTX AEMD CAPE DLPN EEIQ FRSX MDIA PRFX RGC) — held deliberately at a stale basis. scripts/restore_quarantined_prices.py re-bases candidates through SplitAdjuster before INSERT and offers --recompute-ta; scripts/quarantine_unadjustable_prices.py likewise. Neither refreshes mv_52week_extremes. Verified today's repair did not leave a TA hole: price rows with no technical_indicators row before 2021-10-01 is still 522 across the same 7 commodity ETFs (DBC COPX DBA UNG USO COMT PDBC), unchanged from round 1.
- dividends.cash_amount — YES / NO / WRONG (finding 1).
- dividend_identity_conflicts.cash_amount — same class, table is empty (0 rows).
- earnings.eps_actual/eps_estimate — YES / NO / wrong for 1,752 rows across 114 tickers that split after the 2026-02-24 load. Round-1 known, not re-reported. earnings.revenue_actual is an aggregate — split-invariant, nothing to check.
- income_statements basic/diluted EPS and basic/diluted shares outstanding — YES / NO / WRONG (finding 2, plus the served consequence in finding 3). Every other income_statements column (revenue, cost_of_revenue, operating_income, ebitda, the net-income lines) is a currency aggregate and split-invariant.
- financial_ratios.price, earnings_per_share, average_volume — YES / NO / WRONG (finding 4). Its market_cap, enterprise_value, free_cash_flow are currency aggregates and its price_to_*, dividend_yield, roe, roa, debt_to_equity, ev_to_* are dimensionless ratios computed from same-date numerator and denominator — I checked each and none carries split exposure. Snapshot dates: 4,000 rows at 2026-06-12, 2,368 at 2026-02-13, 1,202 at 2026-05-14; 216 splits across 210 tickers have executed since 2026-06-15.
- balance_sheets (19 numeric columns) and cash_flows (12) — no per-share and no share-count column exists in either; every column is a currency aggregate. Split-invariant, nothing to check, nothing wrong.
- companies.market_cap — split-invariant in principle but observed inconsistent with shares x price (finding 6). companies.weighted_shares_outstanding — YES / NO; only 6 active tickers deviate by exactly a recorded split ratio (LGCL, FEED, APH, RUSHA, RUSHB, DVLT), folded into finding 6. companies.share_class_shares_outstanding — finding 7.
- stock_character_baseline (atr_baseline, range_high, range_low, expected_price_today, volume_sma20, hvn_levels, lvn_levels, regression_intercept, residuals_std) and stock_character_scorecard.current_price and stock_character_flags.value — YES / NO / WRONG (finding 5). stock_character_classification (hurst_*, adx_avg, regression_r2, vol_of_vol) is scale-free and split-invariant. Confirmed the scorecard recomputes its baseline in-run (stock_character_scorecard.py:249) rather than reusing a stored one, so the damage is confined to the stored rows.
- trader_cards (atr_14, long_trigger, short_trigger, invalidation_long/short, trade_entry, trade_entry_high, trade_stop, trade_tp1/tp2, upside/downside_magnet) — YES / NO. All 8 rows are dated 2026-03-06 and NONE of the 8 tickers (AAPL AMZN GOOGL HOOD META MSFT MSTR NFLX) has split since, so these are stale-by-6-months but not split-wrong. No finding.
- rrg_relative_strength (rs_ratio, rs_momentum) and rrg_rotation_signals (velocity, heading_deg, rank_score) — normalized against a benchmark, so split-invariant in value; both frozen at 2026-03-06 with no writer (round-1 known).
- turbulence_regime_classifications (vix_level, vix3m_level), turbulence_composite_scores, turbulence_volatility_metrics — index-level or return-based, split-invariant. Noted for the record: turbulence_volatility_metrics is EMPTY (0 rows) while turbulence_composite_scores carries 1,040 rows through 2026-09-02; external project, round-1 known.
- technical_indicator_metadata.validation_min/max — thresholds, not prices.
- SUPPORT/RESISTANCE AND VOLUME-PROFILE CACHE: none exists as a table. mcp_server/tools/support_resistance.py and volume_analysis.py compute on the fly per request. The ONLY persisted volume profile in the database is stock_character_baseline.hvn_levels/lvn_levels (numeric arrays of price levels), covered by finding 5.
- Views: v_company_summary.latest_price is a live lateral over stock_prices (fine); v_company_summary.market_cap/weighted_shares_outstanding/share_class_shares_outstanding and v_company_with_indices.market_cap and v_sector_summary.avg/total_market_cap all inherit companies (findings 6-7); v_latest_fundamentals.basic_eps inherits income_statements (finding 2); stock_prices_live is today-only.
- Documentation: docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:115-128 is the only place a basis contract is written down and it covers stock_prices exclusively. No doctor check compares bases across tables (finding 8).

WHAT I DID NOT DO. No pipeline command, no --apply script, no notification, no external API call (not even the one permitted Polygon GET — the SQL was decisive without it). I did not verify Polygon's own basis for dividends, earnings, income_statements or ticker-details, so findings 1/2/6 establish that sawa's stored values are mutually inconsistent, not which side the provider got wrong. I hand-verified the mixed-basis income_statements pattern only for KLAC, BKNG and COKE and relied on the 138-jump aggregate for the rest. I did not decompose stock_character_flags.value by flag type to say which of the 136,971 flag rows are price-denominated. I did not attempt a full recomputation of mv_52week_extremes outside the 513 2026-split tickers (a 10M-row scan). I did not review news_articles/news_sentiment/index_constituents/indices/economy tables — none has a price- or share-denominated column.

### stock-character-calc (gap)

SCOPE: every file named in the brief plus consumers. Read in full: sawa/calculation/{stock_character,stock_character_detect,stock_character_scorecard,stock_character_baseline,stock_character_config,hurst}.py, sawa/stock_character_batch.py, sawa/database/stock_character.py, sqlschema/27_stock_character.sql, docs/STOCK_CHARACTER.md, plus sawa/weekly.py:600-670, sawa/doctor.py:105-140/640-700, sawa/cli.py:745-770, tests/calculation/test_hurst.py, tests/test_stock_character_atomicity.py.

BOTTOM LINE (asked for explicitly): (a) the maths is *implemented* faithfully to the spec in almost every mechanical detail (ADX is bit-exact vs TA-Lib, DFA is unbiased in the mean, the five sample tickers reproduce bit-for-bit), but the *statistical design* is not sound: the Hurst thresholds are inside the estimator's own sampling error, three of the flag families (DECORRELATION_SPY, COMPRESSION/EXPANSION, VOLUME_SPIKE/DROUGHT) compare a 10-day statistic against a 5-year baseline of a different statistic, and the confirmation gates run on 5.5 years while the classification runs on 1-3. 93% of the published universe (2964/3180) carries at least one "atypical behaviour" flag, against a spec that wanted "a clean list of 30-100 names". (b) NOTHING consumes it. Grepped the whole repo, the live catalog and sibling repos: no MCP tool (mcp_server/ has zero matches), no view or matview (information_schema.view_table_usage: 0 rows), no CLI read command, no script, no cron entry, no sibling project. The six read helpers in sawa/database/stock_character.py (get_latest_classification/baseline/scorecard, get_flags_for_ticker, get_ranked_alerts, get_prices_with_benchmarks) have zero callers. The only reader is doctor.py's own freshness/coverage check. The spec's CSV/JSON ranked alert list was never implemented.

CHECKS THAT FOUND NOTHING WRONG:
- ADX parity: compute_adx(period=20) vs talib.ADX on PFE/TJX/D/JPST (1394 bars each) -> max abs diff 0.0000, identical first-valid index 39. The Wilder seeding comment is accurate.
- DFA bias: Monte Carlo, 300 white-noise replicates per length -> mean H = 0.5036/0.5044/0.5070 for n=755/503/251. The mean-of-RMS (rather than RMS-of-RMS) fluctuation estimator and the dropped tail remainder introduce no material bias. The DFA-vs-R/S choice matches the docstring and the spec.
- Determinism / idempotency: re-ran analyze_stock() offline over prices <= 2026-09-01 for PFE, XOM, TJX, D, JPST and reproduced the stored classification, baseline and scorecard exactly (hursts, adx_avg 19.5197, r2 0.7565, atr_baseline, spy_corr mean/std, range_high/low, cycle days, adherence ratios, price percentile 42.04/80.99, sigma 0.59, atr_ratio, flag sets). Only volume_sma20 differed by <50 counts out of ~19M (prices were rewritten after the run). CPRT differed (stored vol_of_vol 0.7325, recompute 0.3059) solely because its early history was re-adjusted after the run; its hursts still matched exactly, which localises the change to the pre-2023 tail that only vol_of_vol reads.
- Write path: per-ticker single transaction, strict=True loaders, replace_flags does an identity-scoped DELETE + INSERT + read-back verification with a Python-side sort (collation trap already fixed), and _process_ticker cross-checks scorecard.flag_count/flags against the computed set before commit. No partial-write or partially-read-input path found: workers read only stock_prices for their own ticker; benchmarks are snapshotted once before the pool; the weekly runs the character step after corporate actions/split adjustment (weekly.py:641).
- Flag/scorecard consistency: EXTREMUM_HIGH uses a 'weak' percentile while the scorecard stores scipy kind='rank' -> only 1 disagreement in 939+398 rows. |sigma_distance|>2 count (116) exactly equals ABOVE_2SIGMA+BELOW_2SIGMA (70+46). No NULL closes exist in stock_prices, so the NaN paths are dormant.
- Numeric column widths: no overflow found; max stored atr_baseline 1.1e10 still fits NUMERIC(16,4) (the *values* are wrong - see finding - but the writes succeed).
- Day-over-day stability: runs 2026-09-01 vs 2026-09-02 share 3172 tickers with only 2 character changes, so the instability reported below is genuinely week-scale, not per-run nondeterminism.

ADDITIONAL ISSUES NOT REPORTED SEPARATELY (kept under the 15 cap):
- boom_bust names get no character-specific flags at all: detect_flags (stock_character_detect.py:463-487) branches only on 'range_bound'/'trending', so the 79 boom_bust rows can only ever carry the common + SMA flags. The spec likewise defines no boom_bust flag table, so this is a spec gap the code inherits.
- detect_flags wraps its whole body in `except Exception: return []` (stock_character_detect.py:505-510). A detection failure is indistinguishable from "nothing atypical": the scorecard stores flag_count=0 and the batch's own consistency check compares the empty list against itself, so it passes.
- Test coverage for the maths is essentially nil: tests/calculation/test_hurst.py contains only the two ADX/TA-Lib parity tests. There is no known-answer test for DFA (e.g. fBm with known H), no test of the classification thresholds or the lean rule, and no test of baseline/detect/scorecard arithmetic. tests/test_stock_character_atomicity.py covers only the write path.
- classify_stock's trailing-NaN trim (stock_character.py:256-265) is wrong in one branch: if the *last* close is NaN the loop breaks immediately with first_valid=0 and keeps the NaNs; downstream the ticker simply falls out as unclassifiable. Dormant today (0 NULL closes).
- _align_returns (stock_character_baseline.py:150-176) resets prev_stock but not prev_bench when a stock bar has no benchmark bar, so that pair mixes a 1-day stock return with a multi-day benchmark return; also it uses simple returns while detect/scorecard use log returns. Immaterial for SPY/GLD/TLT calendars.
- _compute_atr's first window divides a 13-value TR sum by 14 (nancumsum treats tr[0]=NaN as 0), and it is a simple moving average, not Wilder's - so it does not equal technical_indicators.atr_14. One-bar effect on a 1394-bar mean.
- MIN_HISTORY_DAYS=504 < WINDOW_FULL=756, so 363 of 3180 rows in the latest run have hurst_3yr NULL and were classified on two windows; 104 of them are HIGH confidence, which cannot satisfy the spec's "all three windows agree".
- Each weekly retry re-runs the whole batch: four weekly attempts on the night of 2026-09-01 produced classification writes spanning 22:57 UTC to 02:31 UTC over two different run_date values.

NOT CHECKED: I did not run the batch (pipeline commands are out of scope), so I could not observe the live run's error list or its wall-clock cost; I did not audit stock_character.py's Decimal rounding against every column scale beyond the overflow candidates; I did not attempt to attribute the 2026-09-02 run to a specific log file (logs for that run are not in logs/); and I verified numbers for 6 tickers (PFE, XOM, TJX, D, JPST, CPRT) rather than sampling by market cap band.

### repositories-layer (gap)

SCOPE: sawa/repositories/{base,cache,config,database,factory,polygon_prices,rate_limiter}.py (~3,200 lines) plus sawa/api/client.py, sawa/api/async_client.py, and the production consumers of those modules (sawa/ta_query.py, sawa/cli.py index+ta commands, sawa/live.py, sawa/scanner.py, mcp_server/tools/market_data.py, sawa/database/news.py).

CHECKED, DEFECT FOUND (reported above): (1) screen_by_indicators MAX(date) anchoring, verified live via `screen_indicators({'rsi_14':(70,None)})` returning 7 rows vs 300 for target_date=2026-09-02, and via SQL counts of technical_indicators rows per date; TA write-rate measured at ~100 tickers/min from created_at histogram. (2) AsyncPolygonClient default limiter measured empirically with a script (acquires 6/7/8 at t=12.01/24.01/36.00s). (3) companies column list pulled from information_schema and cross-checked with a live DatabaseCompanyRepository.get_company_info('AAPL') call. (4) SyncRateLimiter per-acquire vs per-request gating traced through client.get/get_paginated/get_single and every caller (daily.py:200, coldstart.py:432/488/626/730, weekly.py:68, quarterly.py:72/136, corporate_actions.py:337, split_adjust.py:383, add_symbol.py:451) and the news path. (5) polygon_prices REST-vs-S3 basis split cross-checked against docs/DATA_SOURCES.md:115-128 ("Flat-file bars are as-traded"). (6) PolygonClient close() call sites grepped across all 8 constructions. (7) ta_query indicator dict compared with DatabaseTechnicalIndicatorsRepository.VALID_INDICATORS. (8) asyncio.get_event_loop deprecation reproduced with -W error::DeprecationWarning. (9) dead-code census via per-symbol grep counts outside sawa/repositories/.

CHECKED, NOTHING WRONG FOUND:
- SyncRateLimiter thread safety in practice: grepped sawa/ and scripts/ for ThreadPoolExecutor/concurrent.futures/threading.Thread — zero hits. multiprocessing.Pool exists only in ta_backfill.py and stock_character_batch.py, neither of which constructs a PolygonClient, so no SyncRateLimiter instance is ever shared across threads or processes today. The missing lock (rate_limiter.py:147-171) is latent, not currently exploited; folded into the pagination finding.
- SyncRateLimiter clock handling: uses time.monotonic() (rate_limiter.py:168,171), so NTP steps and wall-clock changes cannot break it. `_last_request = 0` initial value is safe on Linux because monotonic() is uptime-based. Bursting is correctly absent (fixed min-interval, no burst allowance) and the interval is measured acquire-to-acquire, so the request rate is bounded at <= 5/s as documented.
- Cache staleness reaching the DB or an MCP read: InMemoryCache/NullCache have ZERO production consumers. They are constructed only in RepositoryFactory.__init__ (factory.py:63-68) and consumed only by PolygonPriceRepository (polygon_prices.py:70), which is never instantiated outside tests. mcp_server/ has no cache import at all (only load_cached_query_warning, an unrelated on-disk insights file). So no stale cached response can reach the database or an MCP read. tests/repositories/test_cache.py passes.
- Connection leaks in database.py: every one of the ~20 `with _get_connection(...) as conn` blocks uses psycopg 3.3.2's Connection context manager, which commits/rolls back AND closes. No leaked psycopg connections found. `run_in_executor(None, ...)` uses the default thread pool; the repository objects are stateless (database_url only), so the factory's instance cache is safe to share.
- API-key leakage through exception text: PolygonClient.get() at client.py:172-176 embeds str(HTTPStatusError), which contains the full URL including ?apiKey=. I traced this and found it is NOT a leak in practice — sawa/utils/logging.py installs a RedactingFormatter plus RedactingFilter on every handler, and sawa/utils/security.py:_URL_SECRET_RE redacts `[?&]apiKey=...`. Not reported.
- PolygonClient retry/backoff and 429 handling: get()/get_paginated()/get_single() all retry 429 with 2s/4s linear backoff and 5xx with 1s/2s, and all three raise ProviderError rather than returning partial data when attempts are exhausted. The pagination cursor fix from commit 0781f4b (client.py:225-245) is correct — copy_remove_param/copy_merge_params preserves the cursor. The repeated-URL guard, MAX_PAGINATION_PAGES and MAX_PAGINATION_RESULTS caps all work. The only gap is that none of them honours a Retry-After header, which is a hardening nit, not a defect.
- get_single() rejects status=="DELAYED" (client.py:451) while get()/get_paginated() accept it (client.py:149, 310). Only get_ticker_details and get_ticker_events route through get_single, and Polygon's /v3/reference endpoints return "OK", so I could not evidence any impact. Noted as an inconsistency, not reported.
- DatabaseFundamentalRepository timeframe filtering: verified income_statements/balance_sheets actually store 'quarterly'/'annual' (plus 'trailing_twelve_months' for income), so the Literal["quarterly","annual"] queries are not silently empty. Column names in _row_to_income/_row_to_balance/_row_to_cashflow all resolve.
- DatabaseIndexRepository read paths (list_indices, get_index, get_constituents, get_ticker_indices) backing `sawa index list|show|check`: SQL is correct, parameters bound, index_constituents counts match a direct GROUP BY (dow30 30, mag7 8, nasdaq100 101, nasdaq_listed 4676, russell1000 998, sp500 503, us_active 10406).
- update_constituents transaction boundary: the DELETE + per-ticker INSERT + last_updated bump are all inside one psycopg transaction, so a mid-run failure rolls the whole replacement back. The explicit conn.commit() at database.py:1492 is redundant but harmless. Its real weaknesses are reported separately.
- tests/repositories (63 tests) all pass at HEAD.

NOT REACHED / COULD NOT CHECK:
- No live Polygon call was made, so the 429 backoff and the retry paths in client.py/async_client.py were verified by code trace only, not by provoking a real rate limit.
- sawa/api/websocket_client.py (995 lines), s3.py, fred.py and cboe.py were only skimmed for their adjustment/close semantics; the intraday/live dimension owns them.
- I did not audit database.py's SQL for query-plan cost (that belongs to the mcp-read-path dimension, which already reported get_index_with_prices).
- I did not attempt to run `sawa ta-screen` as a CLI process (only the underlying ta_query functions), to stay clear of the pipeline-command prohibition.

ADDITIONAL LOW-VALUE ITEMS NOT REPORTED AS FINDINGS (so nothing is silently dropped): rate_limiter.py has no test file anywhere under tests/ (`ls tests | grep -i rate` is empty), so the shared gate for every Polygon-calling path has zero unit coverage; RepositoryConfig.cache_max_size and polygon_rate_limit are never read from the environment even though get_config() reads CACHE_ENABLED and CACHE_TTL_SECONDS; InMemoryCache.set would raise KeyError from popitem if max_size were 0; CacheEntry.is_expired uses wall-clock time.time() while TokenBucket uses monotonic, so a backwards NTP step extends TTLs; DatabaseCompanyRepository._search_sync (database.py:508) interpolates the caller's query into an ILIKE pattern without escaping % or _, so a query containing _ silently matches any character; DatabaseTechnicalIndicatorsRepository._screen_sync silently clamps limit with min(limit, 500) (database.py:1208) where the MCP twin raises ValueError, and the result carries no truncation flag, so ta_query.format_screen_results prints the clamped count as "N matches"; PolygonClient.get() re-raises the raw httpx.RequestError on its final attempt instead of wrapping it in ProviderError like get_single does.

### charts-render (gap)

Read every file under mcp_server/charts/ (config.py, core/{colors,formatters,layout,modal,sparkline}.py, themes/{base,mono,osaka_jade}.py, widgets/{box,gauge,table,trend}.py, renderers/{economy,fundamentals,prices,ratios}.py) plus the 5 call sites in mcp_server/server.py:2167-2291 and the tool functions that feed them (mcp_server/tools/market_data.py get_stock_prices/get_financial_ratios, tools/fundamentals.py, tools/economy.py).

RENDERS PERFORMED (read-only, renderers imported directly, PGOPTIONS default_transaction_read_only=on, MCP server never started). Scripts kept in the scratchpad: r1.py (economy dashboard + labor market), r2.py (BIAF/RARE price charts + sparkline index audit), r3.py (AAPL annual fundamentals normal+detailed, AAPL ratios), r4.py (ACHC ratios, market_internals), r5.py (dashboard compact/detailed). All outputs quoted in the findings are verbatim.

CHECKED AND FOUND WRONG (reported): dashboard row ordering vs data[-1]; Sparkline._sample tail truncation + re-normalisation to the sampled subset; job_openings thousands-vs-units in two renderers; annual-vs-quarterly labelling in fundamentals; "Open:" = first close in prices; last-non-NULL-as-current in ratios; NULL gap closing that puts sibling series on different time axes; volume row min/max labels in the first/last position; pp-vs-relative-% mixing in the dashboard trend column; market_internals having no renderer; missing sparklines for 3 of the 6 ratio rows; COLUMNS-driven detail collapse plus a terminal modal injected into the JSON warnings array.

CHECKED AND FOUND OK (no finding): ratios roe/roa/dividend_yield are stored as fractions (AAPL 2026-06-12 roe 1.151, dy 0.0036) and ratios.py:151/152/154 multiply by 100 correctly - the unit fix-up is right, not wrong; income_statements gross/operating/profit margins are already *100 in SQL (tools/fundamentals.py:101-110) and fundamentals.py:155/157/269 correctly does NOT multiply again; unemployment_rate (4.1) and labor_force_participation_rate (61.4) are stored in percent and printed in percent; avg_hourly_earnings printed as dollars; market_cap/EV via format_large_number ($4.3T for AAPL, matches JSON); eps/EPS formatting; treasury_yields has zero NULLs in all 1,388 rows (verified by query) so the yield-curve and time-series sections there are not gap-shifted; treasury renderer's latest=data[-1] is correct because get_economy_data orders ASC (only the dashboard view orders DESC); stock_prices open/high/low/close/volume are all NOT NULL (information_schema) so prices.py's independent per-field None filtering cannot de-align in practice; get_stock_prices' inner-DESC-then-ASC pattern is correct and the chart header reports the truncated range honestly; _tool_response does not truncate data separately from the chart (it raises instead), so no chart/JSON row-count divergence from size limits; fundamentals._render_normal correctly reverses income_statements for its sparklines; Box/pad_to_width handle ANSI width correctly.

NOT REACHED / LIMITS: there is no candlestick renderer anywhere in charts/ (prices.py plots closes only; open/high/low are used solely for the summary line), so "candlestick construction from OHLC" had nothing to review beyond the Open mislabel. Gauge (197 lines) and Table (218 lines) widgets are dead code - no renderer imports them - so they were read but not exercised; the same is true of Sparkline.render(show_minmax=True), render_with_trend, TrendIndicator.render_mini/render_with_sparkline, Box.render_header/render_separator/render_bottom and calculate_column_widths in layout.py. Only 5 of the ~60 MCP tools ever populate `chart` (server.py sets chart=None for all others), so the dimension's premise that "every MCP tool returns a chart" does not hold - not reported as a defect since nothing documents otherwise. Theme colour values in mono.py/osaka_jade.py were read but not evaluated for contrast/accessibility. I did not exercise every chart_detail x tool combination (COMPACT/DETAILED were exercised for prices, fundamentals and the dashboard only). Test coverage: tests/test_mcp_protocol_v2.py:184-229 is the only chart-related test and it stubs the renderer out entirely - no test in the repo asserts the content of any rendered chart, no golden output, no ordering or unit assertion over 3,634 lines of chart code; that gap is why every finding below is unguarded by the suite.

### db-health-concurrency (gap)

Read-only audit of the live cluster (postgres 18.4 in rootless podman, container `stocksdb`, volume `/data/podman/seed/storage/volumes/stocksdb-data/_data`, host 23 GiB RAM / 4 vCPU). Everything below was actually run; nothing was written and no pipeline command was executed.

CHECK LIST AND RESULT

1. Bloat / dead tuples — `pg_stat_user_tables` full dump. stock_prices 10,050,067 live / 1,882,376 dead (18.7%), last_autovacuum 2026-08-17 (16d23h ago), autovacuum_count 5, vacuum_count 0. technical_indicators 10,020,642 / 1,579,531 (15.8%), last_autovacuum 2026-09-02 03:44. Approx dead bytes: 151 MB of the 960 MB stock_prices heap, 387 MB of the 2840 MB technical_indicators heap. -> FINDING 2.
2. Autovacuum thresholds vs the nightly write volume — `pg_settings` (scale_factor 0.2, threshold 50, naptime 60s, max_workers 3, cost_delay 2ms, cost_limit -1/200) and `pg_class.reloptions` (no per-table overrides anywhere). Computed trigger points: stock_prices 2,010,063 dead, technical_indicators 2,003,721 dead. -> FINDING 2.
3. Visibility map / index-only-scan health — `pg_class.relallvisible/relpages`: stock_prices 87,266/122,608 = 71.2%, technical_indicators 261,355/363,472 = 71.9%, mv_52week_extremes 99.9%. Then measured the real cost with `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on the full-universe latest-price shape: Heap Fetches 2,234,539, buffers hit 1,665,816 read 90,591 dirtied 4,069 written 4,677, 3171 ms. -> FINDING 2.
4. Index inventory and redundancy — `pg_indexes` + `pg_stat_user_indexes` for the four big tables. Found the (ticker,date) UNIQUE PK / (ticker,date DESC) secondary pair on stock_prices (346+346 MB), technical_indicators (347+357 MB) and stock_prices_intraday (20+18 MB). Proved the PK alone serves the DESC shape: `EXPLAIN SELECT * FROM technical_indicators WHERE ticker='AAPL' ORDER BY date DESC LIMIT 300` chose `Index Scan Backward using technical_indicators_pkey` (cost 1176.09) and `EXPLAIN ... FROM mv_52week_extremes ...` chose `Index Only Scan Backward using idx_mv_52week_extremes_ticker_date`. -> FINDINGS 4 and 9.
5. Unused indexes — `pg_stat_user_indexes WHERE idx_scan < 200`. Only 2664 kB of never-scanned non-unique index cluster-wide (idx_rrg_signals_crossing, idx_scs_flag_count_confidence, idx_companies_cik). Nothing worth reporting; the big low-use ones (idx_mv_52week_extremes_date 67 MB / 66 scans, idx_ta_date 110 MB / 574 scans) are the MAX(date) lookups and are legitimate. NO FINDING.
6. Missing indexes for the hot MCP shapes — checked `pg_stat_user_tables` seq_scan counters: stock_prices 1,348 seq scans / 7.18 billion tuples read, technical_indicators 410 / 2.12 billion, companies 17,918 / 103 million. EXPLAINed the full-universe latest-price shape: it already uses an index-only scan on idx_stock_prices_ticker_date, so the cost is the 10M-row walk and the VM decay, not a missing index. I found no query shape in mcp_server/tools/ that lacks a usable index. NO MISSING-INDEX FINDING (the cost is folded into finding 2).
7. Connection budget — `max_connections`=100 (configuration file), superuser_reserved 3, no `datconnlimit`/`rolconnlimit` (both -1), no `pg_db_role_setting` rows. Live `pg_stat_activity`: 7 background + 3 `eos`/postgres.js job-queue pollers + 2 idle MCP pool connections + 1 transient. Consumers counted from code: MCP pool `MCP_POOL_MIN_SIZE=2 / MCP_POOL_MAX_SIZE=10` (mcp_server/database.py:63-65), ta-backfill `--workers` default 4 (sawa/cli.py:1271), stock_character batch `--workers` default 4 (sawa/cli.py:1474), weekly `character_workers=4` (sawa/weekly.py:208), daily 1-2, external turbulence cron (weekday 21:15 UTC), eos app 3. Realistic worst case ~25-30 of 97 usable. CONCLUSION: the connection budget is NOT at risk; no finding. (Caveat noted only here: `--workers` has no argparse upper bound and `sawa/ta_backfill.py:54` opens a *fresh* `psycopg.connect` per ticker inside each worker, so a full 10.4k-ticker backfill forks ~10,400 backends; wasteful but not a correctness or availability defect.)
8. Port exposure as a connection-exhaustion vector — `ss -ltnp` shows `*:5432` via rootlessport and pg_hba's last line is `host all all all scram-sha-256`, but `ufw status` is active with `-P INPUT DROP` and only 45454/tcp allowed on tailscale0, so 5432 is not reachable off-host. NOT A FINDING (out of dimension anyway).
9. statement_timeout / lock_timeout / idle_in_transaction_session_timeout — `pg_settings` boot_val and reset_val are 0 for all of statement_timeout, lock_timeout, idle_in_transaction_session_timeout, idle_session_timeout, transaction_timeout; `pg_db_role_setting` is empty; grep over sawa/ mcp_server/ scripts/ finds exactly one setter, `mcp_server/database.py:386` (`SET LOCAL statement_timeout = 30s`). -> FINDING 5.
10. Can a long MCP read block the nightly writer (or vice versa)? Traced `get_connection()` (mcp_server/database.py:245-256) -> `psycopg_pool.ConnectionPool.connection()`, which commits/rolls back on exit, plus a `reset` callback issuing DISCARD ALL; the observed `idle in transaction` backend had xact_age 0.15 s. So MCP holds ACCESS SHARE for at most the 30 s statement bound. The reverse direction is the real one: the only ACCESS EXCLUSIVE taker is `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW mv_52week_extremes` (sawa/daily.py:411) which runs with lock_timeout=0. -> FINDINGS 5 and 8.
11. Locks right now — `pg_locks` grouped: 5 AccessShareLock + 2 virtualxid, all granted. No waiters, `pg_stat_database.deadlocks`=0 for both databases. NO FINDING.
12. Long-running / xmin-pinning sessions — `pg_stat_activity WHERE backend_xmin IS NOT NULL`: 2 rows, both xact_age < 1 s. `pg_replication_slots` and `pg_prepared_xacts` both empty (no WAL/xmin pinning). NO FINDING.
13. Transaction-ID wraparound — max `age(relfrozenxid)` across public is 9,372,847 (gics_overrides) against autovacuum_freeze_max_age 200,000,000; max mxid_age 7,457. NO RISK, no finding.
14. mv_52week_extremes refresh cost — measured size: 893 MB total (550 MB heap + 343 MB index), 10,041,418 rows, 3 lifetime refreshes (n_tup_ins 30,092,805). Did NOT run a refresh. `EXPLAIN` of the defining query shows `WindowAgg` over an `Index Scan using stock_prices_pkey`, cost 1,015,291 — no sort spill, so the cost is the 10M-row walk plus a full 893 MB rewrite and index rebuild under ACCESS EXCLUSIVE (round 1 measured 6m21s in the log). -> FINDING 8.
15. WAL and checkpoints — `pg_stat_wal`: 1,070,107,074 records / 31,329,565 full-page images / 295,684,938,595 bytes (295.7 GB) since 2026-06-04 = 3.25 GB/day; wal_buffers_full 7,071,182. `pg_stat_checkpointer`: num_timed 26,078, num_requested 277, write_time 1,095,869,718 ms. `pg_ls_waldir`: 64 files / 1024 MB (pinned at max_wal_size). wal_compression off, wal_buffers 512 (4 MB). -> FINDING 7.
16. Server configuration vs host — dumped the container's postgresql.conf: it is the untouched postgres:18 image default (shared_buffers 128MB, max_connections 100, max_wal_size 1GB) and everything else reads `source = default` (work_mem 4MB, maintenance_work_mem 64MB, effective_cache_size 4GB, random_page_cost 4, default_statistics_target 100, jit on). Host: 23 GiB RAM / 4 vCPU, DB 7234 MB. `pg_stat_database` for `postgres`: blks_read 270,676,032 (2.06 TB) vs blks_hit 2,511,396,793 = 90.27% hit; temp_files 8,929 / temp_bytes 69 GB. -> FINDING 3.
17. Table and database sizes vs the volume on /data — `df -h /data`: 137 G, 43 G used, 88 G available (33%). DB 7234 MB, all public relations 9385 MB, WAL 1 GB, `/data/db-backups` 34 G (27 G of tars + 7.4 G migration-2026-06-04 dir). Root filesystem `/` is 83% full (7.9 G free) but holds no database data (podman graphroot is under /data). NO SPACE FINDING.
18. Backup / recovery posture — read `/home/seed/scripts/backup_postgres.sh`, `crontab -l`, `/data/db-backups/backup.log`, and `pg_settings` for archive_mode/archive_command/wal_keep_size/summarize_wal. -> FINDING 1 (critical).
19. Database-health monitoring coverage — grepped sawa/, mcp_server/, scripts/ for pg_stat_user_tables, n_dead_tup, pg_stat_activity, pg_database_size, max_connections, VACUUM, autovacuum, backup. Only hits are two read-only uses inside `mcp_server/tools/schema.py`. Listed all 16 `sawa doctor` check names — all are data-domain freshness/coverage checks. -> FINDING 6.
20. Second database on the same cluster — `eos` (62 MB, 14.2M commits, three postgres.js pollers on a `jobs` table). It shares max_connections and shared_buffers with sawa but uses ~3 slots; not a budget risk. Noted, no finding.

NOT COVERED / COULD NOT CHECK
- `pg_stat_statements` is not installed (only plpgsql), so I could not attribute the 8,929 temp files / 69 GB of temp bytes or the 1,348 stock_prices sequential scans to specific queries. The aggregate numbers in finding 3 are real; the per-query breakdown is unavailable.
- `pgstattuple` / `pgstatindex` are not installed, so heap and index bloat are estimated from `n_dead_tup` ratios and bytes-per-tuple, not measured page-by-page. The bytes-per-tuple figures (36.2 for stock_prices_pkey vs 36.1 for the DESC twin, 37.4 for idx_ta_ticker_date_desc vs 36.3 for the PK) are consistent with modest, not severe, index bloat.
- I did not run `REFRESH MATERIALIZED VIEW` (instructed not to), so finding 8's lock duration relies on round 1's measured 6m21s plus the size/plan numbers I did collect.
- Postgres server log files inside the container were not read (no log_min_duration_statement is set anyway, so there is no slow-query log to read).
- I did not test the daily/weekly job under load, so the interaction between an in-flight nightly write and a concurrent MCP read was reasoned from lock modes and code, not observed.

ADDITIONAL SMALLER ITEMS, deliberately not filed as findings: `jit=on` with jit_above_cost 100000 on a 4-vCPU box adds a few ms to every large screener plan (measured 3.7 ms emission on the query in check 3) — noise, not a defect; `autovacuum_worker_slots=16` in postgresql.conf against `autovacuum_max_workers=3` is the PG18 image default and harmless; `maintenance_work_mem=64MB` makes any index rebuild on the 10M-row tables (migrations 24/31/42/48, matview refresh) an external merge sort, which lengthens migration windows but is subsumed by finding 3.

### volume-and-archive (gap)

SCOPE PART 1 — VOLUME COLLAPSE BLAST RADIUS (measured, not estimated)

Checks run and what they showed:
1. Cumulative reverse factor per ticker = exp(sum(ln(split_from/split_to))) over stock_splits. Result: 1,447 tickers have splits; 855 have cumulative reverse factor >10x, 314 >100x, 158 >1000x.
2. Zero-volume census over ALL of stock_prices: 79,909 rows / 1,727 tickers have volume=0. Breakdown: 63,661 have open=high=low=close (the genuine ETF/no-trade class); 16,248 have high>low (proof the stock traded) — and every one of those 16,248 is on a ticker in the >10x reverse-split set (81 tickers, 2021-02-18..2026-04-17). Cross-check: the same join restricted to the deep set returns exactly 16,248 / 81, so the collapsed class and the deep-reverse-split set coincide perfectly.
3. Per-ticker damage (query in finding 1): WHLR 961/1385 bars (69.4%), VMAR 893 (64.1%), XXII 873 (62.7%), ADTX 822 (65.1%), CETX 767, APVO 646, SXTC 636, GNLN 595, ZCMD 579, PAVS 548, SMX 538, PPCB 462, EJH 455, NUWE 428, CMCT 407, RNAZ 394, VCIG 378, CNSP 361, EZGO 350, EDBL 339, GCTK 302, TRNR 272, ACON 267, DBGI 255, WOK 243, ALLR 243, HCTI 234, JAGX 230, KXIN 206, CDT 203 … (45 shown, 81 total).
4. Damage beyond volume=0 (quantization, not just collapse): on the deep set, 22,781 traded bars sit at 1-9 shares and 79,617 at <100 shares across 390 tickers. Adjacent-day proof of quantization: XXII 2024-09-03..09-10 alternates 1,0,1,0,1,1.
5. Verified the earlier sweep's blind spot arithmetically: of the 79,909 zero-volume rows, 28,808 are dated >=2024-09-03 AND have open=high=low=close — matching the "~28,800 cleared as genuine ETF no-trade days". 15,066 traded-but-zero bars predate 2024-09-03 (outside the window) and a further 1,182 fall inside the window but fail the o=h=l=c shape filter. So the entire 16,248-bar class was excluded by construction.
6. Traced the destruction points in code: (a) sawa/domain/price_validation.py:76-79 normalize_provider_volume rounds Polygon's fractional adjusted volume half-up, so <0.5 -> 0; every fetch is adjusted=true (sawa/api/client.py:482, sawa/daily.py:206, sawa/repositories/polygon_prices.py:181 all hardcode it); (b) sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:447-455 SplitAdjuster.adjust_row multiplies volume by the factor and rounds, with a range check but no zero guard; (c) sawa/split_adjust.py:279 does the same in SQL (`round(volume * num / den)::bigint`). No caller anywhere passes adjusted=false (grep for adjusted=False/`"adjusted": "false"` returns nothing).
7. Recoverability: ran ONE Polygon GET (aggs XXII 2024-09-03..09-06, adjusted=false) — returns v=492764 / 338562 / 485253 / 449243 against stored 1/0/1/0. Unadjusted volume is fully available from the provider inside its rolling window. Separately, the coldstart flat-file path receives exact as-traded volume and discards it at write time (sawa/database/load.py:633 passes SplitAdjuster.adjust_row as row_transform), so the local loss is self-inflicted, not a provider limitation.
8. Stored-artifact damage in technical_indicators (deep set): volume_sma_20=0 on 14,428 rows / 66 tickers; dollar_volume_sma_20=0 on 8,744 rows / 44 tickers; volume_ratio NULL on 8,744; volume_sma_20<100 on 67,091 rows. OBV verified frozen (ACON obv stuck at 166 across a month of zero-volume bars). Confirmed the LATEST date is clean: on 2026-09-02, 819 deep-ticker TA rows have zero rows with volume_sma_20=0 or dollar_volume_sma_20=0 (only 12 with volume_sma_20<1000) — the damage is historical, which is why nothing has alerted.
9. MCP tools exercised live: detect_volume_anomalies(LBGJ, 252) returns 44 spikes / 143 drops / 179 divergences / 42 climactic events, all from volumes of 0-6 shares, warnings=[]; get_advanced_volume_indicators(WHLR, 252) returns a series whose volume runs 57 -> 47,701,078 within one window and whose OBV/AD/CMF signal ("OBV bearish, confirms downtrend") is dominated entirely by the last few bars; get_volume_profile(LBGJ, 120) returns POC $112 with 98.8% of volume in one bin across a 2.10-2200 price range while the stock trades at ~$2. get_volume_leaders was read (mcp_server/tools/movers.py:206-276) — it sorts on p.volume / p.volume*p.close / ti.volume_ratio on the LATEST EOD date only, and I verified no ticker on 2026-09-02 has volume_ratio>20 or volume_sma_20=0, so that tool is NOT currently affected. screen_stocks/screen_technical_indicators liquidity filters (mcp_server/tools/screener.py:153, 767-769, 959-961) likewise bind to the latest bar and are not currently affected.
10. Detection gap: grepped sawa/doctor.py for volume — the only check is sawa/doctor.py:363-377, which inspects only the latest stock_prices date and only rejects volume IS NULL / volume < 0. sqlschema/44_ohlcv_completeness.sql's CHECK explicitly permits volume >= 0. No loader counts or logs a bar whose volume collapsed.

SCOPE PART 2 — stock_prices_backup PROVENANCE (established)
11. Profile: 4,680,795 rows, 5,692 tickers, 2021-02-12..2026-02-25, 376 MB, columns NUMERIC(12,4) (the pre-migration-24 shape). Its created_at column runs 2026-02-12 01:19 .. 2026-02-26 02:17, i.e. it carries the created_at of the original coldstart load, so it is a CREATE TABLE AS snapshot of stock_prices taken on/just after 2026-02-26 02:17.
12. Dated the creator: `git log --diff-filter=A` shows sqlschema/24_widen_price_precision.sql was added 2026-02-26 in commit 8209c75 "feat: auto-refresh split-adjusted prices after stock splits" — the same day. The snapshot is the manual safety copy taken before that migration widened NUMERIC(12,4)->(20,8) and before the first split-adjust rewrite. Nothing in the repo creates, reads, references or documents it (grep for "stock_prices_backup" across the whole tree and `git log -S` across all branches return zero hits); pg_stat shows 4,680,795 inserts, 0 updates, 0 deletes, 46 lifetime scans.
13. Price basis established empirically: on 2025-06-02, 3,821 of 4,127 common rows match stock_prices exactly and 306 differ; for every differing ticker backup_close/current_close equals exactly the inverse of the split factor recorded after 2026-02-26 (ADV 0.0400, AEHL 0.0104, AEMD 0.2000, AERT 0.1250, AIFU 0.0500, AKTX 0.0250, APH 2.0000, ...). So it is a coherent split-adjusted snapshot frozen on the 2026-02-26 basis — correct then, stale for every split since.
14. Overlap/coverage: 854 of its 5,692 tickers no longer exist in companies or stock_prices, accounting for 372,924 of the 373,241 backup rows that have no current counterpart. It contains 713 of the 855 deep-reverse tickers, but not WHLR (its NUMERIC(12,4) ceiling could not hold WHLR's adjusted prices; max close in the table is 62,820,360 against a 99,999,999.9999 limit). It overlaps stock_prices_unadjustable_archive on 242 of that table's 379 rows.
15. Its unique value, measured: of the 16,248 destroyed volume bars, 13,426 exist in the backup and 8,125 of those carry a NON-ZERO volume — i.e. the backup is the only local source of pre-collapse volume for half the destroyed bars.

NOT CHECKED / LIMITS
- I did not verify the true raw volume for any ticker other than XXII (one Polygon GET only, per the rules), so per-ticker recovery ratios elsewhere are inferred from the recorded split factors, not measured.
- I did not attempt to distinguish genuinely thin trading from quantization inside the 10-99 share band (40,588 bars); only the 0 and 1-9 bands are stated as destroyed.
- I did not check stock_prices_intraday volume (intraday bars are not split-adjusted retroactively in the same way) or market_internals volume fields.
- I did not measure how far mv_52week_extremes, get_top_movers or the trader_cards/stock_character tables consume the damaged volume columns.
- pytest was not run; no writes of any kind were attempted.
- ADDITIONAL SMALLER OBSERVATIONS not raised as findings: (a) get_volume_profile truncates each bin's share with int(bar_volume*fraction), so bins under-sum total_volume — immaterial for normal tickers (XXII's percentages summed to 100.02) and therefore not reported; (b) SplitAdjuster's docstring at sawa/domain/corporate_actions.py:353 documents the volume multiplication as intended behaviour with no mention of the integer floor, so the collapse is undocumented rather than accepted; (c) technical_indicator_metadata has no entry describing volume_sma_20's bigint rounding.

### scheduling-and-doctor-gaps (gap)

SCHEDULING MAP (established exhaustively; triggers = user crontab only)

`crontab -l` (user seed) has exactly 3 entries: (1) `*/15 * * * * /home/seed/code/sawa/scripts/market_scheduler.sh`, (2) turbulence chart generation (other repo), (3) `0 1 * * 0 /home/seed/scripts/backup_postgres.sh`. `systemctl --user list-timers --all` -> only launchpadlib-cache-clean; system `list-timers` -> no sawa units; /etc/cron.d has no sawa entry. So market_scheduler.sh is the sole sawa trigger.

market_scheduler.sh main() (lines 663-729) can start exactly four things:
- `sawa intraday` — every tick while Polygon says NYSE open (start_intraday, :424).
- `sawa daily` — first tick with hour >= 17 ET on ANY day (weekends included, no trading-day gate), once per ET calendar day via daily_done_<date> flag (:561-608).
- `sawa weekly` — first closed-market tick with hour >= 17 ET in each ISO week (:709-722). In practice this is MONDAY evening, not Saturday: weekly_done flags are dated Mon Jun 29, Jul 6/13/20/27, Aug 3/10/17/24 and stock_character_classification.run_date is Monday for every week since 2026-06-15.
- `sawa doctor --job daily` and `--job weekly` after each successful job (run_doctor, :112-128).

Commands with NO scheduled trigger anywhere (verified by grep over crontab, scripts/*.sh, systemd timers): `sawa quarterly` (grep -c quarterly scripts/market_scheduler.sh = 0; zero quarterly_*.log has ever existed in logs/ — only intraday/daily/doctor/weekly), `sawa index-update`, `sawa index-check`, `sawa add-symbol`, `sawa adjust-splits`, `sawa ta-backfill`, `sawa coldstart`, `sawa data-status`, `sawa doctor --job all|quarterly|coldstart`, scripts/populate_earnings.py, scripts/backfill_{gics_overrides,sic_codes,market_internals}.py, scripts/add_missing_etfs.sh, scripts/{quarantine,restore}_*.py. `sawa character` and `sawa corporate-actions` have no standalone trigger but run as weekly steps; market internals + news + TA + intraday retention cleanup run as daily steps (sawa/daily.py:868 calls cleanup_old_intraday_data, so the cleanup_intraday module docstring's suggested cron entry is not needed). scripts/daily.sh and scripts/weekly.sh are unused alternatives (not in crontab).

DOCTOR CHECK INVENTORY (sawa/doctor.py; severity + threshold + live status today)
Schema existence per table: FAIL, and a failure early-returns (:757-759) masking every other check.
Price block (runs for every job): companies.active_count>0 FAIL | companies.attribute_completeness <=30% NULL sic/mcap WARN (currently 58%/49% -> WARN, accepted) | stock_prices.expected_universe>0 FAIL | stock_prices.latest_date <=5 days FAIL | stock_prices.total_tickers >=85% of universe (all-time distinct) FAIL | stock_prices.total_rows >= universe FAIL (trivial, 10M rows) | stock_prices.latest_coverage >= ceil(0.85 x baseline) FAIL | stock_prices.latest_rows>0 WARN | stock_prices.latest_ohlcv_sanity=0 FAIL.
Daily block: technical_indicators.latest_date <=4 days FAIL | technical_indicators.coverage >=85% (all-time distinct) FAIL | technical_indicators.post_split_recompute WARN (60-day split window, 2% tolerance) | market_internals.{vix,vix3m,hy_spread}.latest_date <=5 days FAIL (live 09-03/09-03/09-02 -> PASS) | news_articles.recent <=14 days WARN | mv_52week_extremes.freshness (max-date equality only) WARN.
Weekly block: treasury_yields <=8 days FAIL (live 2026-08-31 PASS) | inflation/inflation_expectations/labor_market <=120 days WARN (live 2026-07-01/2026-08-01/2026-07-01 -> PASS; a 120-day bar on a monthly series tolerates 3-4 missed releases, and it only inspects MAX(date), never column content — that is why inflation.cpi_year_over_year being NULL since 2025-05 is invisible) | stock_character_classification.latest_run <=21 days FAIL (live 2026-09-02 PASS) | stock_character_classification.coverage >=85% WARN | stock_splits.readable / dividends.readable WARN (row-count only, always passes).
Quarterly block: financial_ratios/balance_sheets/income_statements/cash_flows latest_date <=210 days, FAIL severity — never invoked (see findings).

FAILURE MODES WITH NO DOCTOR CHECK AT ALL (grep of sawa/doctor.py returns 0 occurrences for each term): earnings (any freshness/eps_actual check), index_constituents (110-day staleness), stock_prices_intraday / intraday session truncation, obv, vwap (cumulative-indicator corruption), migration / schema_migrations / column-type drift, backup, delisted / companies.active flipping to false. Round 1 already reported the intraday, migration-drift and index-constituent gaps; the earnings, cumulative-indicator, backup and delisted gaps are reported here.

OTHER CHECKS I RAN THAT FOUND NOTHING WRONG
- Whether the Labor-Day-style holiday breaks the daily: I first suspected the 7 failed daily runs on Mon 2026-08-31 were a market-holiday failure, then verified 2026-08-31 was a normal session (10,025 price rows) and that the failure was the (now-fixed) fractional-volume provider drift ("Rejected 114519/120700 malformed provider price rows", logs/daily_20260831_210002.log:10655). Checked the real holiday 2026-07-03 (no price rows between 07-02 and 07-06); no logs survive from then, and the daily's 14-day catch-up window means a holiday behaves like the Saturday/Sunday runs, which succeed (scheduler.log 2026-08-30 "Daily completed: Latest prices: 2026-08-28"). NOT a defect — dropped.
- The scheduler double-notifying on every failure (visible on 2026-08-31) is already fixed: notify_unreported_failure landed in 0781f4b on 2026-09-02.
- The weekly's repeated failures on 2026-09-01 ("flag replacement verification failed for BBW/2026-09-01: expected 5 identity row(s), found 5", sawa/database/stock_character.py:326-335) are the Postgres-collation-vs-Python-sort bug, also fixed in 0781f4b — the code now sorts both sides in Python. NOT re-reported.
- Verified the daily/weekly retry cadence is hourly, not 15-minutely, because each run takes ~57 min and flock (:31-40) blocks intervening ticks — this softens, but does not remove, the round-1 retry-storm finding.
- Verified get_market_date() (sawa/utils/market_hours.py:10-17) returns the ET calendar date, so doctor freshness comparisons are ET-correct.
- Verified the doctor is only ever run AFTER a successful job, so during the 7-hour 2026-08-31 outage no health check ran at all (consequence of the same code path as finding 3; not separately reported).
- Ran the exact _POST_SPLIT_TA_QUERY live: in_window=163, checked=163, flagged=0 — the check currently passes.
- Compared mv_52week_extremes to a recomputed 252-row window for the 12 re-fetched tickers (ANET APH CMG DECK DELL ETR GE SRE WRB WSM HUIZ): all 0.0% off — tonight's daily run refreshed the matview, so the pre-split APH state round 1 saw has since been repaired.

CAVEATS / NOT COVERED
- The `sawa daily` run of 2026-09-03 was executing while I audited (started 17:00 ET, log daily_20260903_210002.log), so stock_prices/technical_indicators were mutating under me; the mid-run snapshot I cite for the TA-coverage finding was taken at 22:10 UTC and re-checked after completion (9,981/9,981).
- I did not exercise `sawa doctor` itself (it goes through monitored_run, which pushes ntfy notifications and writes logs) — every doctor result above is reproduced by running its own SQL read-only in psql or by reading sawa/doctor.py.
- I did not audit the MCP server's own scheduling/監視, the turbulence cron entry, or log-rotation/disk growth (round 1 covered the latter).
- I did not test restoring one of the surviving backup tarballs, so I can only assert their age, not their integrity.

Reported by a reviewer, then dropped because a verifier could not reproduce it, found it intentional, or judged the impact negligible.
